# Requirements Document
## DRISHTA — Beyond Noise
*Personal Portfolio Intelligence App*

**Version:** 2.0  
**Date:** June 2026  
**Status:** Active Development  
**Operating Posture:** Decides, not informs (see §2A)

---

## 1. Purpose and Scope

This application is a personal investment management and intelligence platform built for a retail investor managing a concentrated US equity portfolio. It replaces manual spreadsheet tracking and fragmented news/analysis tools with a single, always-current dashboard that surfaces actionable signals, manages risk, and builds a track record of decisions over time.

The app is configured to **make decisions, not merely inform**. Recommendations are presented as the call; suppressions are hard rather than soft. The app does not execute trades — the user retains all execution authority — but its recommendations are designed to be trusted and acted upon directly. See §2A for the operating-posture commitments that shape every requirement below.

The app is not a brokerage or order-execution system.

---

## 2. User Profile

- **Primary user:** Individual retail investor
- **Portfolio size:** Variable; moderate risk tolerance
- **Experience level:** Active investor, growing familiarity with technical and fundamental analysis
- **Usage pattern:** Daily briefing check before market open; ad-hoc analysis during trading hours; end-of-day journal entries
- **Access:** Web browser via Railway Hobby at `drishta.up.railway.app` — **primary since the 2026-08-15 cutover**. Streamlit Community Cloud is kept as a **dormant cold fallback** on the same Supabase DB, not the deploy changes are verified against; no mobile-specific UI required

---

## 2A. Operating Posture and Decision Policy

This is an explicit operating-mode commitment that drives functional design. It is the most important policy section in this document because every gate, threshold, and suppression below derives from it.

### 2A.1 Operating principles

| ID | Principle |
|---|---|
| OP-01 | The app makes decisions. Recommendations are issued only when conditions warrant action. The app would rather recommend nothing than recommend wrongly. |
| OP-02 | Gates are hard suppressions, not soft warnings. When a gate fires, the contradicting recommendation is removed from the UI with an explicit explanation, not appended as a caution badge. |
| OP-03 | Data integrity failures fail loud. When a required input is missing (stop price, composite score, daily briefing, portfolio risk metrics) the dependent feature surfaces an explicit "offline" or "unavailable" state. Fabricated fallbacks are never used. |
| OP-04 | Cross-feature coordination is mandatory. Two features that make overlapping decisions never silently contradict each other. The pattern is: one publishes state; downstream features read and gate. |
| OP-05 | Secondary objectives subordinate to investment view. Tax outcomes do not override the investment thesis (HARVEST suppressed on Buy/Strong Buy). Drift-rebalancing does not override active risk reduction (Rebalancer ADD suppressed when Risk Advisor TRIMs the same ticker). |
| OP-06 | All decision thresholds live in `stock_analyzer/constants.py`. Changes to any value are investment-policy decisions, not code tuning. |

### 2A.2 Decision thresholds (policy)

Single source of truth: `stock_analyzer/constants.py`.

| Threshold | Value | Type |
|---|---|---|
| Portfolio beta — target | 1.0 | Baseline |
| Portfolio beta — elevated (soft warn) | 1.3 | Soft |
| Portfolio beta — ceiling (hard breach) | 1.4 | **Hard** |
| Ticker beta — high (combines with elevated portfolio) | 1.5 | Soft |
| Ticker beta — critical (combines with breached portfolio) | 1.8 | **Hard** |
| Sector — ceiling | 35% | **Hard** |
| Sector — elevated | 25% | Soft |
| Single-name — ceiling | 15% | **Hard** |
| Single-name — NEW-position cap, % of net capital (`NET_CAPITAL_POSITION_CAP_PCT`, F-255) | 25% | **Hard** |
| Composite — Buy boundary (entry AND add-to-winner) | 65 | Gate |
| Composite — Strong Buy boundary | 75 | Tier label |
| Composite — Hold floor (below = Sell zone) | 44 | Tier label |
| Fundamentals gate — min core metrics to trust the verdict | 1 | **Hard** (withhold verdict below it) |
| Entry reward:risk minimum (`RR_ENTRY_MIN`) | 2.0 | **Hard** on Watchlist ENTER_NOW (G-13); caveat on Analysis |
| Risk per trade | 1.5% of portfolio | Sizing |
| Earnings imminence window (`EARNINGS_IMMINENT_DAYS`) | 7 days | Caution (binary-event conflict) |
| Earnings "manageable window" — Brief verdict agreed signal (`EARNINGS_MANAGEABLE_DAYS`) | 8–21 days | Tier label |
| Earnings urgency "SOON" tier — Catalyst Watch playbook (`EARNINGS_URGENCY_SOON_DAYS`) | 8–14 days | Display tier |
| Macro imminence window (HIGH-impact event in pick's sector) | 3 days | **Hard** |
| Catalyst Watch forward window (`CATALYST_WATCH_WINDOW_DAYS`) | 7 days | Awareness |
| Review profit-lock — P&L trigger / trim size | 25% / 25% of position | Action target |
| Review stop-tighten ATR multiple (vs 2.0× initial) | 1.5× | Action target |
| Review earnings-overweight — trim trigger / target weight | 12% / 10% | Action target |
| Review weak-large — trim-to target weight | 8% | Action target |
| Review macro-affected — sector trigger / reduction | 30% / 5pp | Action target |
| Exit deterioration — WATCH / TRIM / EXIT drawdown-from-peak floors (`DETERIORATION_*_DD_PCT`) | 6% / 8% / 12% | Action target (exit ladder) |
| Exit deterioration — ATR-scaled TRIM / EXIT floor (capped) | 2.5× / 3.5× ATR; capped 14% / 20% | Action target |
| Exit deterioration — EXIT escalation on unrealized $ loss | $250 | Action target |
| Exit deterioration — trend reference / confirmation | close < SMA50; 2 of last 3 sessions (EXIT skips confirm) | Confirmation |
| Risk-off de-risk — SPY trend trigger (`RISK_OFF_TREND_MA`) | below 200-DMA | Regime (Faber) |
| Risk-off de-risk — VIX trigger (`RISK_OFF_VIX_LEVEL`) | ≥ 25 | Regime |
| Risk-off de-risk — min name β / top-N / trim size | β ≥ 1.2 / top 3 by β×weight / 25% | Action target |
| Concentration — high-beta cluster share (soft warn, `CONCENTRATION_HIGHBETA_SHARE_WARN`) | 60% | Soft |
| Pullback fragility — routine-correction yardstick (`FRAGILITY_PULLBACK_PCT`) | −10% | Awareness |
| Protective alert email — premarket / EOD ET hour (`ALERT_EMAIL_HOUR_ET` / `ALERT_EOD_HOUR_ET`) | 08:00 / ≥ 16:00 ET | Operational |
| Reactive pullback alert — index drawdown trigger (`PULLBACK_ALERT_INDEX_PCT`) | −3% | Awareness |
| Mover — min 1-day gain to qualify | 5% | Discovery gate |
| Mover — shortlist size composite-gated / max picks surfaced | 12 / 3 | Discovery sizing |
| Price cross-check — prev_close tolerance (settled) | 0.5% | **Integrity** (strict) |
| Price cross-check — live-price tolerance (latency) | 3.0% | Soft (loose) |
| Data live-price provider order | Finnhub → yfinance → FMP | Sourcing policy |
| Data general provider order (history/bundle) | yfinance → Finnhub → FMP | Sourcing policy |

The "Review action target" rows translate a *trigger* (when an item lands in Review Before Close) into a *concrete directive* (trim N shares, raise stop to $Y). See §3.2 F-25 and architecture §4.0.1.

### 2A.3 Hard gates currently enforced

| ID | Gate | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| G-01 | Risk Advisor TRIM → Grow Today add-to-winner | Suppress; banner explains |
| G-02 | Risk Advisor TRIM → Rebalancer ADD | Suppress; banner explains |
| G-03 | News Intelligence alert → Rebalancer ADD | Attach `news_warning`; critical drops urgency to bottom |
| G-04 | Single-name ≥ 15% → Grow Today add-to-winner | Suppress; concentration banner |
| G-05 | Sector ≥ 35% → Watchlist ENTER_NOW | Downgrade to NEAR_ENTRY with portfolio-fit card |
| G-06 | Portfolio β > 1.4 AND ticker β > 1.8 → Watchlist ENTER_NOW | Downgrade to NEAR_ENTRY |
| G-07 | Imminent HIGH macro (3d) → Grow Today new picks in affected sector | Suppress; macro banner |
| G-08 | Composite Buy → Tax HARVEST | Suppress; `HOLD_FOR_SIGNAL` action with banner |
| G-09 | Rebalancer drift-overweight → Grow Today add-to-winner | Suppress; banner |
| G-10 | Earnings within 7 days → Buy Candidates verdict | Escalate to "Caution" or "Conflicted" depending on other signals |
| G-11 | Stop data missing → Act Today SELL trigger | Skip mechanical SELL; show "stop unavailable" review item instead |
| G-12 | Composite Signal empty on held position → Confirmed verdict | Route to "🔍 Verify — Composite Signal Missing" |
| G-13 | ENTER_NOW without validated R:R (`rr is None`) | Downgrade to NEAR_ENTRY; R:R must be `≥ 2.0` |
| G-14 | `build_daily_briefing` failure → coordination caches | Set to `None`; dependent features show explicit offline banner |
| G-15 | Fundamentals absent from live sources (< `FUNDAMENTALS_GATE_MIN_METRICS`) AND no fresh cache → Analysis verdict AND Brief new-pick | **First, fall back to last-known-good:** `load_all` serves the persistent `fundamentals_cache` copy when the live leg is sparse and the cache is within `FUNDAMENTALS_CACHE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` — the verdict then renders normally with an amber "📦 Fundamentals as of N days ago" note (`fund_source = "cache"`). **Only when there's no fresh cache** is the verdict withheld: on Analysis, BOTH the Summary Scorecard row (Score → "—", Signal → "🚫 Withheld") and the Detailed Analysis block (red "verdict withheld" note) suppress the fabricated-neutral-50 verdict; price-derived columns (Stop, Target, R:R) remain. `daily_briefing` holds the ticker out of `new_picks` (→ `composite_unavailable`). |
| G-16 | Sector at/above `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%) → Grow Today new picks AND add-to-winner in that sector | Suppress; render "Suppressed — Sector Hard Cap" banner (`sector_blocked_picks` / `sector_blocked_adds`). A Strong Buy in an over-cap sector is a KEEP, not an add — deploy-capital defers to protect-capital |
| G-17 | CPI YoY > `REGIME_CPI_CONTROLLED_MAX` (2.5%) → macro "Rate-Cut Optimism" regime | Block: the regime cannot be selected even if risk-on signals (low VIX, strong SPY) win the score, because its rationale claims "controlled inflation." Reassigns to the next-best regime. Prevents a label that contradicts itself (e.g. 3.95% CPI in a "controlled inflation" regime). |
| G-18 | Held position with breached stop (price ≤ stop, i.e. Act Today's Gap-to-Stop ≤ 0) → Analysis "add to position" sizing | Suppress the add: the Analysis Trade Plan replaces the "Sizing below is for adding" note with a red "⛔ Stop breached — exit signal, not an add" banner (mirrors Act Today's SELL) and hides the position-sizing block. The Buy composite still shows (it rates the STOCK); the stop protects the POSITION — protect-capital overrides deploy-capital. Same detector condition as the Brief, so the two surfaces agree instead of contradicting (ADBE: Brief SELL vs Analysis "add"). |
| G-20 | Active early-deterioration **WATCH** on a held name → Grow Today add-to-winner | Suppress the add (moved to `deterioration_blocked_adds`, rendered as a "🟡 Add Suppressed — Early Deterioration Watch" banner). A name below its trend MA / drawing down from its peak must not be nudged as an add even on a Strong Buy composite. WATCH is **entry-price-agnostic** — this is the principled add gate, deliberately chosen over an entry-price-dependent profit/P&L gate (the whole composite engine ignores the user's entry price). To preserve single-surface, the ticker also joins the "More Buy Candidates" dedup set (F-26a) so it can't reappear there as "ADD — Winning Position" (`_buy_candidates` annotates WATCH rather than suppressing). **Changed 2026-07-21 from annotate-only** — the prior annotate-on-the-add-card behaviour read as "add more to a name I'm telling you is weakening" (the FSLR case). |
| G-21 | Ticker with a same-day BUY (per `trades_df`, America/New_York calendar day) → Risk Advisor `sector_concentration` trim plan | Exclude the ticker from today's `trim_candidates`; the excluded name(s) surface separately in `trim_excluded_recent` and the Rebalance Plan renders a "🕐 Held back from today's plan" caption naming them — never a silent shortfall. Prevents the same Brief recommending a buy (Grow Today / More Buy Candidates) and, hours later on the same session, flagging that exact fresh position as the #1 full-exit trim candidate (the SHOP case, 2026-07-28). The ticker returns to the normal trim ranking the next day. (Implements F-221.) |
| G-19 | **Concentration-gate basis — EQUITY (invested equity). [Current policy, 2026-07-09 — reverses the 2026-06-26 net-capital basis.]** The single-name (15%) and sector (35%) hard gates (G-16, F-39b), the `risk_advisor` `single_name_concentration` / `sector_concentration` trim recs, the Trade-Journal entry nudge, and the watchlist / quick-research / comparison entry-fit checks ALL gate on **plain equity weight** = MV ÷ invested equity (`Gate Weight (%)` == equity `Weight (%)`). **Vocabulary warning, added 2026-08-27 — read this before quoting the word "equity" anywhere a user can see it.** "Invested equity" here means **the sum of every holding's market value, margin-bought shares included** — verified in code at `portfolio.py:383` (`total_val = df["Market Value"].sum()`, then `Weight (%) = Market Value / total_val * 100`): no cash, no margin debit, and no equity/gross split exists anywhere in the code. It is therefore **NOT the account's net worth**, which is the ordinary meaning of "equity" in a margin context and a different number entirely (on the owner's 2026-08-23 measured book: $24,503 of holdings vs $7,802 of capital). **F-254's "gross-book basis" and this row's "invested equity" name the SAME quantity** — the two phrasings have been mistaken for a contradiction, and they are not one. Because of that ambiguity, user-facing copy must say **"% of total holdings at market value"** rather than "equity-basis"; the internal `_gate_denom` metadata still carries `"basis": "equity"`, which is fine as an internal label but must not be echoed to the user (UX review I5, fixed 2026-08-27). **Why the reversal:** the 2026-06-26 net-capital "tighter-of-both" basis (`MV ÷ min(equity, net capital)`) let a **transient margin balance lurch a hard gate** — a sector read 166.9% and the app demanded ~85% liquidation off a temporary financing state, which fights §2B calm/anti-churn and conflates position-sizing (structural) with leverage (a different risk). Concentration is now judged on equity, which is **stable across transient margin**; the ceilings still fire on a genuinely over-concentrated book. **Leverage/margin risk is preserved as a separate awareness-only signal** (F-09d) — never a gate. One wiring point sets it (`app.py` ~2710: `Gate Weight (%)` = equity `Weight (%)`; `gate_denom` = equity → `risk_advisor` `_acct_f` = 1); `concentration.gating_denominator` is retired from the gate path. Do NOT re-base gates to net-capital without an explicit policy discussion (hard rule #1). Beta/Sharpe recs + display weights were already equity-basis. |
| G-22 | **NEW-position size above `NET_CAPITAL_POSITION_CAP_PCT` (25%) of net capital** (equity minus margin debit) — additive to G-04/G-16's gross-book `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`, not a replacement for it, and it does NOT change that gate's denominator (option (b), chosen with the user 2026-08-25; the option (c) recalibration of the existing gate's own basis was explicitly declined). | Cap the suggested size to the affordable share count; when the cap cannot afford even one share — or net capital is ≤ 0 — `risk.position_sizing` returns `None` and `sizing_unavailable_reason` returns `"capital"`, so **no size is proposed at all** rather than a smaller one. Wired into Grow Today, Analysis, Watchlist, the pullback-add flow and the cron/email buy-list; `notify._sizing_cap_note` discloses it in the email when it binds. Holdings already over the cap get an awareness-only 💰 Account banner that deliberately never feeds `risk_advisor`/`exit_advisor`. (Implements F-255.) |
| G-23 | Bear-day market tone → Grow Today new picks AND add-to-winner | `_grow_today` early-returns when the upstream tone is `bear` (S&P ≤ `MARKET_TONE_BEAR_PCT`, -0.5% — the comparison itself lives in the tone derivation at `app.py` ~5200 and its headless twin in `headless_alert_engine.py`, not in `_grow_today`). All new-entry and add-to-winner lanes are suppressed outright — no individual ticker is evaluated at all, and `cooldown_adds` / `deterioration_blocked_adds` are not even keys in that early return. The Gate Suppression Ledger (F-259) therefore records one synthetic row per (`rec_date`, `source`) for the tone gate, `ticker="__MARKET__"`, `gate_value=sp500_pct` — so the day the app performs its single largest act of restraint does not read as a day it found nothing to restrain. **Behaviour already shipped; only the id is new** (2026-08-27) — nothing about when this gate fires changed. |
| G-24 | Post-add cooldown (`ADD_WINNER_COOLDOWN_DAYS`) → Grow Today add-to-winner | Suppress an add-to-winner suggestion when the position was added within the last `ADD_WINNER_COOLDOWN_DAYS` (10) days (days 0-9 are suppressed; day 10 is not — `_recently_added` uses strict `<`). Prevents churning a freshly added position before the new shares can settle. Suppressed names appear in `cooldown_adds`. Behaviour already shipped; this id was added to the registry as part of W5 (2026-08-27). |

### 2A.4 Soft warnings (kept in addition to gates)

| ID | Warning | When |
|---|---|---|
| W-01 | Sector ≥ 25% concentration on a new entry | Soft caution in card |
| W-02 | Portfolio β > 1.3 AND ticker β > 1.5 | Soft caution; sizing recommendation |
| W-03 | Active HIGH risk alerts in portfolio | Resolve-first prompt in Watchlist |
| W-04 | Grow Today and Watchlist same-sector overlap | Inform the user one sector trade is enough today |
| W-05 | Trade Journal entry thesis missing on a weak-large-position review | Prompt user to log thesis on future entries |

---

## 2B. Intended Investor Persona and Time Horizon

This app is built for the **quality-first, medium-term investor with long-term tax discipline** — and a defensive bias. It is explicitly **not** a day-trading tool. This framing is a design constraint: features and gates are evaluated against it, and a request that only serves intraday trading is out of scope (see §5) unless the persona itself is revisited.

### 2B.1 Persona fit by horizon

| Persona | Horizon | Fit | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Day trader | seconds–hours | **Not supported** | The engine actively works against it (see 2B.2). |
| Swing trader | days–weeks | **Partial** | Technical signals, entry zones, ATR stops, R:R, Catalyst Watch and Movers serve this — but quality gates and the no-trade-into-earnings posture temper pure momentum. |
| Medium-term investor | weeks–months | **Primary (sweet spot)** | Composite scoring, sector rotation, macro-regime, rebalancing, diversification advisor all operate on this horizon. |
| Long-term investor | months–years | **Strong** | 40% fundamental weight, valuation/FCF, tax-loss harvesting, 1-yr cap-gains & wash-sale awareness, behavioural discipline. Fit strengthens with horizon. |

### 2B.2 Why the design is investor-oriented, not trader-oriented

| ID | Design choice | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| PH-01 | Composite = Technical 25% + **Business Quality 35%** + **Valuation 30%** + Sentiment 10%; verdict is WITHHELD when BQ fundamentals are unavailable. Analyst consensus (avg_pt + rating label from `analyst_coverage`) feeds the Valuation pillar; individual Ideas Inbox records remain awareness-only. | A combined 65% weight on business quality + valuation is central to an investor and irrelevant to an intraday trader. |
| PH-02 | Composite signals are computed on page load and held; only prices refresh (~60s). Signals do **not** recompute every tick. | Deliberate anti-overtrading choice (also a rate-limit guard). Intraday signal churn is intentionally absent. |
| PH-03 | Proximity gates suppress initiating into earnings; Catalyst Watch is awareness, **not** a buy rec. | The app declines binary-event volatility that a trader often seeks. |
| PH-04 | Stops are ATR-based, R:R is multi-day, sizing is risk-%-of-portfolio. | Swing-to-position constructs (holding across days/weeks), not intraday levels. |
| PH-05 | Macro-regime lens reads CPI / Fed / rates (months horizon); concentration caps, beta/Sharpe/Sortino/VaR, rebalancing, tax/wash-sale all operate at portfolio/horizon scale. | Portfolio-construction and protection, not per-trade edge capture. |
| PH-06 | Even Movers (1-day breakouts) are funnelled through the **same composite quality gate**, never traded on momentum alone. | The most opportunistic surface is still quality-first — the philosophy in miniature. |

### 2B.3 What day-trading support would require (fork, not extension)

Supporting day traders would mean intraday/real-time data feeds, Level 2 / order-flow, tick charts, removal of the fundamental weighting, and removal of the earnings/macro gates that make this app safe. That is a different product with an opposite risk philosophy — a fork, not an increment. Documented here so the boundary stays explicit.

---

## 3. Functional Requirements

### 3.1 My Portfolio (Home)

_Note (2026-07-13): this section's F-IDs describe functionality that originated on Home. Some still render there (F-01–F-05, F-08's core signal). The rest has since moved to standalone nav pages via a 3-phase nav cleanup (see `docs/architecture.md` §10 "Nav follow-up" rows) — **F-06/F-06a/F-07/F-10 → §3.1a Portfolio Overview** (PORTFOLIO nav group), **F-09/F-09a–d/F-14/F-14a/F-87a/F-168 → §3.1b Risk Analysis** (PORTFOLIO nav group), **F-13/F-13a → §3.1c Signals & Advice** (renamed from "Alerts & Actions" 2026-07-22, commit `291b0a9`; moved from the PORTFOLIO nav group into the renamed SIGNALS nav group 2026-07-26). The F-IDs below are unchanged (still the authoritative spec for that functionality) — only their page location changed; §§3.1a–3.1c point back here rather than duplicating the detail, and add F-IDs for the handful of features that never had one before the split (Active Alerts, Custom Price Alerts, Analytics tab)._

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-01 | **Holdings table — moved to 🧾 Summary (§3.1h, F-204), 2026-07-26.** Added to Home 2026-07-26, then relocated the same day once the standalone Summary page shipped, so the table isn't duplicated across two pages — Home itself still has no persistent holdings table (only the live per-ticker price-strip cards: ticker/price/day-change, no shares/value/weight). See §3.1h for the current spec: Ticker, Shares, live Price, Market Value, Day Δ%, Total Δ% (= `P&L (%)`), Weight (%), rendered via the shared `_render_holdings_table()` (module-level, `app.py` near `_m()`). Average Cost and raw P&L ($) are intentionally not repeated (they're on the fuller detail table on 🥧 Portfolio Overview, `app.py:11037`, alongside Stop/Signal/Score) — this remains a quick at-a-glance read, not a duplicate of that one. |
| F-02 | Auto-refresh live prices every 60 seconds during market hours via a Streamlit fragment. Prices come from the multi-source layer (§3.10) — real-time from Finnhub during market hours, with gap-fill to yfinance/FMP. The price-strip caption shows the actual source(s); a fail-loud banner appears if held-position prices fail the cross-check. |
| F-03 | Show real-time daily P&L (total portfolio $ and %) based on prior-close vs current price |
| F-03a | **Today's P&L — Tier B (true positions day-P&L).** Beyond the held-only prior-close mark (F-03), compute the day's P&L as an equity delta against a baseline persisted in the Supabase `daily_snapshots` table (per-ticker shares + close written by the EOD cron, F-141). This captures positions opened/closed intraday that a held-only mark misses. The label fails loud about its scope; Tier B is inert until the one-time `daily_snapshots` DDL is applied. **Still out of scope:** cash/flows reconciliation and full broker reconciliation. **Cross-page sync (2026-07-27):** Home publishes the computed Tier-B result to `st.session_state["_dpnl_cache"]` (dated, so a stale cross-day value is never reused); 🧾 Summary's "Today's P&L" tile consumes it so the two pages show the identical number instead of Summary independently recomputing the cheaper held-only mark. If Home hasn't run yet this session (e.g. landing directly on Summary), Summary falls back to its own held-mark calc, honestly relabeled "Today's P&L (held)" so the narrower scope is visible on the tile itself, not just in a tooltip. |
| F-03b | **Account-level view — account-baseline v1 (cash + total account value).** The app otherwise reasons only about invested equity; setting a user-entered **net cash** balance (single-row Supabase `account_cash`, manually entered now — the same table the Robinhood MCP sync would later auto-fill; **v4: the value is signed — NEGATIVE = a margin debit**, so Total/Growth/Return/account-concentration all net out the loan since they derive from total = equity + cash) unlocks a dedicated **💰 Account** nav page (between Home and Market Scanner) showing **Total Account Value** (equity + cash), **Invested Equity**, **Cash %**, and **true concentration** (each holding as % of the *whole account* alongside its equity weight). **Display-only:** the 15%/35% concentration GATES still fire on equity weight — moving them to account-basis is a deferred investment-policy decision. Inert until the one-time `account_cash` DDL is applied (load returns None → equity-only behaviour + a nudge to set cash). Cash entry is data-sanity validated (non-negative; soft-warns implausible) and read-only-viewer-aware. Out of v1: growth/return (needs a flows ledger, v2) and TWR/IRR (v3). Plan: `docs/plans/account-baseline.md`. |
| F-03c | **Growth vs contributions — account-baseline v2 (flows ledger).** On the 💰 Account page, a cash-flow ledger (Supabase `account_flows`: a `baseline` anchor + `deposit`/`withdrawal` rows; manual now, broker-fillable later) separates money **deposited** from money the **market made you**. **Net Contributed Capital** = baseline + deposits − withdrawals; **Growth $** = total account value − NCC; **Growth %** = growth ÷ NCC (rendered "—" when NCC ≤ 0 or the portfolio/cash isn't loaded — never a bogus number). The baseline defaults to the current total account value (growth-from-today) or accepts lifetime net deposits (all-time gain). A deposit raises total value and NCC equally, so it can never masquerade as growth. **v3 adds the money-weighted (Modified Dietz) return** — `R = (EMV − BMV − F) / (BMV + Σ Fᵢ·wᵢ)` — which corrects for deposit/withdrawal **timing** (the distortion the naive gain/NCC ignores), plus an **annualized** figure shown only once the tracking period ≥ 30 days. Modified Dietz (not daily TWR) because we have daily equity (`daily_snapshots`) but no daily cash history; it needs only endpoints + dated flows. Pure calc in `stock_analyzer/account.py`; **display-only — feeds no gate.** Inert until the `account_flows` DDL is applied (v3 reuses the same table — no extra DDL). The **MWR label now shows the baseline date** ("Return since YYYY-MM-DD") rather than a generic label, so the metric's time-scope is unambiguous next to the weekly email figure. Plan: `docs/plans/account-baseline.md`. **Annualization caveat (2026-07-28):** when the annualized figure renders, `account.annualization_caveat(days, is_levered)` may add an explanatory caption below it — triggered when the tracking window is under `_ANNUALIZE_CAVEAT_MAX_DAYS` (90, a display-only floor kept local to `account.py`, not `constants.py`, since it never affects a gate/recommendation) and/or the account carries a margin debit (`_levered = _cash < 0`), both of which amplify a real but modest dollar move into a much larger-looking annualized rate. Pure display context; the underlying number is unchanged. |
| F-03d | **Capital Trend chart — account-baseline v4 (equity timeline with NCC overlay, SHIPPED 2026-07-19).** On the 💰 Account page, a `📈 Capital Trend` section (below Growth & Contributions, no extra tab) renders a Plotly area chart built from `stock_analyzer/account.build_equity_timeseries()` — daily equity totals aggregated from `daily_snapshots` (shares × close price per date) paired with a forward-filled NCC step function (from `account_flows`). Three visual layers: (1) **green filled-area equity line** (held-position mark-to-market); (2) **dotted NCC reference line** (contributed-capital floor — gap above = market-made growth, gap below = underwater); (3) **amber/green diamond marker** at the last chart date showing `_total_value` (equity + signed cash) — when carrying a margin debit the diamond falls below the equity line, and its distance below NCC is the exact source of the negative "Return since…" figure in Growth & Contributions above. A weekly / monthly / All granularity toggle filters the chart window. Auto-narration below the chart states the equity move in $ and % vs NCC, flags a material peak if one exists, and — when cash is negative — explains net account value vs contributed capital explicitly so the −X.X% return is traceable without ambiguity. **Bridges the 'positions look up but account is down' confusion** inherent in a leveraged account: the chart shows all three levels simultaneously so the story from equity growth through contributed capital to net account value is readable in one view. `build_equity_timeseries()` is a pure function in `stock_analyzer/account.py`; data source is `db.load_daily_snapshots()` accumulated by the EOD cron. Display-only; feeds no gate. |
| F-217 | **Day Shock awareness banner (Home).** Below the live price strip, a conditional banner flags any held ticker moving ≥`DAY_SHOCK_PCT` (5.0%, up or down) same-day, using the `change_pct`/`prev_close` already returned by `fetch_live_prices()` — no extra fetch. Renders only when at least one ticker crosses the threshold (nothing shown otherwise); each flagged ticker gets a red (down) or green (up) pill chip with the exact move and the prev-close→current price. **Awareness only — never alters `exit_advisor.classify_deterioration_tier`'s WATCH/TRIM/EXIT tier or any recommendation.** Exists because a single-day shock that stays above the 50-day MA doesn't trip the deterioration tier's trend-break condition and would otherwise be visible only as one equal-weight badge among several in the price strip, with nothing calling attention to its magnitude. Publishes `st.session_state["_day_shock_cache"]` (`{ticker: {price, prev_close, chg_pct}}`), read-only, no other consumer yet. |
| F-218 | **Structural alert banner (Home) — SHIPPED 2026-07-27.** Below the Day Shock banner (F-217), a conditional banner flags a newly-formed correlation cluster among held positions since the last 🧬 Structural Scan (F-198's tab on 🧩 Intelligence). Full detection mechanism (`detect_new_clusters()`, the `.lte` baseline lookup, the signed direct-edge citation guard) is specified under F-198, which this entry cross-references rather than duplicates. Renders only when at least one new cluster is detected; each flagged cluster shows its member tickers, tier, combined weight, and the specific verified pair(s) driving the flag. Placed after the Home page's `_home_synth_cache` hit/miss synthesis converges (later in the page than F-217) because `_corr_df_cache` isn't freshly published for the current render until that synthesis block completes — inserting it alongside F-217 would read the previous render's stale correlation data. **Awareness only — never alters the composite score or any gate.** Publishes `st.session_state["_structural_alert_cache"]` (`None` = correlation data offline this session, `[]` = checked and nothing new, else the flagged-cluster list), no other consumer yet. See F-198 for the full mechanism, Opus review history, and plan doc. |

| F-05 | Allow user to add, edit, and remove holdings; persist to Supabase `holdings` table |
| F-06 | Display sector allocation pie chart and P&L bar chart |
| F-06a | **Composition Sankey (Portfolio → Sector → Position).** On the 🥧 Portfolio Overview page, a Plotly Sankey shows where capital sits and how concentrated it is in one view; band width = share of portfolio value. **Concentration reads at a glance:** a sector node over `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%) or a single-name node over `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` (15%) is flagged red. Flags fire on the **equity (gate) basis** (G-19); band width = share of portfolio value. Hover shows each node's % of portfolio. Display-only (the gates fire elsewhere). _(The net-capital overlay branch is retired with the equity-basis policy — conditioned on `_acct_gate_cache.basis ∈ {account, over-levered}`, now always `equity`, so it never fires.)_ |

The concentration reference line is drawn at `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%, the actual hard cap — never a hardcoded literal). Bars are the **equity basis** — the same basis the gates fire on (G-19). _(The second net-capital bar per sector is retired with the equity-basis policy — its branch is conditioned on `_acct_gate_cache.basis ∈ {account, over-levered}`, now always `equity`, so it never draws.)_ Display-only. |
| F-222 | **Sector Gaps pointer + widened diversifier-sector coverage — SHIPPED 2026-07-30.** F-07's Sector Exposure chart only ever showed sectors currently held, giving no visibility into sectors at 0% — a real diversification blind spot the user flagged while reviewing 🥧 Portfolio Overview. Two coordinated changes, both in `stock_analyzer/portfolio.py` / `app.py`: (1) **"🧭 Sector Gaps" pointer section**, rendered on 🥧 Portfolio Overview directly below the Sector Exposure chart, reads the already-published `st.session_state["_div_recs_cache"]` (the same cache F-13a's Diversification Advisor consumes — no recompute, no new session-state key) and lists each `ADD`-type rec as a one-line awareness note with a "See candidates in Diversification Advisor →" button (`_pending_page` nav indirection). Deliberately a pointer, not a duplicate: per the single-surface-priority convention, the actionable card (candidates, $ sizing) stays on 📡 Signals & Advice only. (2) **Widened `_DIVERSIFYING_SECTORS` coverage** (`portfolio.py:1004-1024`): of the app's 13 curated thematic sector labels (`TICKER_SECTORS`), only 5 (Healthcare, Energy, Defense, Financials, Clean Energy) had ever been scored as genuine portfolio diversifiers; the other 8 were either scored-but-excluded (Consumer Tech, AI & Cloud, AI & Data — too correlated to a tech-heavy book) or simply never evaluated. Decided with the user: **added** Communications (corr 0.20 — classic telecom is rate-sensitive/defensive, closer to utilities than growth tech), EV & Auto (corr 0.40 — rate/commodity-cycle driven, distinct from the software/AI cycle), and Enterprise Tech (corr 0.45 — legacy IT/capex cycle, more value-oriented than AI/Cloud growth names); **scored but kept excluded** Semiconductors and Cybersecurity (corr 0.65 each — same AI/compute/SaaS cycle as the already-excluded AI & Cloud / AI & Data cluster). New candidate rosters (`_SECTOR_CANDIDATES`) and discovery-universe bucket mappings (`_DIVERSIFY_TO_DISCOVERY`) added for the 3 newly-included sectors so `diversifying_candidate_pool()` can actually return names for them. **Also fixed in the same pass**: F-13a's ADD card caption ("Avg correlation to your existing book") was misleading — `corr_to_tech` was always a static per-sector table value, never computed from the user's actual holdings; reworded to "Typical correlation to a growth/tech-heavy portfolio" (copy-only, no logic change). No new `constants.py` entries — these tables are plain Python dicts/lists co-located with the pre-existing pattern, same as the original 5-sector version. Opus pre-ship code review cited in commit. |
| F-08 | Calculate and display composite signal (Buy/Hold/Sell) for each held position |
| F-09 | Show portfolio-level risk metrics: Sharpe ratio, Sortino ratio, max drawdown, beta, correlation matrix |
| F-09a | **Pullback fragility gauge (pullback-awareness Phase 1).** Surface a portfolio "fragility" read — how hard a routine market pullback (`FRAGILITY_PULLBACK_PCT`, −10%) would hit the book given its beta/concentration — framed as: timing isn't predictable, but exposure and reaction are. Awareness only; it does not issue a buy/sell. Consumed downstream by the risk-off de-risk overlay (F-25e) and the reactive pullback email (F-142). |
| F-09b | **Market-Risk Posture dial (pullback-awareness Phase 3).** On the 🔗 Risk Analysis page, a read-only gauge that COMPOSES two already-computed reads — book fragility (F-09a `severity`) and the current market regime (`risk_off_regime`: SPY below its `RISK_OFF_TREND_MA`-day average OR VIX ≥ `RISK_OFF_VIX_LEVEL`) — into a single 0–3 exposure posture: `score = severity_rank(calm 0 / caution 1 / fragile 2) + (1 if risk-off else 0)` → 🛡️ Steady / 🌥️ Watchful / ⚠️ Defensive / 🔴 Risk-off & fragile. Pure logic in `exit_advisor.market_risk_posture` (no new threshold — reuses the F-09a bands + the F-25e regime legs). **Not a forecast** (reads current exposure, never predicts a pullback's timing) and **not a directive**: when both legs are elevated (`armed` = the SAME AND-gate as the F-25e de-risk overlay), it POINTS to the Brief's de-risk cards rather than duplicating the action (single-surface). **WITHHOLDs** (offline note, not a falsely-calm dial) when fragility is unavailable, mirroring the gauge. Read-only consumer of `_fragility_cache` + `_cached_spy("1y")` + `_cached_vix()`; publishes nothing. |
| F-09d | **Leverage / margin awareness (awareness-only — NEVER gates).** Since the concentration gates are equity-basis (G-19), leverage risk is surfaced separately as a monitoring signal, so a margined book can't look calm just because equity-basis concentration is fine. When the manually-seeded cash (💰 Account) shows a **fresh, real margin debit** (signed net cash < 0, not stale > `ACCOUNT_CASH_STALE_DAYS`), a text read renders on the 🔗 Risk Analysis page: the margin debit $, net capital (equity − loan), the leverage multiple (equity ÷ net capital), and an illustration ("a −10% move on your largest sector ≈ −$X, −Y% of your equity"). The existing ⚖️ note on the 💰 Account page is retained. Published as `st.session_state["_leverage_cache"]` at the same wiring point that sets the gate basis; computed from `db.load_account_cash()` + `total_val`. **It never gates, suppresses, sizes, scores, or forces a trim** — purely awareness (§2B monitoring lane semantics). Fail-soft: no signal when cash is unknown, stale, or not a debit. |
| F-09c | **Cross-Asset Pulse (macro stress signals).** On the 🔗 Risk Analysis page, a read-only panel scoring five cross-asset stress signals from 45 days of daily history (`stock_analyzer/cross_asset.py`): credit spreads (HYG `CROSS_ASSET_HYG_TREND_DAYS`-day trend, declining → stress), VIX term structure (VIX/VIX3M > `CROSS_ASSET_VIX_TERM_RATIO` = 1.0 → inverted), dollar strength (DXY `CROSS_ASSET_DXY_TREND_DAYS`-day trend rising AND `CROSS_ASSET_DXY_ROC_DAYS`-day ROC > `CROSS_ASSET_DXY_ROC_THRESHOLD` = 1.5%), copper as a growth proxy (HG=F `CROSS_ASSET_COPPER_TREND_DAYS`-day trend, declining → concern), and the 3m10y yield curve (^TNX − ^IRX < `CROSS_ASSET_CURVE_STRESS_BP` = −50 bp → deeply inverted). The aggregate score = count of stressed signals **among those with data** → Calm (0–1) / Caution (2) / Stress (3–4) / Alarm (5); the displayed denominator is the available-signal count (never a hardcoded 5), and a total data outage returns label "—" (never a fabricated "Calm"). When score ≥ `CROSS_ASSET_STRESS_BRIEF_SCORE` (2), Today's Brief shows a one-liner pointing to this tab; on a total outage the Brief shows a quiet offline caption rather than going silent. The bare yfinance batch fetch is timeout-wrapped (20 s) so a stalled download can't freeze the render. **Awareness only — never moves a gate or changes a recommendation.** |
| F-10 | Performance attribution: breakdown of return by position and sector |
| F-11 | Earnings calendar for held positions (KPIs, timeline, detail table with Fwd EPS/weight/P&L/signal, Pre-Earnings Playbook) lives on the 🔔 Catalyst Watch page ("Your Holdings — Earnings" tier), NOT a Home tab — consolidated there with the watchlist/universe awareness tier so all earnings detail is in one place. |
| F-12 | Risk advisor: flag positions breaching stop-loss, concentration risk, correlation clusters. Beta and Sharpe recommendations name specific trim targets, which downstream features (Grow Today add-to-winner, Rebalancer ADD) cross-check and suppress to avoid contradicting the active reduce-exposure recommendation. |
| F-12a | **Concentration / sizing discipline (entry-time enforcement).** Closes the asymmetry where manual journal buys bypassed the position-sizing ceilings the recommendation paths already respected (a single name had reached ~23%). Three coordinated surfaces, all from the pure `concentration.py`: (a) an **entry nudge** at trade entry when an add would push a single name past `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` (15%); (b) a `single_name_concentration` MEDIUM recommendation on standing positions over the ceiling; (c) a **high-beta cluster** line when the high-β share of the book exceeds `CONCENTRATION_HIGHBETA_SHARE_WARN` (60%). Concentration breaches are structural, so they stay in **Act Today** (not the slow Tune-up lane). |
| F-12b | **Concentration-gate basis = EQUITY (2026-07-09 — reverses the earlier margin-aware basis).** The hard single-name/sector gates, the entry nudge, and the `risk_advisor` trim recs measure concentration against **invested equity** (MV ÷ equity), NOT net capital — so a *transient* margin balance can't lurch a hard gate. Leverage/margin risk is a **separate awareness-only** signal (F-09d), never a gate. Implements G-19. **Supersedes** the 2026-06-26 net-capital "tighter-of-both" basis (`min(equity, total_account_value)`) — see G-19 and `docs/plans/account-baseline.md` (2026-07-09 reversal §). |
| F-221 | **Same-day-buy trim exclusion (SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — SHOP whiplash fix).** `build_risk_advisor_recommendations()` takes an optional `trades_df` parameter; a ticker with a BUY trade dated today (America/New_York calendar day, converted from the stored UTC `traded_at` timestamp) is excluded from the `sector_concentration` rec's `trim_candidates` — held back from the greedy trim-allocation plan even when its composite is the sector's weakest. Excluded names surface separately in `trim_excluded_recent`; the Rebalance Plan renders a "🕐 Held back from today's plan" caption naming them and noting they're reconsidered in tomorrow's brief, so a resulting shortfall against the trim target is explained rather than silent (reuses the existing `trim_allocation()` shortfall mechanism unchanged). One-day grace only — the ticker returns to normal composite-ascending trim ranking the next day. Pairs with F-39h on the buy side. Implements G-21. |
| F-13 | Rebalancer: suggest target weights and trim/add trades. ADD actions cross-check News Intelligence alerts (suppressed for critical news; warned for negative) and Risk Advisor TRIM recommendations (ADD suppressed on tickers being trimmed). Suppressions render as explicit banners. |
| F-13a | Diversification Advisor ADD card: candidates are sourced from the broad discovery universe (≈20 names per sector, curated roster unioned first so known names are never dropped; capped by `DIVERSIFY_SCAN_CAP`), not a fixed roster — so the best diversifier surfaces, not a frozen list. Each candidate is cross-validated against the quality engine (composite/signal/R:R, the SAME numbers as Analysis, via the `_grow_composites` cache + load_all fallback), gated at `COMPOSITE_BUY`, ranked best-first, and shows the top `DIVERSIFY_DISPLAY_TOP` with a 🎯 best-passer / 🚦 none-clears banner. Failing names stay visible-but-demoted (never silently filtered). Each carries an "▶ Analyze {ticker}" button to the full Analysis scorecard (the bridge from candidate to decision/trade). **Live per-candidate correlation-to-book (2026-08-21):** each displayed candidate now shows its OWN correlation to the user's actual weighted book returns (`portfolio.correlation_to_portfolio`/`portfolio_return_series` — the same live computation the Rebalancer redeploy card (F-13, §3.1c) already used; zero new API/DB calls, reuses each candidate's already-loaded price bundle), banded 🟢 diversifier / 🟡 partial / 🔴 correlated via the new shared `portfolio.classify_book_corr()` (also backing the Rebalancer's own label, migrated onto it in the same change, behavior-preserving). This supersedes the static per-sector `_SECTOR_PROFILES` correlation number for the actual recommendation — that number is now shown only as a demoted "sector-typical prior" (parenthetical when live is available, an explicitly-labeled fallback when it isn't), closing a real over-claim class (e.g. Defense's static 0.12 corr undersold how correlated a cyclical candidate like GE can be to a tech-heavy book). Never re-ranks or changes candidate selection — correlation is a highlight (plus a "🏆 cleanest diversifier" badge among displayed gate-passers), not a gate. Two distinct "n/a" reasons (book-level vs. candidate-level insufficient history) so a missing read is never ambiguous or fabricated as a number. `portfolio.classify_book_corr` + 8 new tests, `tests/test_portfolio.py`. |
| F-14 | Stress test: model portfolio impact under defined macro scenarios (rate shock, recession, etc.) |
| F-14a | **Stress test — historical scenario expansion.** Two new named scenarios added to `stress_test.SCENARIOS` (9 total), each with per-sector overrides in `stress_test._SECTOR_SHOCKS`: (1) **2008 GFC** (`id="gfc_2008"`, `spy_move=−57.0%`) — peak-to-trough Global Financial Crisis (Oct 2007–Mar 2009); Financials −78%, EV & Auto −75%, Energy −50%, Healthcare −22%. (2) **Stagflation** (`id="stagflation"`, `spy_move=−18.0%`) — high inflation + stagnant growth (1970s analogy); Energy +30%, Defense +12%, AI & Data −30%, Clean Energy −12%. Both scenarios apply the sector-override path (non-None `sector_key`) so each holding's projected loss uses the sector-specific drawdown rather than the blunt beta-adjusted SPY move — preserving the "sector matters, not just beta" design of the existing 2022 Rate Shock and COVID Crash scenarios. Awareness only; never issues a rec or gates an entry. `stock_analyzer/stress_test.py`. (Commit 3cbd6a6.) |
| F-200 | **Regime-Aware Adversarial Stress Testing — Phase 1 (P5 of the Agentic Intelligence Roadmap) — SHIPPED 2026-07-24, commit `a3a9a12`, display-only.** New "🎯 Regime-Aware Adversarial Scenario (Beta)" expander at the bottom of the 🔥 Stress Testing tab, additive to F-14/F-14a's 9 named scenarios + Custom Scenario slider (all untouched). Synthesizes three already-shipped systems into a single Haiku-narrated compound scenario: `structural_scanner.blast_radius()` (F-198's cascade estimate — reused verbatim, no new shock formula), `portfolio_intelligence.correlation_clusters()` (F-189), the FRED macro regime detector (`macro_calendar.detect_macro_regime`, aliased `detect_macro_regime_fred` — **not** the unrelated same-named function in `macro.py`), and `cross_asset.py`'s USD/dollar stress signal (F-09c). Names the compound macro condition (2-3 concurrent factors, e.g. rate direction + dollar strength + credit stress) most likely to hurt the SPECIFIC structural weak points identified, citing only supplied evidence. **The roadmap's original "scenario plausibility in the next 90 days" framing was dropped** — the only historical regime persistence (`daily_regime`, since 2026-07-21) has ~3 days of data, far short of a genuine base rate; replaced with the regime detector's real, already-computed confidence score (0-100), explicitly relabeled as "how confident the detector is in its CURRENT read," never a forecast. Reuses the existing session-state regime cache (same `fred_key` three-source fallback as the existing Regime Fit feature, F-188) rather than re-fetching — the two sections on the same page can never disagree. Button-gated (never auto-computed on expander render), day-cached to new `regime_scenario_cache` table; a failed/empty narrative is never cached. The "early indicator watchlist" is selected by the LLM from the regime detector's real signal list only — validated via normalized matching, always displaying the canonical stored label, never the LLM's echoed text, so an indicator can neither be fabricated nor silently dropped over casing drift. No new `constants.py` entries — zero new quantitative modeling. 3-round Opus plan review (FIX-FIRST×2 then SHIP) + Opus pre-ship code review (SHIP, 0 blocking). **Strictly diagnostic — nothing here gates, reorders a scenario, or modifies the composite score.** Plan: [docs/plans/regime-aware-stress-testing.md](plans/regime-aware-stress-testing.md). |
| F-15 | Display market-closed context note in sidebar showing last close date when market is shut |
| F-16 | Tax Advisor: per-position tax-lot analysis with HARVEST recommendations on harvestable losses. HARVEST is **suppressed** on positions rated Buy or Strong Buy (action becomes `HOLD_FOR_SIGNAL`) so the tax tail does not wag the investment dog. The UI explicitly surfaces every suppressed harvest with the position's current signal so the user can revisit if conviction degrades. |
| F-186 | **Tax-Aware Exit/Harvest Lens (Concept F) — awareness-only, NEVER gates.** The exit-direction complement to F-16 (which is the Buy-side HARVEST gate, G-08): holding-period context layered onto exit signals for this taxable account. (1) **Exit-signal tax note:** when an EXIT (Act Today) or TRIM (Review Before Close) card fires on a position within `TAX_LONGTERM_WINDOW_DAYS` (30) of long-term-gains eligibility, the card gains an amber note ("becomes long-term eligible in N days; waiting can cut the tax rate ≈Xpp if selling at a gain"). The investment signal is **unchanged** — never suppressed or delayed. (2) **Wash-sale awareness:** a SELL **at a loss** within `TAX_WASH_SALE_DAYS` (30) of a same-ticker BUY shows a wash-sale note on the SELL confirmation card; the sale is not blocked. (3) **Net-of-cost note:** the opportunity-cost expander clarifies returns are gross, Robinhood equity commissions ≈ $0, and tax is not netted there (see Tax Advisor). Pure arithmetic via `tax_advisor.holding_period_status()` / `wash_sale_risk()` (reuse the F-16 lot replay); no new APIs. Tax constants are **display-only policy** in `constants.py` (never gate). |
| F-17 | Stop data integrity: when a position's stop value is missing or zero, the portfolio table displays "—" for Stop and Gap, marks Stop Type as "Stop Unavailable", and the mechanical SELL trigger in Act Today is skipped (the user is prompted to set a manual stop). No fabricated fallback is substituted. |
| F-187 | **Pre-Mortem Protocol (Concept C) — entry friction only, never a gate.** Before a prospective LIVE Buy is recorded (Trade Journal, including a Grow Today / Analysis "act on this" prefill — never broker/screenshot/split imports or the `recalculate_from_trades` replay, which have no live decision to stress-test), the investor sees a "🔍 Run Pre-Mortem" section: an optional Haiku-generated case AGAINST the buy (3 counterarguments — a composite-pillar concern with its specific driving signals, the portfolio-concentration impact of adding this position, and macro/earnings context — each required by the prompt to cite specific evidence, never a generic "stocks can go down" line), computed once per explicit click and discarded if the ticker changes before submit. The LLM prompt also includes the investor's own past exit lessons (from `lesson_category` on SELL trades in the Trade Journal — ticker-specific exits first, cross-ticker patterns as fallback) so counterarguments can reference the investor's own documented behavioural patterns. Regardless of whether the LLM ran, a chip row of past exit lessons appears above the commitment field — always visible when lessons exist — closing the loop between F-195 (Lessons Learned Library) and the pre-mortem commitment. The **hard requirement** is a required text box ("What would make me wrong about this?") enforced as a validation gate alongside the existing ticker/shares/price checks — **independent of whether the LLM call succeeded**, so an API outage or missing key can never block a legitimate Buy. Stored on `trades.premortem_case_against` (jsonb, the 3 counterarguments or null) and `trades.premortem_commitment` (text, the investor's own answer). Never re-scores, re-gates, reorders, or vetoes the recommendation — SELL and every retroactive/batch write path are untouched. `stock_analyzer/premortem_advisor.py`. |
| F-232 | **State of the Portfolio — standing thesis, SHIPPED 2026-08-06.** New full-width card on 🧾 Summary, below the existing 2×2 pointer grid, collapsed by default to one headline line. Closes the last of the 2026-08-05 brainstorm's Lens 3 items: a single, dated, portfolio-level posture-of-record instead of 70 fragmented surfaces. **Reframed from the brainstorm's original "grades itself against next week" wording** (locked with the user before build) because a literal predictive self-grade would forecast the portfolio's own future state — a breach of the §5.8 no-point-forecast invariant and the "AI narrates, never originates" redline already drawn on Portfolio Q&A (F-225). The shipped version is a **stability/consistency ledger**: 5 claims, each copied verbatim from an already-decided existing source (never a new computed threshold) — risk posture (← `_rag_label`), concentration (← `port_df["Weight (%)"]`/sector groupby vs `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`/`SECTOR_CEILING`, confirmed same basis as the real enforced gate — `Gate Weight (%) == Weight (%)`, `app.py:3822-3836`), correlation & structure (← `_div_label` + `_structural_alert_cache`), holdings composite-health (← `port_df["Score"]`, confirmed the genuine 4-pillar composite for held positions — `combined_score()` via `bundle_loader.py:186`/`portfolio.py:344` — not the unrelated momentum-only "Score" field used in scanner/movers contexts elsewhere in this app), and action posture (← Daily Brief buy_candidates vs `_reduce_calls`). Stored one row per ISO week in a new `portfolio_thesis` table (additive DDL, ships inert until applied — same "ships inert" pattern as `decision_context`/`premortem_trigger_price`); the grade is derived at READ time from the two most recent rows, never stored, so it can't go stale. Per claim: identical state = HELD; changed = SHIFTED(from→to); either side unavailable = `not_comparable` — **never a single aggregate pass/fail for the whole thesis.** **Zero LLM anywhere** — `stock_analyzer/portfolio_thesis.py` imports no LLM client; the prose is a plain deterministic template composing only the 5 classified states, enforced by import-absence as the actual mechanism proving the no-originate invariant, not just a design intention. New constant `PORTFOLIO_THESIS_BASELINE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`=14 (the only new decision threshold this needs — a plumbing/cadence value, not a money-moving one; every claim-classification threshold reused from existing constants). Once-per-ISO-week write guard reuses the already-computed `_home_synth_cache` bundle (no second recompute); renders nothing at all (no card, not even a placeholder) when that bundle is offline. Whole render block wrapped in one `try/except: pass` so a failure here can never break the rest of the Summary page. **Cross-feature coordination checked, not duplicated:** distinct from The Judge's `audit_coherence()` (F-227, per-ticker enforcement coverage) and the Weekly Debrief email (backward-looking behavior/performance narrative) — this is a portfolio-level posture whose own week-over-week consistency is tracked. **Deferred, not wired in v1:** cross-checking the "action posture" claim against any `audit_coherence()` uncovered-risk finding — acceptable for v1 since the thesis never gates and is collapsed-by-default awareness only; revisit if the two surfaces are ever found to visibly disagree. Designed via a `planner` (Opus) pass (verdict: PROCEED WITH REDUCED SCOPE, the stability-ledger reframe + no-LLM decision both locked with the user before any code), mocked and approved (3 states — fresh thesis + ledger, first-ever view, offline — `docs/mockups/state-of-portfolio-standing-thesis.html`) before build, including a follow-up placement decision (full-width collapsed card, not a new Summary tab) confirmed with the user after the mock. Opus review: FIX-FIRST on the first pass (1 blocking — a dangling F-ID reference in the constants doc row with no matching requirements/shipped-log entry, a docs-sync gap, not a logic defect — closed same-session) → SHIP after the docs sync + 2 cheap non-blocking fixes (an `.get(..., {})` that only guards key-absence, not a stored `None`, tightened to `(... or {})`; a doc-row wording imprecision attributing the lookback walk to the wrong function). Plan: [docs/plans/state-of-portfolio-standing-thesis.md](plans/state-of-portfolio-standing-thesis.md). Full suite 3390 passed (+51). |
| F-233 | **Self Track Record — SHIPPED 2026-08-06.** New 6th tab "🧭 Self vs Engine" on 🎯 My Edge, deliberately asking a different question than the 🎯 Engine Track Record card on 🧾 Summary (F-229 — "is the ENGINE good?"): this tab asks "is MY OWN instinct good?" Originated from a live SPCX BUY (2026-08-05 — an earnings-catalyst + ARK Invest 13F-flow + technical-bottom read the engine's own feeds don't capture) and a direct-SQL diagnostic run against production Supabase that found the self-reported `trigger_type` dropdown (MANUAL/RECOMMENDATION/...) is **not** a trustworthy signal for "did the app recommend this": of 93 total BUYs, trades self-labeled RECOMMENDATION still showed zero matching `recommendations` row in 16–22 of them even under a loosened match window, root-caused to two structural gaps rather than mislabeling — some tickers were never in the daily-scanned universe (`scanner.SECTOR_UNIVERSE`) at all, and others were in-scope but `recommendations` rows are only ever written from the interactive session (`app.py`, no cron coverage previously), so a real qualifying day leaves zero trace if nobody opened the app that day. New pure module `stock_analyzer/self_track_record.py`: `classify_buys()` independently re-derives each BUY's origin (never trusts `trigger_type`) into 4 buckets — `app_aligned` (a matching `new_pick`/`buy_candidate` rec exists within `SELF_TRACK_MATCH_LOOKBACK_DAYS`=3 days before, inclusive, the trade date — no lookahead), `self_out_of_scope` (ticker outside the scanned universe/watchlist — graded, date-independent, the purest self-initiated case since scope, not coverage, is the reason), `self_in_scope` (in-scope, no rec, trade on/after `SELF_TRACK_RELIABLE_LOG_START`=2026-08-06 — graded), `coverage_limited` (in-scope, no rec, predates that date — disclosed count only, **never graded either way**, since a missing rec there is ambiguous logging coverage, not a proven absence). `self_vs_engine_summary()` reuses `recommendations_history.compute_outcomes` for the actual alpha-vs-SPY math (never reimplemented) and gates each of the two graded averages (`app_aligned`, and `self_out_of_scope`+`self_in_scope` combined) independently behind `BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N`=8 (reused, no parallel constant). **Companion cron fix, shipped in the same pass** (the prerequisite for `self_in_scope` to ever populate going forward): `cron_runner._run_scan` now also persists today's `new_pick` picks via a new pure `_build_new_pick_rows()` helper + the existing idempotent `db.save_recommendations()` upsert (`on_conflict="ticker,rec_date,rec_type", ignore_duplicates=True`, so a same-day interactive re-write later can't double-count) — closes the exact visit-dependent gap the diagnostic found, the same class of gap already fixed for `exit_signals` but until now unfixed for the BUY-side log. Wrapped in try/except so a DB hiccup here can never abort the scan or the higher-priority buy-list email built later in the same function. Two new measurement-only constants in `constants.py` (`SELF_TRACK_MATCH_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, `SELF_TRACK_RELIABLE_LOG_START`) — both explicit user policy calls (3-day lookback chosen over looser 5/10-day alternatives after the diagnostic; the cron-fix ship date fixed as the coverage-reliability boundary) — never gate a recommendation. The tab itself is read-only/awareness: alpha-vs-SPY framing (never portfolio-%, per `feedback_analytics_integrity`), an explicit "an observed correlation in your own decisions, not a verdict on it" disclaimer, a disclosed (never graded) `coverage_limited` count caption, the logged `user_thesis` text shown beside the biggest self-initiated winners/losers, and a line clarifying it answers a different question than Engine Track Record so the two cards can't read as contradictory. `trigger_type` has zero influence on any bucket by design — the module's own docstring states this explicitly, and it's directly covered by a test. SELL-side (would match against `exit_signals`, which is cron-complete already, instead of `recommendations`) explicitly deferred, following the same buy-side-first precedent Behavioral Fingerprint (F-193) itself used. Designed via a `planner` (Opus) pass before any code (verdict: PROCEED with a 3-way→4-way bucket reframe — the out-of-scope case is the purest self-initiated signal and needs no cron dependency at all, which is what lets V1 carry a real, trustworthy number from day one across full trade history instead of an empty "building" state for weeks); the two remaining open policy calls (exact lookback window, whether to ship the cron fix now vs. defer it) were confirmed with the user before build. 18 new tests (`tests/test_self_track_record.py`) covering both boundary conditions (`SELF_TRACK_RELIABLE_LOG_START`'s inclusive `>=`, the lookback window's exact-3-days-counts/4-days-doesn't edge), the offline-sentinel-collapse guard (`recs_df=None` → `classify_buys` returns `None`, vs. a genuinely empty-but-not-None `recs_df` still classifying normally), out-of-scope purity, `trigger_type`'s zero effect, per-trade (not per-ticker) granularity, and cron row-building determinism. **Opus review: FIX-FIRST on the first pass (1 blocking — the offline-sentinel-collapse guard didn't actually hold: `db.load_recommendations()`'s own `except` branch swallows a real query failure and returns an empty DataFrame indistinguishable from genuine zero-recs, so a transient Supabase hiccup with credentials present would have silently misclassified every `app_aligned` BUY as self-initiated and fabricated a false "you beat the engine" number) → fixed same session by adding `db.load_recommendations_or_none()` (returns `None` on no-creds or any query exception, an empty DataFrame only on a real zero-row result; `load_recommendations()` itself left behaviorally unchanged for its ~20 other callers, docstring corrected) plus 6 new tests (`tests/test_db_load_recommendations.py`) pinning the exception-vs-empty distinction with a raising fake client → confirm-pass SHIP, 0 blocking.** Full suite 3414 passed (+24). **Follow-up 2026-08-22 — the self side's COMPOSITION is now disclosed.** `self_vs_engine_summary` averages alpha across `self_in_scope` + `self_out_of_scope`, and those answer different questions: in-scope means the app **had a view and stayed silent** (a genuine head-to-head — you overrode its silence and one of you was right), out-of-scope means it **never looked** (no engine call to beat). The panel showed only the merged average beside the app-aligned one, so "Self-initiated +Xpp vs App-aligned +Ypp" read as *your judgment beating the engine* when it could be entirely names the engine never evaluated — a reading that could reasonably push the user toward trusting their own sourcing over the app's. Found by a targeted sweep for the "asserts a conclusion, withholds a basis it already computed" class; `n_self_in_scope`, `n_self_out_of_scope` and `n_self_graded` had all been returned since V1 and read by **nothing but tests**. Telling asymmetry it fixes: the panel already disclosed `n_coverage_limited`, the bucket it *excludes*, while saying nothing about the two it *merges*. New returned fields `n_self_in_scope_graded` / `n_self_out_of_scope_graded` compute that split **on the graded population only** — the exact rows behind the average, summing to `self_graded["n"]` by construction via a shared `_is_graded` predicate — because the unfiltered counts include immature and unpriced trades and captioning the average with those would describe a *larger* population than the figure above it (the F-246 mistake, one panel over). Renders as a caption stating how many graded buys are head-to-head, escalating to an **amber note when none are**, since the side-by-side then implies a comparison that structurally does not exist. This also makes CLAUDE.md's own V2 build trigger — "after a few weeks of `self_in_scope` accumulation" — readable for the first time; it was gated on a quantity the panel never showed. Awareness-only, no gate/threshold/formula change. 3 new tests + 2 assertions in `tests/test_self_track_record.py`. |
| F-229 | **Engine Track Record pointer — SHIPPED 2026-08-05.** Compact "🎯 Engine Track Record" pointer card in the 🧾 Summary page's "🧭 Elsewhere in DRISHTA" grid. Surfaces the all-time acted alpha vs S&P for `new_pick` recommendations — the single most important credibility number — without duplicating any computation from 📜 Recommendations History (reads the same `match_recs_to_trades` → `compute_outcomes` → `engine_trust_headline` chain, session-cached for the day). Band display: below `ENGINE_TRACK_MIN_CALLS`=8 matured acted calls → "Building history" with no verdict; 8–14 → amber "EARLY READ" softened verdict; ≥15 → "WORKING" (green, if acted α > 0) or "LAGGING" (amber, honest) firm verdict. Contrast line shows skipped-α (missed new_picks) when available. Coverage caption explains the BUY log is visit-dependent. Navigation button links to 📜 Recommendations History via `_pending_page` indirection. Awareness-only; never gates or influences any recommendation. New pure helper `engine_trust_headline()` in `stock_analyzer/recommendations_history.py`; two display-only constants `ENGINE_TRACK_MIN_CALLS`/`ENGINE_TRACK_FIRM_CALLS` in `constants.py`. **Summary restructure (2026-08-05):** the card is the hero (top-left) of a cleaned-up 2×2 pointer grid (with 🩺 Thesis Review, 🔔 Catalyst Watch, 🔗 Risk Posture — the last moved in from the old Tier-3 row); the KPI row swapped its Alerts tile for **Alpha vs SPY** (Alerts detail lives on 📡 Signals & Advice, still flagged by the sidebar nav badge); the Alpha tile gained an **honesty guard** that suppresses the number to "n/a · capital moved — see 💰 Capital Trend" when a BUY/SELL in the trailing window contaminates the naive equity-return (the source of a misleading +85% read); and the big Act Today card collapsed to a slim pointer to 🏠 Home (reads the identical `split_defensive()` bucket, so it can't under-report). Display-only; no computation changed. **Phase 2 — 🛡️ Defense facet (2026-08-05).** Folded a second facet into the SAME card (not a new sibling card): "did the App's protective EXIT/TRIM calls avoid drawdown?", scored as `protect_alpha_pct = SPY_return − flagged_name_return` from `signal_date` → today (sign-flipped vs Offense's alpha — positive means the flagged name underperformed, i.e. the caution was right). Scoped to EXIT + TRIM only (WATCH is awareness-only, excluded; RISK_OFF is portfolio-wide macro, not per-ticker, excluded). Reads `exit_signals` (complete cron coverage, unlike the visit-dependent BUY-side log) via new pure helpers in `stock_analyzer/protective_track_record.py` — `collapse_by_ticker()` is the critical anti-inflation step, collapsing cron's one-row-per-day-flagged into exactly one row per distinct ticker (earliest signal = longest window, labeled by the highest-severity type reached) before any aggregate is computed. Two new display-only constants `PROTECT_TRACK_MIN_CALLS`=8 / `PROTECT_TRACK_FIRM_CALLS`=15. Distinct badge vocabulary so the two facets can't be confused: VALIDATED (green, firm+positive) / RAN EARLY (amber, firm+flat-or-negative, honest — never suppressed or dressed as failure) / EARLY READ (amber, thin sample) / BUILDING (grey, no verdict) / NO DATA (grey, sample floor cleared but nothing priced). No link-through in v1 — no detail page exists for this facet yet; the acted-dodge secondary metric is deferred to a possible Phase 2b. `load_exit_signals()` returns an empty DataFrame (never `None`) on DB failure, so "offline" and "no deterioration yet" are indistinguishable by design — both degrade to the same neutral "Building protective track record" caption, which never claims a false all-clear. Designed via a `planner` (Opus) pass before any code, mocked and approved (`docs/mockups/engine-track-record-phase2.html`) before build; Opus review SHIP 0 blocking on the first pass. Awareness-only; never gates or influences any recommendation. **Post-ship fixes, same day:** live validation found the aggregate could render "+nan" (a legacy NULL price read back from pandas as `NaN`, fixed via the codebase's existing NaN-safe coercion); traced to a genuine `exit_signals` write-path bug (the interactive app path dropped `price`/`dd_from_peak_pct`/`below_ma_count`/`rel_strength` entirely, and a same-day app write could clobber a good price cron had already captured — fixed forward-only, see `docs/architecture.md` §6.21); and `collapse_by_ticker()`'s anchor-selection rule (always the ticker's globally earliest row) meant any ticker whose earliest row was historically NULL-priced stayed permanently stuck even after the write-path fix — now prefers the earliest row with a usable price, falling back to true-earliest only when none is priced. All three fixes Opus-reviewed SHIP before commit. |
| F-230 | **Correlation Under Stress — SHIPPED 2026-08-05.** On the 🧩 Intelligence page's 🕸️ Correlation Clusters tab, a new "🌩️ Correlation Under Stress" section below the existing calm (6-month) correlation clusters compares the SAME holdings' correlation during a real historical crash window — names that look uncorrelated day-to-day can converge to 0.9+ once a selloff actually hits, exactly when diversification is supposed to help and doesn't. Window selectbox: the 3 existing `HISTORICAL_WINDOWS` presets (COVID 2020 crash, default; 2022 rate shock; GFC 2008) plus a **"Custom range"** option — a user-picked date range (min `2007-01-01`, max today) for testing any personally-remembered event (e.g. a sector-specific selloff that wasn't a broad market crash), via `stress_test.validate_custom_stress_range()`. Reuses the existing calm-side correlation/clustering code and the existing `CORR_HIGH_PAIRS_THRESHOLD`/`CORR_DANGER_PAIRS_THRESHOLD` tiers — no new `constants.py` entry. New pure logic: `stress_test.fetch_stress_window_returns()`/`stress_correlation_matrix()` (date-based historical fetch, mirrors `fetch_historical_drawdowns`'s per-ticker `<5 valid closes` exclusion but retains the aligned return series instead of collapsing to a scalar) and `portfolio_intelligence.correlation_regime_delta()` (restricts both matrices to the ticker intersection before any average/delta — a ticker with partial coverage in only one regime can never skew the headline; reuses `correlation_clusters()` verbatim for the stress-side cluster list). Output: a headline avg-calm→avg-stress delta, a "pairs that newly converged under stress" table (calm below the warning threshold, stress at/above it), the stress cluster list, and a coverage caption (how many held names traded through the window). **Two display-only measurement floors, NOT in `constants.py`** (matching the `account._ANNUALIZE_CAVEAT_MAX_DAYS` local-constant precedent — they only decide whether an awareness-only reading is shown vs. withheld, never gate a recommendation): `stress_test._MIN_STRESS_WINDOW_DAYS`=10 (aligned trading-day floor; all 3 presets comfortably clear it, so only custom ranges can ever be withheld) and `stress_test._EARLIEST_STRESS_START`=2007-01-01. An invalid/too-short custom range disables the Load button with an explicit reason caption — never a silent fallback (a gap found in a similar existing picker elsewhere in the app that this deliberately does not repeat). A mandatory calm-advisor framing caveat ("read this directionally, not as a hidden link") renders whenever any reading is shown, however short — a sharp crash mechanically inflates correlation regardless of any real structural link between the names. A cross-reference caption to 🔥 Stress Testing (its P&L-impact view of the same scenario) renders for presets only, not custom ranges. Designed via 2 `planner` (Opus) passes (base design + a custom-range addendum triggered by a real user question about testing a semiconductor-sector selloff), mocked and approved before build; Opus review SHIP 0 blocking. Awareness-only; terminal display, nothing downstream consumes it. |
| F-231 | **Behavioral Fingerprint at the decision moment — SHIPPED 2026-08-06.** Closes the "diagnosed but only shown retrospectively" gap from the 2026-08-05 brainstorm (Lens 2): 🧬 Behavioral Fingerprint's patterns (My Edge tab) now also mirror at 📒 Trade Journal → 📝 Log Trade write-time, not just after the fact. On a **BUY**, if the ticker's momentum `Score` (from `_last_port_df`/`scanner_results`) places it in the SAME high/low bucket `momentum_recency_pattern` already splits actionable recs on (≥`BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N`=8 per bucket), one muted caption line mirrors that bucket's historical action-rate vs the other bucket's. On a **SELL**, if the ticker has an exit signal (WATCH/TRIM/EXIT/RISK_OFF) within the last `EXIT_SIGNAL_ACT_WINDOW_DAYS`=7 days, one muted caption line mirrors that signal-type's historical action-rate + median lag (reused from `signal_response_rate_pattern`/`signal_lag_pattern`, never recomputed independently, so the live mirror cannot disagree with the retrospective tab). Both end with the exact same disclaimer already load-bearing on the My Edge tab: "An observed correlation in your own decisions, not a verdict on it." **Renders OUTSIDE `st.form("log_trade_form", ...)`** — structurally cannot gate, slow, or block the Record Trade submit; the whole block is one `try/except: pass`, so a classification failure degrades to silence, never a crash. When nothing resolves (off-universe ticker, thin sample, no active signal) it renders **nothing** — no placeholder text, unlike the retrospective tab's explicit withhold captions. New pure helpers in `stock_analyzer/behavioral_fingerprint.py`: `classify_live_buy_momentum()`, `latest_active_signal_type()` (inclusive day-boundary — a signal exactly `act_window_days` old still counts as active), `classify_live_sell_signal()`; `momentum_recency_pattern()` additively gained a `"median"` return key so the live classifier can share the exact same `>= median` split boundary as the retrospective card. **No new `constants.py` entry** — reuses `BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N`, `BEHAVIORAL_MEANINGFUL_ACTION_RATE_DELTA_PP`, `EXIT_SIGNAL_ACT_WINDOW_DAYS` verbatim. Two patterns deliberately excluded from this surface: opening-window (an outcome/alpha stat — surfacing predicted alpha at the entry instant would nudge timing, not mirror a past pattern) and escalation-ignored (no coherent single-trade bucket for a SELL); conviction-tier deferred (composite score isn't reliably in session_state on this page). Designed via a `planner` (Opus) pass before any code, mocked and approved (`docs/mockups/behavioral-fingerprint-decision-moment.html`) before build; Opus review SHIP 0 blocking (confirmed the mirror-not-nudge invariant structurally, the median-boundary and day-boundary risks are both correctly implemented and locked by tests that would fail if either were inverted). Plan: [docs/plans/behavioral-fingerprint-decision-moment.md](plans/behavioral-fingerprint-decision-moment.md). Awareness-only; the engine never reads any of these outputs — nothing here re-ranks, re-scores, or gates. |
| F-228 | **Pre-Commitment Enforcement — SHIPPED 2026-08-03.** The one genuinely unbuilt idea from a 2026-08-03 brainstorm ranked list (the other two turned out already covered — self-calibrating gates by the Monthly Intelligence Report F-153/F-153a, weekly-alpha attribution by the Weekly Debrief's per-position contributor/detractor breakdown): F-187's `premortem_commitment` is captured at BUY but nothing ever checked it against live data — Thesis Red Team and Portfolio Q&A only ever quote it back as passive context. `stock_analyzer/premortem_monitor.py::extract_trigger()` is a ONE-SHOT Haiku call at BUY-submission time (never on every rerun, never retried — the trades grid is delete-only) that parses the free-text commitment into a structured, checkable fact: `checkable=False` (stored as `premortem_trigger_direction='not_checkable'`) for any commitment with no explicit stated price — conservative bias is the whole safety net, the LLM never infers or guesses a number. `detect_premortem_triggers()` is pure Python, ZERO LLM cost, called once per Daily Brief build (same call site as `deterioration_signals()`): for each ticker's **currently open lot** (reusing `tax_advisor._build_open_lots()`, extended to track each lot's cumulative split ratio) it compares the stored trigger — divided by that split ratio — against price history since the lot's `buy_date`. **Lot-scoped, not ticker-scoped:** a sell-then-rebuy can never resurface an unrelated, already-closed lot's commitment against a brand-new position. **Self-resolving:** a trigger only fires while the MOST RECENT daily close is still beyond the level (close-price basis, matching the deterioration ladder's own convention) — "crossed at any point since BUY" would nag forever after a recovered dip, so the gate is always "still beyond the level today," not a historical fact. Fires as a new, first-class Act Today card kind (`premortem_triggered`, `decision_bucket._ACT_KINDS`) — deliberately shown **alongside** any existing same-ticker WATCH/TRIM/EXIT card rather than merged (`_consolidate_act_today()` exempts this kind from its per-ticker consolidation entirely — the investor's own stated condition firing is a genuinely different reason from the algorithm's deterioration read, not a restatement of it). A pre-build research pass found the original brainstorm's literal "Judge-style veto authority" framing would have been wrong here too — this is deliberately an audit/confront pattern (a loud Act Today card), never a new suppression of any recommendation. Two new nullable `trades` columns (`premortem_trigger_price`, `premortem_trigger_direction`) via the same additive-DDL pattern as `premortem_case_against`; ships inert until DDL is applied. No new `constants.py` entries. Opus design review: FIX-FIRST, 3 blocking (stock-split staleness, sell-then-rebuy lot mis-attribution, "ever crossed" stale-nag temporal semantic) + 2 fix-first (underspecified `_act_today()` plumbing, a persistent-daily default that initially conflicted with the calm-advisor principle but was resolved by the self-resolving temporal fix) — all resolved by reusing `tax_advisor.py`'s existing FIFO lot-replay rather than inventing new logic. **Opus code review of the finished code: SHIP, 0 blocking**, 1 cosmetic wording fix applied ("broke $50 above" → "broke above $50.00"). 44 new tests (`tests/test_premortem_monitor.py`, `tests/test_premortem_act_today.py`, extended `tests/test_tax_advisor.py`). **Live-validated post-DDL, 2026-08-03:** confirmed via a direct Supabase query that a purely qualitative commitment correctly extracts to `premortem_trigger_direction='not_checkable'` (never a guessed price).
