Also surfaced a real, previously-undocumented corollary of the close-basis design: a trigger can never fire the SAME DAY its BUY is logged, since that day's close doesn't exist in price history yet when the check runs — the earliest a fired trigger can surface is the next trading day. Added a help-text hint on the Pre-Mortem commitment box (`app.py`) telling the user to include an explicit price if they want it actively monitored. **2026-08-04 correction:** a full-codebase audit found the same-day-fire invariant above was not actually enforced in code (an inclusive `>=` in the price-history filter) — fixed same day (`stock_analyzer/premortem_monitor.py:226`, `>=` → `>`, Opus review: SHIP, 0 blocking; see `docs/reviews/2026-08-04-review.md`). The behavioral claim in this row is accurate again as of that fix. Plan: [docs/plans/premortem-enforcement.md](plans/premortem-enforcement.md). |
| F-18 | **Action Log — manual stop override (closes the recommend→act→log loop):** when Review Before Close advises raising a stop, the user can record the new stop level. It persists to the Supabase `manual_stops` table and overrides the computed ATR/ratchet stop in `build_portfolio_df`. Override-wins is **one-directional**: a manual stop is honoured only when it is ≥ the current computed stop (you can tighten, not loosen below the mechanical floor). Overridden positions show a 📌 badge and Stop Type="Manual" wherever the stop is rendered (portfolio table, Trade Plan, Summary Scorecard). |
| F-19 | A manual stop is auto-cleared (orphan cleanup) when the ticker's share count drops to 0 — `save_holdings` symmetric-sweeps `manual_stops` so a stop override never outlives the position it protects. |

### 3.1a Portfolio Overview

Standalone page (PORTFOLIO nav group, positioned first) extracted from Home in the 2026-07-12/13 nav cleanup. Five internal tabs (restructured 2026-07-15 per the I2 UX remediation): **📊 Overview**, **⚖️ Rebalancing**, **💰 Tax**, **📈 Performance** — distributing the functionality formerly in the single "Portfolio Overview" tab (F-06, F-06a, F-07, F-10, and the F-22c rebalance plan) across four focused tabs — and **📈 Analytics** (F-171–F-173 below, unchanged from the original split). Same "haven't visited Home this session" empty-state gate as every other extracted page (`_render_portfolio_not_loaded`). Both **📈 Performance** and **📈 Analytics** gained their own internal sub-tabs (2026-08-04 UX remediation, I2): Performance → vs S&P 500 / Diagnostics / Sector Attribution; Analytics → Relative Strength / Sector Heatmap / vs S&P 500 (Real Sector) / Rankings — pure UI reorganization of already-shipped content, no new feature.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-171 | **Relative Strength (Analytics tab).** Each holding's 6-month return vs. its sector ETF benchmark (`relative_strength_table`). **Outperforming** = genuine stock-specific alpha, not just riding the sector tide; **Underperforming** = the sector rallied but this position lagged — an institutional rotation flag. Requires ≥1 holding with price history. Awareness only. |
| F-172 | **Sector Rotation Heatmap (Analytics tab).** Button-gated, lazy-loaded (`_fetch_sector_returns`): each sector ETF's 1W/1M/3M/6M return, cross-referenced against the user's own current sector exposure % (`sector_exposure`) — an "is my exposure aligned with what's actually working" read. Awareness only. |
| F-173 | **Rankings (Analytics tab).** Button-gated universe scan (`scan_sectors` + `rank_holdings_in_universe`) ranking each holding's momentum score against the broader scanner universe — Universe Rank, Percentile, and a top-performer callout for names in the top decile. Awareness only. |
| F-223 | **Portfolio vs. S&P 500, Real Sector (Analytics tab) — Phase 3 of the sector-diversification work (F-222 was Phases 1-2), SHIPPED 2026-07-30.** F-07/F-222's Sector Exposure and Sector Gaps use the app's 13 curated thematic sector labels (`TICKER_SECTORS`), which don't map 1:1 onto real GICS sectors (e.g. "Consumer Tech" spans AAPL=Technology, AMZN=Consumer Discretionary, NFLX=Communication Services). This feature answers a different question — "are we over/underweight the actual S&P 500 by real sector" — using a **deliberately separate, parallel classification**: `stock_analyzer/portfolio.py::real_sector_exposure()` reads each held ticker's REAL provider-reported sector (already fetched for every position via `bundle_loader.py` as `loaded_data[ticker]["sector"]`, but normally discarded downstream in favor of the curated thematic label by `resolve_sector()`) — zero new API calls. `_normalize_provider_sector()` maps common provider naming variants (e.g. Yahoo/Morningstar-style "Financial Services"/"Consumer Cyclical"/"Healthcare"/"Basic Materials") onto the 11 real GICS sector names; anything unmapped falls to `"Other"` (matches the existing `UNCLASSIFIED_SECTOR` catch-all — never silently miscounted). `sector_benchmark_tilt()` outer-joins the result against `SP500_SECTOR_WEIGHTS` (a static reference table — no live benchmark-weight data source exists in the provider layer — sourced via the English Wikipedia "S&P 500" article's GICS sector-weighting table, as of 2026-07-01; needs periodic manual refresh, see CLAUDE.md's "What's queued") so a benchmark sector held at 0% still shows correctly as a negative tilt. Renders as a green/red tilt bar chart on 📈 Analytics, alongside F-171/F-172 (both also "vs. a benchmark" diagnostics). **The UI caption explicitly disambiguates** that this is a second, coexisting sector taxonomy from the thematic one used elsewhere on the same page — a deliberate design choice, not a bug, since the two won't sum the same way. Reference data, not a decision threshold — stays in `portfolio.py` alongside `SECTOR_ETF`/`_SECTOR_PROFILES` rather than `constants.py`, same reasoning already established for `_SECTOR_PROFILES`. **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or feeds any recommendation.** 6 new tests in `tests/test_portfolio.py`. |

### 3.1b Risk Analysis

Standalone page (PORTFOLIO nav group) extracted from Home in the 2026-07-12/13 nav cleanup — before that, briefly a merged "Risk & Alerts" Home tab; before that, a separate "Risk Analysis" Home tab (the current name/icon is a deliberate reuse of that original naming). Aggregates: portfolio risk metrics (F-09), fragility gauge (F-09a), Market-Risk Posture dial (F-09b), leverage/margin awareness (F-09d), Cross-Asset Pulse (F-09c), rate sensitivity (F-87a), stress testing (F-14/F-14a), the historical scenario replay (F-168), Regime Fit (F-188), the Regime-Aware Adversarial Scenario expander (F-200, added 2026-07-24 to the 🔥 Stress Testing tab), and the Outcome Range historical-bootstrap Monte Carlo simulator (F-224, added 2026-08-01 as its own 4th tab). Same empty-state gate as Portfolio Overview. (2026-07-15: restructured into three internal tabs — **📊 Dashboard**, **📋 Action Plan**, **🔥 Stress Testing** — for navigability; no content added or removed. 2026-08-01: a 4th tab, **🎲 Outcome Range**, added for F-224. 2026-08-21: a 5th tab, **🧯 After My Rules**, added for F-245 — deliberately adjacent to the other two scenario surfaces, so the progression reads raw shock → outcome distribution → what the app's own rules then do about it.)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-188 | **Regime Fit (Concept D, Wave 3 of the next-evolution roadmap) — Action Plan tab, SHIPPED 2026-07-17, commit `0c6df7a`.** Read-only diagnostic answering "given the current macro regime, is my portfolio's beta and cash cushion where it should be?" — the app already detects a regime (`macro_calendar.detect_macro_regime`, one of `rate_cut`/`inflation_fight`/`recession_fear`/`stagflation_risk`/`neutral`) but never connected it to a target portfolio profile. The section independently resolves the FRED key and detects/caches the regime (same `f"_macro_regime_{date}_{bool(fred_key)}"` cache-key convention as the Economic Calendar page, so a same-day detection there is reused for free; deliberately omits that page's "past HIGH-impact events" gate, which is irrelevant here) and compares current portfolio beta (`_port_risk_cache`) and cash % (`db.load_account_cash()`, same formula as 💰 Account) against `REGIME_BETA_CEILING` / `REGIME_CASH_FLOOR_PCT` (`constants.py`, new investment-policy dicts keyed by the 5 real regime ids, set in a dedicated conversation with the user — anchored to the existing regime-agnostic `PORTFOLIO_BETA_TARGET`/`_ELEVATED`/`_CEILING` baseline: tighter beta ceilings and higher cash floors in defensive regimes, a looser ceiling in rate-cut optimism). On a beta breach, names the top-3 beta contributors (`stock_analyzer/regime_targets.py::regime_position_gap`, pure/None-safe, ranked by β × weight). Regime-detection confidence below `REGIME_CONFIDENCE_MIN_DISPLAY` (40) or a non-`fred+market` source is flagged as low-confidence/estimated rather than shown as certain. **Diagnostic only — never gates, resizes, suppresses, or reorders anything**; the investor decides whether/how fast to close the gap. **Deliberately scoped narrow for this wave** (per the plan's own governance requirement + a live scoping conversation): no Growth/Quality Tilt columns (need Concept B's factor-tilt work, not yet started), no Home "regime changed" annotation (would need day-over-day persistence that doesn't exist yet — no new DDL added this wave). Opus-reviewed SHIP, 0 blocking. |

### 3.1c Signals & Advice

Standalone page (SIGNALS nav group, moved from PORTFOLIO 2026-07-26 — see below) extracted from Home in the 2026-07-12/13 nav cleanup — same lineage as §3.1b (briefly merged with Risk Analysis as "Risk & Alerts", now split back out). **Renamed "⚠️ Alerts & Actions" → "📡 Signals & Advice" (2026-07-22, commit `291b0a9`)**, and split into two tabs: **📡 Active Signals** (Active Alerts, Custom Price Alerts, Rebalancer cards — F-169/F-170/F-13) and **🧩 Diversification** (Diversification Advisor — F-13a). **Nav group move (2026-07-26):** moved out of the PORTFOLIO nav group (which had grown to 9 items) into the ALERTS nav group, which was renamed **SIGNALS** for the occasion — the old "ALERTS" label undersold this page's mix of live signals + advice, and the rename gives the group room to describe all three of its pages (Signals & Advice, 🔔 Catalyst Watch, 📅 Economic Calendar) as one "things that need your attention" family. Same-day follow-up polish, all `app.py`-only with no constants/gate/logic change: `be26c64` replaced full-width alert banners on Active Signals with a 3-column category-card grid; `a89e0c6` removed duplicate severity icons and fixed bold-ticker rendering; `6b88949` moved the "well-balanced, no rebalancing needed" success chip to the top of the tab. **UI label disambiguated 2026-07-26:** the Rebalancer (F-13) section's on-screen heading renamed "💡 Rebalancing Recommendations" → "💡 Signal-Driven Actions" (and the empty-state chip's wording updated to match) — it was colliding in name, but not in mechanism, with Portfolio Overview's "⚖️ Rebalancing Advisor" (weight-vs-target drift; §3.1). The two features are unrelated: F-13 here triggers off a ticker's score/signal deterioration, the Portfolio Overview one off allocation-% drift from a target weight. F-13's internal/gate-table name stays "Rebalancer" (used in G-02/G-03/G-09, F-12, OP-05) — only the user-facing heading changed. Aggregates the Rebalancer (F-13) and Diversification Advisor (F-13a), plus two features that never had a dedicated F-ID before the split:

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-169 | **Active Alerts.** Portfolio-condition-triggered alerts (not calendar/event-triggered — contrast with 🔔 Catalyst Watch, F-11) grouped by category: stop-proximity, signal, concentration, earnings, analyst revisions, and signal-change (a holding's signal degrading/improving/unchanged since the last check). Shown as danger/warning/info counts; "no active alerts" renders a plain success state. **Analyst revisions (Phase 2, 2026-07-31):** fires on either a rating-action spike (3+ downgrades net ≤-2 = danger, 2+ net <0 = warning) OR — only when no rating-action alert already fired for that ticker — a consensus price-target cut of ≥7% (warning) / ≥15% (danger) over a 5-trading-day window with no rating change at all, closing a gap where a firm quietly guts its target without a formal downgrade. Withheld (no alert either way) for any ticker without 6+ days of accumulated target-snapshot history yet. |
| F-170 | **Custom Price Alerts.** Per-ticker user-set take-profit and floor price triggers; checked against the live price strip each run. Distinct from the mechanical stop-loss system (F-17/F-18) — these are user-defined watch levels, not the engine's protective stop. Config form (input fields + save button) rendered in a collapsed `st.expander`; fired alerts (triggers that have tripped) render **above** the expander so they remain visible without opening the config form. |

### 3.1d Intelligence

New standalone page (PORTFOLIO nav group, between Risk Analysis and Signals & Advice) — Concept B ("Portfolio-as-One Positioning Intelligence") from `docs/plans/next-evolution-strategy.md`, Phase 2 of the next-evolution roadmap. Answers what the portfolio's ownership MEANS in aggregate, not position-by-position. Now five tabs; built incrementally (cheapest/most decision-relevant first, per the user's chosen build order). The 4th tab (F-198, added 2026-07-24) is the first Agentic Intelligence Roadmap feature to land here; the 5th tab (F-202, added 2026-07-26) is the second — see §3.12 for the roadmap's other entries (F-196, F-197, F-199, F-201).

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-189 | **Correlation Clusters (Concept B panel 1) — SHIPPED 2026-07-17, commit `5c980b3`.** 🕸️ Correlation Clusters tab. The app already flags correlated PAIRS above `CORR_HIGH_PAIRS_THRESHOLD` (0.65) / `CORR_DANGER_PAIRS_THRESHOLD` (0.80) (`portfolio.diversification_score`), but never grouped them transitively — A-B and B-C flagged as two separate pairs, never surfaced as "these 3 names move together." `stock_analyzer/portfolio_intelligence.py::correlation_clusters()` closes that gap with plain-Python connected-components over the existing pairwise correlation matrix (`_corr_df_cache`, already computed at Home — no new API/compute cost, no new constants, no new library dependency). Each returned cluster carries its tickers, size, average INTERNAL correlation (mean of every pair inside the cluster, not just the edges that formed it), combined portfolio weight, and a danger/warning tier (danger if any internal pair ≥ `CORR_DANGER_PAIRS_THRESHOLD`). Singletons (no correlated pair) are excluded. Sorted by combined weight (biggest real exposure first). **Diagnostic only** — the closing caption explicitly defers to composite score (🔗 Risk Analysis / 📈 Analysis) for which name to act on; correlation never re-ranks or gates. |
| F-190 | **Risk Budget Gauge (Concept B panel 2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-17, commit `698fcd5`.** ⚖️ Risk Budget tab. Capital weight tells you how much money is in a position; it doesn't tell you how much of the portfolio's actual realized VOLATILITY that position is responsible for. `stock_analyzer/portfolio_intelligence.py::risk_budget()` computes a standard Euler / marginal-contribution-to-risk decomposition: rebuilds its own jointly-aligned returns frame from held price history (so per-ticker volatility and the correlation matrix can never drift out of sync), builds a covariance matrix (`vol ⊗ vol × corr`), and attributes total portfolio variance across positions (`w·Σ·w` for portfolio variance; `(Σ·w)/portfolio_vol` for each position's marginal contribution) so `Σ risk_pct ≈ 100%` by construction. Pure numpy/pandas — no new dependency, no new constant (deliberately no "outsize" flagging threshold; the raw weight%/risk%/ratio numbers are shown as-is). Renders a portfolio-annualized-volatility metric, a grouped bar chart (Capital Weight % vs Risk Contribution % per position, risk-descending), and a detail table including the risk÷weight ratio (>1 = a position eating more risk than its capital share). Verified pre-ship against 3 hand-checkable cases: equal-vol/zero-correlation/equal-weight → equal risk share; equal-vol/correlation≈1/70-30 weight → risk share tracks capital weight exactly (no diversification benefit); and a 10%-weight high-vol correlated position contributing 27% of portfolio risk (2.70× its capital share) — the "small position, outsize risk" case this panel exists to catch. Realized/historical only — not a forecast. **Diagnostic only** — identifies where risk concentrates, defers to composite score for any action. |
| F-191 | **Factor Tilt Heatmap (Concept B panel 3, final panel) — SHIPPED 2026-07-17, commit `a4c95cb`.** 📐 Factor Tilt tab. Directional exposure to 5 major style factors (Momentum=MTUM, Value=VLUE, Quality=QUAL, Low Volatility=USMV, Growth=VUG — `FACTOR_ETF_TICKERS` in `constants.py`) via Pearson correlation of each held position's daily returns against each factor ETF's returns over a trailing `FACTOR_TILT_WINDOW_DAYS` (126, ~6 months) window — set with the user over a 3-month alternative for statistical stability. `stock_analyzer/portfolio_intelligence.py::factor_tilt()` — returns-based style analysis, deliberately correlation-only (NOT a regression/beta model, and explicitly NOT FMP `.info` style tags, which the plan warns produces garbage). A (ticker, factor) cell requires ≥20 overlapping trading days (mirrors the existing empirical-correlation floor in `risk.py`'s rate-sensitivity feature) or is `None` — never a fabricated correlation. `portfolio_tilt` (the portfolio-level weighted read per factor) renormalizes weights among only the tickers with a valid correlation for that specific factor. Tickers with zero usable correlations across every factor are excluded entirely (not just from the "N included" count). Button-gated fetch (`app.py::_fetch_factor_etf_returns()`, mirrors the existing `_fetch_sector_returns()` batch-download pattern) — the only Intelligence panel that costs a new API call, unlike Correlation Clusters/Risk Budget which reuse already-cached data. Renders a ticker×factor heatmap (RdBu diverging scale) plus a weighted portfolio-tilt bar and a one-sentence dominant-tilt callout. **Explicitly directional, not precise** — factor estimates are noisy for a 10-20 position book over any single window; stated prominently in the tab's own caption. **Diagnostic only** — defers to composite score for any action. Opus review: FIX-FIRST (2 blocking) → SHIP (0 blocking) on re-review, after fixing an undocumented-constants gate trip and a dead constant (`FACTOR_TILT_WINDOW_DAYS` wasn't actually wired into the fetch on the first pass). **Closes Concept B (Phase 2) — all 3 panels now shipped.** |
| F-198 | **Structural Vulnerability Scanner — Phase 1 (P3 of the Agentic Intelligence Roadmap) — SHIPPED 2026-07-24, commit `9b47117`.** New "🧬 Structural Scan" 4th tab on 🧩 Intelligence, composing the three panels above (F-189/F-190/F-191) with one genuinely new calculation and a daily narrative. **Blast Radius Map**: `stock_analyzer/structural_scanner.py::blast_radius()` estimates cascade portfolio impact from shocking each of the top-3 risk-budget contributors (F-190's `risk_pct` ranking) by a fixed -20% — a single-factor beta approximation (`beta = corr(T,H) × vol_H/vol_T`; `comove = shock% × beta`) using SIGNED correlation (a strong negative correlation is a real hedge/offset) and gated on `abs(corr) ≥ CORR_HIGH_PAIRS_THRESHOLD` (reused from F-189, no new cutoff invented). Derives entirely from already-computed data (`_corr_df_cache` + `risk_budget()`'s own `vol_annualized_pct`) — zero new API calls. Computes unconditionally every render (pure Python). **Structural Narrative**: a single Haiku call per portfolio per Eastern trading day synthesizes the cluster report, weakest-link ranking, blast-radius results, and (if already loaded this session) Factor Tilt data into a 2-4 sentence plain-English explanation of the single most dangerous structural pattern — never inventing a real-world catalyst not present in the evidence. Day-cached to new `structural_scan_cache` table (one row per `scan_date`, portfolio-wide, not per-ticker). **Button-gated, not auto-computed** — Streamlit executes every tab body on every page rerun regardless of which tab is selected, so an auto-compute design would fire the LLM call far more than once/day; a failed/empty narrative is never cached, so a transient failure can be retried immediately. No new `constants.py` entries — the -20% shock and top-3 selection are module-level what-if inputs (same class as `stress_test.py`'s `_SECTOR_SHOCKS`), not investment-policy thresholds. **Diagnostic only — never gates or modifies the composite score.** 3-round Opus plan review (FIX-FIRST×2 then SHIP — Round 1 caught a 100x weight-scaling bug in the portfolio-impact formula and the auto-compute design flaw; Round 2 caught stale auto-compute language surviving in three sections after the authoritative fix) + Opus pre-ship code review (SHIP, 0 blocking). Plan: [docs/plans/structural-vulnerability-scanner.md](plans/structural-vulnerability-scanner.md). **Phase 2 — SHIPPED 2026-07-27** (both gates cleared same day: Phase 1 stable ≥3 days in production, and ≥2 days of real `structural_scan_cache` history verified directly in Supabase — rows on 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-27, with an expected gap on 07-25/07-26 since the narrative is button-gated, not automatic). New 🏠 Home "Structural alert" banner: `stock_analyzer/structural_scanner.py::detect_new_clusters()` diffs today's live `correlation_clusters()` output against `db.load_structural_scan_baseline()`'s **most recent scan on or before today** (deliberately not strictly "prior" — using `.lte`, not `.lt`, so once today's own narrative has been generated, live clusters compare against themselves and the banner self-clears rather than nagging about already-reviewed clusters for the rest of the day). A cluster is flagged only when it contains a ticker pair that (a) wasn't already co-clustered in the baseline snapshot and (b) is a **verified direct positive edge today** (`corr ≥ CORR_HIGH_PAIRS_THRESHOLD`, signed — matching `correlation_clusters()`'s own edge condition exactly) — pair-level, not whole-cluster-set, comparison, so a cluster merely losing a member (decorrelation) is never flagged. A prior snapshot of `None` (no scan has ever run) suppresses the feature entirely; an empty list `[]` (a real scan found zero clusters) does not suppress — every one of today's real clusters then flags as new, the cleanest possible zero-to-some formation signal. Zero new data fetches, zero new LLM calls, zero new `constants.py` entries, zero new table (new read-only `db.load_structural_scan_baseline()` against the existing `structural_scan_cache`). New coordination-pattern cache key `_structural_alert_cache` (see CLAUDE.md's coordination-pattern list). **Diagnostic only — never gates or modifies the composite score.** 3-round Opus plan review (FIX-FIRST×2 then SHIP — Round 1 caught a transitive-pair fabrication risk in the "new pairing" citation and the `.lt`-never-clears-same-day bug; Round 2 caught a signed-vs-`abs()` correlation regression in Round 1's own fix, which would have let an anti-correlated pair be cited as a "new pairing") + Opus pre-ship code review (SHIP, 0 blocking). New regression tests: `tests/test_structural_scanner.py` (also backfills `blast_radius()`, which had zero coverage since Phase 1 shipped). Plan: [docs/plans/structural-scanner-phase2.md](plans/structural-scanner-phase2.md). **Closes the Structural Vulnerability Scanner feature — both phases now shipped.** |
| F-199 | **Hidden Same-Bet Detector (D1 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-24, commit `5e8479f`.** New "🧠 Hidden Same-Bet Detector" section on the 🧬 Structural Scan tab (below F-198's content), gated **only** on saved-thesis coverage — deliberately independent of the price-correlation gate above it, so thin price history never hides this thesis-only signal. Detects positions that look diversified by sector and price correlation but secretly bet on the same underlying assumption (e.g. three names all implicitly long "AI capex keeps ripping") — concentration risk F-198's price-correlation scan structurally cannot see. One Haiku call/day (`stock_analyzer/thesis_cluster.py::generate_thesis_clusters()`) semantically clusters currently-held positions' saved buy theses (`user_thesis`, most recent BUY-with-a-thesis per ticker), grounded by a **two-layer fabrication guard**: every returned ticker must normalized-match the supplied corpus, and every returned "quote" must verifiably appear in that specific ticker's own stored thesis text — protecting against a spurious grouping of two genuinely-held tickers, not just a fake ticker identity (semantic clustering over free prose is fuzzier than citing structured data, so ticker validation alone wasn't sufficient). "No shared assumption found" is a valid, expected, cacheable result — never forced. A pure-Python classification step (`classify_clusters()`, zero LLM, cannot hallucinate) labels each validated cluster against F-189's `correlation_clusters()` output as **⚪ unverified** (no price-correlation data this session — never coerced into a false positive/negative), **🟠 possible** (checked, not already price-correlated — the headline finding), or **🟡 confirmed** (checked, already price-correlated — de-emphasized corroboration), plus any already-price-correlated sub-pairs within a `possible` group. Classification is recomputed fresh every render from the *current* session's correlation data — the cache stores only the validated clusters, never a frozen classification, so a cluster found `unverified` correctly reclassifies once price data becomes available later the same day. Day-cached to new `thesis_cluster_cache` table (one row per `scan_date`, portfolio-wide). No `constants.py` entries — truncation length (~1500 chars/thesis) is prompt-sizing, not a policy threshold. **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or modifies `correlation_clusters()`/`factor_tilt()` themselves.** Opus design review (FIX-FIRST → resolved): added the evidence-span citation requirement; fixed a false "hidden" default when price data is simply unavailable (added the `unverified` state); fixed a render-nesting bug that would have hidden the whole section whenever price data was thin. Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking, 3 non-blocking fixed (a `.iloc[-1]` oldest-vs-newest-BUY bug — also flagged in F-197 Phase 2's exit-debate code for a follow-up fix; a ticker-dedup guard; truncation-note persistence across rerun). Plan: [docs/plans/hidden-same-bet-detector.md](plans/hidden-same-bet-detector.md). |
| F-202 | **Signal Coherence Auditor (D3 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-26, commit `df36c6d`.** New 5th tab, "🧭 Signal Coherence," on 🧩 Intelligence. For every held ticker, mechanically joins three EXISTING, independent advisory surfaces — the composite score's own directional class (`signal_reconciliation.classify_composite_direction()`, a public wrapper over the existing `_composite_class()`), the weekly Thesis Red Team's erosion status (`thesis_reviews.status` ∈ INTACT/WEAKENING/BROKEN), and the most recent Bull/Bear debate verdict across BOTH debate types (`debate_cache.verdict`, new `db.load_debate_verdicts()` read) — and surfaces only the tickers where the present signals **genuinely disagree** (at least one bullish-aligned + at least one bearish-aligned). A ticker needs ≥2 of the three signals present to be evaluated at all; full agreement among evaluated tickers renders no card (the roadmap itself flagged this item as the most noise-prone — only disagreement is shown, never a wall of "everything's fine"). **Zero new LLM calls, zero new cache table** — unlike every other item on this roadmap, this is a pure-Python presence-check + direction-map computed live on page load, since all three inputs already exist as stored/computed data. **Verified invariant, does not flip:** in BOTH entry-type and exit-type debates, `bull_wins` is bullish-aligned and `bear_wins` is bearish-aligned (an exit debate's Bull argues to continue holding, Bear argues to exit — the same polarity as an entry debate). A same-date tie between an entry and an exit debate on one ticker prefers the exit debate (the current hold decision); a selected entry debate that predates the ticker's most recent BUY is tagged "buy-time" in its chip rather than shown as a live re-litigation. **Corrects the roadmap's original one-liner**, which also named "regime-fit" as a 4th input — verified `regime_targets.regime_position_gap()` returns one portfolio-level beta/cash gap, not a per-ticker directional signal, so forcing it into this per-name join would be a category error; dropped rather than shoehorned in. No synthesized prose — each card shows the raw signal chips (type + date on each) as the finding itself, never a templated "these disagree because…" sentence that would invent a causal story neither signal actually asserts. No `constants.py` entries. **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or modifies any of the three source surfaces.** Opus design review: SHIP, 0 blocking, 5 non-blocking refinements folded in (the polarity-invariant documentation, the tiebreak rule, unknown-as-neutral gating semantics, buy-time tagging, always-show-all-present-chips). Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking, 2 cosmetic non-blocking fixes (NaN Signal rendering "nan", null erosion status rendering "None"). Plan: [docs/plans/signal-coherence-auditor.md](plans/signal-coherence-auditor.md). |
| F-203 | **Watchlist Resurrection (O4 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-26, commit `631e6ba`.** On the existing 👁️ Watchlist page, highlights watchlist tickers that are **old** (added ≥ `WATCHLIST_STALE_DAYS` = 30 days ago, user-set policy value) **and** currently **actionable** (`ENTER_NOW` or `NEAR_ENTRY` per the page's existing `build_watchlist_recommendation()` classification) **and not already held**. **Corrects the roadmap's original "back at the setup you originally wanted" framing** — verified the `watchlist` table (`ticker`, `added_at` only) never recorded any original setup/target/reason-for-add to compare against, so the honest reframe is "old + independently actionable today," not a reconstruction of a number that was never saved. **One resurrection predicate drives both the KPI summary count and every per-card caption** (fixed during design review — the original draft would have let a held+stale `ENTER_NOW` ticker get counted in the summary line while its card is silently dropped by the pre-existing "Already in Portfolio" override, the same double-surface mismatch class this app has hit before); the predicate explicitly excludes held tickers for **both** `ENTER_NOW` and `NEAR_ENTRY` (the existing override only covers the former). No new LLM call, no new page, no new cache table — a highlight over already-computed page data plus one new read, `db.load_watchlist_added_dates()` (UTC `added_at` converted to America/New_York before taking the calendar date, matching the app's existing NY-timezone convention). A ticker with no readable `added_at` is silently skipped, not treated as "very stale" or "never stale." Re-adding a removed ticker resets its clock by design (a fresh `INSERT`, not an update) — the correct clock for "days since you last actually re-engaged," not a bug. New constant `WATCHLIST_STALE_DAYS = 30` in `constants.py` (`ANALYST_COVERAGE_FRESH_DAYS = 30` elsewhere is not real precedent — that constant means the opposite, "still fresh," for a passively-accumulated surface; 30 stands on its own as the user's judgment call for an actively-curated watchlist). **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or changes `build_watchlist_recommendation()`'s classification, sort order, or the existing KPI metrics.** Opus design review: FIX-FIRST → SHIP (1 blocking: the single-predicate fix above). Opus pre-ship code review: FIX-FIRST → SHIP (1 blocking: `WATCHLIST_STALE_DAYS` initially missing from the `docs/architecture.md` constants table, the mechanically-enforced `scripts/check_constants_documented.py` CI gate; 2 non-blocking fixed — caption wording overstated `NEAR_ENTRY` as "qualifies to enter," reworded to "actionable again"; the UTC→ET conversion above, originally a raw string truncation that could undercount by 1 day near the midnight ET boundary). Plan: [docs/plans/watchlist-resurrection.md](plans/watchlist-resurrection.md). |
| F-205 | **Sizing Alpha (O5 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-26.** New full-width "📏 Sizing Alpha" section on 🪞 Investor Mirror (My Edge), below the existing Behavioral Biases grid. **Corrects the roadmap's "flat sizing across conviction tiers" framing** — verified no reliable "conviction at time of buy" field exists anywhere in the schema (`recommendations.conviction` only covers same-day engine surfacings, the same coverage-gap class O1 already hit; `decision_context` captures portfolio-level context, never the traded ticker's own score; `investor_mirror.conviction_alignment()` is current-state only). Reframed around the one thing reliably recorded for every trade: dollar size actually committed. Groups `investor_mirror.build_closed_lots()`'s FIFO fragments back to their **originating BUY LOT** (`groupby(ticker, buy_date, buy_price)`, not by sell fragment — bucketing at the fragment level would shred a large, later-scaled-out winning bet into several small-dollar fragments, biasing the comparison toward a false "flat sizing" conclusion), then splits buy lots into **dollar-size terciles** (Small/Medium/Large) via a **tie-robust rank split** (`pd.qcut` on rank, not raw value — guards against round-dollar buys collapsing a plain `pd.qcut` into a degenerate split; explicit `None` if fewer than 3 distinct dollar values exist). Shows each tercile's **dollar-weighted** average realized gain % (dollar-weighted, not share-weighted, since raw share counts aren't comparable across tickers at very different prices). **Explicit, bolded caveat**: shown in absolute dollars, not adjusted for portfolio growth over the account's history — no point-in-time total-portfolio-value history exists to normalize by. Reuses `INVESTOR_MIRROR_MIN_CLOSED_LOTS` as the per-tercile floor (now applied to the buy-lot count, post-grouping) — no new constant needed for O5. Descriptive, not prescriptive: captions cover both directions (large-beats-small and small-beats-large) plus whether Medium sat between them. No new LLM call, no cache table — pure Python, reuses the same `_mi_lots` already computed for Section 2. Opus design review: FIX-FIRST → SHIP (2 blocking, both above: the buy-lot-vs-fragment unit fix, the tie-robust split). Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking. Plan: [docs/plans/sizing-alpha.md](plans/sizing-alpha.md). |
| F-206 | **Premature-Exit Cost (O6 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-26.** New 4th card ("⏱️ Premature-Exit Cost") in the existing 2×2 Behavioral Biases grid on 🪞 Investor Mirror, filling `_mi_bc2`'s one open slot alongside Breakeven Anchoring. **Corrects the "Cost" framing away from any forecasted/counterfactual price estimate** (a "you'd have made $X more if you'd held" figure requires forecasting a hypothetical later price — fabrication risk this app forbids) toward a real, already-realized comparison: among winning closed lots, the share-weighted average gain % of "quick exit" winners (held less than `PREMATURE_EXIT_RATIO`=0.5 × the user's own average winner-hold) vs. "patient" winners (the rest). Filters explicitly on `is_gain == True` **and** a non-null `pnl_abs`/`pnl_pct` — not `is_gain` alone, since `build_closed_lots()`'s `is_gain = (pnl_abs or 0.0) >= 0` evaluates **True when `pnl_abs` is `None`**, which would otherwise admit phantom "winners" with no real P&L. Computes its own average-winner-hold independently rather than depending on `disposition_effect()`'s return, since that function requires BOTH a populated winner AND loser group to return anything and would silently withhold `winner_avg_days` whenever losers are thin. **New feature-specific constant `PREMATURE_EXIT_MIN_LOTS = 5`** (user-set) — deliberately NOT `INVESTOR_MIRROR_MIN_CLOSED_LOTS` (10), since O6's quick/patient buckets draw from a winners-only, twice-split population roughly half the size that floor was sized for; reusing it would likely have left the card permanently dark. Captions cover both directions (patient-beats-quick and quick-ties/beats-patient) and note that some quick exits are deliberate risk management, not impulsiveness, and that one sell decision can contribute fragments to both buckets. No new LLM call, no cache table — reuses the same `_mi_lots`. Opus design review: FIX-FIRST → SHIP (1 blocking: the floor-sizing correction above). Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking, 2 cosmetic non-blocking fixed (mean-vs-median honesty note; Medium-tercile summary clause — shared with F-205 since both shipped in the same pass). Plan: [docs/plans/premature-exit-cost.md](plans/premature-exit-cost.md). |
| F-220 | **Entry Timing — 6th tab on 📊 Predictive Analytics (Phase 1 SHIPPED 2026-07-28, commits `34f4782`, `eea519e`, `29540d1` — plan `docs/plans/entry-timing-tab.md`; full history in `docs/shipped-log.md`).** New "⏱️ Entry Timing" tab, sibling to F-178's existing 5. Answers a narrower question than the other tabs: does technical momentum running ahead of the composite consensus at the moment a `new_pick` fires predict a rough first few days, even when the pick is right over its real weeks-to-months horizon — distinguishing bad entry timing from ordinary short-term noise (motivated by a live case: AMD fired as `new_pick` 5x in 2 weeks in 2026-07 at composite 69–71 / momentum 94–100, losing ~9–11% / alpha −7pp to −10pp vs SPY every time). Five new pure functions in `stock_analyzer/predictive_analytics.py`: `dedupe_repeated_tickers()` (collapses same-ticker `new_pick` re-firings within `ENTRY_TIMING_DEDUP_WINDOW_DAYS` (5) calendar days of a prior kept firing into one opportunity, keeping the first firing of each cluster — the daily scanner re-firing an unbought name is one opportunity, not N data points); `divergence_at_entry()` (= `momentum_score − composite_score`; only positive divergence is in scope — negative divergence is a different question, filtered out downstream, not computed as zero); `forward_alpha_at_horizon()` (Day+1/Day+5 alpha via a live per-ticker forward-close fetch — `providers.orchestrator.get_historical_close`, injectable so `app.py` can wire in a cached wrapper — minus the SPY return over the identical window, reusing `recommendations_history._spy_return_pct`); `by_divergence_band()` (groups into Aligned/Diverging/Extreme bands using `ENTRY_TIMING_DIVERGENCE_ALIGNED_MAX` (15) / `ENTRY_TIMING_DIVERGENCE_DIVERGING_MAX` (25), reporting Day+1/Day+5 forward alpha plus the existing mature-outcome `alpha_pct` as the Day+20 leg — no new SPY fetch, no new DB columns); `find_illustrating_case()` (finds whichever ticker is CURRENTLY the most-repeated same-ticker Extreme-divergence re-firer in the live, un-deduped data — grounds the tab's illustrating-case callout in real, present data rather than a fixed historical citation, so it can never go stale; returns `None`, never fabricated, when no ticker has fired ≥2 times in the Extreme band yet); `band_narrative()` (rule-based, non-LLM one-line plain-English description of a band's Day+1→Day+5→Day+20 alpha trajectory shape — e.g. "recovers quickly," "deepest drawdown of the three," "no recovery pattern yet" — with an optional `illustrating_ticker` callback appended only to the band `find_illustrating_case()`'s ticker actually sits in). **Button-gated, not auto-computed:** the per-ticker forward-close fetches are heavier than the page's existing shared load, so the tab computes lazily into its own `st.session_state["_entry_timing_cache"]` key only after the user clicks "🔍 Analyze Entry Timing" — mirrors the existing Sector Rotation Heatmap load-button idiom, not the page's own always-on `_pac_enriched` load; the page's own "🔄 Refresh data" button also clears this cache. Renders a Plotly grouped bar (one x-axis group per band labelled with its numeric range, e.g. "< 15 (aligned)," one bar per horizon Day+1/Day+5/Day+20, **colored by alpha SIGN** — green = positive, red = negative, not by horizon) alongside a right-hand stack of bordered stat cards (one per band, border-colored by band identity) each showing a "chance of a red Day+1" headline (gated on `PREDICTIVE_MIN_BAND_N`) plus its `band_narrative()` sentence, a dynamic "📌 Illustrating case" callout (from `find_illustrating_case()`, omitted entirely when no qualifying repeat-offender exists yet), and a raw-values expander — same idioms as the page's other tabs, reworked once to match a previously-approved visual mockup that the initial build had drifted from (caught via live screenshot comparison, not verbal recollection — see `feedback_mockup_first_ux` memory). Scoped to `new_pick` only; `add_winner` deliberately not pooled in (different risk story — adding to existing exposure vs. fresh-capital timing — analyzed later as its own cut if ever revisited). `synthesize_directives()` gains an `entry_timing_bands` kwarg — emits a single `caution` directive (never `action`) when the Extreme band's Day+1 sample clears `PREDICTIVE_MIN_BAND_N`, phrased as awareness, not a sizing prescription (the app has no position-sizing mechanism to act on that framing yet). **Diagnostic only — never feeds back into the composite score or the 5-gate new-position pipeline;** if it ever produced evidence strong enough to justify a real gate, that would be a separate, explicit `constants.py` policy decision with the user's sign-off, not something this tab does on its own. **All 3 new constants are explicitly provisional** — fit to the single AMD anecdote, not yet validated against the real divergence-band distribution; re-check before treating as tuned. RSI-at-entry and earnings-proximity-at-entry are explicitly out of scope for v1 (neither is threaded into the `new_pick` dict today — wiring either touches multiple files across `daily_briefing.py`/`app.py`/`db.py`, more than this build's scope). **Phase 2 — SHIPPED 2026-07-28, commit `de12323`** (same day as Phase 1, once the user validated the divergence pattern held over a real sample: Extreme band n=20 matured Day+20 outcomes averaging -14.2% alpha vs SPY — not the 6-row anecdote that motivated the plan). A live caution annotation directly on the Grow Today "New Positions to Initiate" pick card, at the moment a fresh `new_pick` fires — the only mechanism with a plausible causal path to actually preventing a loss, since the retrospective tab alone requires the user to remember to check it. `daily_briefing.py::_grow_today()` attaches `pick["divergence"]` (via `divergence_at_entry()`, reused as-is from Phase 1) to every new_pick candidate — curated and mover picks alike, `add_winner` still excluded — right after the composite/fundamentals/staleness/macro/sector gates have all passed. `app.py`'s Grow Today card render shows a caption when `divergence > ENTRY_TIMING_DIVERGENCE_DIVERGING_MAX` (the existing, already-reviewed Extreme-band threshold — no new constant): momentum is running well ahead of the composite, and similar Extreme-divergence picks have historically looked calm early but underperformed by full maturity — pointing to the ⏱️ Entry Timing tab for current numbers rather than quoting a live percentage (which would require re-running Phase 1's per-ticker forward-close fetch on every brief build, defeating why that tab is button-gated). **Stays a caution, never a block or gate** — the pick has already cleared every gate by the point this attaches. **Opus pre-code design review: FIX-FIRST (1 blocking) → fixed → SHIP** — the first caption draft claimed picks "trade rougher in the first few days," which was backwards from the validated shape (calm early, damage late); reworded to match. **The same review also caught a live bug in Phase 1's own already-shipped `synthesize_directives()` caution text**, which had the identical inversion (claimed picks "open red on Day+1... though the effect fades by maturity") — fixed in the same commit, regated from `day1_n`/`day1_pct_red` to `day20_n`/`day20_alpha` (the horizon the directive actually describes). 4 new tests for the corrected directive logic. |
| F-207 | **Catalyst-Specific Stress (D4 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-26, commit `0dcecf1`. Last item of Phase 4 — closes the roadmap.** New "📅 Catalyst-Specific Stress (Beta)" expander, sibling to F-200's "🎯 Regime-Aware Adversarial Scenario" on 🔗 Risk Analysis → 🔥 Stress Testing. The event-driven twin of that feature: where F-200 asks "what ongoing macro CONDITION threatens this book," this asks "what single upcoming DATED EVENT (a HIGH-impact, sector-specific macro print, or a held position's own earnings report, within `CATALYST_STRESS_WINDOW_DAYS`=14 days) threatens the SAME structural weak points F-198/F-200 already identify." `stock_analyzer/catalyst_stress.py::rank_catalyst_threats()` ranks macro and earnings candidates in **two separate lists, never cross-scored** — summing a many-ticker macro event's overlap against a one-ticker earnings event's weight would always favor macro events, making the "top-ranked catalyst" predictably always the next CPI/FOMC rather than something genuinely informative. **Macro events whose category resolves to the `__ALL__` sector-impact sentinel are excluded entirely** (`macro_calendar.is_all_sector_category()`, new public wrapper over `_SECTOR_IMPACT`) — Fed Policy (FOMC) and Growth (GDP) events threaten every sector equally, so their overlap with any weak-point set is the maximum possible by construction, telling you nothing about which SPECIFIC holding is exposed (the opposite of this feature's purpose). The **weak-point ticker set** is pinned exactly: the union of every `shocked_ticker` and cascade `contributing_tickers` across `structural_scanner.blast_radius()` results, plus every ticker in any `portfolio_intelligence.correlation_clusters()` cluster. Earnings-candidate scoring uses only real, already-computed numbers — a ticker's own portfolio weight% plus (if it's a `shocked_ticker`) that entry's own `portfolio_impact_pct` — never an invented "contribution" figure. One Haiku call synthesizes a narrative covering whichever candidate(s) exist; if the ranking finds neither a macro nor an earnings candidate, the LLM is never called at all — "nothing catalyst-specific stands out" is decided by the pure-Python ranking step, not asserted by an API call with no evidence. **Recomputes `blast_radius()`/`correlation_clusters()` independently** rather than depending on F-200's local variables (never published to session_state), so this feature never silently depends on whether the user opened that section first this session — the same cheap, uncached recompute F-200 itself already pays. New constant `CATALYST_STRESS_WINDOW_DAYS = 14` (user-set) — deliberately distinct from `EARNINGS_URGENCY_SOON_DAYS` (a different feature's display tier) so tuning one never silently affects the other. New `catalyst_stress_cache` table (RLS, mirrors `regime_scenario_cache`'s exact shape). **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or modifies `blast_radius()`/`correlation_clusters()`/`build_macro_calendar()`/the earnings calendar themselves.** Opus design review: FIX-FIRST → SHIP (3 blocking: the cross-scoring bias, an undefined "contribution %" field pinned to the real `portfolio_impact_pct`, an ambiguous weak-point definition pinned exactly). Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking, cosmetic non-blocking fixed (a None-guard on impact-percent formatting; a code comment explaining the deliberate `abs()` in earnings scoring). Plan: [docs/plans/catalyst-specific-stress.md](plans/catalyst-specific-stress.md). |
| F-224 | **Outcome Range — historical block-bootstrap Monte Carlo simulator. New 4th tab "🎲 Outcome Range" on 🔗 Risk Analysis, sibling to 📊 Dashboard / 📋 Action Plan / 🔥 Stress Testing.** Where F-14/F-14a/F-168/F-200/F-207 are all single-POINT-estimate scenarios, this is the app's first probability-weighted DISTRIBUTION of portfolio outcomes. `stock_analyzer/monte_carlo.py` resamples REAL historical daily returns per held ticker (`MC_HISTORY_PERIOD`="5y", via `data.fetch_price_history`) in contiguous `MC_BLOCK_DAYS`-length blocks (=20 trading days), sampling the SAME calendar dates across every ticker in a given trial — this is what preserves the real correlation between holdings (a historically correlated selloff day hits correlated names together in the resample) instead of destroying it via independent per-ticker resampling. **Deliberately does NOT attach a probability to any macro regime** — the same thin-data problem that made F-200 drop that framing (`daily_regime` has only ~3 days of history) applies here too, so this sidesteps it entirely by resampling raw historical returns instead of regime labels. Tickers with fewer than `MC_MIN_HISTORY_DAYS`=252 trading days of history (recent IPOs, fetch failures) are excluded and reported in the UI (never silently dropped); their weight is redistributed among the rest, and the $ outcome table is scaled to the included holdings' value only — not the full portfolio — so the % and $ ranges shown always describe the same thing. User selects a horizon (`MC_HORIZON_OPTIONS_DAYS`=[21,63,252] trading days, ~1mo/1qtr/1yr; default `MC_HORIZON_DEFAULT_DAYS`=63) and clicks an explicit "Run Simulation" button — never auto-run on page load, since the multi-year fetch across ~15-30 held tickers is new cost/latency the app doesn't pay anywhere else; result cached 24h (`_cached_monte_carlo`, keyed on the ticker/weight/market-value tuples + horizon). Renders a `plotly` fan chart (5th/25th/50th/75th/95th percentile bands over the horizon) plus a horizon-endpoint percentile table and a methodology/caveats expander (states the constant-weight, no-rebalancing simplification and the historical-bootstrap-not-forecast framing explicitly). **Diagnostic/awareness only — same class as Stress Testing and Regime Fit; never gates, scores, or modifies any recommendation, WATCH/TRIM/EXIT tier, or the composite score.** New constants: `MC_HISTORY_PERIOD`, `MC_MIN_HISTORY_DAYS`, `MC_TRIALS`(=2000), `MC_BLOCK_DAYS`, `MC_HORIZON_OPTIONS_DAYS`, `MC_HORIZON_DEFAULT_DAYS` (all simulation-method parameters, not investment-policy thresholds). 23 new tests in `tests/test_monte_carlo.py`. Opus-reviewed SHIP, 0 blocking (5 non-blocking findings — a dollar-basis/excluded-ticker inconsistency, imprecise "buy-and-hold" wording, a too-loose correlation-preservation test bound, cache-key drift, and a note on the hardcoded display-percentile tuple — all fixed same session except the cache-key note, accepted as a low-priority TTL-effectiveness observation, not a correctness issue). |
| F-249 | **Sizing coherence — one sizing engine, capped, and a footer that says what it means. CHANGES A LIVE RECOMMENDATION on the primary buy surface and in three cron emails.** Phase 1 of `docs/plans/sizing-calibration.md`; grew out of a user question ("ALB shows 26 shares, I buy 5–10 — what did I leave on the table?") whose honest answer turned out to be that the benchmark was wrong. The app had **three mutually inconsistent answers** to "how much should I buy". **(1)** `daily_briefing._suggest_size()` was a SECOND sizing engine — hardcoded trend-bucket stops (`0.05`/`0.07`/`0.08`, bare literals setting a recommendation, i.e. a live Hard-Rule-#1 violation) and **no concentration cap at all**. Its suggested position reduces algebraically to `RISK_PCT_PER_TRADE / stop_pct`, which is **price-independent**: **30.0% / 21.4% / 18.75% of book** against `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING = 15.0` — every new-pick card suggested **1.25×–2× the app's own hard cap** and printed the breach in its own caption. The module imported `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` and enforced it two other places in the same file, just not in the sizing function; `_suggest_size` had **zero direct test coverage**. Meanwhile `trade_review.py` counts the user's trades that breached the same ceiling and reports them back as a discipline problem — the app scolded the user for a breach its own buy card recommended. **(2)** The add-to-winner path passed a **literal** `"Strong Uptrend"` rather than the row's real trend, so every add suggestion took the tightest 5% stop → 30% of book, on positions that already carry weight. **(3)** `deploy_note` computed the RISK BUDGET (`portfolio_value × RISK_PCT_PER_TRADE × n_trades`) but said "consider **deploying** ~$X today" — at $50k/3 setups it read **$2,250** while its own three cards on the same screen requested ~**$45,000**, a 20× spread. Because the first two defects inflate and the third deflates, a user anchoring on the footer and buying 5–10 shares may have been following one of the app's own figures correctly — so the originating "am I under-sizing?" question was **not evaluable** until this shipped. **The fix:** all four surfaces (Grow Today new picks, add-to-winner, Analysis, Watchlist) plus the emails now route through the single ceiling-capped `risk.position_sizing()`, off the ATR stop the bundle **already computes** (`bundle_loader` → `atr_stop_loss(df, ATR_STOP_MULT)`, exposed as `["stop"]`/`["entry_lo"]`/`["entry_hi"]`), so unification costs **zero additional network calls** — new picks read `composites[ticker]`, adds read `held_data[ticker]`. `_suggest_size` is **deleted**, which removes the hardcoded stop literals rather than hoisting them. Sizing moved to **after** the composite gate, which is what guarantees a bundle exists (the gate `continue`s on a missing composite), so there is no fallback path and none was invented. One private adapter, `_position_size_for_render()`, maps `position_sizing`'s output to the renderer's key shape; `entry_lo`/`entry_hi` now come from the bundle's ATR-derived `entry_zone()` instead of the deleted `stop_pct × 0.40` heuristic. When the cap binds, the card and all three emails **state it** and give the uncapped risk-budget size (never-silently-filter). A new static line states the suggested size is a **per-idea maximum, not a portfolio plan** — at 15% each a book funds `int(100 / SINGLE_NAME_CEILING)` concurrent full positions, derived not hardcoded. `deploy_note` is re-worded to "you'd be risking ~$X if every stop hits" — **wording only, the formula and both dollar figures are byte-identical** (explicit user decision: it is a risk statement). **A SECOND, pre-existing defect was found mid-build by the new invariant test and fixed under a separate user decision.** `position_sizing`'s `ceiling_shares = max(1, int(...))` floor: when one share costs more than the ceiling allows, `int(...)` is 0, the floor forces 1 share anyway, **and `ceiling_capped` stays False** (because `risk_based_shares` is also 1, so `shares > ceiling_shares` never fires) — a $4,500 name against a $10,000 book returned **1 share = 45% of portfolio with no disclosure whatsoever**. Already live on Analysis and Watchlist, reachable via `scan_movers` (which deliberately surfaces names outside the curated universe) and via any ticker typed into Analysis; the breach threshold is `price > portfolio_value × 15%`. **User decision: fail closed.** `position_sizing` now returns `None` when the ceiling cannot afford one whole share, propagating suppression to every caller (all four already guard on a falsy result); the Grow Today adapter returns a marker `{ceiling_infeasible, one_share_pct}` carrying **no `shares` key**, so a renderer gating on `shares` can never print "0 shares", and the card/email explain why no size is shown instead of going blank. Two render guards changed from `if _sz` to `if _sz.get('shares')` accordingly. **No new constants and no constant value changed** — `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`/`RISK_PCT_PER_TRADE`/`ATR_STOP_MULT` are used as-is. **No pre-existing test was modified** — `tests/test_sizing_coherence.py` is a new file, and two of its own tests were rewritten mid-build once the fail-closed decision landed (an earlier draft had asserted the 1-share floor it replaced). It includes a boundary test that exactly-one-share-affordable still sizes, guarding against the fail-closed branch being written `<= 1`. **Opus review returned FIX-FIRST with 2 blocking, both fixed before ship, and both were disclosure failures rather than arithmetic ones.** **(1)** `position_sizing` now returns `None` for two structurally different reasons, but the Analysis and Watchlist fallback captions still said "stop price too close to entry or not set" — so typing a high-priced name into Analysis blamed the stop while rendering a healthy 2xATR stop directly above, and the architecture row added by this same change claimed the suppression "reaches Analysis and Watchlist too". Fixed by extracting `risk.sizing_unavailable_reason()` as the **single** predicate for both conditions (`"stop"` / `"ceiling"` / `None`), which `position_sizing`, the Grow Today adapter and both captions now share — the adapter had been re-deriving the ceiling test, so this also removed a second copy rather than adding a third. Degenerate-stop takes precedence over ceiling, so a user is never sent to fix account size while their stop is also broken. **(2)** The new-pick path sized with the scanner row's live `price` but the bundle's `stop` (derived from the bundle's own last close). Above the stale stop that merely under-sizes (safe); **below** it `risk_per_share` went negative, `position_sizing` returned `None`, and the card rendered a **BUY with no size and no caption at all** — a silent filter on the primary buy surface, a state that could not occur before because the deleted `_suggest_size` derived its stop from the same `price`. Fixed by deriving the stop from the bundle's ATR against the live price (`price - ATR_STOP_MULT x atr`, falling back to the bundle stop when ATR is absent), so `risk_per_share` is exactly `ATR_STOP_MULT x ATR` and `stop_pct` is honest; plus a second `stop_infeasible` marker so that path explains itself on the card and in the emails instead of going blank. Non-blocking items also folded in: the add card's size line is now labelled **"Sizing stop"** to distinguish the fresh-entry ATR stop it sizes against from the ratcheted protective stop the thesis line's "% above stop" measures from (the ATR basis itself is a policy decision recorded in memory `project_stop_ladder_and_display` and was left unchanged); both `sizing` reads are null-hardened with an explicit `isinstance` check rather than `or {}`, **because `or {}` is itself the offline-sentinel-collapse antipattern the recurring-defect gate blocks** — the reviewer's suggested idiom and the repo's own gate were in direct conflict, resolved at the source; a tautological `deploy_note` test (it recomputed the formula and asserted it against itself, never importing `daily_briefing`) was replaced with one that drives `_grow_today` and parses the rendered string; a dead unused test helper was deleted; and three genuinely new end-to-end tests now exercise `_grow_today` so that dropping `sizing` from the pick dict, reading the wrong `held_data` key, or reverting the price-basis fix all fail loudly instead of shipping green. **Round 2 returned SHIP, 0 blocking**, having verified the new detector is exactly equivalent to the guard it replaced across all four input quadrants (so dropping `max(1, ...)` is safe by construction — the helper's ceiling clause and the sizer's cap block now evaluate the identical expression under identical conditions). Eight of its ten non-blocking items were taken before commit, two of them more than cosmetic. **(i)** The ATR fallback keyed off the *input* (`atr > 0`) rather than the *result*, so a name whose ATR is at least half its price produced a NON-POSITIVE stop that hit the missing-input guard and rendered a BUY with no size and no caption — the last instance of the exact silent shape blocking-item 2 existed to remove. It now falls back on the result. **(ii) A regression this change had itself introduced, caught by review:** `entry_lo`/`entry_hi` stayed on the bundle's last-close basis while the size moved to the live price, so a mover's entry zone could sit entirely BELOW the current quote — and the emails' "only act if price is still inside $X-$Y" guard would then contradict its own "GO ... BUY" headline on the same card. The deleted `_suggest_size` had derived the zone from the same price it sized against, so the zone always bracketed it; the zone is now re-derived via `targets.entry_zone(price, atr)` on the live basis, restoring that property. Also taken: the end-to-end tests no longer allow themselves to `pytest.skip` (a skip would let a future gate change turn the only price-basis guard into a permanent green no-op); a **new add-lane end-to-end test** closes the coverage gap that had made an earlier draft of this row's own test claim unsupported; `sizing_unavailable_reason` coerces None/NaN, since it is now public and called from render branches reached *because* values are missing; a whole-module scan for the word "deploying" was replaced with an assertion on rendered output; and the marker-contract docstrings in `daily_briefing` and `notify` were corrected from two states to three. Noted-not-changed: the add lane's suppression captions are unreachable (its `gap >= ADD_WINNER_MIN_GAP_PCT` gate guarantees `price > stop`) and its stop deliberately stays on the bundle basis — now commented at the call site so a future "unify the lanes" edit cannot miss the gate that makes it safe. **3967 passed.** `scripts/antipattern_baseline.json` moved by exactly 2 lines — verified as re-fingerprints of two PRE-EXISTING flagged blocks whose text changed, with distinct keys 401→401 and UNSAFE_HTML_DYNAMIC total 294→294, so nothing new was absorbed. **3957 passed.** |
| F-250 | **Day-P&L baseline reconciliation — the tile says when its own number is wrong, and by how much. Awareness only; the arithmetic is unchanged.** Today's P&L (Tier B) is `current_val − baseline_val + cash_delta`, anchored to a prior-close `daily_snapshots` row. It already flagged **orphans** (a baseline ticker neither still held nor traded today). Found 2026-08-23 by reconciling the app against Robinhood on a closed market: that check is **structurally blind to the two other ways the same disagreement occurs**, and both were live. A DELL share-count drift (2 → 6 shares, no trade logged) moved the tile by **$1,091.62** in silence *because DELL was still held*, so the orphan test — which fires only on a fully vanished ticker — was correct to stay quiet and useless at the same time. `daily_pnl.reconcile_baseline()` now returns all three shapes: `orphans` (unchanged), **`qty_drift`** (held, at a share count today's trades don't explain — error is `drift × price`), and **`unbaselined`** (held, absent from the baseline, no trade today). **`unbaselined` is the worst of the three and was previously invisible:** the position's **full market value**, not a day's move, enters the delta as gain — on an ~$24.5k book one unbaselined $2k name renders a fabricated ~+8% day. It is reachable via a partial `daily_snapshots` write **and routinely via a stale baseline**, since anything opened between the baseline date and today lands there; that is a **pre-existing arithmetic defect this ships a warning for, not a fix for** (verified in review, not assumed). Both new captions quote the **dollar error**, because "this number is wrong" is only actionable with "by how much" — and the `unbaselined` figure is explicitly labelled approximate, since the true error is the cost basis rather than the current mark. **No constant added:** share comparisons reuse `BROKER_DRIFT_SHARE_TOL` (0.001), one question ("real difference or float noise") with two consumers, now named in both the constants comment and the architecture row. **SPLIT rows are skipped, not warned about** — `db.py`'s convention stores `shares` as the adjusted TOTAL rather than a delta, and on a split day there is genuinely no error to report (post-split shares × post-split price reconciles, and the cash leg is correctly zero), so silence is the right answer rather than a concession. **Opus review returned FIX-FIRST with 3 blocking, all fixed before ship, and all three were about the change being HONEST rather than about its arithmetic.** **(1) A unit mismatch would have made this fire on a correctly-journalled book.** `port_df["Shares"]` is `int(shares)` — truncated for display — while trade deltas come from `trades_df` raw to 4dp (`broker_sync` rounds fills, it does not truncate), so a single fractional fill yields a phantom drift ~500× the tolerance. This is the *same* defect the repo had already documented for this constant's other consumer. Fixed with an explicit unit guard: when the held and baseline sides are both integral but the expected value is not, the two sides cannot be compared apples-to-apples, so the ticker is skipped exactly as for a split. **The cost is a deliberate, tested false negative** — a genuine whole-share drift is missed on a day that ticker also had a fractional fill — judged the better error, because a banner that cries wolf on a clean book is one the user stops reading, which costs them the next real $1,091. **(2) The caption diagnosed a cause the detector cannot know.** It read "no trade to explain it … log the missing trade", but `reconcile_baseline` only ever sees **today's** trades. Two live paths make that a false accusation: a **stale baseline** (every trade since the baseline date is invisible → guaranteed drift on a clean book), and **date-only `traded_at`** on imported trades, where `pd.to_datetime("2026-08-23", utc=True).tz_convert("America/New_York").date()` is **2026-08-22** — verified empirically, not reasoned — so a broker-sourced trade logged today is filtered out of today's set. The warning is *arithmetically* true in both cases; the diagnosis was not. Reworded to state the discrepancy, branch on `_dpnl_is_current`, and list candidate causes instead of issuing an instruction. **(3) 🧾 Summary rendered the identical number with no caption, under a tooltip reading "TRUE day P&L … Same figure as 🏠 Home".** The detector output was already in `_dpnl_cache`; Summary just never read it — the exact coordination failure CLAUDE.md's publish/consume rule exists to prevent, where the number crosses surfaces but the contradiction doesn't. Summary now reads the same three lists, drops the word "TRUE" when any is non-empty, and carries a short pointer caption. Its null-handling uses an explicit `isinstance` check rather than `or []`, since a dict cached by a pre-feature session simply lacks the keys and `or []` is the offline-sentinel collapse the recurring-defect gate blocks. **Separately queued, NOT fixed here:** the date-only → ET off-by-one is a **pre-existing money bug in `today_trade_cash_delta`'s input**, not merely a caption problem, and the same `utc=True` parse appears at four other consumers — a class fix needing its own review. 31 new tests (`tests/test_daily_pnl.py`, 20 → 51), including one pinning the deliberate false negative so the trade-off stays a decision rather than a surprise, and one asserting the reported dollar figure equals the actual day-P&L distortion. **A third review round found the orphan caption itself was wrong** — it said the figure "may be off by that position's move" when nothing offsets the baseline subtraction, so the error is the position's **full prior-close value**, one to two orders of magnitude larger and understated in the reassuring direction. `orphans` therefore changed shape from `list[str]` to `list[dict]` carrying a signed `value_impact`, making all three shapes quote a number and making this row's own User Guide claim true. **Two gaps are left open deliberately and are queued, not hidden:** a **fourth** disagreement shape (a ticker traded today that is in neither the baseline nor current holdings — its proceeds enter the cash delta with nothing subtracting the cost) is structurally undetectable from `reconcile_baseline`'s signature, so the docstring and the User Guide both state the three shapes are **not exhaustive**; and the unit guard suppresses an **unbounded** residual to absorb a **bounded** truncation error, so a large drift is missed on a day that ticker also had a fractional fill — bounding it needs a new policy constant set with the user rather than invented here. **UPDATE 2026-08-24: this last gap is CLOSED.** New constant `QTY_DRIFT_TRUNCATION_AMBIGUITY_SHARES = 1.0` — mathematically derived (truncation error per side is provably `[0, 1)`), not an invented judgment call. The guard now only suppresses a residual below that bound; a residual at or above it is always reported regardless of a same-day fractional fill. The old deliberate-false-negative test (a 9.5-share drift silently reported as zero) now asserts the opposite — that exact drift is caught — and a new test pins the narrower remaining blind spot (a genuinely sub-1.0-share residual on a fractional-fill day, which is the mathematical floor, not a policy choice). See `docs/architecture.md`'s constants table and memory `project_today_pnl_scope`. **UPDATE 2026-08-24: the fourth-shape gap is ALSO CLOSED, same session.** New return key `unbaselined_sells` — the sell-side twin of `unbaselined`: a ticker sold today that is in NEITHER baseline NOR current holdings (bought on some day after the last baseline snapshot, held uncaptured, sold today). Its sale proceeds enter `cash_delta` with nothing subtracting the acquisition cost. **No signature change was needed** — an earlier note here claiming `reconcile_baseline`'s signature "does not carry the trade price" was WRONG; `today_trades` was always a parameter and every row already carries `price`, the function just hadn't used it for this shape. Detection: for a ticker traded today but absent from both baseline and held, sum today's BUY and SELL shares separately; a same-day round trip (bought ≥ sold) is NOT flagged — both legs already enter `cash_delta` correctly and the identity is exact, since a name never held at prior close and not held now contributes zero from `current_val`/`baseline_val` alike. Only the EXCESS sold beyond what was bought today is unbacked, valued at the volume-weighted average of today's sell fills (neither a live price nor a baseline close exists for this shape). No new constant — reuses `BROKER_DRIFT_SHARE_TOL`. Home renders a fourth caption (mirroring `unbaselined`'s pattern); 🧾 Summary's coordination-count loop was updated to include the new key (the same silent-drop class F-250's own review round 3 caught for the original three); the in-app User Guide's "Three cases" paragraph is now four and no longer says "not exhaustive" — `reconcile_baseline`'s docstring dropped that caveat too, since this was the last structurally-invisible shape. 10 new tests in `tests/test_daily_pnl.py`. |
| F-251 | **Imported trades land on the right ET day. Fixes a wrong dollar figure in Today's P&L and a protective suppression that was failing open.** `trades.traded_at` is `timestamptz`; the three import writers (broker sync, CSV, RH-text) sent a **bare date**, Postgres cast it to **midnight UTC**, and midnight UTC is the **prior evening in ET** — so every `tz_convert("America/New_York")` reader dated an imported trade a day early. Two live consequences, both silent: **(1)** `app.py`'s Today's-P&L today-trades filter excluded a trade imported the same day, so its cash leg never entered `today_trade_cash_delta` and the displayed figure was wrong by that trade's value (and via `_dpnl_cache`, wrong on 🧾 Summary too); **(2)** `risk_advisor._bought_today` — the buy/trim whiplash suppression — **failed open**, letting the app name a position for trim hours after recommending the buy. **Design routed through `planner` (Opus)** because the readers split into two camps that fail in OPPOSITE directions: `tz_convert(...).date()` shifts an imported row BACK a day, while `.date()` on the UTC value is correct for those rows but shifts a genuine 20:00 ET fill FORWARD. There is no single parse that fixes both, so the value is re-anchored **once, at the single choke point** `db.load_trades_or_none()` — which all ~44 `load_trades()` callers pass through and none can bypass — rather than at ~20 call sites. New pure module `stock_analyzer/trade_time.py`; new writer helper `market_time.et_anchor_iso` stops new rows being created that way, so the repair only ever covers legacy data. **The corruption boundary, and why detection is provenance-gated:** because the column is `timestamptz`, an imported row is **byte-indistinguishable** from a real fill at 20:00 ET, so a value-only rule ("re-anchor anything at midnight UTC") would silently re-date genuine after-hours trades. A row is rewritten only on the **conjunction** of two independent facts — exactly midnight UTC **AND** an import-provenance marker (`broker_txn_id`, or a `notes` marker) — and fails closed when the provenance columns are absent. **New constant `IMPORTED_TRADE_ANCHOR_ET_HOUR = 16`** (market close; user's choice). **Its value is CONSTRAINED, not free: ≥ 0 and < 19**, or the anchor rolls into the next **UTC** day and silently re-dates every UTC-date reader — including `tax_advisor`'s lot dates, where a wash-sale or LTCG boundary can turn on one day, and `broker_sync`'s dedup key, which would re-import every trade. A test pins the safe band so an edit past it fails loudly. **A planner claim was DISPROVED mid-build, and it changed the shipped scope.** The plan asserted "pandas handles mixed offsets fine with `utc=True` alone." On pandas 3.0.3 that is false: bare `utc=True` NaTs rows whenever **precision** varies, which `traded_at` genuinely does (raw-SQL inserts vs Postgres `now()`). Measured: the NaT count is identical before and after the shim, so it is **pre-existing, not introduced** — but critically, **the ET anchor alone would NOT have fixed the money bug**, because an anchored row carries no microseconds and would still have been NaT'd out of the very filter being fixed. The fix would have looked correct in isolation and changed nothing on the real surface. So `format="ISO8601"` was added to eight Series parses of `traded_at` that lacked it, and a test pins the interaction. **Third recurrence of that NaT class (2026-05-28, 2026-08-02, then eight sites at once) earned it a MECHANICAL GATE:** `check_antipatterns.py` gained `MIXED_TZ_PARSE`, which **baselines at zero** — every site was fixed rather than grandfathered, including four scalar parses that are technically immune, because uniformity beats a per-site "is this one safe?" judgment. Scoped to `traded_at` alone: a first draft also gated `snapshot_date` (a plain `date` column, no time to vary) and `surfaced_at` (SDK-only writer), which would have been an inferred premise. **Opus review returned FIX-FIRST with 1 blocking:** the marker set omitted `RH screenshot`, which `broker_screenshot.last_screenshot_sync_date` **already treats as a real import marker** — so those rows would have stayed wrong while being stamped `traded_at_time_known = True`, an affirmatively false claim. The repair was reading a narrower marker set than the detector that already existed (`feedback_validation_reads_detector_source`); the markers are now **exported from the detecting module and consumed by the writers**, so a reworded writer cannot silently disarm the repair, with a test binding the two. Also taken from review: a measured **50× perf regression** (a per-row scalar parse on the app's hottest read path, 168 ms per load on a 300-row journal) replaced with a vectorized parse; the loader and writer were **two anchoring implementations that disagreed by an hour across a DST transition** (absolute offset vs wall-clock) and now share one; `pd.isna` replaces a string blocklist that would have read `pd.NA` as provenance; and `traded_at_time_known`'s **asymmetry is documented at the definition** — `False` is a firm claim that the app invented the time, `True` only means "not re-anchored here", so it is sound to exclude on `False` and unsound to treat `True` as evidence. Review verified no path leaks the new column into a Supabase write. **No DB backfill** — the load-time repair makes it unnecessary, and rewriting history is irreversible. **Round 2 returned SHIP, 0 blocking, and hardened the new gate against a one-token bypass it had shipped with:** `MIXED_TZ_PARSE` checked for the PRESENCE of a `format=` kwarg rather than its VALUE, so `format=None` — definitionally the inferred-format behaviour the rule bans — and an explicit strptime format both read as compliant while reproducing the original defect exactly. It now requires the literal `"ISO8601"`, verified against five synthetic cases. Round 2 also caught that the perf fix covered only the DETECTION half: `anchored_iso` still re-parsed each rewritten row, and — more seriously — could return `None` on a parse miss, which would have been written straight into `traded_at`, destroying a trade's date while stamping it "time known = False". Driving the rewrite off the already-parsed timestamps makes that branch unreachable by construction. Also taken: the gate's failure text gained a `MIXED_TZ_PARSE` remediation line (it was the one zero-baseline rule whose only on-screen advice was "regenerate the baseline", which this file's own docstring forbids); the rule's docstring now states its **aliasing limit** honestly, since a parse whose column name is bound to a local first is not caught; `normalize_traded_at` is wrapped in `db.py` so a pure-logic bug cannot be misreported as a Supabase outage and escalated by F-243's gate into refusing to render the portfolio; the CSV import loop got a real `try/except` matching the RH-text path, because a truthiness guard misses the realistic bad value (the TRUTHY string `"NaT"`); and a test whose name claimed 19:00 ET shifts the UTC date was corrected — 19:00 shifts in EST only, which is exactly why the band is `< 19` rather than `< 20`. 42 new tests (`tests/test_trade_time.py`); 4057 passed. |
| F-253 | **Margin call-distance awareness panel on 💰 Account. AWARENESS ONLY — never gates a trade or recommendation.** Closes the largest known risk gap (`project_margin_maintenance_blindness`): the app modelled leverage as a ratio but had no model of the maintenance requirement, so its own flagship −10% fragility scenario sat past the estimated −9.1% call threshold with no way to say so. New pure module `stock_analyzer/margin.py`; `call_distance()` takes stock value, owner equity, debit, and rate and returns cushion, call-distance %, and in-call flag — or `None` when not applicable (no debit, zero stock value, degenerate rate). New constant `MARGIN_MAINTENANCE_RATE = 0.25` (standard Reg T floor; Robinhood raises this on volatile/concentrated names — awareness only, read by NO gate or advisor). Renders on the Account page inside the existing `if _levered:` block: three `st.metric` tiles (Margin Cushion $, Call Triggers At %, Leverage ×) plus a plotly horizontal stacked bar with a green cushion zone, red call-territory zone, and a dashed orange marker at `FRAGILITY_PULLBACK_PCT`. The fragility marker's position past the red call line is the whole point of the panel. Caption names the assumed rate and states it is never used to block a trade. Hides entirely when not levered or when `_leverage_cache` is stale.
