In-call branch (cushion ≤ 0) renders a red error banner instead of the chart. 13 tests in `tests/test_margin.py` including a founding-measurement test asserting −9.12% on the real book (verifies the correct formula vs the naive `cushion/stock_value` which gives −6.84%); a gate-isolation test asserting `MARGIN_MAINTENANCE_RATE` is not imported by any gate module; and a boundary test at exactly the call threshold. |
| F-254 | **Dual-basis concentration awareness (Slice 1 of the margin capital-basis gap). AWARENESS ONLY — no gate, denominator, or threshold changed.** Closes the display half of `project_margin_maintenance_blindness`'s follow-up finding: every concentration gate (`SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`, `SECTOR_CEILING`) computes weight against gross holdings value, which equals owner capital only when unlevered — at real leverage a "15% of book" position understates what it is worth against the capital actually owned by roughly the leverage ratio. New pure function `stock_analyzer/margin.capital_basis_weight(market_value, net_capital) -> float | None` (returns `None` when `net_capital <= 0`). Three render sites: (1) the ⚖️ Risk Analysis leverage banner's largest-sector line now states the sector's weight in both bases side by side ("X% of book but Y% of your actual capital — the sector-concentration gate reads the first number"); (2) 💰 Account's per-position concentration expander gains a sector-level twin table beneath the existing ticker-level one, both showing Equity Wt (%) and Account Wt (%) columns; (3) the expander's caption was reworded — it previously justified equity-basis gates as protecting against a "transient margin balance," which is factually wrong for a structural leverage position (the owner runs 3.15× by deliberate policy, not day-to-day cash noise); it now states plainly that the gate basis and the account basis diverge *persistently*, not transiently, when leverage is structural. 5 tests in `tests/test_margin.py`: a founding-measurement test (a $3,546 position reads ~45.4% of the real book's $7,802 capital vs ~14.5% of gross book — the exact ~3× gap this closes), an unlevered-equivalence test, two `None`-guard tests (zero/negative capital), and a gate-isolation test mirroring `MARGIN_MAINTENANCE_RATE`'s (`capital_basis_weight` must never be imported by a gate/advisor module, `stock_analyzer.portfolio` included since that module owns the actual gate-feeding weight calculation). Deliberately does NOT touch the emailed buy-list sizing note — that path runs through the headless cron (`daily_briefing.py`/`cron_runner.py`, both `_GATE_FILES`) and needs its own scoped design + review pass, queued separately rather than folded into this render-only slice. |
| F-255 | **Net-capital new-position concentration cap — the second half of the margin capital-basis gap F-254 measured but didn't act on. CHANGES A LIVE RECOMMENDATION: new-position sizing on every surface that proposes one.** F-254 showed the gap; this closes it for NEW sizing only. Real trades ALB ($3,581.25) and OXY ($3,545.90) both cleared the existing `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` (15% of gross book) at 14.6%/14.9%, while each was actually ~45.9%/45.5% of the owner's real $7,802 net capital — the gross-book gate can't see leverage. Decided with the user directly (walked the gross-vs-capital tradeoff, then the concrete ALB/OXY numbers) rather than recalibrating the existing cap's denominator: add a **SEPARATE, additive** `NET_CAPITAL_POSITION_CAP_PCT = 25.0` cap, `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` and its gross-book math left completely untouched. Effective new-position size = min(risk-based, 15%-of-gross, 25%-of-capital). Implemented in the shared `stock_analyzer.risk.position_sizing()`/`sizing_unavailable_reason()` (both take optional `net_capital`/`max_capital_pct` kwargs, default-off — every caller that omits them is byte-identical to pre-F-255) and mirrored in `stock_analyzer.concentration.assess_add_concentration()` (`capital_breach`/`post_name_capital_pct`). New `stock_analyzer.margin.resolve_net_capital(gross_book, account_cash_rec, stale_days_limit, now) -> (net_capital | None, basis)` centralizes the leverage read so every wired surface agrees: `basis` is `"unlevered"`/`"stale"` (cap inert, gross cap still binds — a stale margin figure must not drive a live sizing cap) or `"levered"`/`"called"` (net capital ≤ 0 fails CLOSED, no size suggested). Wired into Grow Today (new-pick + add-winner), Analysis, Watchlist Advisor, the pullback-add-to-winner flow, and the cron/email buy-list (`headless_alert_engine.py` → `notify.py`'s `_sizing_cap_note`, which now renders the book-cap and capital-cap disclosures together when both bind, since capital is applied AFTER the gross cap and can shrink further without ever growing a size). `SIZING_FORMULA_VERSION` bumped 2→3. Existing holdings already over 25% of capital (ALB/OXY today) get a new **awareness-only** banner on 💰 Account (`stock_analyzer.margin.held_over_capital_cap()`) — deliberately never wired into `risk_advisor`/`exit_advisor`/any `st.session_state` publish, since a second independent trigger forcing a trim on a position the app itself sized under the old rule would be an unrequested, conflicting decision; the forward cap prevents the next mistake, it does not relitigate the last one. Two Opus-reviewed commits (`af5d4ca` core engine, `a9e920a` app-layer wiring), both SHIP/0 blocking. |
| F-252 | **🏠 Home says when your book doesn't match your broker, and by how much. Awareness only; no computed number changes.** `broker_sync.diff_positions()` was good code called in exactly ONE place — the 💰 Account panel. **Home never consumed it**, so on 2026-08-23 a live DELL share drift inflated the book by **$1,091.62 (+4.46%)** with no warning on the surface that displays it, while the answer sat computed one page over. The routing gap, not the detection, was the defect. **Direction matters and is why this is not cosmetic:** an inflated book makes every weight read SMALLER, so `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` / `SECTOR_CEILING` **loosen** — a true 15.0% position read 14.36%, a fail-open on a concentration gate. **Design routed through `planner` (Opus), which rejected all three obvious options.** Home computing it live is 1 + N-accounts SnapTrade reads at `SNAPTRADE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SEC = 15` each — a **~90s worst-case stall** on the most-rerun page (the Account panel already needed a 60s cache for exactly this). Compute-on-Account-and-publish fails on a **dependency cycle**, not merely ordering: the Account panel refuses to run until Home has loaded the portfolio. Instead: **the `broker` cron persists the BROKER side only, and Home re-diffs it against the LIVE book on every render.** That asymmetry is the whole design — the side the user actively edits is the live one, so correcting a mis-logged trade clears the banner **immediately** rather than nagging until tomorrow's run, which is precisely how a warning becomes wallpaper. **It costs ZERO additional SnapTrade calls:** the lane's account-selection loop already fetched every account's positions to count them and **discarded the payloads**; this persists what was already paid for. (The lane's docstring claimed drift "is computed LIVE ... so there is nothing for this lane to write" — that described the design rather than justifying it, and is corrected.) **New table `broker_position_snapshot`** (§6.36), single-row JSONB and **manual DDL** — the feature ships INERT until applied, and 🩺 System Trust shows the store red until then rather than letting it silently do nothing. **One JSONB row, not a row per ticker, and that is load-bearing:** a per-ticker table admits a partial write, and a missing ticker reads as "the broker doesn't hold it" ⇒ a fabricated drift on a correct book; delete-then-insert is worse. **AWARENESS, never a gate** — matching the `_leverage_cache` precedent, and for a reason stronger than caution: a drift gate would **fail open exactly when it matters** (it goes silent when SnapTrade is unreachable), the false-positive surface is broad (multi-account aggregation, instrument kinds, settlement lag), and this is a user-fixable data error, so the app routes to the fix rather than locking the doors. `decide_drift_banner` is a pure, Streamlit-free function so every offline state is unit-assertable: no snapshot ⇒ **"not checked"**, never "no drift"; a clean-but-stale check ⇒ **"no mismatch as of ‹date› — not re-checked since"**, never a clean bill of health; partial account coverage ⇒ not a clean verdict; an unreadable capture time counts as **stale, not fresh**; an unloaded book renders nothing rather than fabricating an `rh_only` row per broker position. Silent when clean and fresh — a green tick on every render is the noise that trains a user past the amber one. **Cross-feature coordination:** the banner publishes `_broker_drift_cache`, and F-250's day-P&L `qty_drift` caption **defers** to it for any ticker it already explains (broker drift is EXTERNAL ground truth; the day-P&L check is internal and inferential). F-250 is never suppressed wholesale — it sees two shapes the broker check structurally cannot (`orphans`, `unbaselined`) and is the only one that still works when SnapTrade is down. Conversely the broker check catches drift of **any age**, where F-250 only sees drift that appeared since its baseline and would inherit an error baked into that baseline forever. **No new policy constant:** reuses `BROKER_DRIFT_SHARE_TOL` (now a third consumer of one "is this float noise" question) and `SNAPTRADE_BALANCE_STALE_HOURS`; `constants.py` is untouched. Diffs raw `holdings_df`, **not** `port_df`, because `build_portfolio_df` stores `int(shares)` and a fractional broker holding would otherwise show a permanent phantom mismatch — the same truncation trap F-250 hit. **`broker_sync` had ZERO test coverage** before this despite being the app's only app-vs-broker reconciliation; 41 tests were backfilled first (commit `3551391`) so the split into `normalize_positions` + `diff_position_map` could be *proven* behaviour-preserving over nine fixtures rather than asserted. **Opus review returned FIX-FIRST with 1 blocking, and it was a case where my invariant was the WRONG invariant.** I guarded on "at least one account responded", which the code does guarantee. The invariant that matters is "**the account holding the positions** responded". The real topology is one heavy brokerage account plus several empty auxiliaries; the heavy one is the slowest read and likeliest to time out, and if ONLY it fails the empty auxiliaries still satisfy the selection guard — so the aggregate is `{}`, upserting that says the broker holds **nothing**, and Home renders fabricated `app_only` drift across the entire book (`~$24,503`) while destroying the last known-good snapshot. `all_accounts_ok=False` made it worse, not better: it appends "there may be **more**" to drift that is entirely invented. Fixed by writing only when **every** account responded; skipping lets the prior row age into the honest dated "not re-checked since". The reviewer also noted the live Account panel already guarded this exact case, so the persistence path had been strictly weaker than the path it mirrors. **The invariant was guarded only by a comment and by nothing else** — now pinned by `tests/test_broker_position_snapshot.py`, and verified to genuinely catch it by reverting the guard and watching the test fail. Also taken from review: `captured_at` rendered in **UTC** on an all-ET page, so a 14:14 ET capture displayed as "18:14" — a *future* time to someone reading at 15:00; the "not checked" caption is now silent when no broker is linked at all, rather than nagging permanently about a feature the user never set up; a `>= $1` branch is commented as a sub-dollar rounding guard rather than an unnamed policy number; and plural grammar on the F-250 cross-reference. Three `or {}` instances the author introduced were caught by the repo's own antipattern gate and fixed at the source with explicit `isinstance`, not baselined. **Changes no computed number** — it annotates Portfolio Value and rewords one existing caption. **Round 2 returned FIX-FIRST with 2 blocking, and the first was the MIRROR of the asymmetry this feature is built on — the author praised the asymmetry and missed its inverse.** Because the broker snapshot refreshes once daily while the book is diffed live, correcting a mis-logged trade clears the banner instantly — but **logging a perfectly CORRECT trade creates it.** Buy 30 NVDA at 10:00, log it properly, and Home announced *"Portfolio Value may be overstated by ~$5,400 — fix a missing trade"* above the tile, with no as-of date (the stamp was gated on `is_stale`, and a snapshot is fresh for 25 hours). A confident, quantified, action-directing accusation produced by the app's single most common daily workflow — and a red banner every day you trade is a worse version of the "green tick trains you past the amber one" problem this design cites. Fixed properly rather than cosmetically: `split_awaiting_sync()` separates drift on tickers traded since `captured_at` into a new **`awaiting_sync`** state that renders as an informational caption ("waiting on the broker to catch up on NVDA — you logged a trade after the ‹time› snapshot, so a difference here is expected. Nothing to fix"), and **excludes those rows from the dollar headline** so a mixed book quantifies only the genuinely unexplained part. Deliberately NOT dropped — a trade on a ticker does not prove the resulting share count is right, so it stays visible, just not as an accusation. The drift branch is now **always dated**, and its cause list leads with "a trade logged after that snapshot" ahead of "a missing trade". **Second blocking: the invariant was stated as "EVERY linked account responded" but enforced "every account we ATTEMPTED responded"** — `if not _aid: continue` skipped an account without setting the failure flag, so a payload with a missing `id` would have been written as complete while silently missing that account's positions: the identical fabrication path round 1 blocked, through a different door. One line, plus a test. Also taken: the Account panel's partial-failure rule was **weaker than the cron's** (it rendered a partial diff whenever *some* account returned positions) — tightened to any-failure-means-unavailable, because two surfaces answering one question by different rules is exactly how they end up contradicting each other; `_fmt_asof_et` now includes the year when it isn't the current one, so a year-old snapshot can't read as recent; "No broker mismatch as of unknown" reworded; and a comment records that `all_accounts_ok=False` is currently unreachable on the write path (the column is kept because the DDL default is the fail-safe reading and a future per-account write could set it) so a reader doesn't waste time trying to trigger it. Two more `or {}` instances the author introduced were again caught by the repo's own antipattern gate and fixed at the source. **The write invariant, previously guarded only by prose in four places, is now pinned by `tests/test_broker_position_snapshot.py` — and verified to genuinely catch the defect by reverting the guard and confirming the test fails.** 8 new awaiting-sync tests; 4113 passed. **UPDATE 2026-08-24: the deferred email follow-up SHIPPED.** F-249's suggested share sizes are computed from `portfolio_value`, a book-wide sum — a drift anywhere in the book corrupts sizing for every emailed pick, yet the two picks emails (morning "New Positions to Initiate" and the intraday pullback entry window) carried no drift banner at all, unlike the interactive app. Closed via a new headless call site, not new detection logic: `decide_drift_banner` was already pure, so `_build_context` was extended to also return the RAW `holdings_df`/`trades_df` it already loaded (zero extra reads), `compute_morning_picks` computes a `book_drift` verdict in its own try/except AFTER `build_daily_briefing` succeeds (a SnapTrade/DB fault here can never abort pick computation or the email — `None` flows through as "unknown"), and `notify._book_drift_banner()` renders ONE book-wide banner (not per-pick — the corruption is in the shared denominator, not any one name) only on `state == "drift"`. **Deliberately silent on "unknown"** — diverges from the in-app Home banner, which also renders on unknown because it's a *queried* surface; this is a *push* surface, so silence never asserts cleanliness, and rendering "unknown" on every email a user with no broker linked ever receives would be exactly the amber-fatigue this feature's own design already warns against. `_tickers_traded_since` relocated from `app.py` to `stock_analyzer/broker_sync.py` (renamed `tickers_traded_since`, no leading underscore) so the headless path can call it without importing the Streamlit-laden `app.py`. No new constant. Never alters the suggested share count — annotation only, pinned by an invariant test asserting identical sizing text across `book_drift=None`/`unknown`/`drift`. 15 new test functions (18 collected cases, one parametrized ×4) across `test_headless_alert_engine.py`, `test_notify.py`. One antipattern instance (`verdict.get("impact") or {}`) caught by the repo's own gate and fixed at the source with an explicit `isinstance` check before commit, not baselined — the same bug-class pattern this repo keeps re-finding. |
| F-256 | **Self Track Record — SELL-side. New "📉 Sell-Side" sub-tab beside the existing 📈 Buy-Side on 🎯 My Edge → 🧭 Self vs Engine. (2) A naive "acted SELL" framing would have silently zeroed every alpha value — `recommendations_history.compute_outcomes()` unconditionally returns `alpha_pct = None` for any acted SELL (unknowable holding period). `classify_sells()` instead frames each sell as a **missed rec** (`acted_on=False`, `acted_trade=None`, `price_at_surface=`sell price, `rec_date=`sell date), routing it through `compute_outcomes`'s "what happened to the price after this rec, benchmarked to SPY" branch — the same alpha math F-233 already uses, zero new machinery. Grading window is **open-ended** (sell_date → today, `REC_SCORE_MIN_DAYS`-day maturity floor), matching how F-233's own buy-side actually works — not the fixed 30-day window an earlier mockup draft assumed, which would have needed a new historical-close fetcher. **Sign convention: `alpha_pct` = post-exit stock return − SPY return, kept raw, no inversion — negative = good exit** (the stock lagged the market after you sold); `compute_outcomes`'s `outcome_label` field is calibrated for BUYs and is deliberately NOT surfaced on this tab (a rising price after a sell is bad, the inverse of its buy-side meaning). New `stock_analyzer/self_track_record.py` functions: `classify_sells()` (3 buckets: `engine_aligned` — an EXIT/TRIM `signal_date` within `SELF_TRACK_SELL_SIGNAL_WINDOW_DAYS`=5 days before the sell, date-independent, a matched signal overrides the reliable-log-start boundary; `self_initiated`; `coverage_limited`), `self_vs_engine_sell_summary()` (grades `engine_aligned` vs `self_initiated`, `coverage_limited` counted but never averaged), `detect_missed_exits()` (awareness-only — flags a currently-held ticker whose most recent EXIT/TRIM signal is older than the window with no sell since; never wired into `risk_advisor`/`exit_advisor`/any gate). New `db.load_exit_signals_or_none()` mirrors F-233's own `load_recommendations_or_none()` fix exactly — `None` on missing credentials or any exception, empty DataFrame only on a genuine zero-row result — the same offline-sentinel-collapse guard, since the existing `load_exit_signals()` cannot make that distinction and a transient Supabase hiccup would otherwise silently misclassify every engine-called sell as self-initiated. Leaves `load_exit_signals()` and its existing callers (`app.py`, `cron_runner.py`, `debrief_advisor.py`) byte-identical. Two new constants in `constants.py`: `SELF_TRACK_SELL_SIGNAL_WINDOW_DAYS = 5` (deliberately NOT reusing the buy-side's 3-day `SELF_TRACK_MATCH_LOOKBACK_DAYS` — sell decisions may lag a signal differently than a buy decision lags a rec) and `SELF_TRACK_SELL_RELIABLE_LOG_START`. Discloses survivorship risk: a delisted/acquired sold ticker has no live price and drops out of grading ungraded — and these are disproportionately the BEST exits, biasing the shown average to look worse than the full picture; the ungraded count/tickers are shown in-panel rather than silently omitted. **Opus `reviewer` caught one blocking issue pre-ship:** the sell-side's missed-exits check read `st.session_state["_last_held_tickers"]` and collapsed a `None` (holdings genuinely unknown — e.g. a session that never rendered Home this run) into an empty set, which made `detect_missed_exits` iterate nothing and render a confidently false **"No missed exits"** all-clear — exactly the offline-sentinel-collapse class this whole feature exists to guard against, on the one surface built specifically to avoid it. Fixed by skipping the call entirely when holdings are unknown, rendering the existing "can't check" info box instead of guessing an empty book. Round 2: SHIP, 0 blocking. 64 new tests (`tests/test_self_track_record.py` +44, new `tests/test_db_load_exit_signals.py`); full suite 4279 passed. |

New `SITUATIONAL_CATEGORY_MIN_SAMPLE_N = 3` (deliberately NOT reusing `BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N`=8 — the self-initiated pool is only ~16 graded today and splits 5 ways, so an 8-floor would leave every category permanently "building"). Unlike `self_vs_engine_summary()`'s own `_graded()` helper (which always computes an average at n≥1 and leaves the sufficiency gate to the render layer), this breakdown withholds `avg_alpha_pct` (returns `None`) below the floor at the DATA layer — a deliberate divergence, since this table renders multiple small-n categories side by side and a single forgotten `sufficient` check on one row could otherwise leak a noisy 1-2-sample average; `sufficient` and `avg_alpha_pct is None` are structurally guaranteed to agree. Breakdown table is skipped entirely (not rendered empty) when every category reads n=0. **Opus `reviewer`: SHIP, 0 blocking** — independently traced the BUY-only guard, the reconciliation invariant (both `compute_outcomes` call sites plus the byte-identical predicate), all four `db.py` touch points' pre-DDL read/write safety, and the sufficient/avg-None consistency; 3 cosmetic non-blocking notes taken (a redundant-but-precedented recompute, a test that shape-pins rather than imports the real save-record expression, and a missing "Other" line in the widget's help tooltip — the last fixed before commit). 8 new tests in `tests/test_self_track_record.py`: the reconciliation-sum invariant, explicit passthrough, Uncategorized bucketing (None/empty/missing/stray-legacy-string), the exact N/N-1 sample-floor boundary, offline propagation, bucket scoping, and the BUY-only guard. | F-258 | **"Not checked" is visually distinct from "checked, nothing found" (2026-08-26).** OP-01 says the app would rather recommend nothing than recommend wrongly — which makes a silent non-answer indistinguishable from a clean bill of health the single most dangerous render in the app. New helper `app.py::_coord_cache_state(key)` returns **missing** (producer never ran this session), **offline** (producer ran and FAILED, publishing the `None` sentinel) or **ready**, so a consumer can no longer collapse all three into one falsy branch. | F-260 | **The LLM narratives state when factor-exposure evidence is MISSING, instead of silently omitting it (2026-08-28).** Scoped entry: F-260 as a whole is the surface-proprioception AUDIT programme ([docs/plans/surface-proprioception.md](plans/surface-proprioception.md)), whose Chunk 1 classified 115 coordination-cache reads into 25 CLAIM findings; **most of those remain unbuilt.** What SHIPPED here is the one finding §10 calls out separately from the ranked 25. `_pi_factor_tilt_cache` is written only by 🧩 Intelligence's "📡 Load factor exposure" button, and read by two LLM narrative surfaces on other tabs/pages — 🔗 Risk Analysis's regime scenario and Intelligence's own 🧬 Structural Scan. Both passed the value into a prompt builder whose `_format_evidence` emitted the factor line only `if factor:` and `if valid:`, so when the data was absent it emitted **nothing** — handing the model an evidence block indistinguishable from one where factor concentration had been measured and found unremarkable. The model then wrote, and the app **persisted** (`regime_scenario_cache` / `structural_scan_cache`, one row per ET `scan_date`, served for the rest of the day), a narrative implying complete evidence. Absence is the COMMON case, not an edge case: the consumers live on different surfaces from the button. **Fix:** a new pure `stock_analyzer/util.py::factor_tilt_evidence_line()` that ALWAYS returns a non-empty line across THREE distinct states — `not measured` (and explicitly forbidding any inference of low/balanced concentration), `measured but no usable per-factor correlation` (**deliberately naming no cause** — `portfolio_intelligence.factor_tilt` returns its empty shape from five distinct paths, so naming one would hand the model a fabricated cause to restate as fact, the same class one clause down), and the real reading (byte-identical to the pre-fix string, so a successful measurement is unchanged). Both system prompts updated to instruct on all three states; `structural_scanner._NARRATIVE_SYSTEM`'s old *"do not mention factor exposure if it isn't supplied"* was removed as unreachable guidance once the line became unconditional, and a test pins that removal. **User-facing:** a pre-generation caption on both surfaces when factor evidence is absent or unusable, deliberately BEFORE generation because the result is day-cached. `util.factor_tilt_state()` is the single classifier read by both the prompt and the caption, so the two can never describe different states. **Awareness/narrative only — feeds no gate, no sizing, no recommendation; neither narrative is read by `risk_advisor`, `exit_advisor` or `daily_briefing`.** 17 new tests, mutation-tested in both directions (restoring the silent omit fails 6 tests across 3 files; re-inlining a near-copy fails the divergence contract; reintroducing a named cause fails). Opus review SHIP, 0 blocking. **Known limit, deliberately not bundled:** the persisted row cannot record that it was built without factor evidence — those tables have snapshot columns for blast-radius/regime/cross-asset but none for factor tilt, so a later read of an earlier narrative has no provenance. Closing it needs a DDL column and is therefore its own reviewed `db.py` commit. |
| F-261 | **The app refuses to size a trade it cannot honestly size. CHANGES A LIVE RECOMMENDATION on 📈 Analysis, 📋 Watchlist and the exportable Analysis Summary brief (2026-08-27).** Until this shipped, `app.py` substituted `DEFAULT_PORTFOLIO_VALUE` (50,000) whenever `_portfolio_value` was absent — which is every session that has not visited 🏠 Home first — and then computed and displayed position sizes against it. On the owner's ~$24,500 book that made suggested share counts roughly **2× too large**, presented as fact, with no disclosure at any of the four Analysis sizing call sites, on every Watchlist row, or in the brief header ("Portfolio: $50,000"). **Policy chosen with the user:** when the total is unknown, propose **no size at all** — not a smaller one, not a disclosed-but-wrong one — which is what G-22/F-255 already does when the net-capital cap cannot afford one share, and what OP-01 requires. **The fix is a fourth reason in the single predicate, not a patch at the three substitution sites:** `risk.sizing_unavailable_reason` gains `"portfolio"`, ordered after `"stop"` and **before** `"ceiling"` (the ceiling test is itself gated on `portfolio_value > 0`, so ordering it later would leave the branch dead). `position_sizing` needed no change — it already early-returns `None` for any non-`None` reason. The brief adapter returns a `portfolio_unknown` marker carrying **no `shares` key**, matching the existing ceiling/capital markers so no renderer can print "0 shares". **Why removing the substitution alone would NOT have been enough:** with a zero book the predicate returned `None` ("I would size") and `max(1, int(0 / risk_per_share))` floored the result to **1 share** — an actionable "buy 1 share" at 0.0% of book, not a harmless zero. `DEFAULT_PORTFOLIO_VALUE` is now unused and marked RETIRED in `constants.py`. Opus review: SHIP, 0 blocking, having verified the invariant that makes it safe — for any `portfolio_value > 0` the behaviour is byte-identical across all six changed decision paths — and independently re-derived all six division sites for `ZeroDivisionError` reachability. Found as the #1 ranked finding of the F-260 Chunk 1 audit (`docs/plans/surface-proprioception.md` §10). |
| F-248 | **Working-set honesty on 📊 Predictive Analytics — say WHY matured recommendations were dropped. AWARENESS ONLY; gates nothing, changes no score or recommendation.** The coverage strip reported "**814** outcomes available → **344** used in this analysis" while its caption explained only the 64 excluded for immaturity. ~470 matured rows vanished with no stated reason — and all six lenses plus all four directive cards rest on the 344 survivors. Not a tidiness problem: `alpha_pct` is `None` whenever the live price fetch missed the ticker, and a failed lookup correlates with **delisted / acquired / renamed** names, so the exclusion is **non-random** — survivorship bias in an alpha measurement. An inference sharpened the concern: the benign explanation is acted SELLs (deliberately unbenchmarkable), but only 34 of the graded rows are acted at all, so ~470 acted SELLs would imply acting on well over half of 878 recommendations, contradicting everything else the page shows. Production cannot be queried from a local session, so this ships the **measurement** and the answer is read live — the F-246 / F-247 pattern. `compute_outcomes` gained an **additive** `alpha_unavailable_reason`, set at the branch that forces `alpha_pct` to `None`; `recommendations_history.ungraded_reasons()` **tallies** it rather than re-deriving the conditions, because a consumer re-deriving them would drift from `compute_outcomes` the first time a branch changed — the exact defect class the measurement exists to expose. Buckets partition `n_ungraded` exactly: `acted_sell` (by design), `no_current_price` (the only bucket carrying a bias direction — the tickers are **named** so the list can be eyeballed for dead symbols), `no_entry_reference` (a logging gap, no direction), three separate benchmark causes, and `unexplained`, which must be 0 — non-zero means the taxonomy drifted from the outcome calculation, surfaced rather than absorbed into a neighbour. An ambiguous row (both prices missing) is attributed to `no_current_price` deliberately: over-alarming is the safe side for a diagnostic. **No new constants** — the amber note fires on `n_droppable > n_graded`, a comparison of two measured quantities ("more discarded than kept"), never a tuned floor. **Opus review returned FIX-FIRST with 3 blocking, all fixed before ship, and the first was a live mislabelling:** `_spy_return_pct` returns `None` for three physically different causes, and `app.py` swallows a failed SPY fetch into an **empty dict** — so during a provider outage every matured row would have read "*N* dated outside the SPY series", blaming the user's own recommendation dates for a data-feed failure, on the one surface whose entire purpose is stating the reason correctly (the offline-sentinel-vs-empty-container class; the total would have reconciled while the stated reason was false). Split into `no_spy_series` / `no_rec_date` / `no_spy_window` with distinct copy. Second: the amber asserted "the exclusions are not random" for **any** large `n_droppable`, contradicting this feature's own docstring — `no_entry_reference` has no bias direction and a `no_spy_series` outage excludes rows **uniformly**, the least biased case possible — so that clause is now gated on `no_current_price > 0`; and "every figure **below**" was false in the small-sample case, where `st.stop()` means nothing renders below. Third: this F-row and the User Guide edit were missing from the diff. Review also verified additivity by tracing all 8 `compute_outcomes` consumers (no key iteration, no `pd.DataFrame(enriched)`, no `**row` spreading, both persistence paths whitelist fields explicitly) and confirmed `n_mature`/`n_graded` are definitionally identical to the page's own metric tiles, so the caption cannot disagree with them. 16 new tests in `tests/test_recommendations_history.py`, including one asserting the exact key set so a future addition to a function with 8 consumers has to be deliberate, and one pinning that BOTH `None` and `{}` as the SPY series report `no_spy_series` rather than a window miss. **FIRST LIVE READING, same day — the hypothesis was wrong.** 884 tracked / 825 matured / **355** scored; of the 470 unscoreable, **469 are `no_entry_reference` and ZERO are `no_current_price`.** There is **no survivorship bias** — the price provider is clean — and the real finding is larger: **57% of the matured recommendation history has no entry price logged**, so it can never be scored. The amber note correctly rendered its *conditional* branch ("those exclusions carry no particular direction, so this is a coverage limit rather than a skew") — the branch that only exists because the Opus review rejected the original unconditional "not random" wording, which would have been **false on screen on the very first read**. Static analysis could not identify the writer: `cron_runner._build_new_pick_rows` writes a bare `p.get("price")` with **no fallback** where the interactive path (`app.py` `_price_for`) has a three-level chain, but scanner picks do carry `price` and `tests/test_daily_briefing.py` already pins that as a regression, so the cron is not obviously the culprit. Rather than guess twice, `ungraded_reasons` now also returns `no_entry_reference_by_rec_type` and the bucket's earliest/latest `rec_date` (+3 tests): `rec_type` narrows the writer, since the cron logs **only** `new_pick` and has no `buy_candidate` list at all, and the date span answers whether the gap is still open. **Not yet decided, needs the user:** whether to close the cron fallback (forward-only, small) and/or backfill the 469 from historical closes on `rec_date`, which would take the working set from 355 to ~824 but mixes two different definitions of "entry price" into every alpha figure on the page — a methodological change needing its own `planner`/`reviewer` pass, not a repair. |
| F-247 | **Alpha-attribution readiness audit — measure the gate honestly before building E2. AUDIT ONLY; gates nothing, feeds no recommendation.** The 📊 Alpha Attribution placeholder on 📊 Predictive Analytics already declared a gate (`ALPHA_ATTRIBUTION_MIN_SNAPSHOT_DAYS = 180`) and already read `daily_snapshots` — but it measured coverage as `(latest − earliest).days + 1`, a **calendar span**. That overstates readiness two ways: only ~69% of calendar days are NYSE sessions (so a 180-calendar-day span holds at most ~124 observations), and **a cron gap is invisible** — snapshots for 5 sessions in March plus 1 in August reported 168 days of "coverage" backed by 6 real dates (the case pinned in tests/test_attribution_readiness.py). `daily_snapshots` is cron-written and gaps are demonstrated in this app, not hypothetical (F-239 found a lane outage on 2026-08-16 that read green until 08-21). Same class as F-246: a figure asserting readiness without stating how much data backs it. New pure `stock_analyzer/attribution_readiness.py`: `snapshot_coverage()` counts **distinct snapshot dates** against the NYSE sessions in the same span via `data.is_trading_day` (the codebase's stated single source of truth, so holidays are excluded from the denominator, not just weekends) and reports `missing_sessions` plus `largest_gap_sessions` — the latter because one long outage damages an attribution far more than the same count of scattered misses; `concentration()` reports Herfindahl and **effective** position count on the latest snapshot; `turnover()` reports **window** turnover (traded notional ÷ mean daily book value), annualised **only once the window is genuinely as long as the lookback**, with the formula stated and BUY/SELL legs returned separately so a pure accumulation phase isn't read as churn; returns `None` rather than a fabricated 0% when a leg is missing. The panel now shows the honest count **beside** the old calendar span rather than silently replacing it — the gap between the two *is* the finding — plus an amber gap warning, a churn-and-concentration line (the two things Pass #1 actually warned would make an attribution on this book noisy, which a day count alone cannot answer), and a stated constraint that any sector decomposition would apply the as-of-**now** `resolve_sector` taxonomy to past holdings (the rosters were refreshed 2026-08-16/17, so this is live). **The 180 literal is unchanged, but be honest that the BAR moved:** the quantity compared against it went from a calendar span to distinct captured dates, so the effective requirement is materially stricter (~180 sessions is ~8.6 months of unbroken capture, more with any gap). Saying "the gate is unchanged" is true of the literal and misleading about the bar — the panel copy now states the change explicitly. Retuning the number itself is a separate decision and was deliberately not taken here. Also: `turnover` **requires an `action` column and filters SPLIT rows** (the Apply-Split handler in `app.py` writes a synthetic `db.save_trade` row with `action='SPLIT'`, shares = adjusted TOTAL shares and price = adjusted avg cost, so `shares × price` is the position's whole cost basis — counting one injects a full-position-sized fake notional; a missing column returns `None`, since then splits are indistinguishable from trades), and it **withholds the annualised figure until the window is genuinely as long as the lookback** (annualising a 10-day history multiplies by ~36 off a 10-observation mean book value — the same measurement sin, one function down). Both were caught in review. **Substrate finding worth recording:** `daily_snapshots` is `(snapshot_date, ticker, shares, close_price)` — a genuine **per-position** weight history written daily at the close by cron — so E2 does NOT need to *reconstruct* historical weights from `trades` — the reconstruction half of the objection F-234 recorded. It does **not** extend reach: snapshot history may be SHORTER than trades history, which is exactly what this panel measures. **No new constants.** 25 new tests in `tests/test_attribution_readiness.py`. **Follow-up fix 2026-08-21, same day, found by reading the live panel:** the render showed only the **summed** turnover figure and dropped both legs — the exact ambiguity `turnover()`'s own docstring claimed to resolve, computed and then discarded. It surfaced as an uninterpretable **1564% over 73d**, which has two opposite readings the sum cannot separate: a book *built* inside its own measurement window has a buy-heavy numerator **and** a small mean-value denominator, so it reads as extreme churn by construction, whereas buys ≈ sells would mean ~7-8 real round-trips in 73 days (a ~9-day hold, flatly against the app's medium-term posture). `turnover()` now also returns `buy_turnover_pct`/`sell_turnover_pct` against the same denominator — percentages, so the panel discloses the split without handling dollar figures — and the caption renders them. Also fixed: `window_days` is now an **inclusive** count matching `snapshot_coverage`'s `span_days`, which had the panel printing 74 and 73 days for the same interval (accepted side effects: the annualisation gate opens one day earlier and the `365/window` multiplier is a hair smaller — the conservative direction, on a panel that gates nothing). 4 further tests, including one asserting that an accumulating book and a churning book produce an **identical** total and differ only in the legs. |
| F-246 | **Correlation sample-size honesty — the diversification score must show how much data it has. AWARENESS ONLY; gates nothing, changes no score.** Found 2026-08-21 while validating F-245 on the live book: 🔗 Risk Analysis reported **avg portfolio correlation 0.12** and classified an 18-name, tech-and-semis-heavy portfolio as **"Well Diversified" (44/100)** — while simultaneously flagging CRM×SAP at 0.77 and DELL×HPE at 0.65. NVDA/MU/MRVL, which correlate well above the 0.65 threshold in essentially any 6-month window, appeared nowhere. The two readings could not both be casually true, and **no surface said how many observations the matrix was built on**, so the question was unanswerable from the UI. Root cause of the *ambiguity* (not yet proven to be a defect): `portfolio.correlation_matrix()` uses **listwise deletion** — `pd.DataFrame(series).dropna()` across every ticker at once — so the whole matrix is computed on the *intersection* of all histories, and a single short or degraded fetch silently thins the sample for **every pair in the book**. A thin sample pushes correlations toward zero, which renders as diversification the user does not have. New pure `portfolio.correlation_coverage(held_data)` reports `n_obs` (daily returns shared by all holdings), `n_tickers`, and `shortest_ticker`/`shortest_len`/`longest_len` — naming the holding with the shortest history — usually, but not provably, the one capping the sample (with disjoint indexes every history can be equally long while the intersection is still empty). It shares `portfolio._close_series_map()` with `correlation_matrix` so the coverage figure always describes **the same inputs the matrix used** (a count computed off a different set would be worse than none) and returns `None` on the primary empty-matrix condition (fewer than 2 usable histories; disjoint indexes give a dict with `n_obs == 0` against an empty matrix, so that condition is a strict superset). Rendered on 🔗 Risk Analysis → 📊 Dashboard beneath the classification caption: normally a plain observation count, and a **red `st.error`** below `portfolio.CORR_MIN_OBS_TRUSTED` stating that the correlations, the diversification score and the classification derived from them are not reliable. Also consumed by F-245's stop-out clustering line, which on a thin sample still prints the figure but explicitly withholds any conclusion from it. That line additionally now judges its flat "correlation is not the driver" verdict on the **max** pairwise correlation (`forward_sim.max_pairwise_corr`), never the mean — `CORR_HIGH_PAIRS_THRESHOLD` is a per-PAIR constant, and a subset holding one duplicated pair among several near-zero pairs averages far below it, so denying duplication on the mean would under-alarm on a protective surface. **`CORR_MIN_OBS_TRUSTED = 20` is a display-only measurement floor deliberately NOT in `constants.py`** — same precedent as F-230's `stress_test._MIN_STRESS_WINDOW_DAYS` and `account._ANNUALIZE_CAVEAT_MAX_DAYS`: it decides only whether a data-quality warning renders, never whether a recommendation fires. The value is **not newly invented** — it reuses the app's existing "≥20 overlapping trading days" empirical-correlation minimum (`portfolio_intelligence.factor_tilt`'s `min_overlap_days`, itself mirroring `risk.py`'s rate-sensitivity floor), so no new investment-policy number was set. **Deliberately does NOT change the deletion strategy.** Switching listwise → pairwise-complete would alter a shared decision input feeding the diversification score, the correlation clusters, F-230 Correlation Under Stress, the Diversification Advisor's ADD recommendations and F-245 — a real behaviour change needing its own design pass, not a cleanup bundled into a diagnostic. This ships the *measurement* first so the question can be settled with data instead of argument. 9 new tests (6 in `tests/test_portfolio_intelligence.py`, 3 in `tests/test_forward_sim.py`). |
| F-245 | **Forward Portfolio Simulator ("🧯 After My Rules") — replays the app's OWN mechanical rules against a shocked book. New 5th tab on 🔗 Risk Analysis. READ-ONLY DIAGNOSTIC: issues no directive, gates nothing, writes nothing.** Closes the E1 gap named in `docs/plans/next-evolution-strategy.md` (Experimental Track) and still open per Pass #2 (`next-evolution-2026-08-05.md` Lens 3). The gap: `stress_test.py` shocks prices and reports P&L and contains **no stop or gate logic at all**; F-224 Outcome Range is a per-name bootstrap distribution. Neither answers *"after my own rules fire, what book am I left holding?"* — the only question that tests the **interaction** of ~20 gates, the deterioration ladder and the stop ladder, each of which was set as an isolated policy decision. Every other accountability surface (The Judge, F-229, F-233) grades *individual* calls. **Faithful by construction:** `stock_analyzer/forward_sim.py` re-extracts the deterioration scalars at a substituted price but never reimplements a rule — tiers come from `exit_advisor.classify_deterioration_tier` (the same pure scalar core the Brief uses), the regime read from `exit_advisor.risk_off_regime`, the overlay from `assess_risk_off_derisk`, and the shock from `stress_test.run_scenario`. `tests/test_forward_sim.py::test_zero_shock_matches_assess_holding` pins that extraction against `exit_advisor.assess_holding` at zero shock (tier + 7 scalars) so it cannot silently drift — the module's single biggest risk, since a drifted replay would report a book the app would never produce, authoritatively. **Renders a two-column bracket, not one number:** TRIM requires `DETERIORATION_CONFIRM_REQUIRED` of `DETERIORATION_CONFIRM_DAYS` sessions below the `DETERIORATION_TREND_MA` trend line, which on day one of a shock genuinely has not happened — so "fires immediately" (real pre-shock `below_ma_count`) is shown beside "once the trend confirms" (`below_ma_count = DETERIORATION_CONFIRM_DAYS`), and **the gap between them is the finding** — it quantifies how much of the book's protection depends on confirmation lag. That is the **only assumed input**; everything else is derived from real data, including relative strength — the engine's real trailing RS **plus** the scenario's name-vs-SPY differential (`real_rs + (move_pct − spy_move)`, reducing exactly to the engine's value at zero shock). **Additive, never a replacement:** an Opus review caught a first cut that used the differential alone as a live defect — for the 6 of 9 sector-targeted scenarios, a holding whose sector is absent from `_SECTOR_SHOCKS` gets `est_move = 0.0`, so RS read `0 − (−10) = +10`, a fabricated *positive* strength that switched `trim_active` off on a name the Brief was calling TRIM/EXIT in the same session (same failure for any β<1 name in a broad scenario). Also derived: whether risk-off arms (the shocked SPY close vs its real 200-day mean — `risk_off_regime`'s two legs are OR'd, so **no VIX assumption is needed**), with book fragility taken from the live `_fragility_cache` since fragility is a property of the book, not of the shock. Uses the **ratcheted** protective stop (`port_df["Stop"]` = `max(ATR stop, profit-ratchet floor)` or a tighter manual), never the raw ATR bundle stop, and mirrors the Brief's exact `round((price − stop)/price × 100, GAP_TO_STOP_ROUND_DECIMALS) <= 0` breach test so the two surfaces agree. Output: the bracket counts, **stop-out clustering** (how many stops breach together, their sector concentration, and their mean pairwise correlation via `forward_sim.mean_pairwise_corr` reading the existing `_corr_df_cache` — the headline finding, since highly-correlated simultaneous stop-outs mean N positions were really a handful of bets), the surviving book (positions remaining, value-weighted surviving beta, proceeds raised), the risk-off overlay cards, and a per-position replay table. **Fails loud, never silently benign:** a missing stop reads `None` (⚠️ "no stop") rather than "no breach" and is called out as making the exit counts an UNDER-statement (G-11's data-gap class); a position whose history can't support the trend line is reported as "not judged", and one with no live market value gets its own `no_value` bucket — both surfaced, never assumed safe. The risk-off payload keeps **three** offline states distinct rather than collapsing them: `available` (no SPY frame → "unknown", not "would not arm"), `fragility_available` (`_fragility_cache` is `None` **when its producer failed**, not when the book is calm — collapsing that would render an offline read as a benign "would not arm", the sentinel-collapse class `check_antipatterns.py` exists to catch), and `armed`. Surviving beta is `None` rather than a fabricated 1.0, and `mean_pairwise_corr` returns `None` rather than `0.0`. A name whose *trailing* RS leg can't be read (no benchmark, or under `REL_STRENGTH_LOOKBACK_DAYS` of history) is collected into `rel_strength_degraded` and named — because the engine's unknown-RS-is-0.0 fail-safe (never open an action tier on unknown RS) does **not** survive composition here, so those rows are less reliable in both directions and say so. The overlay's `exclude_tickers` covers **every** name carrying any flag in either column, not just TRIM/EXIT — matching `decision_bucket.all_flagged_tickers()` on the live path, which deliberately includes WATCH because the 2026-07-29 audit (H6) found a narrower filter let a WATCH card and a same-render risk-off "trim now" card coexist for one ticker. The overlay also renders **derived facts in past-conditional, never `assess_risk_off_derisk`'s verbatim `directive` string** — that string is a live imperative whose stop-tighten level is computed off the *shocked* price, so printing it would state an instruction carrying a dollar figure that is wrong for today, crossing the diagnostic/directive line this tab exists inside. A TRIM is deliberately **not** liquidated in the aggregate (its size is a per-card directive; a fixed haircut would invent a number). The proceeds figure is deliberately **not** compared against `REGIME_CASH_FLOOR_PCT` — that comparison implies a redeployment directive, which is out of scope here. **No new constants** (no investment-policy value is introduced or changed). 64 new tests. Honesty framing carried in the UI: a simulated stop-out is not a filled order (`protective_stop` is stateless and has no high-water memory, so a breach means "the stop the app would recommend today was crossed"), the model is a trough not a path, and it is a mechanical consequence rather than a forecast — no probability is attached to the scenario and no expected return is implied for any name, preserving the §5.8 invariant. The UI also states plainly that the app's de-risk rule is the **exit half of a two-sided trend rule and the re-entry half does not exist**; measuring whether that asymmetry actually costs anything in this book is the express purpose of the feature, and any re-entry discipline is a separate future decision requiring its own policy conversation, `planner` design pass and `reviewer` pass. |

| F-226 | **Personalized Discovery — runs Behavioral Fingerprint's analysis FORWARD.** Behavioral Fingerprint (F-193) only ever compares two buckets of past decisions against each other (e.g. high-momentum vs low-momentum action rate) — it has no notion of "what did a typical winner look like at entry." This feature builds that profile and uses it to flag which of TODAY's already-fully-gated 🏠 Home Grow Today picks resemble the user's own REALIZED winning trades. New module `stock_analyzer/personalized_discovery.py`: `build_winner_profile(closed_lots, matched_recs, min_n, pctl_low=25, pctl_high=75)` joins `investor_mirror.build_closed_lots()`'s realized round-trips (filtered to `is_gain=True` AND a non-null `pnl_pct` — `is_gain` alone is insufficient, matching the guard `investor_mirror.py`'s own winner-filtering functions already apply) back to `recommendations_history.match_recs_to_trades()`'s matched, acted-on, actionable (`new_pick`/`add_winner`) rows via `ticker` + `rec_date == buy_date` (sound because `match_recs_to_trades()` only ever matches a rec to a SAME-DAY trade). Multiple sell-fragments of the SAME scaled-out entry collapse to one sample via `drop_duplicates(subset=["ticker","buy_date"])` before counting — a position sold in 3 tranches is one entry decision, not three. Returns `None` below `min_n` matched winning entries (reuses the existing `BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N`=8 floor, not a parallel constant) — withheld, never a profile fabricated off a handful of trades. `score_candidate_match(composite_score, momentum_score, sector, profile)` checks 3 traits independently (in-band composite, in-band momentum, sector-in-top-sectors) against a candidate, `None`-safe throughout. Zero new fetches — entirely a replay of already-loaded trades/recommendations; deliberately avoids `compute_outcomes()`'s mark-to-market path (would need a live current price for every historically-recommended ticker, including delisted/no-longer-held ones). **Two surfaces:** (1) `stock_analyzer/daily_briefing.py::_grow_today()` attaches `pick["personalized_match"]` to every `new_pick` (NOT `add_positions` — same "brand-new entry only" scoping F-220 Phase 2 already established) after every existing gate has passed; `app.py`'s Grow Today card renders a "🧬 Matches your historical winning profile (…)" caption only when `n_matched >= PERSONALIZED_DISCOVERY_MIN_MATCH_TRAITS` (2 of 3) — silence, never a warning, when it doesn't match, since a non-match isn't itself a negative signal on an already-vetted pick. (2) A new "🔭 Your Winning Entry Profile" card on 🎯 My Edge → 🧬 Behavioral Fingerprint, computed independently and locally there (own full-history `db.load_recommendations(start_date=None, end_date=None)` call, distinct from that tab's existing 30-day-default read) rather than via a shared session_state cache — same "cheap pure recompute beats cross-page order-dependency" precedent already used by `catalyst_stress.py`. New constants: `PERSONALIZED_DISCOVERY_MIN_MATCH_TRAITS`=2, `PERSONALIZED_DISCOVERY_PROFILE_PCTL_LOW`=25, `PERSONALIZED_DISCOVERY_PROFILE_PCTL_HIGH`=75. **Diagnostic/awareness only — never changes which tickers clear the 5-gate `_grow_today()` pipeline, never re-scores or re-ranks a pick, never suppresses a non-matching one.** `_home_synth_cache`'s `_SYNTH_SCHEMA_VER` bumped 5→6 (new `personalized_match` key on `new_picks`). 21 new tests (`tests/test_personalized_discovery.py` + extended `tests/test_daily_briefing.py`). Opus round 1: FIX-FIRST (2 blocking — `is_gain` alone missed a null-`pnl_pct` trap `investor_mirror.py` already guards against elsewhere; `n` counted winning SELL-fragments instead of distinct winning ENTRIES, letting one scaled-out position triple-count and defeat the sample-size floor) → both fixed, 2 regression tests added. ### 3.1g Investor Mirror (F-194)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-194 | **Investor Mirror — Conviction Alignment + Behavioral Biases — SHIPPED 2026-07-20.** New 5th tab "🪞 Investor Mirror" on 🎯 My Edge. New pure module `stock_analyzer/investor_mirror.py` (no Streamlit, no DB, no yfinance). **Awareness only — zero gates, zero recommendations, zero scoring impact.** Answers two questions that complement the existing My Edge tabs: (1) does the user's portfolio allocation actually reflect their composite conviction? (2) does their sell behaviour reflect rational action, or recognisable behavioural biases? **Section 1 — Conviction Alignment:** computes the Spearman rank correlation between each position's composite Score and its portfolio Weight (%) using `pd.Series.corr(method='spearman')` — no scipy dependency. A ρ near +1.0 means the largest bets are the strongest convictions; ρ near 0 means allocation is essentially random relative to beliefs. Three named misalignment patterns identified: **Orphan Conviction** (Score ≥ `COMPOSITE_STRONG_BUY` and weight < median — high conviction, undersized), **Accidental Overexposure** (Score < `CONVICTION_WEAK_SCORE` (50) and weight > median — significant allocation on weak conviction), **Legacy Overhang** (top-`CONVICTION_LEGACY_TOP_N` (3) by weight and Score < `CONVICTION_FADED_SCORE` (60) — a large position whose conviction has drifted out of Buy territory, often from price appreciation rather than a deliberate sizing decision). Rendered as a headline Spearman ρ metric + plain-English label (Disciplined ≥ `CONVICTION_ALIGNMENT_HIGH` 0.60 / Partial / Random < `CONVICTION_ALIGNMENT_LOW` 0.30), a Score-vs-Weight scatter chart with an ideal-alignment reference diagonal, and three callout containers listing each misaligned position. Uses today's composite scores (historical composite scores are not stored — stated clearly in the methodology note). Requires `_port_df_enriched` to be populated (Home visited); shows an info gate otherwise. **Section 2 — Behavioral Biases:** three cards derived from `investor_mirror.build_closed_lots(trades_df)` — a FIFO lot-matching function adapted from `tax_advisor._build_open_lots`, extended to track buy_price per lot so P&L can be computed per matched fragment. Partial trims are handled correctly via lot depletion. SPLIT rows scale both share count and buy_price per lot so cost basis stays accurate. Cached in `st.session_state["_mirror_closed_lots_{n}"]` keyed by trade count (auto-refreshes when trades change). **Card A — Disposition Effect:** share-weighted average holding period for gain-lots vs loss-lots; ratio = loser avg ÷ winner avg; ≥ `DISPOSITION_CONCERN_RATIO` (1.5×) renders an amber concern note (consistent with the published retail benchmark range of 1.5–2.0×). **Card B — Win/Loss Closure Ratio:** groups matched lots by (ticker, sell_date) to get per-transaction net P&L; ratio = gain-realising transactions ÷ loss-realising transactions; ≥ `WINLOSS_CONCERN_RATIO` (2.0×) renders a loss-aversion note. **Card C — Breakeven Anchoring:** buckets each closed lot by P&L% at exit into 10 fixed presentation brackets (< −20% through > +20%); computes share-weighted average hold-time per bracket; flags when the −2 to 0% bracket avg hold-time is ≥ `BREAKEVEN_ANCHOR_DWELL_RATIO` (1.3×) the mean of the adjacent loss brackets, indicating positions are held unusually long when close to breakeven. Rendered as a horizontal bar chart (loss brackets red, gain brackets green). Each card individually suppresses to "insufficient data" when below `INVESTOR_MIRROR_MIN_CLOSED_LOTS` (10) in its required comparison group. **Opus review: SHIP** after 4 blocking constants-externalization findings applied (bare literals `>= 75`, `< 50`, `< 60`, `1.3` lifted to `COMPOSITE_STRONG_BUY`, `CONVICTION_WEAK_SCORE`, `CONVICTION_FADED_SCORE`, `BREAKEVEN_ANCHOR_DWELL_RATIO`). 10 new display-only constants in `stock_analyzer/constants.py` F-194 block. **Cross-feature coordination fix (2026-07-30, the BKNG incident):** Orphan Conviction candidates now exclude any ticker carrying an active Act Today/Review reduce call (`decision_bucket.suppress_orphans_under_reduce_call`, reads the published `_reduce_calls` cache) — previously a "size up" Orphan Conviction card and a same-week "size down" reduce/trim call could fire on the same ticker with no cross-check, which is how the fix was found (the user sized up on the Orphan Conviction cue, which then itself tripped the earnings-overweight-trim threshold). Excluded tickers render a visible "⛔ excluded" note naming the active reduce call rather than being silently dropped; the filtered set is also published to `_mirror_orphans` so the Analysis page's 🪞 Investor Mirror banner (a second, independent consumer of the same data) can't surface the same contradiction. |

Gated the same way as every other Home-extracted page (`_render_portfolio_not_loaded()` + `st.stop()` on `_port_df_enriched`/`_last_held_data`). Renders: (1) an 8-metric KPI row (Portfolio Value, Unrealized P&L, Today's P&L, Alerts, Avg Score, Diversification, Best/Worst position) — Portfolio Value and the Alerts/Diversification figures read the same published `_portfolio_value`/`_home_synth_cache` caches Home populates; the rest (Unrealized P&L, Avg Score, Best/Worst) are cheap independent recomputations from `port_df`, so no session-state coupling is added. **Portfolio Value tile also shows a trailing sparkline** (shipped 2026-07-27) — daily equity (`Σ shares × close_price`) grouped from the optional `daily_snapshots` table via `db.load_daily_snapshots()` (date-filtered to the trailing ~45 calendar days so the window is exactly what's shown, not row-count-limited), rendered by the shared `_render_sparkline()` helper (bare Plotly line, no axes); silently omitted (not an error) when fewer than 2 days of snapshot history exist. Alerts/Avg Score deliberately carry no delta/trend — point-in-time counts with no natural "vs. what." **A second row (Tier 3, shipped 2026-07-27)** sits below the 8-tile row: **Alpha vs SPY (Nd)** — portfolio equity return vs SPY return over the same trailing window (N up to 30 trading days, shrinking honestly and relabeling when less snapshot history exists; withheld below 5 days), with its own sparkline of the running differential; tooltip is explicit that this is an **approximate, equity-level** read NOT adjusted for mid-window buys/sells (the precise money-weighted alpha vs benchmark lives on 🎯 My Edge → 📐 Benchmark Mirror). **Relabeled 2026-08-05 (coherence audit):** the tile now reads "Alpha vs SPY (actual, Nd)" — the "(actual)" qualifier disambiguates it from 🥧 Portfolio Overview's separately-labeled "Alpha vs SPY (backtest)" tile, which computes a hypothetical return applying today's current weights to historical prices; the two were previously both bare "Alpha vs SPY" with no cross-reference, and the tooltip incorrectly pointed to 💰 Account → Capital Trend (which carries no alpha/benchmark figure at all) instead of 🎯 My Edge's actual money-weighted alpha. See `docs/plans/next-evolution-2026-08-05.md` Lens 1 and memory `project_alpha_coherence_audit`. **Confirmed live 2026-07-27 that this contamination is a real, not just theoretical, risk** (a mid-window capital add produced a misleading +15.9% headline) — a same-day follow-up added an always-visible caption ("⚠️ Capital added/removed during this window — not a pure performance read"), checking `trades_df` for any BUY/SELL dated within the joined window, since a hover-only tooltip wasn't enough to stop the number being read at face value. **Risk Posture** — a read-only pointer badge (🔗 title + severity emoji/label from `market_risk_posture()` + one-line summary + a "→ Risk Analysis" **button**, `_pending_page` navigation), reusing the identical `exit_advisor.market_risk_posture()` call and the already-published `_fragility_cache` the Risk Analysis dial uses — never a second independent verdict; withheld (no card) when fragility isn't computed yet. **Fixed 2026-07-27 (live-review catch):** originally shipped with a plain non-clickable caption instead of a real jump button — inconsistent with the two pointer cards below, which both navigate. Corrected to the same `st.button` + `_pending_page` pattern; also added the 🔗 title-icon prefix (matching Risk Analysis's own nav icon) since the card title previously carried none, unlike 🩺 Thesis Review / 🔔 Catalyst Watch below. **A new "🧭 Elsewhere in DRISHTA" pointer section (shipped 2026-07-27, card #2 of `docs/plans/summary-page-pointer-cards.md`)** sits between Act Today and Holdings: **🩺 Thesis Review** shows a count of held tickers whose most recent `thesis_reviews` row (F-1's per-holding INTACT/WEAKENING/BROKEN review — NOT the ⚠️ Red Team tab's separate quantitative erosion score) is WEAKENING or BROKEN, reusing the identical "most recent review per ticker" logic the AI Insights page's own "🩺 Positions" header count and the Act Today "Thesis broken" button already use — never a second independent verdict. Shows "All Intact" (calm, not hidden) when none need attention; withheld entirely only when no held ticker has any saved review yet. A "→ AI Insights" button (`_pending_page` navigation) lands on that page's default first tab, 🩺 Positions, where this exact count already lives. **A second pointer card, 🔔 Catalyst Watch (shipped 2026-07-27, card #3), sits alongside it:** a count of held names reporting earnings within `CATALYST_WATCH_WINDOW_DAYS` (7) days, reusing the SAME 24h process-wide `@st.cache_data`-cached `_cached_held_earnings_dates()` call (identical tickers-tuple + 90-day range) Catalyst Watch's own "Your Holdings — Earnings" tier uses — whichever page runs first pays the cached fetch once, the other gets a free hit, never a second independent computation. Prefers `held_data`'s own bundle-loaded earnings date first (zero cost) before falling back to the cached backfill, matching `_render_holdings_earnings()`'s exact preference order. Always shows a state (including "None soon") rather than hiding, since earnings timing is informative even when the answer is none. A "→ Catalyst Watch" button navigates there. **No RAG banner** ("Action Required"/"Monitor"/"All Clear") — dropped 2026-07-26, same day as ship: it duplicated Home's identical Command Center bar, and its `n_danger`-driven label (Active Alerts, F-169) can legitimately disagree with an empty Act Today bucket (a different signal — same-day trade decisions), which read as a flat contradiction on this short page; the Alerts KPI tile already carries the same counts calmly. Today's P&L uses the simpler held-mark calc (F-03) rather than Home's fuller Tier-B upgrade (F-03a), to avoid a second `daily_snapshots` round-trip on a second page. (2) **Act Today**, reusing Home's exact `_daily_brief` data (read from `_home_synth_cache`) and the identical pure `split_defensive()` call Home uses for its own bucket — never an independent recommendation computation. Cards are a deliberately **leaner** rendering than Home's (`_render_simple_action_card()`: tier badge + ticker + one-line rationale, matching the approved Option-A mockup) — no Analyze button, Exit Red-Team debate, journal context, or tax notes; those stay Home-only. (3) The full-width **Holdings** table (`_render_holdings_table()`, module-level, shared/reusable) — this is now its ONLY home; it was briefly also on Home (same-day, F-01) but removed from there once this page shipped, so it isn't duplicated across two pages. Both the Holdings table and the shared severity-color constants (`_HOME_URGENT`/`_HOME_ELEVATED`/`_HOME_CALM`/`_HOME_GAIN`/`_HOME_LOSS`) and `_fmt_action()` were hoisted from Home-local closures to module-level functions as part of this change so Summary could reuse them — a pure hoist, verified to leave Home's own rendering unchanged. |

### 3.1i The Judge (F-227)

New standalone page (MAIN nav group, between Home and Summary), the North
Star portfolio-judgment layer — see `docs/plans/judgment-layer.md` for the
full design history and Opus review log. Built forward-only across all 5
phases, all shipped 2026-08-03.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-227 | **The Judge — a portfolio-level judgment layer reconciling the app's other features into one decomposable posture, never a black box.** Existing subsystems (composite score, momentum scanner, verdict reconciliation, exit_advisor deterioration tiers, the concentration gate, the fragility gauge) each already emit a decision-relevant call; the Judge collects them as tagged **opinions** (`stock_analyzer/judgment_opinion.py::build_opinion()` — `source`/`dimension`/`signal` normalized −1..+1/`confidence`/`as_of`+`is_live`/`evidence`/`ticker`/`advisory`) and reconciles same-ticker (and portfolio-wide) opinions into one read via `stock_analyzer/judgment_synthesis.py::synthesize()`. **Three routing outcomes, never a plain average:** (1) **PROTECTIVE VETO** — a protective-dimension opinion (`position_health`/`concentration`/`structural_risk`/`leverage`) at/below `JUDGMENT_VETO_PROTECTIVE_THRESHOLD` (−0.4) hard-suppresses EVERY same-ticker positive acquisitive-dimension opinion (`quality`/`momentum`) at once — the most severe (lowest-signal) protective opinion wins when more than one qualifies; suppressed opinions are shown as dissent, never blended in. (2) **CONTRADICTION AUDIT** — two different sources on the same dimension with opposite-sign signals, both at/above `JUDGMENT_CONTRADICTION_MIN_MAGNITUDE` (0.3), are flagged rather than silently averaged away. (3) **WEIGHTING** — everything else blends via a confidence-weighted average. Advisory opinions (`verdict_reconciliation` — a meta-witness that already synthesizes momentum+composite+news+earnings) are shown for context but excluded from veto/blend/contradiction routing, so a meta-witness never double-counts its own inputs. **Read-only, no override/gating authority at any phase built so far** — visiting the page changes nothing about any recommendation. **Phase 0 (instrumentation, log-only):** 5 core-set witnesses wired at their existing call sites (exit_advisor→`position_health`, composite_score+scanner_momentum+verdict_reconciliation→`quality`/`momentum`, fragility_gauge→`structural_risk`, concentration_gate→`concentration`, the last being a real single-name/sector ceiling-breach check against `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`/`SECTOR_CEILING`), persisted to `judgment_opinions` (architecture.md §6.29). **Phase 1 (read-only decomposition):** the "🧑‍⚖️ The Judge" page itself — an overall posture line, per-ticker and portfolio-wide vote decomposition with veto/contradiction callouts, and a "reduced visibility" caption when a protective dimension had no live witness this run (an offline protective sensor must never silently read as "clear"). **Phase 2 (grading harness):** `stock_analyzer/judgment_grading.py` grades individual WITNESSES (never the aggregate posture — posture-correctness is a derived, secondary read) against realized forward alpha vs SPY over a per-dimension horizon (`JUDGMENT_HORIZON_MOMENTUM_DAYS`=5, `..._QUALITY_DAYS`=20, `..._POSITION_HEALTH_DAYS`=10, `..._CONCENTRATION_DAYS`=20, `..._STRUCTURAL_RISK_DAYS`=10), reusing `predictive_analytics.forward_alpha_at_horizon()` (the Entry Timing tab's own mechanism) for per-ticker grading and a net-new `portfolio_value_series_from_snapshots()` for portfolio-wide grading. **Protective dimensions are deliberately WITHHELD from grading (return `None`, distinct from "pending maturity"), not graded naively** — sign-matching a TRIM/caution call against forward alpha would mark the witness "wrong" for every risk that correctly never fired, the exact anti-caution posture inversion the design's Gap B exists to prevent; a real counterfactual grader is a future gap, not yet built. A manual "▶ Run grading" button persists new grades to `judgment_grades` (architecture.md §6.30); results roll up per (source, dimension) via `track_record_summary()`, gated on the shared `BEHAVIORAL_MIN_SAMPLE_N` (8) floor — below that, a witness's accuracy shows as "building history," not a number. **Phase 3 (evidence-based weighting, still no authority):** once a (source, dimension) pair clears the min-sample gate, its blend weight in `synthesize()` is scaled by a track-record multiplier (`_weight_multiplier()` in `judgment_synthesis.py`) — `accuracy / JUDGMENT_TRACK_RECORD_NEUTRAL_ACCURACY` (0.5, coin-flip-is-neutral), clamped to `[JUDGMENT_TRACK_RECORD_WEIGHT_FLOOR, JUDGMENT_TRACK_RECORD_WEIGHT_CEILING]` (0.25x–2.0x, user-confirmed "moderate" band) so no single witness can be fully silenced or dominate the blend alone. **The multiplier only ever touches the blend — the protective veto and the contradiction-audit magnitude floor are never weighted**, preserving them as hard gates rather than softening them into a vote (the same structural principle the original Q1 design review required). Each opinion's effective weight is shown next to its signal on the page (e.g. "1.8x weight") whenever it differs from neutral, so the adjustment is never hidden. Below the min-sample gate, or with no track record supplied, behavior is byte-identical to Phase 1/2's equal-weight blend. **A real production bug was found and fixed via a live screenshot during Phase 1** (not caught by design/code review): opinion-capture code had been written only inside the Home `_home_synth_cache` cache-MISS branch, so it silently never ran on the common cache-HIT case; fixed by consolidating capture to the hit/miss reconvergence point. **Phase 4 (authority — audit only, never a new gate):** a pre-build research pass found that granting the Judge's protective veto literal override/gating authority over all 4 dimensions, as the original one-line design sketch described, would either duplicate mature enforcement that already exists elsewhere (`position_health` via the `_reduce_calls` mechanism across 5+ surfaces — Grow Today, Rebalancer, Investor Mirror, Analysis add-sizing, deterioration-WATCH blocking; `concentration` via the entry-gate pipeline's own `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING`/`SECTOR_CEILING` checks, fully decoupled from the Judge) or silently reverse a standing, deliberately-documented house policy (`leverage` — CLAUDE.md's coordination pattern explicitly states `_leverage_cache` is "read-only, never gates"). Presented with this finding, the user chose an **audit-only scope**: `stock_analyzer/judgment_synthesis.py::audit_coherence()` cross-checks every ticker under an active protective veto against `_reduce_calls` (the app's one other already-published cross-feature risk surface, read directly from `session_state` with no new plumbing) and reports `"covered"` (a veto exists AND the ticker has an active reduce call — validating: the Judge agrees with what's already flagged) vs `"uncovered"` (a veto exists with NO active reduce call — a genuine coherence gap the Judge alone caught this run). This operationalizes the design's own "Job 3: audit for cross-feature contradiction systematically" as a standing, automatic check rather than something only caught reactively (the class of incident the BKNG orphan-conviction collision and SHOP buy/sell whiplash represent). **Never suppresses or modifies any recommendation — purely surfaces a finding loudly** (a red banner per uncovered gap, a calm confirmation when none exist, and an explicit "unavailable — visit Home first" state when `_reduce_calls` hasn't been published yet this session, never silently treated as zero reduce calls). No new `constants.py` entries — reuses the existing veto computation and threshold. Includes the portfolio-wide bucket under a `"_PORTFOLIO_WIDE"` sentinel key for completeness, though a portfolio-wide veto cannot currently fire (no portfolio-wide acquisitive opinion is emitted yet). **This closes the Judge's full 5-phase build (0-4).** Opus 4.8 reviewed Phase 1 (FIX-FIRST, 2 blocking, both fixed), Phase 2 (FIX-FIRST, 2 blocking, both fixed), Phase 3 (SHIP, 0 blocking, 2 non-blocking hardening tweaks applied anyway), and Phase 4 (SHIP, 0 blocking, 3 non-blocking findings fixed anyway — see the plan doc's status log). Plan: [docs/plans/judgment-layer.md](plans/judgment-layer.md). |

### 3.1j Model Lab (F-234)

New standalone page (RESEARCH nav group, positioned after Predictive
Analytics), the entry point to the **Predictive Modeling Shadow Layer**
(Phase 1) — see `docs/plans/predictive-modeling-shadow-layer.md` for the
full design/leakage-guard rationale.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-234 | **Model Lab — quarantined forecast-calibration harness, owner-only, EXPERIMENTAL, MEASUREMENT-ONLY. Not shipped/reviewed yet.** New `🔬 Model Lab` page — the **first nav entry ever fully hidden** (not merely write-disabled) from a read-only viewer, since it has no meaning for anyone but the owner. Logs a v1 volatility forecast (`stock_analyzer/vol_forecast.py::forecast_vol_ewma()` — RiskMetrics-style fixed-λ EWMA, `VOL_FORECAST_EWMA_LAMBDA`=0.94, NO fitted parameters, NO LLM, NO deep learning) for **20-trading-day forward realized volatility** (`VOL_FORECAST_HORIZON_DAYS`=20, annualized), per currently-held ticker AND the portfolio aggregate, once daily via the EOD cron lane (`cron_runner.py`) — reusing the SAME 6-month bars that lane's other steps already fetch, no new fetch call. A naive persistence baseline (trailing realized volatility, `vol_forecast.realized_vol()`) is logged **at prediction time**, never recomputed at scoring, into the new `model_predictions` table (§6.31) — ships inert until its DDL is applied manually via the Supabase dashboard. A second daily cron step matures prior rows once their horizon has fully elapsed (trading-day aware), writing the realized outcome from fresh price history. A one-off, rerunnable backfill script (`scripts/backfill_vol_predictions.py`) additionally seeds `source='backfill'` rows for currently-held tickers using `PREDICTION_BACKFILL_PERIOD`="5y" of history (same fetch path as Outcome Range/F-224) — walking a strided set of historical `as_of` points (~5 trading days apart) so every backfilled row already has a fully-realized outcome computed in one shot; **PORTFOLIO-scope backfill is deliberately NOT built** (would need historical portfolio weights only available as far back as `trades` history, ~3 months). `stock_analyzer/prediction_scoring.py::score_predictions()` is the pure scoring harness: MAE(model) vs MAE(baseline), a skill score (`1 − MAE(model)/MAE(baseline)`) **withheld (`None`, never 0/fabricated) below `PREDICTION_MIN_MATURED_N`=20 matured rows**, a live-only skill variant (same floor applied to live-only rows, so a mostly-backfilled headline number can never masquerade as live-validated), a regime-stratified breakdown (never hidden even on a thin/calm-only slice — visibly labeled instead), and a stride-based "effective n" caveat note (overlapping daily/near-daily windows against a 20-day horizon overstate independent information). The page itself renders three states (approved mockup: `docs/mockups/predictive-model-lab.html`) — an always-visible "EXPERIMENTAL — measurement only" banner; "offline" (`db.load_model_predictions()` returns `None` — no DDL applied yet, or a query failure, distinguished from a genuinely-empty result); "warming up" (matured count below the floor — shown honestly, not withheld); and the full calibration readout (skill blended + live-only, regime split, a predicted-vs-realized Plotly scatter against the perfect-calibration diagonal, and a recently-matured table with a visible `source` tag per row). **Changes NO gate, NO recommendation, NO composite score, NO threshold anywhere in the app** — predicts risk (volatility), never a stock-level directional/return point-estimate (the binding §5.8 invariant from `next-evolution-strategy.md`). Consumes nothing from `st.session_state`; publishes nothing back — a dead end by design, verified against every existing coordination cache key in CLAUDE.md before ship. A future Phase 3 (months out, gated on the skill score clearing 0 across a real window including ≥1 stress episode, with its own `planner`+`reviewer` pass) could propose wiring a validated signal into an existing protective gate as **tightening-only** — never loosening a gate or manufacturing a buy — but that is an explicit future decision, not an implied green light from this build. |

### 3.1k System Trust (F-235)

New owner-only page (RESEARCH nav group, positioned after Model Lab), the
surface for **System Proprioception Phase 1** — pipeline-trust diagnostics. See
`docs/plans/system-proprioception.md`.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
