| F-236 | **New Positions transparency — firmness badge + intraday drop-off trace + pre-open note (SHIPPED, DISPLAY-ONLY, gates nothing).** Makes the live-recompute behaviour of 🆕 New Positions to Initiate legible instead of silent, motivated by a real observation: two Strong Buys surfaced pre-/at-open and only one remained an hour later, leaving the user unsure whether the earlier read was "wrong" (it wasn't — the panel is a render-time recompute, and a marginal name re-priced across a threshold at the open). Three additions, all in a new pure module `stock_analyzer/grow_dropoff.py` (logic) + `app.py` (render): **① Firmness badge** per `new_pick` card — `firmness(composite, tier_floor, margin)` badges a pick "at the line" (amber) when its current composite is within `COMPOSITE_FIRMNESS_MARGIN` (3) points of its tier floor (`tier_floor_for()` → `COMPOSITE_STRONG_BUY`=75 for a Strong-Buy-tier pick, `COMPOSITE_BUY`=65 for a Buy-tier pick; inclusive `<=` boundary), else "firm"; sets the expectation that a marginal name can flip intraday. **② Drop-off trace** (muted grey panel footer, never a pick card, no buy button) — `derive_dropoffs()` lists names that surfaced as `new_pick` earlier today but are no longer clearing this pass (`surfaced_today − current − acted_or_held`, so a name still showing or already bought/held never appears), each with an **honestly-attributed reason** via a fixed priority chain: active Reduce/Exit call → composite-below-bar (`composite_skipped`, quotes the real re-scored composite) → sector cap (`sector_blocked_picks`) → macro (`macro_blocked_picks`) → data unavailable (`composite_unavailable`) → **`unattributed`** (a name filtered at the momentum pre-filter leaves no bucket trace; this branch carries **no fabricated composite number**, only a timestamp + "re-run Refresh Signals"). Output dicts deliberately carry no sizing/entry/stop/shares keys — structurally incapable of reading as a buy. Copy explicitly disclaims a retraction/buy signal and points to the F-39g reach funnel for the aggregate story (no numeric double-count: funnel = this-pass counts, trace = named tickers with times). **Offline contract:** renders nothing unless the recommendations-log load is known-good this session (`_rec_log_load_state` present, no error) — absence is never rendered as "nothing dropped off." **③ Pre-open note** — a single panel-level caption "prices provisional until the 9:30 ET open" shown only when `market_status()` label is `Pre-Market` (holiday/weekend/after-hours omit the "open" framing); no naive datetime. New constant `COMPOSITE_FIRMNESS_MARGIN = 3` in `constants.py` (display-only, documented in the architecture constants table). **Opus reviewer: FIX-FIRST (2 blocking, both mechanical — the new constant's doc-table row + antipattern-baseline regen; zero logic defects, all safety invariants confirmed) → SHIP.** 34 tests (`tests/test_grow_dropoff.py` + repo hygiene). |
| F-235 | **System Trust — owner-only pipeline-health diagnostic, INFORMS ONLY, changes no decision.** New `🩺 System Trust` page (RESEARCH group, after `🔬 Model Lab`), hidden from a read-only viewer via the same `db.is_readonly()` nav filter (`_OWNER_ONLY_PAGES`) that hides Model Lab. Answers one question — *can I trust what the app told me today?* — via four **pull-based, render-time, never-raising** checks in `stock_analyzer/system_health.py` (nothing depends on its own background job): **① Cron liveness** — reads the new `cron_heartbeat` table (§6.32), written at the end of every `cron_runner.main()` lane invocation; a lane with no heartbeat row yet reads **"unknown" (⚪), never "down"** (silence right after ship, or before a lane's first run, is not a fault), a stale-but-present timestamp is amber, and a `status='failed'` row is red. **② Data-store existence + freshness (the "DDL-catcher")** — probes an inventory of the stores each cron lane writes; a **provably-missing table reads red/"down"** (the exact class of the 2026-08-07 `model_predictions` unapplied-DDL silent failure that motivated this feature — detected by matching the PostgREST/Postgres "relation does not exist" markers, distinguished from a transient error which reads "unknown"); unconditional-daily stores (`daily_regime`/`daily_snapshots`/`model_predictions`/`scanner_cache`) also get a trading-day- and time-of-day-aware freshness check (stale → amber); conditionally-written stores (e.g. `exit_signals`, only written when deterioration exists) are **existence-only** (absence is legitimate, never flagged). **③ Provider health** — session-scoped `api_health` per provider (Finnhub/Yahoo/FMP/Supabase/FRED); zero calls this session → "unknown", not degraded. **④ In-session caches** — which `session_state` producer caches populated this run (read via `util.get_or_offline` so an offline `None` is preserved). A **top-of-Home degraded-only chip** (owner-only, `if not db.is_readonly()`) renders a single amber/red line ONLY when a check is degraded, linking to this page — its severity rolls up **checks ①②③ only** (④ excluded so a cold Home run, before Home populates its caches further down the same run, can't false-positive the chip); invisible when healthy. **Changes NO gate, NO recommendation, NO composite, NO threshold** — a diagnostic that reports the pipeline's state, never acts on it (that hard suppression is the deliberately-deferred Phase 2, gated on its own `planner`+`reviewer` pass). The `cron_heartbeat` write is observability-only and can never affect a lane's real work or exit code (inert until its DDL is applied; `save_cron_heartbeat` swallows the pre-DDL error). Approved mockup: `docs/mockups/system-proprioception-phase1-mockup.html`. **Lane inventory (`system_health._LANES`) — updated 2026-08-17 (F-244):** seven lanes are graded — `premarket`, `scan`, `intraday`, `eod`, `broker` (daily) and `thesis`, `maintenance` (weekly, `_WEEKLY_LANE_OK_DAYS` = 8). Any new `cron_runner` lane MUST be registered here or it writes heartbeats nobody grades — a lane that can stop firing unnoticed defeats the dead-man's-switch. `broker` graded "daily" to match `SNAPTRADE_BALANCE_STALE_HOURS`=25's implicit cadence; it legitimately shows no heartbeat at all until SnapTrade credentials are configured, which reads "unknown" (⚪), never "down" — the same non-alarm as any lane before its first run. |
| F-243 | **DB-outage honesty gate — the app refuses to render a portfolio it cannot read, and stops reporting itself healthy while blind.** Closes the two silent-wrong-state follow-ups deferred from F-239. **Defect 1 — 🩺 System Trust was GREEN over a blind app:** `system_health.check_providers` graded a provider with zero calls as `"unknown"`, which ranks 0, so `chip_severity` rolled up to `"ok"`. With no Supabase credentials *nothing ever calls Supabase*, so a credentials-missing deploy rendered a fully green System Trust while the app could read nothing. Now special-cased to **`"down"`** when `db.has_db()` is False, with a detail naming the missing variables and the consequence. Scope stated precisely: the *reachable-but-broken* case never needed help — `db.py` records `api_health` on every read, so real errors already graded the row red. `has_db()` is a **credentials** check, not reachability; the special-case is defensible only because credentials-missing provably means the app can read nothing, and the code says so to stop that drifting into "has_db() means the DB is up" — the conflation F-239 already paid for once. **Defect 2 — the `db_loaded` latch:** the startup block ran three LENIENT loaders and latched `db_loaded = True` unconditionally, so a failed first load showed an empty portfolio, an empty journal and a **fabricated default watchlist** — with no banner and no retry — for the rest of the session, *even after Supabase recovered*. Hit live during the 2026-08-15 Railway cutover. Now uses the strict `_or_none` loaders; `db_loaded` means only "the initial load succeeded", and a new `_db_load_failure` record drives the gate. **The gate refuses to render any page that would misrepresent the book** — an empty portfolio under a banner is still a wrong state, and banners get scrolled past. It is placed **after the sidebar renders**, not at the load: at the load `page` does not exist yet and nav has not drawn, so stopping there would strand the user with no route to 🩺 System Trust — the page that diagnoses this exact outage. `DB_OUTAGE_SAFE_PAGES` (🩺 System Trust, 📖 User Guide) stay reachable; both render **no portfolio state**, so neither can mislead. **Holdings is the HARD gate** (the input every decision depends on, and the probe `db.unavailable_detail()` uses); a watchlist/trades-only failure is a **SOFT** persistent warning, because the book itself is still correct. Auto-retry is bounded by `DB_RELOAD_RETRY_SEC` (30s) with an explicit retry button, so an outage cannot cost three Supabase reads on every widget interaction while still self-healing. **A third surface was aligned, not left contradicting:** the sidebar read "🟡 Local session only — configure DB to persist" off the same `has_db()`, so shipping a red chip alone would have had the app say 🔴 and 🟡 about one fault simultaneously. The banner is the only surface that **suppresses**; chip, provider row and sidebar **inform**. **The sharpest defect was one nobody set out to fix:** `db.load_watchlist()` returned `list(_DEFAULT_WATCHLIST)` with no credentials — a watchlist the user **never created, presented as theirs**. A wrong *assertion*, worse than a wrong *absence*; the app now renders `[]`. `load_watchlist()` and its seed are retained deliberately (deleting them would silently reverse the 2026-06-26 review's recorded C3 decision) but have zero production callers and are pinned by a test as a documented trap. `cron_runner._db_unavailable_detail` now delegates to `db.unavailable_detail()` so the outage **email** and the in-app **banner** explain one fault in one wording. **Changes no gate, no threshold, no recommendation** — it only decides whether a page may render at all. |
| F-242 | **Diversification roster refreshed to match its own purpose.** 🩺 System Trust check ⑤ flagged `_SECTOR_CANDIDATES` amber at 104d. All 56 names were **alive**, so — as with F-240 — a **fitness** problem, not rot. The roster names the tickers the app *asserts* as a sector's representatives when suggesting an ADD to **reduce concentration**, and three sectors seeded that with names that **add** idiosyncratic risk: Clean Energy median market cap **$17.3B** (ENPH $5.3B, BEP $10.4B), EV & Auto carrying **LCID at $2.5B**, AI & Data carrying pre-revenue **IONQ $18.7B**. **The scope finding that made this more than a list edit:** `diversifying_candidate_pool` unions the roster *first* with the sector's discovery bucket and truncates at `DIVERSIFY_SCAN_CAP` (10), so the roster only ever fills the leading slots — the live Clean Energy pool contained **SEDG $2.0B, RUN $2.4B, PLUG $3.2B** regardless of roster contents. A roster-only change would have swapped which *bucket* names got cut and left the defect live, so the three micro-caps were removed from `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE` too — justified by **that file's own stated rule** ("avoid thin micro-caps where a 10% move is noise, not signal"), not a new policy. `BE` ($67.7B) was deliberately **kept**: the plan proposed removing it and a market-cap check showed it is not sub-scale. **Stated because the pool did not get strictly cleaner:** removing three names advances later ones past `DIVERSIFY_SCAN_CAP`, so `VST` — previously cut — is now a pool candidate. Accepted rather than papered over: it is a pool candidate, not a roster name the app *asserts* as a representative, and `_SECTOR_PROFILES["Clean Energy"]`'s correlation is unchanged from before, so no claim the app makes has moved. A genuine exclusion would need a per-sector deny-list inside `diversifying_candidate_pool` — not an ordering trick, which is what a first attempt tried and which instead promoted `CEG`/`VST` *into* the pool. **Clean Energy deliberately still means RENEWABLES.** A first attempt re-seeded it to regulated utilities (DUK/SO/D/AEP) and was **reverted before shipping** when review traced that the string `"Clean Energy"` is a *key* in three other tables: `earnings_advisor._SECTOR_WATCH` would have told the user to watch "module costs / IRA subsidy commentary" before **Duke Energy's** print, `SECTOR_ETF` would have benchmarked a regulated utility against **ICLN**, and `stress_test._SECTOR_SHOCKS` would have shocked it **−70%** in the 2008 scenario. Utilities need their **own** sector (~13 policy values), not a borrowed label — recorded so the relabel isn't retried. Roster is now `NEE, FSLR, BE` (median ~$68B). `_SECTOR_PROFILES["Clean Energy"]` corr **and** `why` are therefore **unchanged** — "policy/subsidy driven" still describes what's in it. **TSLA dropped from EV & Auto** (user-approved): it trades on the AI/robotaxi narrative, not the auto cycle, so its true correlation to a growth-tech book far exceeds the sector's stated 0.40 — it remains in `SECTOR_UNIVERSE`, `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE` and `TICKER_SECTORS`, so it stays fully discoverable and correctly classified when held; it simply stops being offered **as a diversifier**. RIVN/LCID dropped as cash-burning single-product manufacturers; `TM`/`RACE` added because EV & Auto has no discovery-bucket mapping, so its roster *is* the whole pool. All 8 additions were pre-checked for fundamentals coverage (4/4 core metrics against `FUNDAMENTALS_GATE_MIN_METRICS`=1) — an unscoreable suggestion renders "score unavailable", which is honest but useless. Three sectors with no discovery mapping widened for the same reason (Consumer Tech +BKNG/UBER, AI & Cloud +NOW, Enterprise Tech +CSCO/ACN); note those three are **not** in `_DIVERSIFYING_SECTORS`, so they feed only the Rebalancer redeploy path, never the ADD card. AI & Data deliberately left at **3** names rather than inventing a fourth. Net 56 → 58. **A live instance of the F-240 held-path bug was found sitting in this roster:** `F`, `GM` and `LCID` had **no `TICKER_SECTORS` entry** despite being names the app can suggest buying — a bought suggestion would have resolved to the raw provider GICS string, invisible to the held-side macro exposure math. Fixed and locked by two invariants (every roster ticker has a curated sector; its value equals its roster key). Also guarded the `if not candidates: continue` path that would silently suppress a sector's ADD card. **Changes which names are suggested, never the trigger that decides a sector is under-weight.** |
| F-241 | **Weekly ticker-liveness sweep — the machine half of reference-data upkeep.** F-238's shelf clock measures a roster's AGE, which structurally cannot detect ticker ROT: on 2026-08-16 `CFLT` was found delisted inside `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE` while that table read **green** at 79d. New `stock_analyzer/ticker_liveness.py` runs as sub-job ⓪ of the Saturday `maintenance` cron lane and checks the ~230-name union of all three curated rosters. **Two-stage by design:** one batched fetch screens the whole roster; if fewer than `TICKER_LIVENESS_MIN_BATCH_HEALTH_PCT` (90%) of names resolve, it returns `"inconclusive"` and reports **no** dead-ticker verdict, because the false positive being defended against — a rate-limited or down provider — hits the entire batch at once and is therefore measurable inside a single run. Only above the floor do the handful of suspects escalate individually through the multi-source Finnhub→yfinance→FMP layer; a miss across **every** provider is the semantic "unknown symbol". Yahoo's literal 404 is deliberately **not** parsed (yfinance swallows it, and reaching it directly means depending on an unofficial crumb-gated endpoint). Confirmation is in **space** (across providers, one run) rather than in **time** (across weeks) — the latter would need persistence, coupling a roster-rot check to the very DB whose outage F-239 addressed, and would delay a true finding by a week. **Ordering is load-bearing:** ⓪ runs BEFORE the DB sub-jobs, which can return early on a Supabase outage — a check needing no DB must not be starved by one. **A dead ticker is a CURATION CHORE, never a lane failure:** it emails via a separate informational message and never touches `failures`/`rc`/`_notify_failure`, because a red maintenance heartbeat must keep meaning "the lane died". Only an exception *in the check itself* fails the lane. **Emails only on a finding** — never a weekly all-clear (the heartbeat already proves the lane ran), but `"inconclusive"` and the offline sentinel DO email, stating plainly that there is no verdict and why: silence-because-degraded is a wrong state, not health. `reference_shelf.shelf_status()` rides along with a severity split — an *expired* (`down`) table emails on its own; merely-aging (`warn`) rows are appended only when an email is already going out. **Changes NO gate, NO recommendation, NO composite, NO threshold.** |
| F-240 | **Scan-universe refresh + a live macro-gate hole closed.** 🩺 System Trust check ⑤ flagged `SECTOR_UNIVERSE` amber at 103d (shelf life 90). All 73 existing names were verified **alive** against live market data, so nothing was removed — the amber was age, not rot. **15 large-caps added (73 → 88, 12 → 14 buckets)**, selected on market cap / liquidity / **sector coverage**, explicitly NOT on trailing momentum: `_quick_score` awards 22 pts to RSI<40, so the net exists to catch quality names when they are *cheap*, and dropping a laggard would delete it precisely when it should be found (equally, adding names *because* they have already run is momentum-chasing at the roster level). Two new buckets — `Industrials` (CAT/GE/GEV) and `Communications` (T/VZ/TMUS) — close the only sectors with no representation at all; `Healthcare & Biotech` += JNJ/UNH/MRK/TMO, `Financials & Fintech` += BAC/WFC/MS/SCHW, `Consumer Staples & Retail` += HD. Tech share **53% → 44%**, correcting an incoherence where the Diversification Advisor recommended out of tech while the daily buy net was majority-tech (`Communications` in particular matched the trio `_SECTOR_CANDIDATES` **already** recommended but the scanner could not score). GOOG deliberately excluded: `scan_sectors` dedups by **symbol, not issuer**, so both Alphabet share classes would compete for the fixed 12-slot finalist window and double-count in any sector-weight math. **The widening is net-TIGHTENING**, not loosening — all 15 were already reachable via the `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE` **mover** path, which is explicitly *not* subject to the curated momentum bar or the flat-day verdict gate; moving them into `SECTOR_UNIVERSE` removes that bypass. Every downstream gate is per-candidate and universe-size-independent, so `COMPOSITE_BUY` holds the bar constant. **The defect found en route:** `daily_briefing` resolves a candidate's sector via `resolve_sector(ticker, <bucket label>)` then tests `sector in _macro_blocked_sectors`, so a sector label absent from `macro_calendar._SECTOR_IMPACT` could **never be macro-suppressed** — failing OPEN, silently, with no banner. **13 of 73 scan names were in that hole** (all of Enterprise Tech, all of Consumer Staples & Retail, plus BA, RIVN, PYPL), as was the held name SPCX. BA is the sharpest case: LMT/RTX/NOC/GD were suppressible and BA was not, from the same bucket, purely by omission. Closed by four new `_SECTOR_IMPACT` rows (Industrials, Communications, Consumer Staples & Retail, Enterprise Tech — severities user-approved) plus explicit `TICKER_SECTORS` entries so the **pick path** (bucket label) and the **held path** (provider GICS string, e.g. `"Communication Services"` for VZ) resolve identically. Now **0 of 88** unblockable, asserted by a test whose exception allowlist is deliberately **empty** so the next hole fails immediately instead of being recorded as debt. **Scope stated precisely, because the absolute version of this claim is what let the hole be forgotten once already:** closed for the pick path (bucket label) and for every curated `TICKER_SECTORS` value. It is NOT closed for a **held** ticker with no curated entry — `resolve_sector` returns the raw provider GICS string there ("Technology", "Financial Services"), which `_SECTOR_IMPACT` doesn't know, so such a holding drops out of the held-side macro **exposure** sum and *under*-counts a pre-event trim. That fails in the calm direction (a trim under-fires; it can never manufacture a suppression), and the standing rule — add a `TICKER_SECTORS` entry when opening a position in an uncurated name — is recorded at the map itself. **A second live hole, found by the review of this very change and fixed in it:** the keyed macro categories score **ragged** sector sets, and `Employment` — which carries Non-Farm Payrolls at HIGH — was missing `Cybersecurity` entirely, so all 7 cyber names (PANW/CRWD/ZS/NET/FTNT/OKTA/S) were unsuppressible ahead of **every payrolls print**. Set to 2 (user-approved), matching that sector's own Inflation score and its AI & Cloud / AI & Data cluster peers. The remaining ragged entries (`Consumer` omits 2, `Activity` omits 4) are genuinely inert — `Consumer` carries only a MEDIUM event, `Activity` has no `_STATIC` rows at all, and both gate sites filter to HIGH — so they are pinned by an allowlisted test that also fails if the allowlist ever outlives its debt. The lesson recorded with them: **check a category's actual `_STATIC` impact levels before calling it inert**, since the first pass at this filed the live Employment gap alongside the dead ones. Also: delisted `CFLT` removed from `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE`; `as_of` re-stamped and the rule amended so a documented "no change needed" review **earns** a date — otherwise churning a roster is the only way to clear amber, which would degrade the very tables the check protects. |
| F-239 | **Database-outage alert — a cron lane that cannot reach Supabase EMAILS the owner instead of failing silently.** Fixes a live defect: `db.load_holdings()` collapsed a failed read into an empty DataFrame, so `headless_alert_engine._build_context` could not distinguish "Supabase unreachable" from "the owner holds nothing". Every lane logged one line, returned 0, and `main()` recorded a **healthy** heartbeat — an outage was indistinguishable from a quiet day. Worst case: the pre-market **protective** lane (stop breaches, deterioration EXIT, risk-off trims) silently not running during a selloff while 🩺 System Trust showed it green. The nastiest variant is a *partial* outage (only `holdings` unreadable — a dropped RLS policy, a PGRST205 schema-cache miss): there the heartbeat write succeeds, so the green row is genuinely fresh. **Fix at source:** new `db.load_holdings_or_none()` returns `None` when the table is unreadable and an empty frame when it is genuinely empty (same contract as `load_recommendations_or_none`); `load_holdings()` keeps its lenient contract for the interactive app. `_build_context` now carries a `reason` — `db_unavailable` / `no_holdings` / `no_bundles` / `empty_port_df` — propagated into all three `compute_*` payloads. **All 8 cron lanes** branch on it (premarket/eod/scan/thesis via `reason`; intraday/debrief/monthly via a `_db_unavailable_detail()` probe that re-reads *holdings* specifically, so a partial outage can't hide behind a healthy `select 1`; maintenance via both). On detection the lane emails via `notify.render_db_outage_email` (pure string building — **no Supabase in the notification path**, which is the whole premise), records `status="failed"`, and **returns 1** so Railway's own run list says so too. **A provider outage (`no_bundles`) never sends the database email** — different fault, different fix. **False-positive discipline is the invariant that matters most:** an owner who genuinely holds nothing must never receive this, or they would mute it and the safety net would die; the `db_unavailable`/`no_holdings` split is exact at all 8 sites and locked by tests. Deliberately **no retry-before-declaring** threshold: if Supabase was unreachable when the protective scan needed it, the scan did not run — a retry that "recovers" would suppress the email while the lane still produced nothing. |
| F-238 | **Reference-data shelf life — 🩺 System Trust check ⑤, AWARENESS ONLY, deliberately OFF the Home chip.** The app carries hand-maintained static reference tables that drift **silently**: a stale ticker universe never errors, it just quietly stops surfacing names. A 2026-08-15 audit found `SECTOR_UNIVERSE` (then ~73 names, scanned **daily**; 88 since F-240) unrefreshed since 2026-05-05 with **no recorded date at all**, and the same for `DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE`. New registry `stock_analyzer/reference_shelf.py` records six tables with two staleness mechanics: **KIND_AS_OF** (membership/value tables — stale when age exceeds `REFERENCE_SHELF_LIFE_DAYS`; can only ever reach amber, never red, because it's a chore not an outage) and **KIND_HORIZON** (forward-dated tables that *run out* — stale when runway drops below `REFERENCE_HORIZON_MIN_DAYS`, red once actually expired). Horizons are always **derived from the table itself**, never hand-written, so extending the table clears the warning with no second thing to update; the macro-calendar horizon is the **earliest per-event-series expiry**, not the global max, so one freshly-extended series can't mask five expiring ones. `as_of` dates are the **honest** last-refresh dates from git history (specifically the last *deliberate curation* — a mechanical ticker rename doesn't reset the clock), not the registry's creation date, so `sector_universe` **and** `sector_candidates` (both last curated 2026-05-05, 102d) read amber on day one, which was true — both were refreshed on 2026-08-16/17 (F-240/F-242) and now read green. A third row, `macro_event_calendar`, is also amber but for the unrelated horizon reason: its GDP series expires 2026-10-29 and BEA has not yet published 2027 dates. All four `REFERENCE_SHELF_LIFE_DAYS` entries were set to 90 on 2026-08-15 (three tightened from 180 the same day they shipped). Rendered on 🩺 System Trust with the **consequence** stated, never a bare age. **Excluded from the Home status chip and from `n_warn`/`n_down` by design:** unlike every other check (transient faults), this is a *standing condition* that stays amber for weeks until a human does manual work — rolling it in would park a permanent amber the user can't clear and train them to ignore the chip that also reports dead cron lanes. Likewise it never touches the maintenance lane's failure list or return code, so the cron dead-man's-switch can't report "a lane died" because a ticker list is old. Both invariants are locked by tests. **Changes NO gate, NO recommendation, NO composite, NO threshold.** |

### 3.1l Broker Sync (F-244)

New "⚡ Broker Sync" section on the 💰 Account page — Robinhood synced via
SnapTrade, a REST brokerage-aggregator middleware (replaces the earlier
Robinhood MCP path, rejected for its interactive-auth-only constraint — no
headless cron possible). See `docs/plans/snaptrade-broker-integration.md`.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-244 | **SnapTrade broker sync (Robinhood) — balance sync, live position drift, and a review-queue transaction import. Supplements, never replaces, manual Trade Journal entry and the F-87 CSV import.** Three capabilities: **(1) Balance sync** — the new `broker` cron lane (7th Railway Cron Job service, alongside premarket/scan/intraday/eod/thesis/maintenance) writes `account_cash` from SnapTrade's live balance, same signed convention as the existing manual entry (negative = margin debit). A `SNAPTRADE_BALANCE_STALE_HOURS`=25 staleness check warns on the Account page if the last sync is stale. **(2) Position drift** — deliberately **NOT** cron-written or cached: `stock_analyzer/broker_sync.diff_positions()` runs live on every Account-page render, diffing SnapTrade's live positions against the app's **raw** `holdings_df` (not the display-truncated, `int()`-cast `port_df` — a 2026-08-17 review finding: Robinhood's common fractional-share holdings would otherwise show a permanent phantom mismatch). Three buckets (Robinhood-only / App-only / qty-mismatch beyond `BROKER_DRIFT_SHARE_TOL`=0.001) — **awareness only, never gates, never auto-corrects `trades`.** **(3) Transaction import — Option A flow (mockup-approved: `docs/mockups/broker-sync-trade-log-flow.html`).** The `broker` cron classifies fetched SnapTrade activities via `broker_sync.classify_transactions()` into: BUY/SELL candidates → `snaptrade_pending_imports`, a **review queue that never auto-writes `trades`** (imported data lacks the app's required decision documentation — thesis, pre-mortem, decision context, trigger type — and the operating posture requires every position to carry one); dividend/interest/fee → `snaptrade_income_events` (**display/trend ONLY** — deliberately never read by `stock_analyzer.account`'s Modified Dietz return math, since a dividend credit is performance, not a contribution, and routing it through `account_flows` would silently inflate net-contributed-capital and suppress reported growth%); only CONTRIBUTION/WITHDRAWAL activities reach `account_flows`. **Dedup is two-tier:** Tier 1 exact-match on a new `trades.broker_txn_id` column (nullable, `NULLS DISTINCT` unique index — existing manual/CSV rows are unaffected); Tier 2 content-match (same `(date, ticker, action, shares, price)` key F-87's CSV import already uses) backfills `broker_txn_id` onto a pre-existing un-linked row instead of creating a duplicate pending import. **The "Log This Trade →" flow, on 📒 Trade Journal:** clicking a pending-import row's button pre-fills the **existing** Log Trade form (not a parallel form) via a `_tj_broker_prefill` session dict — ticker/action/shares/price/date are **locked** (`disabled=True`), the three interactive sanity-check overrides (price/ticker/sell-without-holding) are force-bypassed with an explanatory caption (broker-confirmed data is ground truth, same precedent as F-87's CSV import), and a purple "🔮 Retrospective Pre-Mortem" banner reframes — but does **not waive** — the existing pre-mortem hard gate for BUY ("what would have made you wrong **at the moment you decided**, not what you know now"). SELL gets the form's existing lighter path (no pre-mortem — that gate is structurally BUY-only already). Decision context is auto-captured at **save time**, with a caption acknowledging the honest gap between the broker's actual fill time and the click. A dedicated "✗ Not now — unlock the form" escape hatch (plus both existing Cancel handlers) fully releases the lock — added after a 2026-08-17 review found the original design had no way out of a locked form short of completing that exact trade. On save, the record gains `broker_txn_id`/`traded_at` (broker's real fill date, not the manual-entry default of DB `now()`) and the matching `snaptrade_pending_imports` row flips to `status='logged'`. **Credentials:** `SNAPTRADE_CLIENT_ID`/`SNAPTRADE_CONSUMER_KEY` are the **only** two Railway environment variables, never stored in the app's DB. **Corrected 2026-08-18** (post-ship, during live setup): the original build wrongly assumed a Commercial SnapTrade API key (register a `userId`, get a one-time `userSecret`, `SNAPTRADE_USER_ID`/`SNAPTRADE_USER_SECRET` as a second credential pair). The user's actual account is a **Personal** SnapTrade key (free, single-account — the correct tier for this app), confirmed against SnapTrade's own Dashboard, which states: *"Personal accounts do not register a SnapTrade user — skip registerUser, and do not send userId or userSecret."* Every SnapTrade call, and `has_snaptrade()`, now uses only the two credentials above; the Account page's setup flow is a single "Connect Robinhood" button (no one-time-secret-display step). New tables (all `FOR ALL TO service_role` RLS): `snaptrade_config` (single-row connection state), `snaptrade_pending_imports`, `snaptrade_income_events`; ships inert (loads return `None`/`[]`, saves no-op) until the DDL is applied manually via the Supabase dashboard, same convention as every other optional table in this app. **Opus reviewer, per chunk:** `stock_analyzer/broker_sync.py` SHIP (0 blocking); `stock_analyzer/db.py` FIX-FIRST→fixed (3 blocking: a unique-violation on `broker_txn_id` was falling into the wrong retry path and silently duplicating trades; the pending-imports upsert could clobber a `logged`/`dismissed` row's status back to `pending` on a re-sync; three writers were missing the `is_readonly()` viewer guard); `cron_runner.py`'s 7th `broker` lane SHIP (0 blocking); `app.py`'s UI FIX-FIRST→fixed (2 blocking: the Log-This-Trade lock had no escape hatch; the live drift check diffed against `int()`-truncated shares, producing permanent false mismatches on any fractional Robinhood holding). The credential-model correction (2026-08-18) — dropping the Commercial `userId`/`userSecret` flow for the Personal-key model — was verified empirically against the live SnapTrade API rather than sent through a second full Opus pass, following the same review-economy reasoning as the `timeout=`-kwarg fix earlier that day. |

### 3.2 Today's Brief (Daily Briefing)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-20 | Show market tone header (bull / bear / flat) based on S&P 500 daily change (≥+0.5% bull, ≤-0.5% bear). Header date uses ET timezone; when market is closed, appends "data as of [last trading day]". |
| F-21 | Display date, S&P 500 %, Nasdaq %, and top 2 leading sectors by 1-week return |
| F-22 | Act Today (defense / right column): prioritised list of urgent actions — stop breaches, sell signals, critical news, macro events, risk-advisor trims. Each item carries a structured directive (what to do), why, and trigger — not a one-line label. (See F-22a/F-22b.) |
| F-22a | Act Today items are **consolidated per ticker**: a mechanical-exit signal (stop breach or sell signal) on a ticker suppresses any risk-trim card for the same ticker (you don't trim what you're exiting); multiple risk flags on one ticker merge into a single card. Consolidation logic lives in `daily_briefing._consolidate_act_today`. |
| F-22b | Each Act Today item exposes `kind` (stop_breach / sell_signal / critical_news / macro / risk), a `directive` (concrete action), `why`, and `trigger`; risk items additionally carry a `risk_flags` list. A back-compatible `reason` field is synthesised for legacy consumers. |
| F-22d | **Stop-breach live-price re-evaluation.** The Brief is memoized on portfolio composition (ticker + shares), not on price, so a `stop_breach` Act Today card can reflect yesterday's close while the intraday live price has already recovered above the stop. At each Home render, every `stop_breach` card is re-classified against `_port_df_enriched["Gap to Stop (%)"]`, which is rebuilt on live prices every render (via the `_live_prices` strip). Three outcomes: (a) **Recovered** — live price is above the stop by more than `STOP_RECOVERY_MARGIN_PCT` (0.5%) — the card is demoted from Act Today into an amber **"⚠️ Stop Breach — Resolved"** monitoring section rendered between Act Today and Monitoring, with the live price and stop annotated and an Analyze button. The Act Today badge and chip count both exclude it. (b) **Active** — live price is still at or below stop + margin — the card stays in Act Today and gains a confirmation caption: "Breach confirmed at live $X — still below stop $Y." (c) **Unavailable** — no live price data yet — the card stays in Act Today with a neutral offline note: "Live price unavailable — signal is from yesterday's close. Verify the current price before acting." The 0.5% margin prevents flickering at prices oscillating a penny around the stop. |
| F-22c | **Rebalance plan on the sector Hard-Cap-Breach card.** A `sector_concentration` Act Today card (Risk Advisor "Hard Cap Breach" / "Elevated Sector Concentration") completes the trim→redeploy loop its copy promises but previously left blank. An **opt-in, button-gated** "🔎 Rebalance plan — what to trim, where to redeploy" reveals: (a) **Trim first** — the breached sector's held names ranked by **engine composite ascending** (`risk_advisor` `trim_candidates`), so "trim your lowest-conviction names first" is concrete; names with **no computed composite are excluded** (None-preserving `score_raw`, never coerced to 0 → they can't masquerade as lowest-conviction). Each trim name shows its **basis** so a bare conviction number never proposes a trim blind: the composite's three **pillars** (`tech`/`fund`/`sentiment`, from the held bundle's `t_score`/`f_score`/`s_score`) + **recent momentum** (1wk/1mo `portfolio.trailing_return` off the bundle `df`) + a factual "conviction capped by {weakest pillar}" note. The list is also a **quantitative plan that reconciles to the headline directive**: `portfolio.trim_allocation` greedily distributes the rec's own `trim_target_dollar`/`trim_target_pp` (= `excess_dollar`/`excess_pp`, same `_gd` denominator so Σpp = the headline pp) across the conviction-ordered names — **fully exit the weakest first, partial-trim the last** — and each row shows **$ · pp · ≈shares · [full exit|partial]** plus a **directional tax flag** (🧾 realizes a gain/loss/breakeven, from the position's unrealized P&L — informs take-profit-vs-cut-laggard; not lot-level accounting). A running total (`✓ $X of ~$target · sector → SECTOR_ELEVATED`, 25%) reconciles to the target, with a rolled-up remainder past a display cap and an honest **shortfall** note when the scored names can't cover the target. **Advisory, not an order** — shares are `≈`, the $/pp are authoritative, nothing auto-executes. Framed honestly as **display-only** context: order stays composite-ascending (engine authority), small gaps are called out as within scoring noise, and the two legitimate schools when a sector MUST be cut — **trim the laggard** (momentum) vs **take profit on the runner** (rebalance) — are named so the user reconciles the engine's conviction read with their own and makes the final call. And (b) **Redeploy into** — engine-vetted buy candidates in the under-represented sectors (`redeploy_sectors`), scored and gated by the **SAME** pipeline as the Diversification ADD card (`portfolio.diversifying_candidate_pool` → `annotate_add_candidates`, gate = `COMPOSITE_BUY`, cached `_grow_composites`/`load_all`, bounded by `DIVERSIFY_SCAN_CAP`), globally re-ranked composite-first across sectors and showing the top `DIVERSIFY_DISPLAY_TOP` with a ▶ Analyze bridge. Each redeploy candidate is annotated with its **correlation to YOUR ACTUAL BOOK** (`portfolio.correlation_to_portfolio` vs `portfolio_return_series`, data-driven — supersedes the tech-heavy-assuming `_SECTOR_PROFILES` for this surface; labels `REDEPLOY_CORR_DIVERSIFIER_MAX` 0.40 / `REDEPLOY_CORR_CORRELATED_MIN` 0.70 are **display classifications, not gates**), and the lowest-correlation gate-passer is badged "🏆 cleanest diversifier." **Invariants:** the engine composite + `COMPOSITE_BUY` are the **sole ranker/gate**; correlation is a display **highlight** (never re-ranks past the composite); the analyst note (per F-154a) is **awareness-only**. **Calm-advisor (§2B):** button-gated (not an `st.expander`, whose body always executes) so no scoring/correlation compute runs until the user asks. The rebalance payload rides on the item's `risk_flag` (not the item top-level) so it survives `_consolidate_act_today`, which keeps only the primary item's keys but gathers all flags. `app.py _render_act_card`. |
| F-23a | **Sold-today suppression on Grow Today and Buy Candidates.** When a SELL is logged during the current ET session, the ticker is added to the `sold_today` set, which merges into `_act_blocked` at the top of both `_grow_today()` and `_buy_candidates()`. A just-sold ticker is suppressed from all Grow Today new-pick / add-to-winner / buy-candidate surfaces for the remainder of that session, even if its composite score is still high, preventing the anomaly where an exit call is acted on and the name immediately re-appears as a BUY. The set is computed from `trades_df` SELL rows dated today (ET), using ISO8601 datetime parsing to handle the mixed UTC-offset timestamps that result from the F-251 import-repair. |
| F-23 | Grow Today (offense / left column): market-tone-aware growth setups — new positions and add-to-winners on bull/flat days; deferral message on bear days |
| F-24 | Each Grow Today pick includes: ticker, sector, scanner score, thesis one-liner, suggested entry zone (lo–hi range), position size (shares, cost, stop) |
| F-24a | **Movers** (discovery picks from outside the curated universe — see F-60a) surface in the SAME "New Positions to Initiate" list as curated picks, not a separate developer-facing section; the user sees one action list regardless of how the ticker was sourced. Movers face the composite gate (≥65), macro gate, and act-today block like curated picks, but get their OWN allowance (`MOVER_MAX_PICKS`=3) separate from the curated cap, skip the curated momentum bar and the 1-per-sector diversity rule, and are **exempt from the flat-day high-conviction suppression** (a composite-Buy stock up ≥5% today is itself the clearer direction the flat-day caution waits for). They still respect bear-day risk-off (no Grow Today processing at all on bear days). |
| F-25 | Review Before Close (defense / right column, under Act Today): approaching stops, near-term earnings, weakening large positions. Each item issues a **quantitative directive** — not prose. Item types: WATCH, TIGHTEN_ONLY (raise stop to current − 1.5×ATR), TRIM_AND_TIGHTEN (profit-lock 25% of position + tighten stop when P&L ≥ 25%), TRIM_TO_TARGET (earnings-overweight → trim to 10%; weak-large → trim to 8%), PROTECTIVE_TRIM (macro-affected sector → reduce lowest-conviction holding by 5pp). Each carries `headline`, `action`, `why`, `trigger`. |
| F-25a | **Calm-advisor split (§2B persona enforcement).** The defensive column is split by URGENCY into **🔴 Act Today (decisions only)** — stop_breach / sell_signal / risk / critical_news + active trims (TRIM_AND_TIGHTEN / TRIM_TO_TARGET / PROTECTIVE_TRIM) — and **👁️ Monitoring / Awareness (FYI)** — macro / WATCH / TIGHTEN_ONLY + anything unrecognised (fail-to-calm). Borderlines are policy flags (`BUCKET_CRITICAL_NEWS_IS_ACT`=True, `BUCKET_TIGHTEN_ONLY_IS_ACT`=False). When the Act bucket is empty, a prominent **"✅ Nothing to act on — you're set for today · monitoring N items"** banner replaces it (derived from empty-Act; no new persistence). Logic: `decision_bucket.split_defensive`. **Risk-metric refinement:** slow-moving Risk-Advisor drags (Sharpe / beta / volatility / drawdown / tail risk — `_TUNEUP_RISK_TYPES`) are NOT same-day decisions, so they route to a separate **🔧 Portfolio Tune-up** lane (`portfolio_tuneup`) rendered below Act/Awareness, not into Act Today. Concentration breaches stay in Act (structural). |
| F-25b | **Position lifecycle + settling grace.** Each held position has a lifecycle state (`position_lifecycle.classify_position_state`: exit / at_risk / settling / winning / established) from age (oldest still-held lot via `tax_advisor._build_open_lots`), P&L, and gap-to-stop. A "settling" position (held < `POSITION_SETTLING_DAYS`) is exempted from the routine approaching-stop tighten nudge (it sits at its normal entry-to-stop distance by construction); exits and ≤3%-gap items are NEVER suppressed (danger beats age; no-journal age=None never settles). A 🌱/📈/⚠️ lifecycle badge renders on Review cards. |
| F-25c | **Signal hysteresis ("steady vs yesterday").** A Grow-Today pick whose composite moved ≤ `HYSTERESIS_COMPOSITE_DELTA` since its most-recent prior surface AND whose verdict didn't flip is marked with a calm grey "↔ Steady vs yesterday" chip (`signal_hysteresis.apply_hysteresis`, fed by a 4-day look-back over `db.load_recommendations`). Continuity reads as continuity, damping the urge to re-litigate persistent picks daily (§2B). **Annotate-only — never adds, removes, re-orders, or suppresses a pick** (cannot fight the buy gates); skipped under the AM lock and when offline. |
| F-25d | **Exit-discipline deterioration ladder (`exit_advisor.py`).** Idiosyncratic (name-specific) deterioration is graded into WATCH / TRIM / EXIT off **drawdown-from-peak** (peak re-anchored on material adds — Phase 1.1), gated by a broken trend (close < SMA50) and confirmation (2 of last 3 sessions; a deep EXIT skips confirmation). Floors are the larger of a fixed % (6 / 8 / 12) and an ATR-scaled level (capped 14 / 20), so volatility cannot quietly disable the stop; a TRIM escalates to EXIT on an unrealized loss past `DETERIORATION_EXIT_DOLLAR_LOSS` ($250). Renders as a Review item with the lifecycle badge. Motivated by a trade-log loss review. |
| F-25e | **Risk-off market-wide de-risk overlay (exit-discipline Phase 2).** When the **book is fragile** (F-09a) AND the regime is risk-off (SPY below its 200-DMA OR VIX ≥ 25 — Faber trend rule + high-vol cut), surface a single `risk_off_derisk` action that trims the **top-3 beta contributors** (β × weight, only genuinely high-β names with β ≥ 1.2) by a modest 25% (or tighten their stops instead) — it does not touch the whole book or defensives. Computed AFTER the act/review buckets and **single-surfaced**: tickers already flagged by an idiosyncratic exit (F-25d) or other reduce action are excluded so a name is never double-asked. Ranked highest-urgency in the defensive column. |
| F-25f | **Brief tone-staleness reconciliation (annotate-only).** Pre-market, when the tone banner is stale (computed from the last scanner run, e.g. Friday's close — `not is_open AND last_close != today`) AND live pre-market futures materially contradict it (tone bear + futures bull, or tone bull + futures bear; reuses the existing futures bull/bear direction, no new threshold), append an amber reconciliation note to the tone banner, e.g. "Reflects Fri Jun 20 close — live futures currently higher (ES +0.6%); refresh after the open." **Annotate only — pre-market futures NEVER flip the tone, gates, or recommendations.** Resolves a red "Protect Mode" banner silently sitting above green live futures. |
| F-25g | **Action Log — Phase B "Log this trim" (closes the recommend→act→log loop for trims).** Each Review trim card (`TRIM_TO_TARGET` / `TRIM_AND_TIGHTEN` / `PROTECTIVE_TRIM`) carries a one-click "📒 Log this trim" button that pre-fills the Trade Journal SELL form (trim ticker + suggested shares + decision context, `followed_intent="yes"`) so an executed trim is logged without leaving the Brief; once logged, holdings recompute and the recommendation stops re-firing. The macro `PROTECTIVE_TRIM` case correctly targets `action.trim_ticker` (the weakest sector holding; the card's own `ticker` is `None`) — the trim subject is resolved before the button gate. Button keys use a per-render card index so two macro cards trimming the same name can't collide. (Extends F-18's manual-stop loop from stops to trims.) |
| F-25h | **Act Today — "Log this sell" on mechanical, fully-quantified exit cards.** A `kind="stop_breach"` card (`Gap to Stop (%) ≤ 0`) or a `kind="deterioration_exit"` card (`exit_advisor` EXIT tier — a full-position drawdown/trend-break exit) carries a one-click "📒 Log this sell (N sh TICKER)" button alongside "▶ Analyze", pre-filling the Trade Journal SELL form (full held share count + decision context, `followed_intent="yes"`) so the user isn't forced through an Analysis detour to act on a signal the engine already fully quantified. Analyze stays available alongside it (not replaced) in case the user wants to reconsider before executing. Deliberately excludes `sell_signal` and the sibling `deterioration_trim` tier — `exit_advisor` computes a full-exit share count for EXIT but has no equivalent computed quantity for TRIM (its directive is qualitative, "trim into the weakness"), so a button there would need a fabricated share count. `daily_briefing._act_today` (adds `shares` to the item — `None` on TRIM items so the button can't render for them), `app.py _render_act_card`. |
| F-26 | Buy Candidates (offense / left column, under Grow Today; titled "More Buy Candidates"): scanner picks cross-referenced across 5 layers (Momentum, Composite, News, Earnings, Revisions) with a confidence verdict (Confirmed / Mixed / Conflicted / Caution / Unverified). `add_winner` rows carry no Momentum layer by construction (no scanner read exists for a held position; the layer is omitted rather than fabricated); their reconciliation verdict's summary sentence is honest about having a single analytical source, not two (no claim that "momentum and composite agree" when both numbers came from the same composite score). Earnings risk is checked against a unified `earnings_lookup` derived from held positions AND pre-fetched composites, so non-held new picks are also screened. |
| F-26a | Buy Candidates must be **de-duplicated against everything already shown in Grow Today** — both the shown lists (new picks, add-to-winners, composite-skipped, macro-blocked, composite-unavailable) AND the suppressed-add buckets (deterioration-blocked, cooldown, sector-blocked, concentration-blocked, risk-blocked) — so the same ticker never appears in both lists on the same screen. The suppressed-add buckets matter because `_buy_candidates` *annotates* (does not suppress) a WATCH name, so without deduping `deterioration_blocked_adds` a WATCH add would leak into Buy Candidates as "ADD — Winning Position" right below its suppression banner (the exact double-surface contradiction). The other four are defensive (they don't leak today only because `_buy_candidates` independently skips them). |
| F-26b | The Brief renders as a two-column **offense / defense** layout: left column = Grow Today + More Buy Candidates (where to deploy capital); right column = Act Today + Review Before Close (what to protect). Section chips must stay within their column and not bleed across the page when the opposite column is empty. |
| F-26c | **Add-to-winner Brief-wide coordination fix (2026-07-30).** Both `_grow_today`'s `add_positions` block and `_buy_candidates`'s own separate `add_winner` block (F-26 — the two are independently implemented, not shared code) previously cross-checked ONLY `act_today` before recommending an add to a held Strong Buy position, via a naive `_ai.get("ticker")` loop. A **review-origin** reduce call (`review_list`'s `TRIM_TO_TARGET` — earnings-overweight or weak-large-position trim) never suppressed either add-to-winner surface, so a same-day "add to X" could sit next to (or precede) a same-week "trim X" with no cross-check — the identical class of gap found in the Orphan Conviction fix (F-194) above, in two more places. Both blocks now use `decision_bucket.all_flagged_tickers(act_today, review_list)` — the canonical broad "any card, either lane" set (also used by the risk-off de-risk exclusion, 2026-07-29 audit H6) — which additionally resolves macro `ticker=None`/`action.trim_ticker` card shapes a naive ticker lookup would miss. |
| F-27 | Each Buy Candidate card shows: ticker, sector, score, verdict badge (amber for Verify, not blue), technical summary, conflicts and agreed signals |
| F-28 | Quick Research panel: user enters any ticker (e.g. from external news), app returns up to 5-bullet actionable summary — signal, momentum, entry timing, key context, and (when portfolio_ctx is supplied) portfolio-fit including sector-level Act Today awareness |
| F-29 | Entry timing verdicts (boundaries inclusive): High Risk — Avoid Chasing (RSI≥80 or 1D≥15% or 5D≥25%), Wait for Pullback (RSI≥68 or 1D≥5% or 5D≥12%), Oversold — Potential Entry (RSI≤35), Normal Entry Conditions otherwise |
| F-30 | All Analyze buttons navigate to Stock Analysis with a Back button returning to Today's Brief |
| F-31 | On flat market days, Grow Today must output confirmed-verdict picks before unverified-verdict picks so highest-conviction ideas lead |
| F-32 | Pre-Market Intel panel: visible 4:00–9:29 AM ET weekdays only; appears at top of Today's Brief |
| F-33 | Pre-Market Intel shows US futures (S&P 500, Nasdaq 100, Dow, Russell 2000) with price and % change vs prior close |
| F-34 | Pre-Market Intel shows overnight % change for major global indices (Nikkei, Hang Seng, DAX, FTSE, CAC 40) |
| F-35 | Pre-Market Intel shows pre-market movers (≥0.5% change) for all held positions and watchlist tickers; held positions are visually distinguished. Each mover is cross-checked against an independent price source (`data.crosscheck_price`); on disagreement beyond tolerance the mover is still shown (never silently filtered) but tagged with an "⚠ unverified" badge, a summary caption counts how many are flagged, and Pre-Market Stance's narrative is instructed not to assert an unverified mover's direction as settled fact. Added 2026-07-30 after an incident where the AI narrative asserted a mover's move in the wrong direction with nothing to catch it. |
| F-36 | Pre-Market Intel shows today's HIGH and MEDIUM impact economic events as "catalysts" |
| F-37 | Pre-market expected open tone (bull/bear/flat) is derived from ES=F futures % change (≥+0.4% bull, ≤-0.4% bear) |
| F-37a | **Catalyst Watch** — its OWN nav page (🔔 Catalyst Watch, before Economic Calendar; viewable any time, not gated to pre/post-market). TWO tiers: (1) **Your Holdings — Earnings** = the full per-position detail + Pre-Earnings Playbook (rendered from the canonical port_df/held_data the Home brief built, via `_render_holdings_earnings`). The held-name earnings DATE comes from the bundle (`held_data["earnings"]`); when that is missing (a yfinance hiccup — the date is a different yfinance endpoint than `.info`, so it can be blank even when fundamentals are present), it is backfilled for DISPLAY ONLY via `_cached_held_earnings_dates`, which uses the SAME two-source path as the Radar tier: the FMP market-wide calendar first, then a per-name yfinance fallback (`fetch_next_earnings`) for names the (sparse, on free tier) FMP calendar didn't cover — so held names get equal earnings coverage to watchlist/universe names. This is awareness-only and never feeds a gate. The underlying bundle date itself is also repaired at the data boundary: `orchestrator.get_bundle` backfills the earnings date from FMP's light per-ticker accessor independently of whether `.info` was sparse, so the earnings-proximity gates (which read the bundle date) stay armed; (2) **On Your Radar — Watchlist & Universe** = upcoming earnings within `CATALYST_WATCH_WINDOW_DAYS` (7) for NON-held tracked names, grouped Today/Tomorrow/Next-7d, each row ticker · sector (🔥 when leading) · date · before-open/after-close · watchlist/universe chip. Both tiers have a ticker→Analysis "Analyze" control. **Awareness only** — must NOT recommend initiating into earnings (proximity gates still suppress that); removes the blind spot of a tracked name reporting unannounced (the PANW case). Radar dates: FMP market-wide calendar (one call) then a per-name yfinance fallback for names FMP didn't cover, so universe coverage doesn't depend on FMP's tier. 24h-cached. **Presented as three tabs since the 2026-07-16 I11 restructure — 📋 Holdings (tier 1) / 📡 Radar (tier 2) / 🎯 Entry Candidates (F-37b); structural only, no logic change.** |
| F-37b | **Entry Candidates (Earnings Catalyst Scanner) — third Catalyst Watch tab.** `earnings_advisor.build_earnings_catalyst_candidates()` surfaces NON-held **watchlist** names reporting earnings within 30 days that clear three filters: historical **beat rate ≥ `EARNINGS_MIN_BEAT_RATE_ENTRY`** (70%), **composite ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY`** (65), and a post-earnings **reaction that is not bearish** (bullish/mixed). Ranked by beat-rate × composite × reaction. Beat-rate/reaction are sourced from CNBC earnings context pasted via 🧠 AI Insights → Ideas Inbox → 📅 Pre-Earnings (`db.load_earnings_context_batch`); with no context pasted the tab prompts the user there. **Awareness only — never a Buy recommendation** (each candidate carries an Analyze bridge to the Analysis page). Shipped 2026-07-13 (commit 0cac9ee); Earnings Playbook Phase 3. |
| F-38 | When `build_daily_briefing()` fails, the page must render an explicit "offline" state on dependent features (notably the Watchlist) rather than silently disabling coordination gates. The user must see that gates are inactive. (Implements G-14.) |
| F-39 | Grow Today must surface a "🔍 Couldn't score N candidate(s) — data source returned an error" banner when the composite pre-fetch failed for any of the intended top picks, so the user knows the multi-factor gate did not run for those tickers. |
| F-39a | Grow Today must hard-suppress new picks in any sector with a HIGH-impact macro event scheduled within `MACRO_IMMINENT_DAYS` (3 days), and surface a "Picks Suppressed — Imminent HIGH-Impact Macro Event" banner with the affected sectors and event date. Same-day HIGH macro events (e.g. CPI, NFP, GDP — pre-market 08:30 ET releases) lift the gate once the release time has passed (primary: ET wall clock ≥ scheduled `time_et`; secondary: FRED `released` flag). FOMC ("Fed Policy") is never lifted same-day — post-announcement volatility makes it the wrong window to open new positions. (Implements G-07.) |
| F-39b | Grow Today add-to-winner blocks must suppress: (a) tickers Risk Advisor recommends trimming, (b) positions at or above the single-name ceiling (15%), (c) positions drift-overweight beyond equal-weight + tolerance, and (d) held names under an active early-deterioration WATCH. Each suppression class renders a distinct banner with the conflict reason. (Implements G-01, G-04, G-09, G-20.) |
| F-39c | Review Before Close items flagged as weak-large-positions must pull the entry thesis (most recent BUY notes) and up to two recent lessons from the Trade Journal for that ticker, rendered as an amber-bordered block below the mechanical assessment. When no journal entry exists, prompt the user to log thesis on future entries. |
| F-39d | Grow Today must hold a candidate OUT of new picks when its composite was computed without real fundamental data (`fundamentals_available == False`, i.e. < `FUNDAMENTALS_GATE_MIN_METRICS` core metrics present). Such a ticker is routed to the `composite_unavailable` bucket, never surfaced as a "new position to initiate". (Implements G-15.) |
| F-39e | Grow Today must suppress BOTH new picks and add-to-winners whose sector is at/above `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%), surfacing a "Suppressed — Sector Hard Cap" banner. This keeps the deploy-capital signal from contradicting the Risk Advisor's concurrent sector-trim recommendation (the ESTC case). (Implements G-16.) |
| F-39f | Portfolio sector classification resolves **curated `TICKER_SECTORS` → provider `.info` sector → persisted last-known sector (`sector_cache`) → `"Other"` catch-all**, so unmapped tickers carry a real sector and the "Other" bucket cannot accumulate a spurious hard-cap breach. The `sector_cache` layer exists because `.info` is the ONLY live sector source and comes back sparse on flaky provider days — without persistence, unmapped holdings collapsed to "Other" and, since the gate **excludes** "Other", **silently dropped out of sector-concentration gating** (~half the book, observed 2026-07-06). `bundle_loader` write-throughs a good `.info` sector and reads the cache when the live fetch is empty (sector is near-static, so a cached value never staleness-expires); scoring stays on raw `.info` (NOT the cache) so this resilience cannot move composite/fundamental scores. `risk_advisor` and the F-39e gate both read this same `port_df["Sector"]`, so the Act Today breach card and the Grow Today gate agree. |
| F-39g | **Reach-funnel Sankey (decision transparency).** Grow Today shows a Plotly Sankey drawing the **same real numbers as the 🔭 reach line** to scale: **screened universe** (tracked + watchlist + discovery) → **reached full composite scoring** (the finalists) → **New Positions to Initiate**, with the complements as drop nodes (*Not scored this pass* = universe − scored; the scored set splits into picks + *Composite below Buy bar* + *Other filters*). **Honest by construction:** every band is pure subtraction off the reach counts, so it always balances and never fabricates a universe — "Other filters" is the residual (scored − picks − below-bar) covering sector-cap / macro / data / flat-day-caution drops, which the suppression banners name specifically. Display-only; shown when ≥ 1 candidate reached composite scoring. |
| F-39h | **Sector elevated-band warning, not suppression (SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — SHOP whiplash fix).** Grow Today's new-pick and add-to-winner loops, AND the separate "More Buy Candidates" surface (`_buy_candidates`), now compute an `_elevated_sectors` set (weight in `[SECTOR_ELEVATED, SECTOR_CEILING)` = 25–35%) alongside the existing hard-cap `_breached_sectors` (F-39e/G-16). A pick in the elevated (not yet hard-capped) band is NOT suppressed — it carries a `sector_elevated_warning` string, rendered as an amber caption on the pick/add card — extending W-01's soft-caution policy to two surfaces that previously had no elevated-band awareness at all. **`_buy_candidates` also gained the G-16 hard-cap suppression itself in the same pass** — before this, that surface had zero sector-concentration awareness of any kind (not even the hard cap), a wider gap than Grow Today's. Root cause: the buy side only checked whether a sector was ALREADY over the hard cap; it had no signal for a sector Risk Advisor was already watching in the 25–35% band, so a buy recommended just under the cap could tip the sector over it and — same brief, hours later — that exact fresh position became the trim engine's #1 full-exit candidate (paired with G-21 on the trim side). |
| F-180 | **New-position data freshness gate.** Grow Today suppresses a pick from "New Positions to Initiate" when its underlying bundle data is stale, routing it to the `composite_unavailable` bucket → "Pending Verification" banner with a Refresh button. Two checks in `_grow_today()` in `stock_analyzer/daily_briefing.py`, inserted before the existing `fundamentals_available` gate (F-39d): (a) `_comp_data.get("stale_as_of") is not None` — the bundle was served from the Supabase `bundle_cache` fallback (potentially up to `BUNDLE_CACHE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (5) days old rather than from live providers); (b) `fund_cache_age_days > GROW_TODAY_MAX_FUND_AGE_DAYS` (2 calendar days) — fundamentals older than the allowed floor for a new-position recommendation. New constant `GROW_TODAY_MAX_FUND_AGE_DAYS = 2` added to `stock_analyzer/constants.py` after `BUNDLE_CACHE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`. `GROW_TODAY_MAX_FUND_AGE_DAYS` imported in `daily_briefing.py`. **Rationale:** new-position recommendations carry higher trust expectations than held-position display — a stale bundle cache can flip a Sell-composite ticker to a Buy score, producing a false recommendation (INTC incident, 2026-07-14: bundle fallback produced composite ≥ 65 / Buy; fresh data showed composite 32.1 / Sell 30 min later). The 2-day floor is more conservative than `FUNDAMENTALS_CACHE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (7), which governs held-position display only. `_SYNTH_SCHEMA_VER` bumped 1 → 2 to flush warm session caches. |
| F-181 | **Composite freshness auto-refresh on Home cache-HIT path.** When `_home_synth_cache` is a HIT (synthesis signature unchanged), the app now selectively refreshes stale Grow Today composite bundles before rebuilding the Brief. For each ticker in `_grow_composites`, if the bundle's `fetched_at` timestamp is older than 1 800 s (the `@st.cache_data` TTL of `load_all()`), `load_all()` is re-called — hitting the Streamlit data cache at no API cost while warm, re-fetching fresh provider data when stale. If any ticker was refreshed, `build_daily_briefing()` is re-run with the updated composites so the Grow Today card picks and Analysis page always show the same composite score. Falls back to the snapshot bundle if `load_all()` fails transiently (no pick disappears). Skips the rebuild when Setup is Locked (mirrors the auto-refresh fragment behaviour). `_grow_today_sectors_cache` is updated to reflect the rebuilt brief. The snapshot is updated in-place so the next HIT reads the refreshed `fetched_at` values. `_SYNTH_SCHEMA_VER` bumped 2 → 3 to flush warm caches that predate the new `composite_fetched_at` pick-dict field. **Root cause closed:** prior to this fix, the two-TTL mismatch between the synthesis cache (no sub-day TTL) and `load_all()` (30-min TTL) meant Grow Today could show a stale composite (e.g. 67/100 Buy) while Analysis showed fresh data (56.2/100 Hold) — a financial risk for a decision-making app. Implementation: `app.py`, injected at the end of the `_home_synth_cache` HIT block (~line 3 195). |
| F-183 | **🎯 My Edge — Benchmark Mirror (SHIPPED 2026-07-16; commit `aac34b2`).** New standalone page "🎯 My Edge" in the PORTFOLIO nav group. New module `stock_analyzer/benchmark_mirror.py`; **awareness only — zero gates, zero recommendations.** Answers: "Am I beating passive investing?" by computing a **shadow portfolio** — the same cash flows (baseline + deposits from `account_flows`) invested in SPY or QQQ at each flow date instead of individual stocks. **Modified Dietz money-weighted return** applied to both the real portfolio and the shadow portfolio so the comparison is fair regardless of when deposits were made (same formula as `account.money_weighted_return`). KPI strip shows annualised return for both (falls back to period return with "(total, <30d)" label when the period is < 30 days, the floor for reliable annualisation). Three chart views on a segmented control: (1) **Cumulative Growth** — both portfolio and shadow indexed to 100 at baseline, revealing compounding divergence over time; (2) **Cash Flow Attribution** — per-deposit gain/loss in the shadow portfolio (how well each investment tranche would have done in the benchmark); (3) **Benchmark Drawdown** — how far SPY/QQQ fell from its own peak at each date, for passive-investing context. Portfolio drawdown deliberately excluded: `daily_snapshots` tracks open equity only (shares × close price), so selling positions makes equity appear to drop even though cash was received — the figure would mislead. Requires 💰 Account baseline to be set; renders an info gate otherwise. Benchmark selector (SPY recommended / QQQ) at the top of the page, shared across all three tabs. **Risk-adjusted alpha added 2026-07-17 (commit `7148890`; next-evolution-strategy.md Phase 2):** 5th KPI card "Beta-Adj. Alpha" = `benchmark_mirror.beta_adjusted_alpha()` — Your Return minus (portfolio beta × benchmark return), isolating stock-selection skill from beta exposure (a portfolio running high beta in a rally can show strong raw alpha and near-zero beta-adjusted alpha). Portfolio beta read from the existing `_port_risk_cache` session-state cache (published by Home); shows "—" with an explanatory tooltip if that cache isn't populated yet (Home not visited this session). Pure arithmetic, no new constants, no gate/scoring touch. **Ending-value basis fix (2026-07-28):** "Your Return"'s ending value is **Total Account Value** (invested equity + net cash, matching the 💰 Account page's own `_total_value = _equity + _cash` and the baseline-setting form's "current total account value" default) — computed the same way (`db.load_account_cash()` + `_last_port_df["Market Value"].sum()`), not the bare `_portfolio_value` cache (equity only, ignores a margin debit). The prior equity-only basis compared a total-account-value baseline against an equity-only ending value, inflating the Modified Dietz gain by the full margin balance and exploding further once annualized (observed: +138,927% on an account carrying an $8,271 margin debit). Falls back to equity-only when cash isn't set this session, same as the Account page. A caption states which basis is in effect. **Annualization caveat (2026-07-28, same commit as the fix above):** shares `account.annualization_caveat()` with the Account page (F-03c) — when "Your Return" annualizes over a short window (< 90 days) and/or the account is levered, an explanatory caption clarifies that the figure is amplified, without changing the number itself. |
| F-184 | **🎯 My Edge — Workflow ROI (SHIPPED 2026-07-16; commit `aac34b2`).** Second tab on the My Edge page. New module `stock_analyzer/decision_quality.py`, function `classify_all_buys()` / `build_workflow_roi()`. Answers: "Does in-app prep lead to better trades?" Classifies every BUY trade into a **prep tier** based on signals saved before the trade date: **Full Prep** (thesis + analyst notes + earnings context all present within their look-back windows), **Thorough** (thesis + one of analyst/earnings), **Basic** (thesis only, `WORKFLOW_MIN_THESIS_LENGTH` ≥ 10 chars), **Cold Entry** (none). Look-back windows: analyst `WORKFLOW_ANALYST_LOOKBACK_DAYS` = 90 days before-only; earnings `WORKFLOW_EARNINGS_WINDOW_DAYS` = 30 days before-only (forward-looking data excluded to prevent leakage). Outcome matching uses FIFO lot replay from `tax_advisor._build_open_lots` to correctly attribute sell proceeds to the right lot. Coverage strip at top: total BUY trades / since prep tracking started / with thesis / with analyst / with earnings context. Warning banner if most trades predate prep tracking (they land in Cold Entry by definition, not because prep wasn't done). Three chart views: (1) **Tier Comparison** — bar chart of avg P&L % / alpha vs SPY / hold days by tier (zero-trade tiers show "No data yet" greyed card and are excluded from the bar chart); (2) **Trade Scatter** — one dot per closed trade, hold days × metric, color = prep tier, with a tier definition key below explaining each color; (3) **Coverage Funnel** — Plotly funnel showing how many trades had thesis / analyst research / earnings context / all three. No new gates. |
| F-185 | **🎯 My Edge — Decision Quality Timeline (SHIPPED 2026-07-16; commit `aac34b2`).** Third tab on the My Edge page. `decision_quality.build_monthly_grades()` / `build_quarterly_grades()`. Answers: "Am I improving month over month?" Each calendar period earns a composite score (0–100) and an **A–F grade**: win rate sub-score + profit factor sub-score + alpha vs SPY sub-score (when benchmark loaded, scaled by `DECISION_QUALITY_ALPHA_SCALE` = 5.0), with an overtrading penalty applied when trade pace ≥ 1.5× the 12-month average. Minimum `DECISION_QUALITY_MIN_TRADES` = 2 closed trades required to grade a period; fewer shows n/a. Grade bands: A ≥ `DECISION_QUALITY_GRADE_A` (80) / B ≥ 65 / C ≥ 50 / D ≥ 35 / F < 35 — same boundaries as Portfolio Health. KPI strip (custom HTML cards): Best Period / Latest Period grade + win rate + PF / Trend (3-period rolling avg direction). Granularity toggle (Monthly / Quarterly). Metric selector (Composite Grade / Win Rate / Profit Factor / Alpha vs SPY). Three chart views: (1) **Timeline** — bar chart with grade-band background zones and 3-period rolling avg line, lower panel shows winner/loser trade counts per period; (2) **Heatmap** — metrics × periods grid (Win Rate / Profit Factor / Alpha vs SPY / Overtrading ×) with RdYlGn scale and a definition-key strip below explaining each row; (3) **Cohort Compare** — pick any two periods, side-by-side horizontal bars with delta annotations. Awareness only; never gates or alters any recommendation. |
| F-182 | **Portfolio Construction Health Score page (SHIPPED 2026-07-15; Portfolio Dynamics enhanced 2026-07-16).** New standalone page, nav label **"🏆 Health"** (page title "Health Score"), in the PORTFOLIO nav group between 🥧 Portfolio Overview and 🎯 My Edge — position/label corrected 2026-07-27; originally documented as "🏆 Portfolio Health" between Portfolio Allocation and Risk Analysis, which had drifted from the actual nav (My Edge shipped later and now sits between Health and Risk Analysis). Pure-logic module `stock_analyzer/portfolio_health.py`; **awareness only — never gates or alters any recommendation.** Two public functions: `compute_health_score()` and `compute_portfolio_dynamics()`. **Five construction sub-scores (0–100 each), averaged to an overall A–F grade:** (1) Concentration — three-zone model: score is 100 below the elevated threshold (10% single-name / `SECTOR_ELEVATED` (25%) sector), decays linearly to 0 at the hard caps (`SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` (15%) / `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%)); binding = min(name, sector); (2) Sector Balance — Shannon entropy of sector weights, normalised 0–1, ×100, capped at 55 for n=2 sectors; (3) Diversification — `(1 − avg_corr) × 100` on the 0–1 equity correlation range (reads `_avg_corr_cache`); (4) Beta / Fragility — base score by fragility severity (calm 85 / caution 55 / fragile 20) minus high-beta-share penalty (≥60% → −25, ≥40% → −12); (5) Signal Integrity — % of book weight at composite ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65). Grade bands: A ≥ 80 / B ≥ 65 / C ≥ 50 / D ≥ 35 / F < 35. **Portfolio Dynamics section** (below the 5 dimension cards): interactive scatter (X = months held, Y = P&L% or annualized return, size = weight, color = verdict/sector/age cohort — user-toggled via two dropdowns); tenure cohort bar (Fresh <30d / Growing 1–6mo / Established >6mo, avg P&L% + count); engine alignment donut (BUY/HOLD/WATCH/EXIT split, Vitality % in centre); **Sleeping Capital / Working Hardest return efficiency panels** (see below). `compute_portfolio_dynamics()` reads only session state (`_port_df_enriched` + `trades_df`) — zero extra API calls. Verdict tiers: BUY ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65), HOLD ≥ `COMPOSITE_HOLD` (44), WATCH ≥ `COMPOSITE_SELL` (30), EXIT < 30. No new constants added. Session state consumed (read-only): `_port_df_enriched`, `_div_score_cache`, `_avg_corr_cache`, `_highbeta_share`, `_fragility_cache`, `_port_risk_cache`, `trades_df`. Commits: `042f4ba` → `1696471`. **2026-07-16 Portfolio Dynamics enhancements (commits `9e6ef20` → `7010d2a`):** (1) **FIFO-aware tenure** — `months_held` now uses `_build_open_lots()` from `tax_advisor.py` (replays BUY/SELL/SPLIT in FIFO order) instead of the earlier `dates.min()` across all historical BUYs; a fully-closed-and-re-entered position correctly resets the clock to the new buy date. `days_held` (exact open-lot days) added to each position dict. (2) **Period toggle** — `st.segmented_control` (key `ph_return_period`) with options Weekly / Monthly / Yearly (default Yearly) above the Sleeping Capital / Working Hardest panels. Each selection re-normalises P&L to that cadence (Weekly = `pnl × 7 / days_held`; Monthly = `pnl × 30.44 / days_held`; Yearly = `pnl × 12 / months_held`) and re-sorts both panels by the resulting rate. (3) **Loss-always-visible gate** — positions with negative P&L bypass the 15-day minimum-hold check and always surface in Sleeping Capital regardless of how recently they were entered; the 15-day gate applies only to profitable positions to avoid inflating short-hold gains by annualisation. (4) **Panel UX** — each panel header shows "N of M holdings" and the ranking criterion; negative period rates render in red (positive in green); positions held under 1 month display "< 1 mo" instead of "0 mo"; a footer caption explains the gate logic. Panel split: `ceil(n/2)` positions in Sleeping Capital, remaining (capped at 3) in Working Hardest — no ticker appears in both, no ticker is excluded to a gap. |

Page-level gate: < `PREDICTIVE_MIN_BAND_N × 2` (10) graded outcomes → info banner, `st.stop()`. **"📋 What This Means For You" synthesis panel** (above tabs): `synthesize_directives(bands, thresh, avm, conv, rtype, sec_alph, n_graded, sentiment_alignment=None)` emits 2–5 rule-based directives ranked action → caution → watch → context. threshold dotted line; n label); raw dataset expander. **Tab 2 — ⚖️ Decision Quality:** `acted_vs_missed_comparison` — side-by-side acted/missed metrics (n, avg_alpha, hit rate); verdict callout (action/caution/neutral/insufficient); grouped bar chart of acted vs missed alpha by score band. **Tab 3 — 🏷️ Signal Breakdown:** `by_conviction` and `by_rec_type_stats` — avg_alpha + hit rate by conviction tier (BUY/Strong BUY) and rec type (new_pick/add_winner/buy_candidate); bar charts + metric tiles; cells with n < 3 omitted. **Tab 4 — 🌐 Sector Alpha:** `by_sector_alpha` — ranked bar chart of avg_alpha by sector; best/worst sector callouts; sector × broad-band (< 65 / 65–74 / 75+) heatmap from `calibration_by_sector`; cells with n < 3 omitted. **Tab 5 — 🧭 Sentiment Alignment (F-179):** see F-179. **Alpha Attribution (collapsed expander below tabs):** placeholder; rendered as `st.expander("📊 Alpha Attribution — activates after 180 days of snapshots", expanded=False)` below the 5 live tabs so the feature is discoverable without occupying a tab slot that currently delivers nothing; `daily_snapshots` coverage progress bar toward 180-day activation threshold (factor attribution by sector / hold-duration / score-tier / market-regime). **UX update (2026-07-15; commits `421b0fe`, `97852d4`):** Three metric cards renamed to plain English ("Recommendations tracked" / "Outcomes available" / "Used in this analysis"). Data context strip added below metrics showing scored-outcome date range, count of recommendations excluded due to the 5-day results window, and a session-cache note. Raw dataset expander renamed "Full outcome history" with an inline caption explaining what each row represents and why recent entries are absent; table now sorted date-descending. Four user-facing directive strings in `synthesize_directives()` updated from "recs" shorthand to "signals" / "recommendations". **Panel presentation regrouped (2026-07-28, mockup-approved "Option 3 Variant" — render-layer only, `synthesize_directives()` itself unchanged):** Action-type directives render as side-by-side cards (1 full-width / 2 columns / 3-column grid wrapping past 3, count-based); Caution/Watch/non-footnote Context directives collapse into a single "📎 Also worth noting (N)" expander, closed by default; the always-on confidence footnote stays a plain caption below everything, unchanged. **2026-08-21 — the Decision Quality directive now states both sample sizes** (`"adding 8.3pp of alpha (34 acted vs 310 passed)"`). It was the only card asserting something about the *user's own judgment* with no basis on screen, while the sector and signal-breakdown cards both quote their `n`. It matters more here than elsewhere because the two sides are **structurally lopsided** — you act on a small fraction of what the engine surfaces — and `acted_vs_missed_comparison`'s floor is deliberately `acted_n < 3 **and** missed_n < 3` (pinned by a test named for that choice), so a 1-vs-300 split still classifies as a confident verdict; showing the counts is what lets a reader discount it. Text-only: the firing conditions (`edge == "acting"`, `edge_pp >= 0.5`) are untouched, and the clause is omitted entirely when a caller passes an `avm` carrying only `edge`/`edge_pp` rather than rendering `"None acted vs None passed"`. Consistent with `feedback_recommendation_transparency` — never propose an action on a bare number. **Separately noted and NOT changed:** the 8.3pp headline is partly a *mix* effect, since on the live book the acted set spans only score bands 60-79 while the passed set spans 30-84, so the passed average is dragged down by names never in contention; the per-band chart directly below it is the unconfounded read (green beat blue in 4 of 4 overlapping bands) and a proper selection-vs-mix decomposition is exactly what E2 exists to build. |
