| F-177 | **Today's Brief auto-refresh timer (SHIPPED 2026-07-13).** The "📌 Built at HH:MM ET" freshness chip's "auto-refreshes in N min / stale — click Refresh Signals to update" countdown is backed by a real mechanism, not just a label: a 60-second `st.fragment` watchdog (`app.py`) checks whether the Brief is older than `BRIEF_AUTO_REFRESH_MINUTES` (30, `constants.py`); once stale, AND the market is in premarket or regular trading hours, AND "Today's Setup" isn't Locked, AND the shared heavy-refresh cooldown isn't currently armed, it bumps a dedicated `_brief_auto_refresh_nonce` (part of the Home-page synthesis memoization signature, alongside the existing manual Refresh/Lock/Unlock nonce) and forces a full-page rerun so the Brief rebuilds. **Deliberately narrow scope to protect metered API quotas (FMP free tier, Finnhub, yfinance):** the auto-tick never calls `st.cache_data.clear()` and never re-runs the market-wide scanner — it only re-reads whatever is already sitting in the existing 30-minute `@st.cache_data` provider cache, so the incremental provider-call cost is bounded to the pre-existing cache cadence, not multiplied by the timer. The scanned candidate universe (Grow Today's discovery pool) still only refreshes via a manual "Refresh Signals" click or the ~9:45 ET cron scan — unchanged. |

### 3.3 Stock Analysis

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-40 | Analyse any ticker (not limited to held positions) |
| F-41 | Summary scorecard table: price, **Composite Score**, signal, position/entry zone, stop, base target, R:R, shares, P&L/cost, earnings. When a single ticker is analyzed, a compact verdict banner (label + composite score + rationale) renders **above** the scorecard for immediate readability; the full formula breakdown remains in the Detailed Analysis tabs below. |
| F-42 | Scorecard adapts to context: held tickers show actual shares and P&L%; non-held show entry zone and suggested entry cost |
| F-43 | Signal banner shows composite score breakdown: **Technical × 25% + Business Quality × 35% + Valuation × 30% + Sentiment × 10%**. Pillar names are spelled out in full in the formula display (not abbreviated) — Business Quality carries 35% weight and must not be misread. |
| F-43a | When fundamentals are unavailable from all sources (`fundamentals_available == False`), the Analysis page must WITHHOLD the Buy/Hold verdict — suppressing the signal banner and composite number — and render a red "Verdict withheld — fundamentals unavailable" note. The note must explain the data gap and the possible Brief↔Analysis mismatch (a ticker that appeared as a Brief new-pick when data was present), and advise re-checking / broker verification. The verdict must not be computed on the fabricated neutral-50 fundamental. (Implements G-15.) |
| F-43b | When the Analysis verdict is Buy/Strong Buy but the entry R:R is below `RR_ENTRY_MIN` (2.0), the Trade Plan must surface a caveat distinguishing stock quality (composite) from entry timing (R:R): per-share risk/reward, a suggested pullback level, and "KEEP, not an add here — user's judgment." The verdict must NOT be downgraded (Analysis is a research/judgement surface, not a hard gate; the hard R:R block is G-13 on Watchlist ENTER_NOW). |
| F-44 | Analyst upside note shown only when it reinforces signal direction (upside on Buy; suppressed on Sell and when downside on Buy) |
| F-45 | Trade Plan tab (Buy/Strong Buy): entry zone, stop loss, R:R, position sizing (shares, cost, max risk) |
| F-45a | **Held-position protect-overrides-deploy reconciliation (Trade Plan).** On the Buy/Strong-Buy Trade Plan, add-on Position Sizing is suppressed and a "not a place to add" banner shown when the held position is either (a) **stop-breached** or (b) under an active **Reduce/Exit call on the Daily Brief** (deterioration ladder / sell signal / risk-off trim / review trim). The stop-breach detector **mirrors the Brief exactly**: it compares the **live-merged price** (the same `_live_prices` snapshot the Home synthesis fed `build_portfolio_df`, merged into the Analysis `results` before the gates) against the **ratcheted/manual protective stop** `_sa_holding["Stop"]` (NOT the raw ATR `r["stop"]`), using the Brief's own `round(Gap%, 1) ≤ 0` test (precision single-sourced as `GAP_TO_STOP_ROUND_DECIMALS`) — so a ratchet-wins breach and a manual stop in the `[ATR, floor)` gap are treated identically on both surfaces. When the position has **no computable stop** (Stop Type "Stop Unavailable" → Gap-to-Stop `None`), the breach gate is **skipped**, exactly as the Brief skips it (`gap is None → continue`), so no breach banner fires on a name the Brief is deliberately silent about (tested via `pd.isna` so it holds whether the missing gap is `None` or the float-column `NaN` it coerces to). The manual-override acceptance itself is unified with `build_portfolio_df` through the shared `portfolio.manual_stop_wins` predicate (a manual wins only when ≥ the ratcheted `protective_stop` = `max(ATR, floor)`; avg-cost read from the same frozen `_port_df_enriched` frame the Brief used). Add sizing & R:R still compute off `r["stop"]` (ATR fresh-entry) by design. The composite Buy score is NOT changed — it rates the STOCK (and rewards the oversold setup); the exit discipline protects the POSITION. The Brief's reduce state is consumed via `decision_bucket.reduce_call_items()` published to `_reduce_calls` (same `_is_reduce`/`_ticker` canon as F-75), so the Analysis and Brief surfaces can't contradict (the "Buy 68.7 · add 18 shares" vs "REDUCE — Deterioration Exit" case). Stop-breach takes precedence; degrades to showing sizing if the Brief hasn't built this session. |
| F-46 | Exit Plan tab (Sell/Strong Sell): current price, shares held, position value, P&L if sold now; exit recommendation banner; ATR downside level |
| F-47 | Position Monitor tab (Hold): stop loss, shares held, P&L; guidance to maintain with stop; specific 7-day re-check date and two concrete action triggers (add if score ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65); exit if price closes below stop). For a **held** position the displayed stop (metric, narrative, scenarios line) is the **ratcheted / manual stop the engine actually acts on** (`_sa_holding["Stop"]` = `max(ATR stop, profit-ratchet floor)`, or a tighter manual override) — NOT the raw ATR entry stop `r["stop"]`, which under-reports the protective stop once a profit-ratchet tier engages. This keeps the Analysis metric, the Brief, and the F-47a explainer in agreement. Non-held Hold uses `r["stop"]` (a fresh ATR entry stop is correct). |
| F-47a | **Stop explainer + what-if simulator (Hold monitor).** An opt-in expander ("How your stop is set — and what happens next") on the held-position Hold tab. Read-only / educational — it never changes a gate, threshold, or recommendation. (a) A **stop-ladder chart** places entry, the profit-ratchet floor, the ATR stop, the active stop, an optional tighter level and current price on one price line. The stop actually **in force** is a large ✓ marker; the losing candidate stop is **dimmed** and a "tightest of these wins → your stop" arrow points to the winner (so the "tier label vs. binding number" case is legible at a glance); **red/green zones** mark stop-out vs holding territory; a colour-coded **role legend** + hover text name what each level is. (a2) A **profit-lock staircase** companion chart (from `stop_ladder`'s `ratchet_rungs`): a stepped floor-vs-price line showing how the locked floor climbs through each ratchet trigger, with reached/ahead/current-tier markers, a "you are here" line at price, and a dotted line at the actual active stop (which usually binds early because it's the higher of the floor and the ATR stop). Suppressed under a Brief Reduce/Exit call. (b) A **plain-English walk-through** of the three layers (volatility ATR floor → profit ratchet → manual override) with the position's live numbers, explicitly resolving the "tier label vs. binding number" case (e.g. AAPL labelled "Breakeven guard" while the ATR stop is the number in force). (c) A **what-if price slider**: downside tests the hypothetical price against the stop held **today** and reports realized P&L at that stop; upside recomputes the stop the app **would recommend raising to** at the higher price. Faithful by construction — all numbers come from `portfolio.stop_ladder`, which reuses `protective_stop` + the shared `ATR_STOP_MULT` formula (the current-state active stop is pinned to the engine's `r["stop"]` via `atr_stop_override`, so it is bit-exact with the metric/Brief). The ratchet is framed as a **per-run recommendation the user places** (not an automatic non-retreating guarantee, since `protective_stop` is stateless). When the position is under a Brief Reduce/Exit call (`_reduce_calls`), the "keep-climbing" nudges (tighten-option, next-ratchet) are suppressed so the explainer never softens an active exit directive. The same explainer also renders on the **Buy/Strong-Buy tab of a position you already hold** (`buy_add=True`, when not stop-breached and not under a reduce call): there it opens with a note distinguishing the **sizing stop** (`r["stop"]` = the ATR fresh-entry stop, or a manual override — what the add's shares & R:R are sized off, deliberately, so new capital is risked at today's volatility rather than off the tighter ratchet the held shares earned; sizing off the ratchet would over-buy into strength) from the **protective stop** (the ratcheted active level), plus a nudge that adding raises blended cost and can drop the ratchet tier. **Add-sizing and R:R are unchanged — this is display-only** (the policy question "should add-sizing use the ratcheted stop?" was decided NO: the ATR fresh-entry basis is the disciplined choice). |
| F-162 | **Analysis page — "📈 What would change this signal?" expander** (collapsed, below verdict banner). Shows upgrade path: composite pts gap to next verdict tier + top-2 easiest pillar levers with current→needed score. Shows downgrade path: pts buffer above current verdict floor + most-weight pillar named as highest-deterioration risk. Display-only; reads existing pillar scores and verdict thresholds. No new data dependency. |
| F-163 | **Analysis page — Gate checklist.** 5 Pass/Fail chips in Trade Plan tab (Buy/Strong Buy branch only): Data Quality (BQ metrics available), R/R ≥ 2.0×, Concentration < `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` (15%), Sector < `SECTOR_CEILING` (35%), Earnings > `EARNINGS_IMMINENT_DAYS` (7d) clear. Shows "—" with page pointer when account/catalyst data not loaded. Makes implicit entry gates visible without changing gate logic. |
| F-48 | Price scenarios chart (Bull / Base / Bear) in all signal modes; sell mode shows position $ impact per scenario |
| F-49 | Chart tab: candlestick with Bollinger Bands, SMA 20/50, RSI panel, optional volume |
| F-50 | Risk tab: Sharpe, Sortino, max drawdown, beta, volatility vs SPY |
| F-51 | Deep Dive tab: fundamental signals, analyst revisions, earnings proximity, news sentiment |
| F-237 | **🧾 Prior Trades tab — your own history in this name.** A 6th per-ticker tab (after 🏦 Analyst Coverage) reconstructing the user's past positions in the analysed ticker as **round trips** (0 shares → held → back to 0), newest first. Backed by `stock_analyzer/ticker_history.py` (`build_ticker_history`, `build_pnl_series`), reading `st.session_state.trades_df` — already loaded at bootstrap, so **no new fetch and no new DB call** for the history itself. Tab label carries the episode count (`🧾 Prior Trades (2)`) when history exists. Each round trip shows entry avg / exit avg / realized $ & % / **vs-SPY over that episode's own window** / hold days, plus trigger and followed-vs-deviated badges, a neutral "since you exited, it's at $X" line, and an expander replaying **what the user wrote at the time** — `user_thesis`, the Concept-C pre-mortem (`premortem_case_against`, `premortem_commitment`, trigger price/direction), `lesson` + `lesson_category`, `deviation_reason`, and a conditions-at-entry line rendered from the Concept-E `decision_context` snapshot. A currently-open position renders on top as an OPEN card with unrealized P&L; `vs_spy_pct` stays `None` (never 0) until it closes, and realized totals are labelled closed-trips-only. **Realized P&L sums the STORED `realized_pnl` on the episode's SELL rows** — the same weighted-average-cost convention `db.recalculate_from_trades` writes — so this tab can never disagree with the Portfolio page; a null leg is recomputed from the replayed basis and the card is captioned as estimated. **Awareness only: never gates, never scores, never annotates a verdict.** |
| F-237a | **Prior Trades — journey chart.** Two stacked Plotly panels sharing one x-axis at the top of the tab, with the four headline stats merged into the card header (round trips · net realized · W·L record · avg hold) so the chart is the first thing on the tab. Panel 1: price line with the user's own fills marked (buy ▲ green, signal exit ▼ blue, stop-out ▼ red — shape as well as colour, so identity survives colour-vision deficiency) and each holding period shaded by that trip's outcome. Panel 2: position P&L % while held, one arc per episode against the **running** average cost (so a mid-episode add visibly shifts the basis), plus a dashed "had you held" continuation from the most recent exit to today. **Deliberately two panels, never one plot with two y-axes** — a dual-axis chart aligns two arbitrary scales and manufactures a correlation absent from the data. Every plotted value is also written out on the cards below, so the chart is an enhancement and never the only way to read a number. The chart resolves **its own** price-history span (`6mo → 1y → 2y → 5y`, whichever first covers the earliest entry) rather than reusing the sidebar's "History period" selector, which defaults to 6mo and would silently crop older round trips; the fetch is `@st.cache_data(ttl=1800, max_entries=200)` and is skipped entirely when the loaded bundle already reaches back far enough, or when the ticker has no history. |
| F-237c | **Prior Trades — Trade Plan pointer (Phase 2).** A one-line `st.info` note on the 📈 Analysis **Trade Plan** tab (after the R:R caveat banner) and the **Exit Plan** tab (after the ATR-level caption), surfacing the ticker's own history at the decision moment rather than requiring a tab click. Built by `_prior_trades_pointer()` from the same pre-tab `build_ticker_history` result the tab label's count uses — **no extra fetch, no extra DB call**. **Strictly factual by design: a round-trip count, net realized dollars, first-entry month, and last-exit price against today's price. It carries NO alpha / vs-SPY figure and NO verdict on the user's skill in the name.** That redline is deliberate and load-bearing — an outcome/alpha judgment at the decision moment is the class explicitly *rejected* for Self Track Record (see F-233 notes and memory `project_self_track_record`), because it editorialises on the very decision being weighed; ticker-specific *facts* are the accepted class, which F-195 already establishes by feeding prior lessons into the Pre-Mortem at BUY. **Two asymmetries between the Buy and Exit branches, both deliberate (Opus review, 2026-08-14):** (a) the **last-exit-vs-today** clause is suppressed on the **Exit Plan** (`include_last_exit=False`) — under a protective "exit this position" call, *"your last exit was $118; it's $160 today"* reads as "last time you sold, it kept going up", i.e. a counter-argument to the recommendation it sits beneath, undercutting protect-capital-overrides-deploy-capital; on the Buy side the same fact is the accepted anchoring trade-off (plan §8). The count / first-entered / net-realized facts still render there. (b) The **held-but-never-closed** variant renders only on the Buy side; on an exit it says nothing about the decision and reads as a remark on the user's selling behaviour. **Journal-gap honesty:** when `warnings` contains `orphan_sell` or `bad_row`, `net_realized` is *understated* (those rows are skipped outright, so their P&L never enters the total), so the pointer appends "*(journal gap — see the tab)*" rather than stating a bare figure with more confidence than the tab one click away. `oversell` / `split_in_window` are excluded from that test — they distort percentages and per-share prices, neither of which the pointer prints. Renders `""` (nothing) when the ticker has no history, when the journal is unavailable, or when there is nothing truthful to say. **Awareness only — never gates, suppresses, reorders, or annotates any recommendation, and nothing downstream reads it.** Gated on Phase 1's numbers being verified against a real multi-episode ticker before shipping (DELL, 3 closed round trips + 1 open, 2026-08-14 — the open position's unrealized reconciled to the cent with the Summary Scorecard). |
| F-237d | **Prior Trades — vs-SPY window coverage guard.** `_spy_covers()` in `ticker_history.py` checks, **per episode**, that the SPY frame actually reaches back to that episode's entry date before computing `vs_spy_pct`. `trade_review._spy_return_between` anchors on the first close *at or after* `start_d`, so a SPY frame beginning after an entry silently returned a return measured over a **truncated window** — a *wrong* comparison rather than an absent one, and the only remaining path by which this module could report a wrong number instead of `None`. Per-episode rather than all-or-nothing, so an old trip correctly reports `—` while a recent one still gets a real figure. |
| F-237b | **Prior Trades — incomplete-journal integrity.** The tab must never show a confident number over a journal it can't account for. A SELL with no matching prior BUY (rebaselined holdings, pre-import history) emits an `orphan_sell` warning naming the date, is skipped rather than creating a phantom episode, and surfaces a visible amber banner; an `action='SPLIT'` row inside an open round trip **rescales that episode's recorded legs onto the post-split basis** (`shares × factor`, `price ÷ factor`) and emits a `split_in_window` warning saying the share counts and per-share prices are shown split-adjusted and won't match the original confirmations. The rescale is required for correctness, not cosmetics: leaving `entry_avg` pre-split while `shares_sold` went post-split made `entry_avg × shares_sold` a mismatched product and understated `realized_pct` by exactly the split factor (caught in Opus review pre-ship). Dollars are split-invariant and each SELL's dollar P&L is frozen at append time so a *recomputed* leg isn't rescaled with the per-share figures; the chart also depends on the rescale, since its price history comes from yfinance with `auto_adjust=True` and is therefore already split-adjusted (true of the primary leg; the FMP failover maps FMP's `close`, not `adjClose`, so a split-spanning chart served entirely from the failover can still show an unadjusted step — a known, accepted limitation of the failover leg, not of this feature). SPLIT overwrites running state as `db.recalculate_from_trades` does and never opens or closes an episode — but, unlike the db replay, a SPLIT with **no** open episode is skipped rather than seeding state, since there is no round trip to attribute it to. A SELL for more shares than the journal shows held emits an `oversell` warning (the same inconsistency `recalculate_from_trades` warns on) rather than silently clamping. `build_ticker_history` returns **`None`** when trade history is unavailable (the offline sentinel), distinct from a populated result with `episodes == []`. **The consumer must not treat `episodes == []` alone as proof of a first position:** `db.load_trades()` returns an *empty DataFrame* on a read failure (RLS / Supabase down) exactly as it does for a genuinely empty journal, so the module's `None` can never be reached through that producer. The Analysis tab therefore claims "never traded this name" only when the journal itself has rows; a wholly-empty journal renders indeterminate copy naming both possibilities. (Making `load_trades` return `None` on its exception path is the real upstream fix — ~25 call sites of blast radius, deliberately not in this change.) `vs_spy_pct` is `None`, never `0.0`, when SPY data doesn't cover an episode's window. |
| F-237e | **Prior Trades — situational-category tag (2026-08-25).** The "What you wrote at the time" expander now shows the entry's `situational_category` (Institutional Flow / Earnings Catalyst / Technical Read / Macro-News / Other — F-257's locked vocabulary, `decision_journal.SITUATIONAL_CATEGORIES`) as a `:blue-background[📍 …]` badge, read from the opening BUY row via `ticker_history._build_journal`. Purely additive display of an already-captured, already-vocabulary-locked field — no new column, no new decision content, never gates. Closes a carry-over noted in `docs/plans/prior-trades-tab.md` §7 that predates F-257 (the field didn't exist yet when that note was written). |
| F-52 | Analysis Summary expander: formatted markdown summary for all analysed tickers |
| F-53 | Source links: Yahoo Finance, Finviz, SEC filings, Yahoo News |

### 3.3a Compare (⚖️ Compare page)

**Added to spec 2026-07-27 — a doc gap, not a new build; the page has existed since before shipped-log.md tracking began.** `app.py:17625-17833`, `elif page == "⚖️ Compare":`, backed by `stock_analyzer/comparison.py` (`build_comparison`, `_compute_verdict`, `_portfolio_fit`).

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-208 | **Two-ticker side-by-side comparison.** Exactly two tickers via two text inputs, triggered by a Compare button; identical tickers rejected with a warning. Quick-pick buttons auto-fill from "Watchlist top 2" and/or "Portfolio top 2 by weight"; defaults pre-fill from the last comparison run or the first two watchlist tickers. Data via `load_all()` (30-min `@st.cache_data` freshness, not reused from another page's session state). No charts — custom HTML tables per section (Headline, Overview, Technicals, Business Quality, Valuation, Sentiment & Analyst, Risk, Setup), with the winning cell in each row shaded green. Result cached in `st.session_state["_cmp_result_cache"]` keyed by `(ticker_a, ticker_b)` so it survives reruns from the page's own "Deep dive" buttons (→ 📈 Analysis) without recomputing. |
| F-209 | **Verdict never fabricates a pick.** `_compute_verdict()` returns `"tie"` with `confidence: "low"` when the composite-score gap between the two tickers is under 3 points — a close call is shown as a close call, not forced to a winner. |
| F-210 | **Portfolio-fit awareness (not a gate here).** `_portfolio_fit()` checks each ticker's sector weight against `SECTOR_ELEVATED`/`SECTOR_CEILING` (same basis as the hard sector-cap gate, G-19) and flags "🚫 hard ceiling breached" or "⚠️ elevated" alongside the comparison — informational on this page; the actual gate still lives where a real BUY would be gated (Grow Today / Watchlist ENTER_NOW). |

### 3.4 Market Scanner

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-60 | Scan a curated universe of **88 tickers across 14 sectors** (refreshed 2026-08-17 by F-240; was ~73/12) for technical momentum, **extended at runtime with the user's Watchlist tickers** (tagged sector="Watchlist") so watched names are scored alongside the curated set |
| F-60a | **Movers discovery (close the discovery gap):** a separate `scan_movers()` pass scans the broad ~200-name `discovery_universe` for today's 1-day gainers ≥ `MOVER_MIN_DAY_GAIN_PCT` (5%). The top `MOVER_SHORTLIST_SIZE` (12) gainers are composite-gated; those clearing `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65) feed the unified New Positions list (see F-24a). The composite gate is the noise filter — a 1-day pop without fundamentals/sentiment behind it is rejected. Cached 30 min via `_cached_scan_movers`. **Two distinct sources:** (1) the candidate *list* is a **static, hardcoded curated set** (`DISCOVERY_UNIVERSE` in `discovery_universe.py`, ~200 liquid large/mid-caps grouped by sector, deduped against curated/held/watchlist at runtime) — NOT scraped from an index provider and NOT pulled from a screener API; it is treated as DATA and refreshed manually (~quarterly). (2) the price *data* used to compute each name's 1-day change comes from **yfinance** (a single batched `yf.download(..., period="3mo")`) — the same market-data source as the rest of the app; there is no paid/third-party movers feed. Consequently the only discovery limits are (a) a breakout must be one of the ~200 listed names, and (b) yfinance must return its data. Widening the net (full S&P 500 / live screener API) is a documented future-expansion path, not current behaviour. |
| F-61 | Score each ticker 0–100 using RSI, trend alignment (SMA 20/50), and price momentum |
| F-62 | Surface top picks ranked by score; display sector, signal, RSI, trend, momentum |
| F-63 | Scanner results persist in session state and feed the Daily Briefing buy candidates list |

### 3.5 News Intelligence

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-70 | Aggregate news headlines for all held positions from Yahoo Finance |
| F-71 | Score each headline using VADER sentiment (Positive / Neutral / Negative) |
| F-72 | Flag tickers with predominantly negative news as "Requires Attention". A minimum of `NEWS_CRITICAL_MIN_HEADLINES` (2) qualifying headlines per held ticker is required before the card fires (qualifying = VADER compound ≤ `NEWS_SENTIMENT_CRITICAL`, tier ≤ 2), preventing a single borderline headline from triggering a stop-tighten directive. VADER compound values are pre-processed by a suppress-only LLM rescorer (`rescore_news_items_llm`, Claude Haiku, 8s timeout, VADER fallback) that can only raise a score — removing false-positive negatives — never lower one, so the LLM cannot create new Act Today cards or flip buy-candidate verdicts. (`stock_analyzer/news_intelligence.py`, `stock_analyzer/daily_briefing.py`, commit `6c055ec`, 2026-07-13) |
| F-73 | Provide Analyze button navigating to Stock Analysis for flagged tickers |
| F-75 | **Opportunity Signals ↔ Brief reduce-call reconciliation.** The Overview's "Opportunity Signals" (positive news on held names, composite Score ≥ `NEWS_OPPORTUNITY_SCORE_MIN` (55), VADER compound ≥ `NEWS_OPPORTUNITY_COMPOUND_MIN` (0.10)) never frames a position as "may support adding on a pullback" while the Daily Brief carries an active **Reduce/Exit call** on the same ticker — the protect-capital directive leads the composite (the deterioration ladder fires before the composite reacts), so an add prompt there would contradict the Brief (the PLTR deterioration-exit case). It reads the Brief-published `_reduce_calls` set (built once by `decision_bucket.reduce_call_items` — the SAME `_is_reduce`/`_ticker` canon the Act-bucket reconciler uses: act-origin stop / sell / risk / deterioration / risk-off reduces AND review-origin trim variants, across both the Act and Awareness buckets since deterioration cards bucket to Awareness), the same published cache the Analysis Trade Plan consumes (F-45a) — so all three surfaces read one definition and can't drift. Suppressed names are NOT silently dropped: an amber "⚠️ NOT SHOWN AS ADDS" note lists them with the reason, and the full headline still appears under "All News for Your Holdings." Display-only — no gate/score/verdict change. |
| F-74 | **News sentiment awareness (Finnhub).** Beyond VADER headline scoring (F-71), surface Finnhub's pre-computed `/stock/news-sentiment` read (`stock_analyzer/news_sentiment.py`) for held positions on two surfaces: (1) a **News Sentiment** awareness row in the Home Position Drill-Down showing a label — 🟢 Bullish (bullish_pct ≥ `NEWS_SENTIMENT_BULLISH_THRESHOLD` = 0.60) / 🔴 Bearish (bullish_pct < `NEWS_SENTIMENT_BEARISH_THRESHOLD` = 0.40) / 🟡 Neutral — plus bullish %, buzz (× average coverage), and pp-vs-sector-average; (2) a **News Sentiment Shift** card in Today's Brief for any held name where bullish_pct < `NEWS_SENTIMENT_SHIFT_ALERT_BULLISH` (0.40) **AND** buzz_score > `NEWS_SENTIMENT_SHIFT_BUZZ_MIN` (1.0) — both required, so thin/stale low-buzz bearishness is not alerted. **Strictly additive / zero runtime dependency:** when Finnhub is unconfigured or offline the row/card simply does not render and nothing else changes. **Awareness only — never a gate and never a buy/sell call.** The drill-down row reuses the Brief's batch sentiment cache (same sorted-CSV key) so the two surfaces can't show drifting data or duplicate the fetch. |

### 3.6 Trade Journal

Three internal tabs (restructured 2026-07-16 per the I12 UX remediation): **📝 Log Trade** (the log-a-trade form — F-80/F-81/F-81a-c/F-82), **📊 Performance** (Performance Dashboard, Behavioral Analytics F-83–F-86, Opportunity Cost, Decision Journal, Engine Trust by Composite Band), and **📋 History** (the Trade History table + broker-statement import F-87b). The shared preamble (title, drift-check banner, recommendation-prefill consumption, SELL confirmation card) renders above the tab strip; Log Trade is deliberately the first tab so a recommendation prefill (F-106) lands on it.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-80 | Log all buy and sell trades with ticker, action, shares, price, notes, and trigger type |
| F-81 | Persist trades to Supabase `trades` table |
| F-81a | **SELL integrity guard:** a SELL whose shares exceed what the logged BUYs can cover is hard-blocked at form submit. The guard validates against `db.recalculate_from_trades()` — the SAME trade-replay source the drift detector reads — so the guard and the drift detector can never disagree (validating against the `holdings_df` cache instead let an unmatched SELL slip through after a rebaseline; that was the May 2026 COIN double-SELL bug). An explicit override is required to record a SELL beyond accountable shares. |
| F-81b | **Double-submit dedupe:** an identical `(ticker, action, shares)` submission within 15 seconds is rejected with a "duplicate ignored" warning. The live-prefilled price is deliberately EXCLUDED from the dedupe signature — it ticks between reruns and otherwise disguises a double-click as two different trades (the mechanism behind the COIN double-SELL). |
| F-81c | **SELL confirmation card:** a SELL that passes all validation gates is not immediately written to the database. The verified record is stashed to `st.session_state["_tj_pending_sell"]` and the page reruns. A confirmation card renders in the shared preamble above the tab strip (so it stays visible regardless of active tab) showing the exact trade (ticker, shares, price, and realized P&L if computable) with [✅ Confirm SELL] and [✗ Cancel] buttons. Confirm executes the save + holdings sync; Cancel restores `_tj_prefill` so the form reopens pre-populated. BUY submissions skip the intercept — zero extra clicks for buys. (Commit 7793be2.) |
| F-82 | Decision Context capture on each trade: signal seen at time of trade (auto-filled from current portfolio signal; help text shows load-time timestamp so pre-fill is clearly dated), whether signal was followed (yes / no / discretionary), deviation reason, lesson learned |
| F-83 | My Patterns section: analyse historical trades to compute signal accuracy vs override accuracy |
| F-84 | Surface costly deviations (ignored signal, position lost money) and good overrides |
| F-85 | Behavioural insight: compare signal-follow win rate vs override win rate; flag if ≥2 costly deviations |
| F-86 | Lessons library: aggregated lessons from all logged trades |
| F-87a | **Rate sensitivity table (🔗 Risk Analysis page).** A per-holding rate sensitivity table, below the correlation heatmap and above the Risk Action Plan, combining two complementary reads: (1) the **structural sector score** from `macro.RATE_SENSITIVITY` (−1.0 to +1.0; e.g. Semiconductors −0.70, Financials +0.70 — sign convention: negative = hurt when rates rise) and (2) the **empirical Pearson correlation** of each holding's daily returns vs TLT (iShares 20-Year Treasury ETF), computed by `risk.pearson_corr_vs_benchmark()` over the 3-month window (`_cached_tlt("3mo")`, 30-min TTL). TLT falls when long rates rise; a negative TLT correlation means the holding tends to drop when rates rise (rate-sensitive); positive = rate beneficiary. The table sorts **most rate-sensitive first** (TLT Corr ascending; sector score as fallback when TLT data is unavailable for a holding — requires ≥ 20 overlapping trading days). **Third case, added 2026-08-16:** a sector with no `RATE_SENSITIVITY` key reports `Sector Score = None` and renders `—` rather than a fabricated `+0.00`, and a row with neither a correlation nor a label reads "Unknown — no rate data and no sector label" and sorts **last** (unknown is not neutral). Found from a live screenshot where held NEM (`Basic Materials`) and MRVL (`Technology`) — both lacking a `TICKER_SECTORS` entry, so both falling back to the raw provider GICS string — showed a confident `+0.00`. The same fabricated neutral was closed on the second surface that renders this value, `macro.portfolio_macro_exposure()` (🌐 Macro → "Your Portfolio — Macro Alignment"), where unrated positions are additionally **excluded from the bar chart and named in a caption** rather than plotted at a NaN x-position that would make the bar silently vanish. A **weighted-portfolio TLT correlation summary line** caps the table (weighted avg of per-holding TLT corrs by `Weight (%)`). Columns: Ticker / Sector / Weight (%) / TLT Corr / Sector Score / Implication. **Awareness only — never gates, suppresses, or scores.** `risk.rate_sensitivity_per_ticker()`; `data.fetch_tlt()`. (Commit f0b946c.) |
| F-87b | **Broker history text import (paste-based).** A "📋 Paste history" tab on the 📒 Trade Journal broker-import expander (alongside the CSV tab). User pastes text copied from the Robinhood Account → History page; `broker_screenshot.parse_robinhood_text()` (pure-Python regex, no Vision API) extracts executed Buy/Sell trades, skips "Canceled" orders, and resolves company names to tickers. Each Robinhood History order is four lines: `[Company Name] [order_type] [buy|sell]` / `Individual · [Month Day]` / `$[total]` / `[N] shares at $[price]`. **Key design decisions:** (a) Ticker resolution uses a local lookup table (~60 company names); unknown names fall back to the Claude text API if the Anthropic key is available (no API call required for common tickers). (b) Ticker and Date columns are **editable** in the preview — user must verify before importing. (c) **Sync-since date picker** defaults to the day after the last import, derived from `broker_screenshot.last_screenshot_sync_date()` (reads `trades.notes` for "RH screenshot" or "RH text import" tags — backward-compatible). (d) **Three-bucket reconciliation**: import summary + editable new-row preview (new pre-checked, likely-duplicates unchecked) + "in app, not in paste" expander for date-range-scoped discrepancies via `broker_screenshot.find_app_only_in_range()` (content-match: same ticker/action/shares/price). (e) **No auto-deletes ever** — the discrepancy list is informational only. Write path is identical to the CSV import path (human-in-the-loop `st.data_editor` preview → `db.save_trade` + `recalculate_from_trades`). `stock_analyzer/broker_screenshot.py` (pure — no Streamlit or DB imports). (Commits 97590fb + 4e4f46c.) |
| F-87 | **Broker statement import — manual Robinhood sync (v1).** Upload a Robinhood account-activity CSV (📒 Trade Journal → "📥 Import from broker statement") to sync **Buy/Sell** trades into the `trades` table, keeping the app reconciled with the broker until Robinhood ships a mature integration (the automated MCP path stays deferred). `broker_import.parse_robinhood_csv` (pure) handles the export quirks (multi-line Description/CUSIP, `$`/comma/paren/space prices, `M/D/YYYY` dates, trailing junk + tax-disclaimer rows) and keeps only Buy/Sell; non-trade rows (dividends/ACH/interest/fees) are counted in a skipped summary (deferred to a v2 Account-ledger sync), and rows with shares/price ≤ 0 are surfaced in an `invalid` bucket, never silently dropped (DB `check (shares>0)`/`check (price>0)`). **Dedup is content-match, no schema change** (`classify_against_existing`): an **exact same-day** match on `(date, ticker, action, shares, price)` **plus a date-agnostic** secondary match — because trades logged via the interactive form store `traded_at` as the *logging timestamp*, not the trade date, a hand-logged trade is flagged **"possible duplicate — different date"** and left **unchecked** so a mixed manual+import workflow can't double-count; count-consistent so genuine repeat fills aren't over-flagged. **Human-in-the-loop:** an editable `st.data_editor` preview (new rows pre-checked, likely-duplicates unchecked, per-row Status) — the source-of-truth `trades` table is **never bulk-written without explicit confirmation**. On import, rows insert **chronologically** (date asc, BUY before SELL) via the existing `db.save_trade`, then `recalculate_from_trades` → `save_holdings` → `update_trade_realized_pnl` (the same tested reconciliation/rebuild path) produce a **reconciliation view** (resulting holdings + warnings). Read-only-guarded; parse call wrapped so a malformed file can't crash the page. **Honest v1 limit:** the CSV is a transaction log, not a positions snapshot — reconciliation is shown for the user to eyeball against Robinhood (a SELL whose BUY predates the app's history surfaces as a recalc warning). Plan: [docs/plans/broker-statement-import.md](plans/broker-statement-import.md). |

| F-164 | **Opportunity Cost expander (Trade Journal — Behavioral Analytics section).** A collapsed `st.expander` ("💸 Opportunity Cost — what you passed on") that computes lazily on open. Scope: the 90-day window of `new_pick` recommendations (`rec_types=("new_pick",)`) the user never acted on, using `recommendations_history.distinct_missed()` + `missed_split()` + `compute_outcomes(min_days=5)`. Renders a 4-KPI strip (recs passed / would've won / dodged losers / avg missed return) and a top-5 named ticker list with per-ticker alpha vs SPY. **Scope = new_pick only** (gate-cleared recommendations — never the `buy_candidate` awareness feed; passing on those is correct). No new DB table — reuses existing `db.load_recommendations()` + the `recommendations_history` helpers. Pointer to 📊 Recommendations History for the full chart. (Commit f0b946c.) |
| F-165 | **Engine Trust by Band (Trade Journal — Behavioral Analytics section).** A collapsed `st.expander` ("🔬 Engine Trust by Composite Band — did you act at the right conviction levels?") that computes lazily on open. Groups all enriched recommendation outcomes by composite band: sub-threshold (<65) / BUY (65–74) / Strong BUY (≥75). Excludes `outcome_maturing` rows. For each band: action rate (did you act when the engine said this?), avg alpha when you acted, avg alpha when you passed, and a plain-English **edge comment** comparing the two. Answers the discipline question "did you trust the engine more at higher conviction, and was that trust well-placed?" `recommendations_history.engine_trust_by_band()`. Wrapped in `try/except` for graceful degradation when history is sparse. (Commit f0b946c.) |
| F-166 | **Behavioral KPI strip on AI Insights, 🩺 Positions tab.** Four columns: **Win Rate** (green ≥ 55% / amber 45–54% / red < 45%), **Profit Factor** (green ≥ 1.5 / amber ≥ 1.0 / red < 1.0), **Trades This Month** (red prefix when overtrading is elevated), **Behavioral Alert** (count of HIGH-priority behavioral insights or "none"). HIGH-priority behavioral insights are also surfaced as `st.warning()` banners so they can't be missed. Only renders when ≥ 5 trades exist. Reads from `trade_analytics.build_full_analytics(trades_df)` (already computed globally). Caption points to Trade Journal. **Relocated 2026-07-22 (commit `6e150a4`)** from a page-level row above the cadence tabs into the 🩺 Positions tab (rendered before the Thesis Reviews section) — it had been reading as a page-level header rather than a Positions-tab concern; no logic changed, indentation only. (Original: commit 3cbd6a6; relocated: commit 6e150a4.) |
| F-167 | **Overtrading detection (behavioral analytics).** `trade_analytics._build_overtrading_stats()` computes the current calendar-month BUY+SELL count (excludes SPLIT rows) vs a rolling 12-month average. Returns `{current_month, current_month_count, rolling_avg, multiplier, is_elevated}`. `build_behavioral_insights()` surfaces two overtrading insight cards: **HIGH** at ≥ 2.0× rolling average (pace is markedly above normal, with institutional-lens framing on transaction costs and reactive trading risk); **MEDIUM** at ≥ 1.5× (elevated pace with context). `build_full_analytics()` now includes `win_rate` (was computed but not returned in prior versions — now in both the `empty` sentinel and the return dict) and `overtrading_stats` in the returned dict. (Commit 3cbd6a6.) |
| F-168 | **Stress test — historical scenario replay (model vs actual).** For scenarios that have a real event window (`stress_test.HISTORICAL_WINDOWS`), an optional "📅 How did your holdings actually perform?" expander appears below the stress-test results (2026-07-13: now on the 🔗 Risk Analysis page, moved from Home in the nav cleanup). Three scenarios have windows: COVID Crash (2020-02-19 → 2020-03-23), 2022 Rate Shock (2022-01-03 → 2022-10-13), GFC 2008 (2007-10-09 → 2009-03-09). Loading is button-gated and lazy (user clicks "📥 Load historical data"); results are cached in `st.session_state[f"_hist_stress_{scenario_id}"]`. `stress_test.fetch_historical_drawdowns(scenario_id, tickers)` fetches actual OHLCV via yfinance for each held ticker in the event window and computes peak-to-trough drawdown as `(min_close − first_close) / first_close × 100`. Tickers with fewer than 5 trading days in the window (IPO after event start, delisted) return `None` and display N/A. Results render as a **Model Est. (%) / Actual (%) / Δ (Actual−Model)** table; rows with Δ > 5 are green-highlighted (model over-estimated the loss — actual was better than feared), rows with Δ < −5 are red-highlighted (model under-estimated — actual was worse). A "🔄 Refresh data" button clears the session cache. Custom Scenario and scenarios without a `HISTORICAL_WINDOWS` entry show no expander. Awareness only; never issues a recommendation or gates an entry. (Commit 0e8dc9f.) |

### 3.6a Trade Review (🪞 Trade Review page)

**Added to spec 2026-07-27 — a doc gap flagged internally back on 2026-07-24 (see `docs/shipped-log.md`'s Agentic Roadmap P6 closing note: "🪞 Trade Review... itself discovered mid-session, undocumented in memory") and never closed until now.** `app.py:20449`, `elif page == "🪞 Trade Review":`. Pure-logic backing module `stock_analyzer/trade_review.py` (no Streamlit/API calls). Answers: "are app-followed trades outperforming ones made off external info?"

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-214 | **Nine independently sample-gated behavioral diagnostics**, each rendered only above its own minimum-sample floor (never a fabricated read on thin data): Holding-Period Imbalance (winners cut short vs. losers held longer, ≥3 wins & ≥3 losses), Signal-Defying Bias (outcome when the user traded against the recorded signal, ≥3 defying trades), vs-SPY Drag (% of closed trades beating SPY + cumulative $ alpha, ≥3 closed), Re-Entered Tickers (net P&L on tickers bought ≥2 times, ≥2 trades/ticker), Trigger-Type Effectiveness (win rate/P&L by MANUAL/RECOMMENDATION/WATCHLIST_ENTRY/etc., ≥2 types × ≥3 trades each, total ≥6, spread ≥20pp), Lesson-Capture Rate (% of trades with a logged lesson, and whether lesson-writers outperform, ≥6 judged trades), Day-of-Week Timing (win rate/P&L by execution weekday, ≥10 total, ≥2 weekdays × ≥2 each, spread ≥30pp), Position-Size Discipline (vs. `SINGLE_NAME_CEILING` 15%), and Sector Mix (vs. `SECTOR_ELEVATED` 25%). All computation (FIFO buy/sell pairing, per-trade P&L, diagnostics) happens in `trade_review.py`; the page only calls `build_trade_review`/`build_recommendations`/`build_insights` and renders. |
| F-215 | **Position-size % uses current portfolio value, not historical** (an explicit, documented approximation — a trade's size-at-the-time isn't reconstructed from point-in-time portfolio value, which doesn't exist as a stored series). Findings are described in the page's own copy as "never speculative." |
| F-216 | **Deliberately NOT merged with Behavioral Fingerprint.** A plan to combine these 9 diagnostics with Behavioral Fingerprint's 6 patterns into one "Blind Spots" list was scoped and then shelved after Opus design review — Behavioral Fingerprint has no severity tiers, and folding its explicitly neutral cards into a "blind spot" label would have contradicted that tab's own non-accusatory posture. The considered fallback (a Trade-Review-only severity-ranked list) turned out to duplicate this page's own already-existing "🎯 Course-Correction Recommendations" section — so nothing was built; the two pages remain independent by design, not by oversight. |

### 3.7a Macro (🌐 Macro page)

**Added to spec 2026-07-27 — a doc gap, not a new build.** `app.py:14312-14484`, `elif page == "🌐 Macro":`, single linear flow (no tabs), backed by `stock_analyzer/macro.py`.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-211 | **Manual-load regime read (TLT/SPY/VIX).** A "Load macro signals" button fetches 3 price series and caches them in `st.session_state["_macro_raw"]` — not auto-loaded on page open, not auto-refreshed. `detect_macro_regime(tlt_ret, spy_ret, vix)` (`stock_analyzer/macro.py`) classifies: TLT 3-month return < -3% → `rising_rates`, > +3% → `falling_rates`, else neutral on rates; VIX ≥ `RISK_OFF_VIX_LEVEL` (25) → `risk_off`, ≤ `RISK_ON_VIX_LEVEL` (15) → `risk_on`, else neutral on volatility (SPY return only labels bull/bear/sideways, doesn't drive the combined regime). Rate signal wins if non-neutral, else the risk signal, else `neutral`. Output regime keys: `rising_rates` / `falling_rates` / `risk_off` / `risk_on` / `neutral`. |
| F-212 | **⚠️ Landmine — do not confuse with the FRED-based regime detector used elsewhere.** This page's `detect_macro_regime` (`macro.py`) is a SEPARATE, independent function from `detect_macro_regime_fred` (`stock_analyzer/macro_calendar.py`), which feeds real gates/targets elsewhere (Premortem stance narrative, Regime-Conditional Targets, the Risk Analysis regime cache). The two use different inputs (TLT/SPY/VIX price action here vs. FRED macro data there) and different label taxonomies. This page's regime read is diagnostic-only, lives in page-local session state, and is not consumed by any other page or gate. |
| F-213 | **Sector Rotation Playbook + portfolio macro alignment.** For the detected regime, shows an overweight/underweight sector list from a static `REGIME_FAVORED` table. `portfolio_macro_exposure()` labels each holding Tailwind↑/Headwind↓/Neutral↔ purely by whether its sector is in the current regime's overweight/underweight list (a categorical match, not a continuous alignment score), alongside a static per-sector `RATE_SENSITIVITY` score and a horizontal bar chart. No cross-asset signals (credit spreads, dollar, copper, yield curve) are used on this page — that's a distinct feature (Cross-Asset Pulse, F-09c). Awareness only; never gates or gets consumed by any recommendation. |

### 3.8 Watchlist

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-100 | Allow user to add tickers to a watchlist; persist to Supabase `watchlist` table |
| F-101 | Show composite signal and key metrics for watched tickers |
| F-102 | Provide advisor recommendations for watchlist entries: REMOVE / HOLD_OFF_EARNINGS / ENTER_NOW / NEAR_ENTRY / WAIT_ENTRY / WAIT_CATALYST |
| F-103 | ENTER_NOW must require composite score ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65), price in/near entry zone, AND validated `R:R ≥ 2.0` (rr=None is NOT a green light). Tickers without R:R fall through to NEAR_ENTRY. (Implements G-13.) |
| F-104 | ENTER_NOW must apply the portfolio-risk gate using `_port_risk_cache`, `_risk_high_alerts_cache`, and `_grow_today_sectors_cache`. Hard breaches (sector ≥ 35% OR portfolio β > 1.4 + ticker β > 1.8) downgrade to a "Setup Ready, But Portfolio Fit Blocks Entry" NEAR_ENTRY card. Soft concerns keep ENTER_NOW with an amber caution banner. |
| F-105 | When `_daily_brief_offline` is True, the Watchlist must surface an explicit warning that coordination gates are disabled and instruct the user to revisit the Portfolio page. |
| F-106 | "Log Planned Trade" button on an ENTER_NOW card must prefill the Trade Journal form with ticker, price, stop, and trigger=`WATCHLIST_ENTRY`, and route via `_pending_page` (never assign `nav_page` directly — that key is widget-bound and raises `StreamlitAPIException`). |
| F-219 | **Actionable-first restructure (2026-07-27).** The page defaults to a **🎯 Actionable** filter (`ENTER_NOW` + `NEAR_ENTRY` only) on load instead of showing all watchlist tickers — additional `st.segmented_control` chips (Hold / Waiting / Remove / All) narrow the view, alongside a ticker search box and a sort dropdown (Priority default / Score / Readiness / Days watched). Rows render collapsed by default as custom `st.container(border=True)` blocks with a colored action-badge + left-border summary line (ticker, score, readiness bar, price, R:R, earnings countdown, and a 👁️ resurrected-days chip); clicking a row's toggle expands the same detail content the page has always shown (metrics strip, conditions, position sizing, quick actions) — no change to that content. Default display order is `stock_analyzer/watchlist_advisor.sort_key_for_action()`: `ENTER_NOW → NEAR_ENTRY → REMOVE → HOLD_OFF_EARNINGS → WAIT_ENTRY → WAIT_CATALYST` — a deliberate change from the prior order (`REMOVE`/`HOLD_OFF_EARNINGS` first), which buried actionable opportunities under earnings-hold cards on watchlists with many names on hold. Every row also gets a **"▶ Analyze"** button (the same `_pending_page`/`_analysis_ticker`/`_nav_origin` navigation pattern used elsewhere in the app) linking to the full 📈 Analysis page for that ticker. Purely a presentation-layer restructure — does not change `build_watchlist_recommendation()`'s classification, scoring, or the portfolio-risk gate (F-104), and does not conflict with F-203's invariant (that feature never reorders cards; this default ordering is separate, page-level logic). |
