### 3.10 Market Data Layer (multi-source: failover + cross-check)

The app must not depend on a single market-data source. A provider abstraction (`stock_analyzer/providers/`) sits behind `data.py`'s public functions so the rest of the app is source-agnostic. See architecture §4.0.4.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-120 | Market data must be served through a provider chain with automatic failover. A `DATA_MULTISOURCE_ENABLED` master switch toggles the layer; when off, `data.py` behaves exactly as the single-source yfinance code (instant rollback). |
| F-121 | **Live prices** use `DATA_LIVE_PRICE_ORDER` (Finnhub → yfinance → FMP). Finnhub (real-time US quotes) is primary; the chain gap-fills — later providers supply only tickers the primary couldn't. A Finnhub outage must degrade to yfinance (delayed), never fail outright. |
| F-122 | **History, the analysis bundle, indices, and risk-free rate** use `DATA_PROVIDER_ORDER` (yfinance → Finnhub → FMP). yfinance stays primary; when a yfinance call hard-fails (e.g. rate-limited), the request fails over to FMP so composite scoring does not go dark. The broad scanner/movers scans stay on yfinance only. |
| F-123 | **Price cross-check guardrail:** held-position prices are validated against an independent source (cached 5 min). `prev_close` is compared strictly (`DATA_XCHECK_PREVCLOSE_TOL_PCT` 0.5%) — a settled value whose mismatch signals a real integrity fault (missed split, wrong-symbol, poisoned feed). Live price is compared loosely (`DATA_XCHECK_LIVE_TOL_PCT` 3.0%) to tolerate delayed-vs-real-time latency. A breach renders a fail-loud red "Price unverified — sources disagree" banner naming the tickers and gaps. (Implements OP-03 at the data boundary.) |
| F-123a | **Validator-health gate (no false alarms during a validator outage).** The cross-check is **skipped** when its independent validator (the second live-price provider, yfinance / `"yahoo_finance"`) is RED in `api_health` (sustained degradation — `rate_limits ≥ 3` OR `consecutive_errors ≥ 5`, e.g. Yahoo 401 Invalid Crumb / 429 from a datacenter IP). A "disagreement" against a degraded validator is the validator's own fault, not a real integrity fault, so surfacing the red F-123 banner then would be a false alarm. In that state both surfaces (Home, Analysis) show a grey "Price cross-check paused — validator degraded" caption (transparent, never silent) and the check auto-resumes when the validator recovers. **Red-only by design:** a genuine integrity fault with a HEALTHY validator still fires the F-123 red banner — the gate must not mask a real fault. Single chokepoint in `orchestrator.crosscheck_batch`; the gate and the UI caption share one threshold (`api_health` red). |
| F-124 | **Source transparency:** every live-price record is tagged with its provider; the price-strip caption shows the actual source(s) ("Finnhub (real-time)" / "Yahoo Finance (15-min delayed)" / "FMP"), and the Data Health sidebar shows per-provider call / error / rate-limit counts. The user can always see where a price came from. |
| F-125 | **Keyed-provider configuration:** Finnhub/FMP API keys live in Streamlit secrets (`FINNHUB_API_KEY`, `FMP_API_KEY`). A missing key makes that provider report unconfigured and be skipped silently (no error). The secret reader tolerates a key mis-nested under a TOML `[section]` and falls back to an env var for offline self-test. |
| F-126 | **Information Asymmetry Detector — Phase 1: price cross-check history (P4 of the Agentic Intelligence Roadmap) — SHIPPED 2026-07-24, commit `6e3ebd2`, display-only.** Persists F-123's already-computed price cross-check result to a new `price_xcheck_history` table, one row per (ticker, ET trading day) — the check previously had zero history (a 5-minute `st.cache_data` TTL only). When a held ticker is currently failing cross-check, its bullet on the existing red banner is annotated per failing leg — "prior-close gap widened from X% to Y% since `<date>`" or "live-price gap widened from X% to Y% since `<date>`" — when the most recent prior recorded row (within a 21-day lookback) shows a smaller gap than today's. `stock_analyzer/data.py::divergence_widened()` is a pure diff+threshold helper (`min_widen_pp=1.0`, a display-annotation default, not a `constants.py` value). **Written from the interactive Home page path only, day-deduped via session state — never from the headless cron** — the premarket cron path does not call the cross-provider validator today, so logging from cron would add a new per-ticker second-provider fetch every run; the interactive path already computes this for free every 5 minutes via the existing `_cached_price_xcheck`. **Scope was deliberately narrowed from the roadmap's original 3-source fundamentals-divergence idea** (research found Finnhub supplies live price only — no fundamentals capability at all — and FMP's own forward PE is sometimes a synthetic derivation from its trailing PE rather than an independent read, making a naive "sources disagree on forward PE" comparison both structurally impossible for a true 3-way check and methodologically ambiguous for a 2-way one). No new LLM, no new tab. 2-round Opus plan review (FIX-FIRST then SHIP) + Opus pre-ship code review (SHIP, 0 blocking). **Strictly additive: the annotation never changes whether the banner fires, never gates a stop/P&L decision.** Plan: [docs/plans/information-asymmetry-detector.md](plans/information-asymmetry-detector.md). |

### 3.11 Headless Automation & Email Alerts (out-of-app cron)

The app must deliver its protective signals even when the user never opens it. A **headless runtime** (Railway native Cron Job services since 2026-08-07, no Streamlit) runs the same decision logic and emails the user. This is the app's **second runtime**: `cron_runner.py` (dispatch) → `headless_alert_engine.py` (compute) → `notify.py` (Resend HTTP email), sharing the app's data path via the extracted `bundle_loader.py`. Credentials resolve **env-first** (Railway service variables) then `st.secrets`, and the Supabase client is a module-global singleton (the cron has no `st.cache_resource`). See architecture §4.0.x and memory `project_email_alerts_cron`.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-140 | **Pre-market protective alert (exit-discipline Phase 3).** ~08:00 ET on trading days, recompute protective signals headlessly and email the user when (and only when) the actionable set is non-empty: breached/triggered stops, deterioration EXIT (F-25d), and the risk-off de-risk overlay (F-25e), single-surfaced via a shared `reduced` set so a ticker is never listed twice. |
| F-141 | **EOD snapshot + reactive pullback (post-close).** After `ALERT_EOD_HOUR_ET` (≥ 16:00 ET) on trading days, write the day's `daily_snapshots` baseline (per-ticker shares + close — the Tier B Today's-P&L baseline, F-03a) and evaluate the reactive pullback condition. |
| F-142 | **Reactive pullback email (pullback-awareness Phase 2).** When the index draws down past `PULLBACK_ALERT_INDEX_PCT` (−3%) AND the book is fragile (F-09a), email a reactive pullback notice — framed as exposure/reaction, never a market-timing call. |
| F-143 | **Once-per-ET-day idempotency + DST safety.** Railway cron is UTC with no DST, so each affected weekday lane's Cron Job service carries a **comma hour list** straddling EST/EDT (e.g. `cron-premarket` `0 12,13 * * *`); `cron_runner` guards on trading-day + ET-hour + a per-day Supabase `alert_state` flag so exactly one run fires per ET day per lane — never a double-send. Six `alert_state` dedup rows exist (`cron_runner.py:91-96`): 1 = premarket/protective, 2 = EOD pullback, 3 = morning buy-list, 4 = intraday pullback entry, 5 = DB-unreachable notice, 6 = broker-lane failure. **`cron-eod` deliberately keeps a SINGLE slot** (`30 21 * * 1-5`) — its gate boundary sits far enough from the schedule that a DST shift cannot cross it; do not 'fix' it into a comma list. The weekday mode is derived from ET hour (< 12 ⇒ premarket, else eod); `ALERT_RUN_MODE` is set as a **fixed per-service Railway variable** and is honoured only for `scan` / `intraday` / `thesis` / `debrief` / `monthly` / `maintenance` / `broker`; `premarket` / `eod` override values are intentionally ignored so a schedule change cannot accidentally fire the wrong weekday lane — those two services set no `ALERT_RUN_MODE` at all. The Sunday `thesis` lane **chains** the weekly debrief (F-152) in the same run. `.github/workflows/alerts.yml` survives as a manual-dispatch-only fallback and schedules nothing. |
| F-144 | **Email delivery + dedup.** Email is sent via the Resend HTTP API (no SMTP); failures surface the HTTP status/reason in the run log (the API key is never logged). The premarket lane also dedups on a content fingerprint so an unchanged action set does not re-email the same day. A `workflow_dispatch` manual trigger exposes `mode` / `force` (bypass guards) / `test_email` (prove Resend→inbox without touching dedup state). |
| F-145 | **Fail-safe posture — AMENDED by F-239 (2026-08-16), which deliberately carved out one exception.** The cron is still **inert without `RESEND_API_KEY`** (absent secrets mean it computes nothing and emails nothing rather than erroring), and still exits 0 for ordinary failures. **The exception: a lane that cannot reach Supabase now emails the owner, records `status="failed"` on its heartbeat, and EXITS 1** — the original blanket "always exits 0" made a total outage indistinguishable from a quiet day, which is the precise failure F-239 exists to prevent. Secrets live in **Railway → Variables** (all **7** lanes are Railway native Cron Jobs — 6 since 2026-08-07, plus the daily `broker` lane added 2026-08-18 with F-244; the original GitHub repo secrets applied to the retired Actions workflow). Same service-role Supabase key class; RLS stays on. |
| F-146 | **Mid-morning scan persistence (cold-load buy candidates).** A third cron mode `scan` (~9:45 ET, post-open-gated; a single Railway Cron Job on the two-slot expression `45 13,14 * * 1-5` with `ALERT_RUN_MODE=scan` fixed on the service) runs the full-universe sector scanner headlessly and persists the result to the single-row `scanner_cache` Supabase table (`save_scanner_cache`, whole DataFrame as JSON, overwrite — no dedup). On a **cold load** the app hydrates `scanner_results` from `scanner_cache` once per session (`load_scanner_cache`), so the Home "More Buy Candidates" + Grow Today new-pick lists populate **without** the user running the ~20s scanner; a "📡 From the {date} auto-scan" stamp shows freshness, and a manual full-universe Home scan re-persists. Inert until the `scanner_cache` table exists (load → None → today's empty-state, unchanged). The Market Scanner page's user-selected **subset** scan is intentionally NOT persisted (it would seed the cold-load list with a non-representative subset). |
| F-147 | **Morning buy-list email (the "offense" alert).** The same `scan` run, after persisting, emails the **high-conviction "New Positions to Initiate"** so the user can act from mobile. `headless_alert_engine.compute_morning_picks` reuses `_build_context` and assembles the SAME inputs the app feeds `build_daily_briefing` (market tone, per-pick composites, news from loaded bundles, **and the macro calendar** — so no gate is looser than Grow Today), then calls the SAME `build_daily_briefing`; the cron keeps only the green **"✅ Go — Composite Confirms"** set (`xref.verdict_reconciled.verdict == "go"` AND composite present — same field as the Home badge), excluding More-Buy-Candidates / unverified / conflicted / over-cap-sector / macro-suppressed names. Each pick is mobile-actionable (ticker · momentum + composite · entry zone · suggested shares/$ · stop) with an **entry-zone "only act if still in $X–$Y" guard** and a "scores as of the morning scan" stamp; *advisory — the user places the trade, nothing auto-traded*. **Silent when nothing clears the bar** (automatic on flat/bear tape — Grow Today defers new entries). Dedups on a per-day ticker-set fingerprint (`alert_state` row 3), saved only on a successful send (so a transient failure is retried by the later DST slot). Inert without `RESEND_API_KEY`. |

### 3.12 AI Insights (LLM intelligence layer)

All LLM-generated output is consolidated on a single dedicated **🧠 AI Insights** nav page. This layer is **strictly additive**: the core app has **zero runtime dependency on LLM connectivity** — if the Anthropic API is offline, rate-limited, or the key is absent, every other page and every gate operates exactly as it does today, and AI Insights shows an explicit offline/"not yet generated" state. **The LLM narrates and synthesizes; it never moves a gate, changes a composite score, or issues a buy/sell** — that authority stays with the rule-based engine. Full design + approval history: [docs/plans/ai-intelligence-layer.md](plans/ai-intelligence-layer.md). Cost-routing: Claude **Sonnet** for these batch jobs (Opus reserved for plan/review). All LLM features ship **inert** until `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set.

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-150 | **Thesis capture (F-1.1).** The Trade Journal BUY form carries an optional "Investment thesis" field (multi-line, nudged but not required, so quick trade logging is unblocked), stored in `trades.user_thesis` (nullable; legacy rows backfill NULL per the backward-compatible-column convention). Pure text storage — **no LLM call** at capture, so it always works. For existing positions with no captured thesis, the AI Insights page offers a "Set thesis" form that writes to the most recent BUY row for that ticker (`db.update_user_thesis`). |
| F-150a | **Thesis authoring at BUY (F-5, Phase 1).** The Trade Journal BUY form offers a "✨ Draft thesis" action (rendered **outside** the form so it can seed the field; disabled when no Anthropic key or in read-only) that assembles the engine's **entry-time** evidence for the ticker — composite + band, fundamentals (revenue growth, margin, earnings trend), next-earnings catalyst, recent headlines, technicals *labelled as entry-timing only*, market tone — reusing the `_grow_composites` cache (else one `load_all` fetch), and asks the LLM to draft a **candidate, falsifiable** thesis (durable claim + evidence-from-package + a "Breaks if …" clause; no price targets / buy-sell language / probabilities / gate values). The draft **seeds an editable field — it is never auto-saved**: the user edits and owns the final text (**author-of-record** invariant), which saves to `trades.user_thesis` exactly as today and feeds F-151 review. `trades.thesis_source` records provenance (`manual` / `ai_draft` / `ai_edited`) so analytics can tell authored from hand-written theses. Strictly additive / zero runtime dependency: offline → the field is a plain text box; a failed draft shows a visible warning, never a silent fake. `thesis_advisor.build_authoring_inputs` / `draft_thesis` (Sonnet). Ships **safe before** the additive `trades.thesis_source` DDL (`save_trade` retries without the column so trade logging never breaks). Plan: [docs/plans/thesis-authoring-analyst-desk.md](plans/thesis-authoring-analyst-desk.md). |
| F-151 | **Thesis review — INTACT / WEAKENING / BROKEN (F-1.2).** For each open position with a non-null `user_thesis`, an LLM reviews the original conviction against current evidence (last-30-day headlines, fundamental + technical trend, last earnings result — all from existing pipelines; the LLM cannot invent events) and returns a verdict + ~100-word summary. Runs **weekly (Sunday evening cron lane)** and **on-demand** ("Re-evaluate" per position). Conservative by default (defaults to WEAKENING when uncertain; BROKEN requires a clear, specific contradiction). **BROKEN does NOT issue an exit** — the rule-based deterioration ladder fires independently; this is awareness ("your reason for being in this has changed"). Stored in `thesis_reviews` (with `inputs_hash` for staleness). Surfaced on AI Insights only — no chip embedded on core pages. |
| F-152 | **Weekly Portfolio Debrief (F-3).** A weekly retrospective: the LLM narrates a structured 4-section debrief (What happened · Decisions you made · Patterns this week · One thing to watch) from a **Python-assembled data package** (portfolio vs. SPY weekly performance + alpha, top contributors/detractors, recs surfaced vs. acted-on) — the LLM narrates only the package, it cannot add facts. Delivered as a **Sunday email** (Resend; the `thesis` cron lane chains into the debrief) and **on-demand** ("Generate Now") on AI Insights. **Build-time guard:** requires ≥ 5 trading days of `daily_snapshots`; below that it shows a data-accumulation message rather than fabricating a trend. Stored in `weekly_debriefs` (unique on `week_ending` → upsert-safe). Email uses the **light-mode-first** template (`render_debrief_email`) with `**markdown**` → HTML rendering. The in-app view adds a **weekly alpha trajectory** bar (portfolio − SPY per week, green = beat) once ≥ 2 weeks of debriefs exist — the trend behind the single-week metric. **Email metric clarification (2026-07-19):** the three KPI cells now carry subtitle lines ("this week · equity positions" / "this week · SPY" / "vs benchmark") and the email closes with a footnote explaining that the weekly ± figure is a **simple equity-positions delta** (end − start equity / start equity, from `daily_snapshots`) — NOT the same metric as the Account page's money-weighted return (Modified Dietz, all-time, flow-adjusted). A leveraged account makes this difference stark: equity positions can show +17.6% for the week while the all-time MWR reads −7.4% (margin debit reduces net account value below contributed capital). The debrief system prompt was also tightened to say "equity positions gained/fell X%" rather than "the portfolio returned X%" to prevent the LLM from framing a positions-only delta as a full account return. **Relabeled 2026-08-05 (coherence audit):** the email KPI cell now reads "Alpha (portfolio, unadjusted)" rather than a bare "Alpha," matching the monthly intelligence report's more qualified "Engine alpha (acted)" label — the underlying calculation and this section's existing clarification are unchanged, only the on-screen label. **NaN guard (2026-07-27):** the SPY-return fetch (`yfinance`) can return a ≥2-row frame with a NaN Close (holiday gap / upstream glitch) rather than raising, which previously survived as `float('nan')` and rendered as the literal "+nan%" in the KPI tiles; `build_debrief_package` now sanitizes this to `None` at the shared entry point both the cron and on-demand callers use, so it displays "N/A" like any other unavailable SPY read. **Capital-flow caption (2026-07-27):** when a BUY/SELL landed inside the joined week (`package['week_had_trades']`), the email now adds an always-visible amber caption under the KPI tiles noting the figures reflect position-size changes as well as price moves — the same always-visible-caption treatment the Summary page's Alpha vs SPY tile got on 2026-07-26, since a footnote-only caveat wasn't preventing the numbers from being read at face value. |
| F-153 | **Monthly Portfolio Intelligence Report (F-4).** A monthly behavioural retrospective that answers the slower questions the weekly debrief cannot — led by **question 0: is the *entry engine* picking well?** Every position originates from a ticker that cleared all gates, scored **Composite ≥ `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65)**, and surfaced under "📈 High-Conviction Entries Only → 🆕 New Positions to Initiate"; v1 grades that engine quality (Q0) and the user's **signal discipline** (Q1: acted vs. ignored, and what each cost/saved). Headline **acted / not-acted counts are distinct tickers** (via `signal_flow`, not daily surfacings) so they match the decision-flow Sankey and never read as misleadingly large name counts; graded-outcome aggregates (alpha, bands) stay surfacing-based means. **Narration count-discipline:** the LLM prompt feeds *only* the distinct name counts (surfaced / acted / not-acted) and explicitly forbids stating any name/recommendation/outcome count greater than the distinct total or inventing a separate "graded-outcomes" tally — closing a prose-vs-chart contradiction where the per-surfacing maturity count (e.g. 26 surfacings) leaked into a distinct-name story (22 names) and read as fabricated. The per-band / per-verdict alpha the narrative quotes is `avg_alpha` — the engine's alpha across **all graded names** in the band (acted or not), the **same** figure the band chart plots — not the acted-only subset (which made the prose say a band was −3.0% while the chart showed it +0.8%). Reuses the existing rule-based scorecard in `recommendations_history.py` (`match_recs_to_trades` → `compute_outcomes` → `summary_stats`/`by_verdict`/`by_composite_band`); the LLM narrates the aggregates, it does not recompute them. Only **matured** recs (`days_since ≥ REC_SCORE_MIN_DAYS`, 5 days) feed graded outcomes. First Sunday of month (existing Sunday cron lane) + on-demand on AI Insights. Stored in `monthly_reports` (unique on `period_end` → upsert-safe). **Shipped 2026-06-27** (v1 = Q0 + Q1 + a pattern; Q2 thesis discipline / Q3 cross-month patterns deferred until data matures, `section_thesis` null in v1). `intelligence_report.py`; the entry-threshold value in the prompt is interpolated from `COMPOSITE_BUY` at call time (never hand-transcribed). The on-page report display is **visual-first**: a **decision-flow Sankey** (Q1 anchor — New Positions → acted/missed → win/loss, via the shared `signal_flow` + `_signal_flow_sankey`, scoped to the report window), an **alpha-by-composite-band bar** (Q0 — does higher conviction deliver higher alpha? via `by_composite_band`'s `avg_alpha`), and the **ranked missed-opportunity bar** (F-161 — named magnitudes a Sankey can't show). All three are built from **one snapshot** (`recommendations_history.report_viz_snapshot` — `signal_flow` + scoped `by_composite_band` + `distinct_missed`/`missed_split`), are fail-soft, and share the pure helpers so they can't drift from the narrative or the Recommendations History page. The snapshot is computed from the **full** (all-rec_type) enriched set so the "acted on via ANY surfacing" safeguard holds across rec_types (a name bought on a buy-candidate day is not mislabelled a missed New Position); the aggregate means are then scoped to `new_pick`. **Report freezing (immutable dated artifact):** the snapshot is **saved with the report** (`monthly_reports.viz_json`, jsonb) and re-rendered verbatim, so a report reads identically whenever it is opened rather than drifting as prices move and names mature; display prefers the frozen snapshot and falls back to a live recompute (same helper) only for rows saved before freezing. The page offers a **month-by-month period picker** (`load_monthly_reports(limit=12)`, deduped to the latest run per calendar month, newest first). Ships inert until the `monthly_reports` DDL (incl. the `viz_json` column) + service-role RLS are applied. |
| F-153a | **F-153 hard boundary — surfaces patterns, never tunes gates.** The monthly report MAY observe that a composite band underperforms (e.g. "the 65–74 band trailed the ≥75 band by X — you may want to review the entry threshold") but MUST NOT change `COMPOSITE_BUY` or any threshold, move a gate, or auto-issue a buy/sell. Threshold changes remain an **investment-policy decision** made in conversation with the user (CLAUDE.md hard rule #1; `constants.py`). |
| F-153b | **F-153 data-maturity guards.** A section is **suppressed** (not fabricated) when its data is too thin: entry-quality (Q0) needs ≥ `MONTHLY_REPORT_MIN_GRADED` (5) matured graded entries; thesis discipline (Q2) needs ≥ M thesis reviews with an outcome window; cross-month patterns (Q3) need ≥ 2 monthly reports. These floors are **measurement windows, not gates** — they never affect what the engine recommends. Q1 (signal discipline) is available from month 1. |
| F-154 | **Analyst Coverage — Ideas Inbox (F-6, Phase 1).** Captures professional analyst research (CNBC Pro, broker notes) into structured, queryable intelligence that both **surfaces new tickers** as watchlist candidates and **enriches** names already held/watchlisted. **Input = paste raw article text** (the source is paywalled/bot-protected so the app cannot fetch it; the user's subscription lives in their browser). The LLM (`analyst_intel.extract_report`, Sonnet) extracts **only atomic per-firm facts** — firm, analyst, rating, price_target, upside_pct — plus ticker/company/date/report_type/thesis/catalysts/risks, and produces **one record per covered stock**: each analyst's rating/target attaches ONLY to the stock they discuss, so a firm's multi-stock "top picks" roundup yields one record per stock rather than a merge (fixing a bug where a BofA roundup mislabeled Visa's/Walmart's targets as Spotify's); names merely *listed* without a rating/target/write-up are skipped, sidebar/consensus widgets for other tickers are ignored, and it never fabricates a number (`null` for anything not stated). `extract_report` returns `list[dict] | None`. **All aggregates (avg/high/low PT, consensus label) are computed in pure Python** (`analyst_intel.derive_consensus`), never by the LLM, so no arithmetic is hallucinated. The extraction is shown in an **editable preview** — **one card per extracted stock** (include/exclude toggle; the human safety net — the user corrects any misread before save), "Save selected" writes each as its own row (partial-save failures are surfaced and kept for retry, never silently dropped) — and the **raw text is persisted** (`analyst_coverage.raw_text`) for re-processing. Saved rows render as an **Ideas Inbox** card library (recent = within `ANALYST_COVERAGE_FRESH_DAYS`, 30) with held/watchlist badges and per-card **▶ Analyze** (routes the ticker to the 📈 Analysis page → existing 5-gate scoring) + **➕ Add to Watchlist**. Awareness-only: analyst PTs/ratings **never modify a composite score, gate, or verdict** — the value is the **"Wall Street vs. your engine" tension** (e.g. five firms rate a one-month-old IPO Buy while the engine scores it below `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65) on thin history). Strictly additive / zero runtime dependency: extraction returns None offline; ships **inert** until the additive `analyst_coverage` DDL + service-role RLS are applied (load returns empty). Consensus **label** boundaries (`ANALYST_CONSENSUS_STRONG_BUY_FRAC` 0.80 / `ANALYST_CONSENSUS_BUY_FRAC` 0.50 / `ANALYST_CONSENSUS_SELL_FRAC` 0.50) are **display-only classifications, not decision thresholds**. `ANALYST_MIN_UPSIDE_PCT` (15) reserved for the Phase-2 Brief chip (unused in Phase 1). **Large-roundup robustness:** a CNBC "biggest analyst calls" roundup (20-30 separate calls) extracts in one pass — the call is budgeted `ANALYST_EXTRACT_MAX_TOKENS` (8000 output tokens) so the JSON array doesn't truncate mid-record, and `ANALYST_EXTRACT_TIMEOUT_SEC` (90s, vs the shared 30s) so the multi-thousand-token generation doesn't time out; both failure modes had surfaced as an identical blind "extraction failed". On any failure the real reason is captured (`analyst_intel.LAST_EXTRACT_ERROR`) and shown as a "Details:" caption, with guidance to paste a section at a time for an unusually large article. Plan: [docs/plans/analyst-coverage.md](plans/analyst-coverage.md). |
| F-154a | **Analyst Coverage Phase 2 — enrichment (SHIPPED, parts ①+②).** ① **Analysis drill-down "🏦 Analyst Coverage" tab** reconciles the two analyst-target sources so they never contradict: the **data-provider consensus** (`analyst_target`=`targetMeanPrice` mean, `num_analyst_opinions` breadth, `target_low`–`target_high` range, implied upside vs current price — the same mean the Base scenario in `targets.py` already uses) shown alongside **your saved Ideas Inbox research** (named firms + PTs + `consensus_rating` + thesis/catalysts/risks from `analyst_coverage`), with a factual reconciliation caption when both exist. ② **F-151 thesis-review evidence injection:** the newest saved coverage row for a held ticker is threaded into the reviewer's evidence (a new optional `analyst_consensus` key in `thesis_advisor.build_review_inputs`, rendered as an "External analyst coverage" prompt line) at BOTH call sites (on-demand + weekly Sunday cron), and flows into `inputs_hash` so fresh coverage can trigger a re-review. **Hard boundary:** `_SYSTEM_PROMPT` amended so provided consensus is **real, citable CONTEXT ONLY — it never upgrades a verdict** (a thesis whose stated rationale is contradicted stays WEAKENING/BROKEN even if analysts remain bullish); the reviewer still grades the user's specific claim against evidence, and analyst data never touches a composite/gate/verdict. Both parts awareness-only, fail-soft, no new constant. **Part ③ (general per-card Brief awareness chip) still NOT built** — a chip on every Brief card runs against the calm-advisor / anti-noise persona (§2B). **Narrow exception (2026-07-09, F-22c):** the awareness-only analyst note IS now shown on the **redeploy candidates inside the opt-in, button-gated Hard-Cap-Breach rebalance plan** — a rare, user-invoked surface (not the always-on Brief), where the user is actively choosing what to buy and the "Wall Street vs. your engine" context is directly decision-relevant. It reuses the F-154b render pattern (`_cached_analyst_coverage_recent` → per-candidate caption) and holds the same invariant: **display-only, never ranks/gates** (the engine composite remains the sole ranker/gate). |
| F-154b | **Analyst Coverage Phase 3 — Grow Today annotation (SHIPPED, display-only).** When a name the engine surfaces under "🆕 New Positions to Initiate" (`_grow_today` → `new_picks`) ALSO has recent saved coverage in the Ideas Inbox, its card carries a small awareness caption (`🏦 Your saved research: <consensus_rating> · avg PT $X (N firms) — awareness only`) — the corroboration moment where the user's paid research and the engine independently flag the same name. Render-layer only (`app.py`): an hourly `@st.cache_data` snapshot of recent coverage (one `load_analyst_coverage(days=ANALYST_COVERAGE_FRESH_DAYS)` query, newest row per ticker) + a per-card caption; renders only when coverage exists, fail-soft. **`daily_briefing.py` untouched — pick order, filtering, scores, and gates are unchanged.** The originally-optional **"soft tie-breaker" (rank picks by analyst conviction) was deliberately NOT built:** reordering recommendations by analyst data would influence the recommendation surface, violating the feature's locked awareness-only invariant and CLAUDE.md's "AI surfaces patterns but never tunes a gate." No new constant. **All Analyst Coverage phases now complete except the deferred Brief chip (③).** |
| F-154c | **Analyst Research Accountability — Research Scorecard (SHIPPED, display-only).** A dedicated 📊 Research Scorecard tab on 🧠 AI Insights tracks whether pasted analyst calls were directionally correct and whether price targets were hit — pure awareness, never feeds scoring or a gate. **Four blocks:** (A) Summary KPI row: Directional Accuracy %, PT Hit Rate %, Evaluable Calls count. (B) Per-call accuracy table: sortable columns (Ticker, Article Date, Consensus, Avg PT, Price @ Article, Exit Price, Return %, PT Proximity %, Status, Window); color-coded rows (green hit, red miss, grey pending). (C) Firm Leaderboard: per-firm aggregates (Calls, Dir. Accuracy %, PT Hit Rate %, Avg Return %) for firms with ≥`ANALYST_ACCURACY_LEADERBOARD_MIN_CALLS` (2) calls. (D) Best & Worst Calls highlight cards (top/bottom 3 by return %, gated on ≥`ANALYST_ACCURACY_HIGHLIGHTS_MIN_EVALUABLE` (5) calls). **Directional accuracy = endpoint close** (where the call ended up); **PT hit = intra-window HIGH** (≥75% of avg_pt, `ANALYST_ACCURACY_PT_HIT_PCT`, accommodates the short `ANALYST_ACCURACY_DIRECTION_DAYS` (30) window). Measurement endpoint is the earlier of (a) the sale date (if ticker sold after article), or (b) article date + 30 days. Pending calls (< 30 days), no-anchor rows (missing price_at_article_date), and rows with no consensus rating are excluded. Two new analyst_coverage columns: `price_at_article_date` (populated at save-time or via `scripts/backfill_analyst_prices.py`) and `composite_score_at_save` (reserved for Phase 3 engine-vs-analyst calibration, deferred). Rows with `no_anchor` status can be fixed on-demand via a per-row "Fetch" button (calls `analyst_intel.fetch_anchor_price()` live with yfinance→FMP failover) rather than waiting for the periodic backfill workflow. `classify_call()` and `is_bullish_rating()` in `stock_analyzer/analyst_intel.py`; `update_analyst_coverage_price()` in `stock_analyzer/db.py`. Awareness-only invariant locked: never touches `valuation_score()`, scoring gates, or any recommendation. |
| F-174 | **Pre-Earnings Playbook — CNBC enrichment (Earnings Playbook Phase 1, SHIPPED 2026-07-13; watchlist auto-add added 2026-07-13).** A "📅 Pre-Earnings" mode on the Ideas Inbox (alongside F-154's "📰 Stock Research" and F-175's Post-Earnings mode) lets the user paste a CNBC Pro earnings-preview article; `earnings_intel.extract_playbook()` (Sonnet 4.6) extracts, per covered stock, `beat_rate_pct` (ONLY from explicit Bespoke-style "beats estimates X% of the time" phrasing, never inferred from qualitative language like "strong history"), `recent_reaction_direction` (bullish/bearish/mixed/unknown, only from an explicit stated pattern), `consensus_growth_pct`, and `what_to_watch_cnbc` — an editable per-stock card preview saves selected records to `earnings_context` (§6.18). **"💾 Save earnings preview" also adds every saved ticker to the watchlist** (skipping tickers already present, `db.save_watchlist`) — a CNBC pre-earnings mention is a research signal in its own right, and without this the ticker would never enter `scan_sectors()`'s universe (curated `SECTOR_UNIVERSE` + watchlist `extra_tickers`) and so could never surface under Grow Today regardless of its composite score, mirroring F-154's Stock Research mode (which already treats Ideas Inbox saves as watchlist candidates). `earnings_advisor.build_earnings_playbook()` takes an optional `earnings_context` batch and threads `beat_rate`/`reaction` into `_recommend()`, which gains two new REDUCE conditions (weight ≥ 5% required for both): poor beat history (`beat_rate < EARNINGS_BEAT_RATE_REDUCE_THRESHOLD` (60.0) AND composite < `COMPOSITE_BUY` (65)) and bearish reaction history (`reaction == "bearish"` AND composite < `EARNINGS_BEARISH_REACTION_COMPOSITE_GATE` (75)) — and annotates the pre-existing HOLD_OR_ADD condition with the CNBC context when beat rate ≥ `EARNINGS_BEAT_RATE_STRONG_THRESHOLD` (75.0) or reaction is bullish. **CNBC data is evaluated strictly after `_recommend()`'s pre-existing EXIT check and can never override an already-determined EXIT.** Playbook cards on 🔔 Catalyst Watch show a "📰 CNBC" badge when context exists, plus beat-rate/consensus-growth lines, color-coded reaction-history text, and the CNBC "what to watch" note as the first bullet. `_earnings_posture_alerts_cache` (EXIT+REDUCE count) badges the Catalyst Watch nav entry. Ships inert until the `earnings_context` DDL is applied. Extends F-11. Plan: [docs/plans/earnings-playbook.md](plans/earnings-playbook.md). |
| F-175 | **Post-Earnings results capture — Finnhub auto-fetch (Earnings Playbook Phase 2a, SHIPPED 2026-07-13).** A "📥 Post-Earnings Results" mode on the Ideas Inbox: the user multi-selects held tickers that just reported (from `_last_held_tickers`), sets a report date, and clicks "⬇️ Fetch EPS from Finnhub" — `earnings_intel.fetch_recent_results()` calls Finnhub `/stock/earnings?limit=1` per ticker (90-day lookback, **no LLM call**) and returns `actual_eps`/`estimated_eps`/`eps_beat`/`eps_surprise_pct`/`quarter_period`. Revenue and guidance are NOT available on Finnhub's free tier, so each result card shows a Beat/Miss/Unknown chip with editable EPS fields plus a guidance-direction selectbox (raised/maintained/lowered/withdrawn/unknown) the user always sets manually, and an optional note. An "Enter manually" path shows blank cards for tickers Finnhub doesn't cover. Saves to `earnings_results` (§6.19). **Design note:** this replaces an original paste-based design — CNBC does not publish consolidated post-earnings roundups, making a per-ticker paste flow impractical; the LLM paste path (`earnings_intel.extract_results()`) remains in the module but is not wired to the UI. Ships inert until the `earnings_results` DDL is applied. Plan: [docs/plans/earnings-playbook.md](plans/earnings-playbook.md). |
| F-176 | **F-1 Earnings Thesis Checkpoint (Earnings Playbook Phase 2b, SHIPPED 2026-07-13).** When a held position has both a saved `user_thesis` (F-150) and an `earnings_results` row within the last 14 days (`db.load_earnings_result(ticker, lookback_days=14)`), an "Earnings checkpoint" prompt appears on 🧠 AI Insights → Positions. "Review earnings checkpoint" calls `thesis_advisor.generate_earnings_thesis_update()` (Sonnet 4.6), which derives a simple `earnings_signal` (beat/miss/mixed/unknown) from `eps_beat`/`rev_beat`/`guidance_direction` and returns a suggested `INTACT`/`WEAKENING`/`BROKEN` status + a 2–3 sentence rationale. **Suggestion-only — it never auto-saves.** Confirm writes a `thesis_reviews` row (F-151's table) tagged `summary="[Earnings checkpoint <date>] <rationale>"`; Dismiss clears the pending suggestion unsaved. Returns `None` on any failure (no API key, timeout, missing thesis/result) — the checkpoint prompt simply doesn't appear, matching the rest of the AI layer's graceful degradation. Plan: [docs/plans/earnings-playbook.md](plans/earnings-playbook.md). |
| F-196 | **Thesis Red Team Agent — Phase 1 (SHIPPED 2026-07-23, display-only).** New "⚠️ Red Team" tab on 🧠 AI Insights. Every trading day, each held position is scored 0–100 on four adversarial signals: (1) deterioration tier from `exit_signals` (WATCH=10pts, TRIM=22pts, EXIT=30pts); (2) 20-session relative strength vs SPY — negative RS contributes linearly up to 30pts via `compute_relative_strength()` in `exit_advisor.py`; (3) 5-session composite score delta from `thesis_erosion_cache.signals_snapshot` — falling composite contributes up to 25pts (self-referential bootstrap, inert for first 5 trading days); (4) analyst PT revision direction from `analyst_target_snapshots` — cut=15pts, flat=7pts, up=0pts. Total 0–100; display bands: 0–24 Intact · 25–49 Softening · 50–74 Eroding · 75–100 Breaking. Each signal renders with a 🔴/🟢 direction indicator and a plain-English interpretation. Cache: `thesis_erosion_cache` table, one row per (ticker, ET date). **Phase 2 — SHIPPED 2026-07-27, display-only.** Adds a Haiku-generated **counter-evidence** narrative (2–3 grounded counter-arguments, `stock_analyzer/thesis_red_team.py::generate_counter_evidence()`; UI-labelled "Counter-evidence," deliberately not "Bear case" — that term is reserved for the ⚔️ Debate feature's distinct `bear_case_score` mechanism, and the two sit near each other on Act Today cards) once a position's erosion score crosses `THESIS_EROSION_HAIKU_MIN` (30) **and** a `user_thesis` is on record, plus the **Pre-Mortem loop**: the investor's own `trades.premortem_commitment` (F-187) is read back as LLM context, and the model is instructed to quote it explicitly when current evidence supports it — closing the loop between "what would make me wrong" (ex-ante) and "here's what's actually going wrong" (continuous). **Deliberately excludes the erosion score/label and the still-inert PT-revision placeholder from the prompt** — an earlier review round caught that the aggregate score always launders a hardcoded PT placeholder back in, so the narrative is built only from primitive, individually-real signals (tier, RS vs SPY, composite delta, price/entry/age), each independently coerced through a finite-value check before ever reaching the prompt (a `None`/`NaN`/`inf` producer failure must never surface as a fabricated-looking citation). Output is `[{claim, severity, signal_basis}]`, 0–3 items — an empty list is a valid "no grounded counter-evidence found today" result, explicitly distinct from `None` (the call failed); this distinction is load-bearing for the day-cache (`counter_evidence` persists even when empty) and is tested via regression tests in `tests/test_thesis_red_team.py`. **Second surface:** a read-only "⚠️ Red Team — since you bought" expander on 🏠 Home's Act Today deterioration TRIM/EXIT cards (`_render_act_card`), sitting alongside the existing "⚔️ Challenge This Exit" debate button — a pure `thesis_erosion_cache` read, triggers no compute. **6 Opus design-review rounds** (plan `docs/plans/thesis-red-team-phase2.md`) — most catching a variant of the same bug class (a value assumed clean that could actually be `None`, `NaN`, or a truthy-but-garbage string) on a different signal each round; Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking. **Phase 3 — SHIPPED 2026-07-28, commit `0aa1349`, display-only.** A "⚠️ Thesis Under Pressure" annotation at the end of 🏠 Home's Daily Brief (after the Buy Candidates overflow section, before Evening Debrief), for any held ticker where today's erosion score meets `THESIS_EROSION_BRIEF_MIN` (50) and its baseline (the most recent PRIOR trading day with an actual scored row — walked back up to `THESIS_EROSION_BASELINE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`=10 calendar days, since a `thesis_erosion_cache` row exists only on a day the user visited the Red Team tab, so literal calendar-yesterday is frequently blank) was below that floor, OR the score jumped `THESIS_EROSION_BRIEF_JUMP` (15) points or more since that baseline. No baseline found within the lookback window ⇒ treated as a first-ever observation, gated on the absolute floor alone. **Pure `thesis_erosion_cache` read — never triggers a compute** (compute only happens when AI Insights → Red Team is visited), so this annotation is usually empty on the day's first Home load and only populates once that tab has been visited today. **Deduped against tickers already shown as a deterioration/Act card in the same Brief run** via `decision_bucket._ticker()`'s canonical resolver (not a raw `ticker` key read — macro `PROTECTIVE_TRIM`/`WATCH` items carry `ticker=None` with the real subject in `action["trim_ticker"]`) — erosion score's tier component is sourced from the same `exit_signals` detector that fires those cards, so re-flagging an already WATCH/TRIM/EXIT ticker here would restate, not add, information. **Opus plan review: FIX-FIRST (2 blocking) → fixed → SHIP.** Round 1 caught that a literal calendar-yesterday baseline would silently re-fire every Monday for every stable ≥50 name (Sunday never has a cache row, so the code fell into the first-observation branch every week — a stable-state-noise regression against the app's calm-advisor posture) and the deterioration-card double-surface above; both fixed. Round 2 (re-review of the fix) caught the fix's own new gap — the dedup set read the raw `ticker` key, missing macro trim/watch items' `ticker=None`/`trim_ticker` shape — and a hardcoded `range(10)` lookback that should be a named policy constant per Hard Rule #1; both fixed (new constant `THESIS_EROSION_BASELINE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, canonical `_ticker()` resolver). Final Opus review: SHIP, 0 blocking. **Neither the erosion score nor the counter-evidence narrative nor this annotation modifies composite score, any gate, or any recommendation — display-only, permanently. Closes the Thesis Red Team Agent feature — all 3 phases now shipped.** Plan: [docs/plans/thesis-red-team-agent.md](plans/thesis-red-team-agent.md) (Phases 1 & 3), [docs/plans/thesis-red-team-phase2.md](plans/thesis-red-team-phase2.md) (Phase 2). |
| F-195 | **Lessons Learned Library (Trade Journal Decision Context).** On every SELL / TRIM trade write in the Trade Journal, a structured `lesson_category` dropdown appears in the Decision Context expander — 10 fixed taxonomy options (Followed the signal, Thesis broke, Held too long, Cut too early, Ignored the exit signal, Position sized beyond conviction, Macro/sector headwind, Earnings surprise, Panic/fear sell, Pre-mortem call was right) plus "— (skip)" — complemented by the existing free-text `lesson` field (relabeled "Details (optional)"). `decision_journal.compute_patterns()` returns `lesson_category_counts` (frequency dict per category) and `lesson_category_by_outcome` (wins/losses/avg_pnl cross-tab per category). Trade Journal Decision Journal section upgraded to "Pattern Library": displays each category's frequency count + W/L outcome summary in a KPI grid, then the full entry log with category chips on each trade. Awareness only — zero gates, zero investment recommendations. |
| F-197 | **Multi-Agent Debate Agent — Phase 1: entry candidate debates (SHIPPED 2026-07-23, display-only).** A "⚔️ Debate" button on every 📈 Grow Today entry candidate card runs a structured 4-round Bull vs Bear debate followed by an impartial Judge verdict — 5 sequential Haiku calls (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`): Bull opens → Bear responds → Bull rebuts → Bear closes → Judge scores both sides 0–100 and issues `verdict` (`bull_wins` when the score gap ≥ `DEBATE_WIN_MARGIN` (20, module-level in `stock_analyzer/debate_agent.py`, not a gate/policy threshold) / `bear_wins` / `contested`), a one-sentence `key_dispute` (the specific claim Bull and Bear most disagree on), and a `grounded` flag (false when either side argued generically instead of citing supplied evidence). Both agents debate the identical evidence corpus (`build_entry_corpus()`: composite score + 4 pillar scores, sector, 5-/20-day momentum, 20-session relative strength vs SPY via `compute_relative_strength()`) — no information asymmetry between agents in Phase 1. **User-triggered only** — no cron, no page-load compute. Day-cached to `debate_cache` (one row per `ticker` × `debate_type` × ET date); a cache hit renders instantly with no new LLM calls. Capped at `DEBATE_SESSION_CEILING` (3) new debates per session — cache hits don't count against the cap. The button is hidden unless the ticker has a fetched composite score, and a failed/empty debate run is never cached and never consumes a session slot, so a transient API failure can be retried immediately rather than showing a false "Contested" verdict until the next day. **Debate verdict never modifies the composite score, reorders Grow Today candidates, or feeds any gate — purely a deliberation aid the user reads before deciding.** **Phase 2 — "Challenge This Exit" (SHIPPED 2026-07-24, D2 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2):** a "⚔️ Challenge This Exit" button on single-name deterioration TRIM/EXIT cards (Today's Brief 🔴 Act Today lane; gated on the card's `kind` field, never WATCH) runs the same 5-Haiku debate in `debate_type="exit"` mode — the Bull defends continuing to hold (anchored on the saved `user_thesis` when present, and explicitly barred from sunk-cost "hold because we're underwater" reasoning), the Bear argues the fired deterioration signal citing the real `exit_advisor.assess_holding` numbers (drawdown-from-peak, sessions below trend, relative strength) via `build_exit_corpus()`. The 4 round prompts branch entry/exit; the Judge is unchanged (`bull_wins`=hold defensible, `bear_wins`=exit supported, `contested`). Shares the `debate_cache` table (`debate_type="exit"`) and the `DEBATE_SESSION_CEILING` per-session cap with entry debates. **A second opinion only — never suppresses the card, changes the deterioration tier, or touches the composite.** **Phase 3 — Debate Log (SHIPPED 2026-07-27):** a new "⚔️ Debate Log" 6th tab on 🧠 AI Insights, read-only and diagnostic, showing every stored `debate_cache` row (both `entry` and `exit` types) chronologically via new `db.load_all_debates()` (most-recent-first, `debate_date` then `created_at` as a same-day tiebreak; excludes `corpus_snapshot`, not needed for display). Built **forward-only** per an explicit user decision — `debate_cache` held only ~4 rows (all entry-type) when this shipped, and the feature renders an honest "no debates yet" state at any volume rather than waiting for more data to accumulate (same precedent as Behavioral Fingerprint, F-193). Each row expands to show the full transcript, scores, key dispute, and grounded warning via two functions extracted from the two existing render sites: `_debate_verdict_icon(verdict, debate_type)` (pure — icon/label only, used by the caller for the expander title, entry/exit-only tabs, and the log alike) and `_render_debate_result(row, debate_type)` (body rendering, including the exit-only "does not change the recommendation" disclaimer). This extraction closed a real risk caught in Opus design review: the original refactor sketch silently dropped that exit-only disclaimer and used a circular return-value contract that was unusable at either existing call site — both fixed before any code was written. No filter/search UI or aggregate chart in this phase (deliberately deferred — the original plan's own "What NOT to build" section defers a historical chart to "Phase 3+," and a filter UI has nothing to filter at today's volume). No new `constants.py` entries, no new cache table, no new LLM call — the cheapest class of addition on this roadmap. **This closes the entire Multi-Agent Debate feature and the last of the three originally-tracked Agentic Intelligence Roadmap gated phases.** Plans: [docs/plans/multi-agent-debate.md](plans/multi-agent-debate.md), [docs/plans/exit-red-team.md](plans/exit-red-team.md), [docs/plans/multi-agent-debate-phase3.md](plans/multi-agent-debate-phase3.md). |
| F-225 | **Portfolio Q&A (💬 Ask) — SHIPPED 2026-08-01, display-only.** New 7th tab "💬 Ask" on 🧠 AI Insights, sibling to Debate Log. A retrospective natural-language Q&A surface over trade history and past recommendations — **deliberately not a live `session_state` reader**: the two supported question shapes are "how many trades did I make in a date range, and what was the gain/loss on each" and "why did a past recommendation lose/gain money over N trading days." Two-step LLM pattern (`stock_analyzer/portfolio_qa.py`), not an agentic tool-loop: (1) `parse_question()` (Haiku) turns free text into a structured `{intent, ticker, start_date, end_date, horizon_days}` query — today's date is passed in explicitly so relative phrases ("last week") resolve against the app's actual calendar, not the model's guess; a question that doesn't fit either shape, or a `rec_outcome` question with no resolvable ticker, returns `intent: "unsupported"` rather than guessing; (2) a plain-Python deterministic lookup runs (`trades_in_range()` — SELLs report the already-stored `realized_pnl`, BUYs report unrealized P&L against the ticker's current price only if still held, else labelled "position_closed" rather than attempting new FIFO-lot matching; `recommendation_outcome()` — exact ticker/date match against `recommendations`, **never guesses the nearest one**, returns `found: False` explicitly when no row matches); (3) `narrate_answer()` (Haiku) turns the result into plain English, instructed to use ONLY the given facts and state plainly when a pillar sub-score wasn't recorded rather than inferring a reason. **The parsed query is shown back to the user as a caption before the answer** (e.g. "Looking at: AAPL, surfaced 2026-07-20, +5 trading days") so a misparse is visible, not silently wrong. Fails open throughout (`None` on any API error/missing key) — shows the same explicit "AI layer offline" state as every other AI Insights tab. **Companion change (same date):** `recommendations` gained three nullable columns (`t_score`, `bq_score`, `val_score`, alongside the existing `s_score`) so recommendations surfaced from this date forward carry the full 4-pillar breakdown that explains *why* a composite score landed where it did — before this, only the bare `composite_score` was persisted even though the pillar breakdown was already computed in memory at brief-build time and simply discarded. **Forward-only — existing rows are not backfilled**, so `rec_outcome` answers for pre-2026-08-01 recommendations are capped at composite score + the auto-generated thesis line, and the narration says so explicitly rather than fabricating a pillar reason. New constants: `QA_REC_OUTCOME_DEFAULT_HORIZON_DAYS` (5), `QA_MAX_RANGE_DAYS` (365) — query-scoping parameters, not investment-policy thresholds. **Never gates, scores, or modifies any recommendation — display-only, same class as the rest of AI Insights.** **v1.1 (same day):** live use surfaced two real UX gaps. (1) A third question shape was added — `trade_lookup` ("what was my trade on AAPL?", no date range needed) via a new `trades_for_ticker()` function (shares the same `_row_outcome()` P&L logic `trades_in_range()` uses, refactored out to avoid duplicating it). (2) An `unsupported` decline used to give zero indication of *why* — now every `unsupported` result carries a `reason` string (Python-determined for the two structural gaps — "needs a date range" / "couldn't confidently identify a ticker" — or the model's own short explanation for anything else), shown directly in the UI instead of a generic three-bullet fallback. The strict "never guess a ticker" rule itself was deliberately kept (a real live decline: "Robin Hood" wasn't confidently mapped to `HOOD`) — the fix is telling the user why it declined, not loosening the anti-hallucination guard. (A separate, prior same-day fix already closed a `parse_parsed_query` JSON-parsing bug where Haiku's trailing prose after a `` ```json `` fence broke `json.loads` — see `docs/plans/portfolio-qa.md` for both incidents.) **v2 (2026-08-02), three "complete the circle" additions, all retrospective narration only — no new advice, gate, or recommendation:** (1) Answers now surface a trade's own recorded `user_thesis`/`notes`/`lesson` (whichever are non-empty, joined into one short "reasoning" line) inline in `trades_in_range`/`trade_lookup` answers and in the "Show the underlying trades" table's new Notes column. (2) The Ask tab is now conversational (`st.chat_message`/`st.chat_input`, first use of this Streamlit pattern in the app) — `st.session_state["_qa_history"]` holds the turn list; `parse_question()` gains an optional bounded `history` param (last `QA_HISTORY_TURNS` exchanges) so a referential follow-up ("what about MSFT instead?") resolves, but `narrate_answer()` deliberately still sees ONLY the current query's facts, never conversation history — multi-turn context helps interpret what's being asked, never what's true. (3) A `rec_outcome` answer now cross-references the matching BUY trade (found via a new `_find_buy_trade_for_rec()`, matching on ticker + a `QA_PREMORTEM_TRADE_MATCH_WINDOW_DAYS`-day window after the recommendation date — never guessing further out) and narrates its recorded Pre-Mortem risk case (`premortem_case_against`) and exit commitment (`premortem_commitment`) against what the price data actually shows, retrospectively only; when no matching BUY exists at all, the answer says so plainly rather than staying silent. **A fourth idea — a forward-looking "what's the current call on X" intent — was explicitly considered and declined**: the user chose to keep the "AI narrates, never originates" redline closed for this pass; recorded as a parked, not-yet-approved provision in `docs/plans/portfolio-qa.md` rather than built. New constants: `QA_HISTORY_TURNS` (3), `QA_PREMORTEM_TRADE_MATCH_WINDOW_DAYS` (3). **v2.1, same day — closed-lot P&L bug fix (a live user cross-check of an AAPL trade history caught this):** the "still held → unrealized" check above was ticker-level only (is this ticker held ANYWHERE right now), not per-lot — so a ticker with a full sell-then-rebuy round trip had **every** historical BUY row marked "Unrealized (still held)" against today's price, including lots already fully closed weeks earlier, double-reporting P&L already shown on the matching SELL row. Fixed via a new `_closed_shares_by_buy()` that runs the existing, already-tested `investor_mirror.build_closed_lots()` FIFO matcher over the ticker's full trade history (not just the queried range) to tell a fully-closed lot apart from a genuinely open one; only the fully-closed case was corrected — a partially-sold lot still marks against the full original share count, which the UI already caveats separately. Plan: [docs/plans/portfolio-qa.md](plans/portfolio-qa.md). |

### 3.13 Recommendations History (retrospective scorecard)

The 📊 Recommendations History page is the **rule-based** retrospective over every pick Today's Brief has surfaced (no LLM): it cross-references the `recommendations` table with `trades` to grade acted vs. missed outcomes on **alpha** (return minus SPY over the same window). It is the data substrate the F-4 monthly report (F-153) narrates. Logic lives in `recommendations_history.py` (pure — no Streamlit / DB / API). (2026-07-15: restructured into three internal tabs — **📊 Summary**, **📈 Trends**, **📋 Full Table** — for navigability; no content added or removed.)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| F-160 | **Scorecard rollups.** Action rate, avg acted vs. missed outcome + **alpha**, best/worst named outcomes, daily acted-vs-missed volume chart, and breakdowns by composite band (`by_composite_band`), cross-check verdict (`by_verdict`), and rec type. Outcome aggregates exclude recs younger than `REC_SCORE_MIN_DAYS` (5 days) — shown but flagged ⏳ maturing. "Total recs" counts *surfacings* (a name recurs each day it qualifies), not distinct tickers. **Known historical gap (fixed going forward 2026-07-26, commit `51b2441`):** `buy_candidate` recs surfaced *before* this fix never captured a price snapshot, so the Conflicted verdict bucket and the Hold-zone/Sell-zone composite bands show blank missed-alpha for those older rows — not a data-maturity issue, a permanent gap for that historical slice. New `buy_candidate` recs resolve correctly. |
| F-160a | **Signal-flow Sankey.** A Plotly Sankey funnels New Positions to Initiate → acted / not-acted → win / loss / flat-or-open (`signal_flow`). **Distinct by ticker** (not surfacings); scoped to `new_pick` (awareness feed excluded, same as F-161); win/loss count **matured** outcomes only (⏳ maturing → Flat / Open) so it can never disagree with the F-160 aggregates. Built from the pre-status-filter snapshot so both branches always render regardless of the acted/missed filter. |
| F-161 | **Missed Opportunity analysis.** Collapses **actionable** recommendations the user **never acted on** to **distinct tickers** (`distinct_missed`, default `rec_types=("new_pick",)`), split into **missed winners** (rose) vs **dodged losers** (fell) with named outliers (biggest miss / biggest dodge, `missed_split`) and a ranked green/red horizontal bar of skipped-name outcomes. **Scope = New Positions to Initiate only** (`new_pick` — names that cleared all gates); the awareness-only **More Buy Candidates** feed (`buy_candidate` — Conflicted / Unverified / sub-`COMPOSITE_BUY` names the App flags to skip) is **excluded**, because skipping those is the *correct* call, not a miss. The acted-on check spans **all** of a ticker's surfacings (a name bought on a buy-candidate day is not wrongly flagged), but the representative outcome + count come from its `new_pick` surfacings only. Magnitude is framed **per-$1k notional** (a read on *selection* quality) — **never** a fabricated portfolio-level "you would have gained X%" counterfactual, because capital, the 15%/35% concentration caps, and the entry gates all bind. Same actionable scope governs the F-153 monthly report (`build_report_package(rec_types=("new_pick",))`). |
| F-201 | **Missed-Opportunity Pattern (O1 of Agentic Intelligence Roadmap v2) — SHIPPED 2026-07-24, commit `b11e34a`.** New "🔍 Missed-Opportunity Pattern" section on 📊 Recommendations History → Summary tab, sibling to (not nested inside) F-161's flat missed-opportunities list — built from the **unfiltered** `_rh_enriched_all` snapshot, deliberately not the page's status-filtered `_rh_enriched`, so `distinct_missed()`'s "acted via ANY surfacing" safeguard can't silently break under the Acted-only/Missed-only dropdown. Deliberately the "P6 done right" idea (P6/Autonomous Pattern Discovery was shelved the same day — its only untapped dimension, `decision_context`, had 17 rows); this points the same unsupervised-discovery instinct at real, already-accumulating data instead. A naive statistical rebuild (sector/price-band bucketing with a `min_n` gate, mirroring `predictive_analytics.py`'s `by_sector_alpha`/`by_conviction`) was ruled out during planning — only 44 distinct tickers have ever surfaced as `new_pick`, so most buckets would sit at 0-1 members, the same wall that sank P6. Instead, one Haiku call/day (`stock_analyzer/missed_opportunity.py::generate_missed_opportunity_patterns()`) finds a **descriptive** pattern (never causal — "what these skipped names have in common," not "why you skipped them," to avoid inviting an unverifiable psychology claim that reads as forward-looking advice) across F-161's missed-ticker corpus, enriched with 3 pre-computed **closed-category** fields — `sector` (empty→"Other"), `price_band` (fixed bands), `composite_band` (reuses `by_composite_band`'s exact `COMPOSITE_STRONG_BUY`/`COMPOSITE_BUY`/`COMPOSITE_HOLD` thresholds, no new breakpoints) — alongside the already-present `verdict`/`outcome_label`. A **two-layer fabrication guard** (pure Python): every cited ticker must exist in the real corpus, AND the claimed `shared_dimension`/`shared_value` must mechanically match that specific ticker's real value for that specific dimension — a non-conforming ticker is dropped from its pattern, not the whole pattern discarded. Every field here is a closed, checkable category, so this guard is **stricter** than free-text quote-matching, not a weaker analog of it. Includes ALL outcomes (not winners-only — a posture safeguard against becoming a FOMO amplifier) and renders each pattern's real win/dodge/flat mix, so a pattern can never read as "these were all winners, buy the next one like it." **Never cross-references a found pattern against current Grow Today candidates, the watchlist, or held positions** — the one design choice that would turn a retrospective pattern into a disguised forward-looking buy signal; any future proposal to add such a join needs its own Opus review. Day-cached to new `missed_opportunity_cache` table (RLS included). No `constants.py` entries — sample-size floors (`_MIN_MISSED_TICKERS=3`, matching `predictive_analytics.py`'s `min_n=3` convention; `_MIN_PATTERN_TICKERS=2`) are module-level tuning, not policy thresholds. **Diagnostic only — never gates, scores, or modifies `distinct_missed()`/`missed_split()` themselves.** Opus design review (FIX-FIRST → resolved): the original "no verification layer needed" reasoning was backwards (structured fields are *easier* to mechanically verify than free prose, not harder) — fixed by adding the predicate-verification guard; also fixed a data-source bug where the naive implementation would have read the page's filtered snapshot instead of the unfiltered one. Opus pre-ship code review: SHIP, 0 blocking, 2 of 4 non-blocking fixed (a constant-drift risk between `app.py` and the module; a cosmetic composite-band label mismatch vs `by_composite_band`). Plan: [docs/plans/missed-opportunity-pattern.md](plans/missed-opportunity-pattern.md). |

## 4. Non-Functional Requirements

### 4.1 Performance

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-01 | Portfolio data (held tickers) must load within 15 seconds on app open |
| NF-02 | Individual stock analysis (load_all) must complete within 10 seconds for a single ticker |
| NF-03 | Market Scanner full run must complete within 30 seconds for the 73-ticker universe |
| NF-04 | Cached data (load_all, sector returns) must be served from cache within 1 second on repeat calls |

### 4.2 Data Freshness

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-10 | Live prices refresh every 60 seconds during market hours via Streamlit fragment. During market hours the live-price primary (Finnhub) returns real-time US quotes (vs yfinance's ~15-min delay); when the market is closed all sources report the last regular-session close. |
| NF-10a | The held-position price cross-check is cached 5 minutes (`_cached_price_xcheck`) — a periodic integrity check, not a per-rerun live call — to bound keyed-provider quota usage. |
| NF-11 | load_all() cache TTL: 30 minutes (1800 seconds) |
| NF-12 | Sector ETF returns cache TTL: 60 minutes (3600 seconds) |
| NF-13 | Market indices cache TTL: not cached — fetched on each Daily Briefing load |
| NF-14 | When market is closed, the app must display a context note indicating data reflects last close |
| NF-15 | Pre-market intel cache TTL: 5 minutes (300 seconds); keyed on held tickers + watchlist to ensure correct data after holdings change |
| NF-16 | Risk-free rate (^IRX) cache TTL: 24 hours (86400 seconds); fallback to 4.5% if Yahoo Finance unavailable |
| NF-17 | All date comparisons must use America/New_York (ET) timezone via pytz to prevent midnight UTC rollover producing wrong calendar dates — both hosted containers (Railway primary, Streamlit Cloud fallback) run UTC, so the pytz conversion is never optional on either |

### 4.3 Reliability

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-20 | Yahoo Finance API failures must be caught and displayed as warnings, not crash the app |
| NF-21 | Rate-limit (HTTP 429) responses from Yahoo Finance must be retried with linear backoff (3 attempts, 3s base) |
| NF-22 | API health events (`success`, `rate_limit`, `error`, `empty`, `auth`, `quota`, `parse`) must be recorded via the `api_health` module per provider (`yahoo_finance`, `finnhub`, `fmp`, `fred`, `supabase`). Event routing goes through `classify_error()` (`providers/_util.py`): `auth` = 4xx credential/plan errors, `quota` = 429 with quota-exceeded wording, `rate_limit` = generic transient throttling, `parse` = JSON-decode or empty-body failures, `error` = everything else. Health thresholds: RED on `auth_errors ≥ 1` or `rate_limits ≥ 3` or `consecutive_errors ≥ 5`; YELLOW on `quotas ≥ 1` or `parse_errors ≥ 3` or error-rate > 20%. The provider circuit-breaker cooldown trips on `auth_errors ≥ 3` (matches `rate_limits` threshold). |
| NF-23 | Supabase connectivity failures must display a user-facing error without exposing credentials |
| NF-24 | No single market-data source is a hard dependency for live prices or the analysis bundle. A provider hard-failure (error, rate-limit after retries, or empty) must fail over to the next configured provider in the chain; the request fails only if every capable provider fails. |
| NF-25 | Keyed-provider errors must never leak the API key — error text surfaced or logged must have the key redacted (e.g. FMP's `?apikey=` in request URLs). |
| NF-26 | The headless alert cron (§3.11) must be **fail-safe and non-blocking**: inert when `RESEND_API_KEY` is absent (computes/emails nothing rather than erroring), and guarded by per-ET-day idempotency (Supabase `alert_state`) so a job firing on both DST-straddling UTC slots delivers at most one email per lane per day. **Exit-code clause amended by F-239 (2026-08-16):** ordinary failures still exit 0, but a lane that cannot reach Supabase **must exit 1** and email the owner. Silence must never be the app's way of reporting that it could not see. |
| NF-27 | The cron and the app must share one data + credential path: `bundle_loader.load_bundle` (extracted from the app's `load_all`) and env-first credential resolution (`SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_KEY` → `st.secrets`). The cron's Supabase client is a module-global singleton (`db._CLIENT`), not `st.cache_resource`, so it runs without a Streamlit context. |
| NF-28 | **FMP daily quota counter (rate-limit-resilience Phase 3).** `db.increment_daily_quota("fmp")` must fire after every successful FMP request; `db.get_daily_quota("fmp")` reads today's count from `api_quota_log` (§6.17 schema). When count ≥ `FMP_DAILY_SOFT_CAP` (220), `orchestrator._providers_for()` must drop FMP from the capable list; it falls through to the full list if all providers are suppressed — never a hard-block. **DDL applied in Supabase (2026-07-10) — feature is active.** `api_health.get_fmp_daily_quota()` caches the Supabase read (5-min TTL) so the orchestrator hot-path is never a per-call DB hit. |

### 4.4 Security

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-30 | All secrets (Supabase URL/key, LLM API keys, market-data keys `FINNHUB_API_KEY` / `FMP_API_KEY`, `[fred] api_key`) must be stored in the hosting platform's secret store, never in code or committed files. On Railway (primary) that's flat Service Variables; on the Streamlit Cloud fallback it's the Secrets dashboard (native TOML), materialized into a real `.streamlit/secrets.toml` at container startup by `railway_start.sh` (docs/architecture.md §9.1b) so the ~50 direct `st.secrets.get(...)` call sites work unmodified on either platform. A missing market-data key degrades gracefully (provider skipped), never crashes. The headless cron (§3.11) reads the same secrets from **Railway service variables** (incl. `RESEND_API_KEY` / `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` / `ALERT_EMAIL_FROM`) independent of either UI host; its Supabase key is the same service-role key class as Streamlit (RLS stays on per NF-32). |
| NF-31 | Single-user personal app, gated by a password screen (`app.py::_check_password()`) — not multi-user/role-based auth. `[app] password` / `APP_PASSWORD` grants full (owner) access; an optional `[app] readonly_password` / `APP_READONLY_PASSWORD` grants a read-only viewer role (§ readonly viewer, `project_readonly_viewer`). A failed-attempt counter adds a 2s delay after 3+ fails and a 5-minute lockout after 10 fails (`_login_fails`/`_login_locked_until` session_state) — added for the Railway pilot, which has no Streamlit-native "Private app" OAuth layer on top of the password gate. |
| NF-32 | Row Level Security is **enabled** on all public-schema tables with `FOR ALL TO service_role` policies. The Streamlit secret `[supabase] key` must be the service-role / secret key (bypasses RLS); the publishable/anon key has no matching policy and is denied. This is defense-in-depth: a leaked publishable key cannot access portfolio data. |

### 4.5 Usability

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-40 | All navigation between pages must use session-state-based routing (nav_page) without page reload |
| NF-41 | Analyze buttons must navigate to Stock Analysis and set a Back button to return to the origin page |
| NF-42 | The app must be usable on a standard desktop browser; no mobile-specific layout required |
| NF-43 | Urgent action count must be visible on Today's Brief without any click (2026-07-12: Today's Brief is a promoted full-width section, not a tab, so this is now always-on rather than a tab-badge) |
| NF-44 | Discovery movers surface under "New Positions to Initiate" with the context label **"Breakout today"** (not the scanner momentum score). The momentum score is not applicable to movers — they enter via day-change breakout, bypassing the momentum gate, and the score does not appear in Analysis Deep Dive. `reconcile_signals()` in `signal_reconciliation.py` accepts `is_mover: bool = False`; when `True`, `mom_str = "Breakout today"`. The "Momentum X/100" prefix is omitted from the mover header chip in `app.py`. |

### 4.6 Maintainability

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| NF-50 | All domain logic (scoring, signals, risk, briefing) must live in the `stock_analyzer/` package, not in `app.py` |
| NF-51 | `app.py` is responsible for UI rendering and orchestration only |
| NF-52 | New database columns must be backward-compatible: `load_trades()` must backfill None for missing columns in older rows |
| NF-53 | Every decision threshold (beta tiers, sector limits, single-name ceiling, composite boundaries, risk-per-trade, imminence windows) must be defined in `stock_analyzer/constants.py`. Features import from this module — they never hardcode threshold values. Changes are policy decisions, not code tuning. |
| NF-54 | Cross-feature coordination must use `st.session_state` caches. One feature publishes; downstream features read and gate. When a producer fails, the cache is set to `None` (not an empty container) so consumers can render an explicit "offline" state rather than silently disabling the gate. |
| NF-55 | Data integrity failures (missing stop, missing composite, missing daily briefing) must surface visibly to the user rather than being masked with fabricated fallback values. |
| NF-56 | Date-aware temporal chips on Grow Today cards. Every Grow Today card displays two pill chips at the bottom: the existing grey **⏱ First surfaced** chip and a new blue **🕐 Score as of** chip (color `#7dd3fc`, border `#1d4ed8`). The Score-as-of chip shows when the composite was last evaluated; the First-surfaced chip shows when the pick first appeared in Grow Today. Both chips apply the same date-aware formatting rule: if the timestamp's ET date equals today (`_et.date() == _today_et()`), show time only (e.g. `11:25 AM ET`); if it is a prior day, prepend the date (e.g. `Jul 14 · 4:58 PM ET`). This prevents overnight-cached or Supabase-fallback bundles from appearing to be today's evaluation. `_fmt_first_seen()` in `app.py` (covers all four First-surfaced call sites: new picks, add candidates, Evening Debrief ×2); `_score_as_of_str` in the Grow Today card render block. The `fetched_at` field is added to `load_bundle()`'s return dict in `stock_analyzer/bundle_loader.py` and flows through `composite_fetched_at` in the pick dict (`stock_analyzer/daily_briefing.py:845`) to the chip render. |

---

## 5. Out of Scope

- Order execution or brokerage integration
- Multi-user / multi-portfolio support
- Mobile app
- Real-time streaming data (WebSocket feeds)
- International or non-US equity markets
- Options, ETFs, or fixed income instruments
- Automated trading or algorithmic execution
- Day-trading / intraday support (Level 2, order flow, tick charts, momentum-only signals) — counter to the intended persona; see §2B
